# SketchUp Ruby API — Scene (Page) Serialisation Reference

```
Module   : 28__SourceCode__SceneDataTransfer
Plugin   : Na Noble3d Modelling Tools
Target   : SketchUp 2026 (Ruby 3.2.x)
Compiled : August 2026
Source   : ruby.sketchup.com, forums.sketchup.com, community.sketchucation.com,
           github.com/SketchUp/api-issue-tracker, github.com/SketchUp/ruby-api-stubs
```

This is the reference document for what a SketchUp **Scene** actually contains, what the
Ruby API exposes of it, and what can and cannot be moved between two `.skp` files.

It exists because the SketchUp Ruby API is inconsistent in ways that are not discoverable
from the method names. Several of the most important behaviours **fail silently** — no
exception, no warning, just a wrong result. Those are called out in bold throughout.

**How to read this document**

- **Parts 1–5** are the narrative: the verdict, the architecture, and the per-domain rules.
- **Part 6** is the gotcha index — every trap found during research, grouped by area. If you
  read only one section, read this one.
- **Part 7** is the generated API reference: every method, constant and dictionary key found,
  with exact spelling. Treat it as the lookup table, not as reading material.

---

## Part 1 — The feasibility verdict

The whole tool rests on one question:

> From model A, which is open, can Ruby read data previously written into model B, which is
> a `.skp` file sitting on disk?

### 1.1 What does NOT work

| Route | Verdict |
| --- | --- |
| `model.get_attribute` on an unopened file | **Impossible.** No such API exists. |
| Model-level dictionaries surviving `definitions.load` | **No.** They are not promoted across. |
| `Sketchup.open_file(path)` | **Destructive on Windows.** One document window — it closes model A. |
| `Sketchup::Model#import` for a `.skp` | **No.** Wraps in a component, places geometry, renames colliding materials. |
| Reading `.skp` metadata directly | **Only `Sketchup::Skp.read_guid`** (2021+), which returns a GUID and nothing else. |

The blunt statement from the SketchUcation archives is that Ruby can only read the open
model, and that reading models on disk requires the C API. Nothing in the 2021, 2022, 2023,
2024, 2025 or 2026 release notes changed this. Full external reading would need a compiled
Ruby C extension around `SUModelCreateFromFile` + `SUModelGetAttributeDictionary`.

On the model-level dictionary question specifically, DanRathbun's answer on the SketchUp
forum is definitive in both directions: when a definition is saved out to a `.skp` it becomes
the global model in the new file, model dictionaries must be **hand-copied down** onto the
definition before saving, and on re-insertion those dictionaries stay definition-level and
are **not recopied up** to the model object. The corroborating symptom reported by others is
that `model.description` and Dynamic Component attributes also come back empty after
`definitions.load`.

### 1.2 What DOES work

**Definition-level attribute dictionaries survive the trip.**

`Sketchup::DefinitionList#load(path)` pulls a `.skp` into the current model as a single
`ComponentDefinition` whose `entities` are the source model's reconstructed **root entities**.
Any `ComponentDefinition` inside that reconstruction keeps its own attribute dictionaries.

So the payload is written onto a purpose-built **carrier ComponentDefinition**, and a hidden,
locked **instance** of it is placed in the source model's root. The instance is not optional:

- `definitions.load` rebuilds the source model's *root entities*. A definition with no placed
  instance is not reachable from that reconstruction.
- An unreferenced definition is a casualty of any Purge Unused the user runs.
- A `ComponentInstance` is used rather than a `Group`, because a group's definition is
  silently made unique the moment the group is edited, which would orphan the attributes.
- **Instance-level** dictionaries are the classic trap and are lost on export/re-import.
  Always `instance.definition.set_attribute`, never `instance.set_attribute`.

### 1.3 The architecture this tool uses

```
MODEL B  (capture)                          MODEL A  (import)
─────────────────────                       ─────────────────────
walk model.pages                            copy B.skp -> unique temp path
  serialise each domain                     start_operation(disable_ui: true)
build payload Hash                            definitions.load(temp, allow_newer: true)
JSON -> deflate -> Base64                     find carrier definition in defn.entities
split into 16 KB chunks                       read + reassemble chunks -> JSON -> Hash
  write to Sketchup::Model      <-- local     definitions.remove(defn)
  write to carrier definition   <-- travels   abort_operation
user saves the .skp                           delete temp file
                                            pages.add(name, PAGE_USE_CAMERA | ...)
                                            mutate page.camera in place
```

The payload is written **twice** in model B on purpose. The model-level copy is what makes the
data readable and inspectable while model B is open. The carrier copy is the only one another
model can actually reach.

### 1.4 The four traps the reader must defend against

1. **`DefinitionList#load` caches on path.** It associates a definition with the path it came
   from and refuses to reload an unmodified path — on the second read you silently get a
   **stale** definition and therefore stale scene data. Mitigation: copy the source file to a
   uniquely named file in `Sketchup.temp_dir` for **every single read**.
2. **Model pollution.** Loading model B drags in all of its nested definitions and materials.
   Mitigation: run the probe inside an operation that is **always aborted, never committed**,
   remove the definition explicitly first, and compare the definition count before and after
   so any leak is reported rather than hidden.
3. **The undo corruption bug.** `start_operation` → `definitions.remove` → `commit_operation`
   → `Sketchup.undo` is a documented crash (api-issue-tracker #75). Use `abort_operation`;
   never commit-then-undo.
4. **`allow_newer` really defaults to `false`.** The documentation claiming `true` is wrong
   (api-issue-tracker #997). Pass it explicitly, and never write back to a file opened that way.

> **Never use `purge_unused` for cleanup.** It is indiscriminate and silently destroys every
> unused definition in the user's model. The docs themselves warn that silent purging causes
> data loss.

### 1.5 Prior art

Every existing extension that moves scenes between models does it through an **external
intermediary file**, not by reading the other `.skp`. `PageExIm.rb` (Smustard) exports and
imports scenes via an export file; the SketchUcation Camera Settings Export/Import plugin
writes a `.txt` and matches scenes by identical name on import. Nobody solved it by reading
the foreign `.skp` directly — which is strong corroboration that no clean Ruby route exists,
and that the carrier-definition approach is the right one.

---

## Part 2 — What a SketchUp Scene actually is

`Sketchup::Page` subclasses `Sketchup::Entity` — **not** `Drawingelement`. So it has
`entityID`, `persistent_id`, and attribute dictionaries, but it has **no** `hidden?`,
`visible?`, `layer`, `material` or `bounds`. `page.hidden?` raises `NoMethodError`. There is
no API to hide a scene tab.

A Page is a bundle of **eleven independently-toggleable property slots**. Each has a
`use_X?` predicate and a `use_X=` setter:

| Slot | Predicate | Flag constant | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Camera | `use_camera?` | `PAGE_USE_CAMERA` | |
| Rendering options | `use_rendering_options?` | `PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS` | Mostly owned by the Style |
| Shadow info | `use_shadow_info?` | `PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO` | One word — no underscore |
| Axes | `use_axes?` | `PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS` | **There is no `PAGE_USE_AXES`** |
| Hidden (legacy) | `use_hidden?` | `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN` | Deprecated, superseded by the two below |
| Tag visibility | `use_hidden_layers?` | `PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY` | Names do not match |
| Section planes | `use_section_planes?` | `PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES` | |
| Hidden geometry | `use_hidden_geometry?` | `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY` | SU2020.1+ |
| Hidden objects | `use_hidden_objects?` | `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS` | SU2020.1+ |
| Environment | `use_environment?` | `PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT` | SU2025.0+ |
| Style | `use_style?` | **none** | Sits entirely outside the flag system |

> `PAGE_*` constants live in the **top-level** namespace. Write `PAGE_USE_CAMERA`, never
> `Sketchup::PAGE_USE_CAMERA`.

Observed integer values: `PAGE_USE_CAMERA` 1, `PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS` 2,
`PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO` 4, `PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS` 8, `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN` 16,
`PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY` 32, `PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES` 64, `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY` 128,
`PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS` 256, `PAGE_USE_ALL` 4095, `PAGE_NO_CAMERA` 4094.
`PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT` is unpublished — by bit progression almost certainly 512, but treat
that as **unconfirmed**.

**Trimble's guidance is that constant values are not contractual across versions.** This tool
therefore stores flag **names** as strings and rebuilds the mask with `Object.const_get`,
guarded by `Object.const_defined?`. That also gives free forward-compatibility for
`PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT` and graceful degradation on older releases.

### 2.1 `Pages#add` — snapshot semantics

```ruby
add(name = nil, flags = PAGE_USE_ALL, index = self.size) -> Sketchup::Page
```

`add` does not merely record intent. **At the moment of creation it snapshots the current
model/view state for every bit set in `flags`, and switches those `use_*` slots ON. Slots
whose bit is absent are created OFF.**

That behaviour is exactly what this tool needs: passing only `PAGE_USE_CAMERA` produces a
scene that overrides the camera and *nothing else* — no style, no shadows, no fog, no tag
visibility. It is the mechanism behind the "toggle each aspect independently" requirement.

`index` is the only ordering control on SketchUp 2024 and older; `Pages#reorder` is 2025.0+.

### 2.2 `Page#update(flags)`

`update` **copies the current view/model state into the page** for each bit in `flags`, and
switches those slots on. It cannot take values as arguments — it can only take them from the
current view. Bits not passed are left alone, so `page.update(PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY)`
re-bakes only tag visibility, and `page.update(PAGE_NO_CAMERA)` re-bakes everything *except*
the camera.

On SketchUp 2026 you largely do not need it: the class documentation explicitly demonstrates
direct mutation of a page's axes, camera, shadow_info and rendering_options. You still need
`update` for state you cannot address directly — chiefly which section planes are active.

**Timing trap:** after changing the view, SketchUp may not have applied the change when
`update` runs, so the page bakes the *old* view. The documented workaround is to defer with
`UI.start_timer(0.1, false) { page.update(flags) }`, and to set
`model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition'] = false` around bulk work.

### 2.3 Order of operations — the rule that prevents silent failure

> **Set the `use_X` flag BEFORE writing the property.** Writing a property while its `use_X?`
> is false is the classic silent failure: the value goes nowhere and is discarded.

Flag-independent properties that can be set at any time: `name`, `description`, `delay_time`,
`transition_time`, `include_in_animation`, and attribute dictionaries.

Also note that `page.camera`, `page.rendering_options` and `page.shadow_info` return **live
objects even when their flag is false**, handing back plausible-looking values the scene will
never apply. Gate every export on the `use_X?` predicate, not on whether the accessor
returned something.

And `page.layers` / `page.layer_folders` return **`nil`, not an empty array**, when
`use_hidden_layers?` is false. This is the single biggest source of `NoMethodError` in
scene-export code.

---

## Part 3 — Domain by domain

### 3.1 Camera

**There is no `Camera#eye=` and no `Camera#up=`.** Position and orientation change only
through `Camera#set(eye, target, up)` or the constructor — the docs say all three must be
supplied together so the camera definition stays valid.

**There is no `Page#camera=` either.** Mutate the live object from `Page#camera` in place, or
set `view.camera=` then `page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA)`.

**The mandatory assignment order:**

| # | Assignment | Why it must come first |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `perspective=` | `fov` is valid only in perspective; `height` only in parallel |
| 2 | `aspect_ratio=` | It drives `fov_is_height?`, which decides whether `fov` is read vertically or horizontally |
| 3 | `image_width=` | `focal_length=` derives fov from the *current* `image_width` |
| 4 | `fov=` **or** `height=` | Exactly one branch, never both |
| 5 | `set(eye, target, up)` | A projection-mode flip can nudge the internal eye distance |

`aspect_ratio == 0.0` means "match the viewport", and then `fov` is the **vertical** angle.
Any non-zero `aspect_ratio` means `fov` is the **horizontal** angle. Assign `aspect_ratio`
after `fov` and the identical number is silently reinterpreted as a different angle.

**Reading the wrong property does not raise.** `fov` on a parallel camera, and `height` on a
perspective camera, both return a stale plausible-looking number. The C API signals this
(`SU_ERROR_NO_DATA`); the Ruby layer swallows it. Always branch on `perspective?`.

**Serialise `yaxis`, not `up`.** `up` can be parallel to the view direction, which is a
degenerate camera. `yaxis` is documented as the up direction recomputed to be perpendicular
to the direction, so it is valid by construction.

**Two-point perspective cannot be rebuilt.** `is_2d?`, `center_2d` and `scale_2d` are
read-only with no setters (api-issue-tracker #88, open since 2018, still unfixed in 2026). A
two-point scene round-trips as an ordinary perspective view without the lens shift —
verticals that were parallel in the source will converge in the destination. This tool
captures the values, detects the case, and warns rather than silently producing a wrong view.

**Units:** `eye` / `target` / `height` are raw **inches**; `image_width` and `focal_length`
are **millimetres**; `fov` is **degrees**. Inches are SketchUp's internal unit and are
independent of the model's display units, so coordinates transfer between models with
different unit settings with no conversion.

**Do not call `view.zoom_extents` or `view.zoom` after applying a restored camera** — both
overwrite eye, target and ortho height. A very common copy-paste bug.

`view.camera = [cam, 2.0]` animates, but **asynchronously** — a loop finishes in milliseconds
while the first animation is still running, so every intermediate camera is discarded. Use the
plain form for programmatic restore. `pages.selected_page = page` also starts an async
transition, which is why this tool never selects a page during capture or import.

### 3.2 Axes

`Sketchup::Axes` (SU2016+) is small and read-mostly: `origin`, `xaxis`, `yaxis`, `zaxis`,
`axes`, `to_a`, `transformation`, `sketch_plane`, and exactly one mutator —
`set(origin, xaxis, yaxis, zaxis)`. There is **no `Model#axes=` and no `Page#axes=`**; mutate
the existing object in place.

Trivially serialisable: four arrays of three floats (inches), restored with a single `set`.

### 3.3 Rendering options

`Sketchup::RenderingOptions` is a fixed-schema, hash-like wrapper over the model's style and
display state — roughly **69 String keys**. You cannot add or delete keys, only read and write
via `#[]` and `#[]=`. Values come back as exactly four Ruby classes: `TrueClass`/`FalseClass`,
`Integer`, `Float` and `Sketchup::Color`.

**Iterate the live keys at runtime (`ro.keys`) rather than hard-coding a list**, because the
key set differs between versions: the `AmbientOcclusion*` keys are 2024+/2026+,
`FaceColorMode` was removed in 2019.1, and `DrawHiddenGeometry`/`DrawHiddenObjects` are 2020+.

Rebuilding into a different model works well because every value is absolute and
model-independent: colours are RGBA 0–255, widths are pixel integers, fades are 0.0–1.0
floats, and `FogStartDist`/`FogEndDist` are floats in internal inches.

> **The structural trap:** a Page's rendering options are not really owned by the Page. The
> official note on `Page#rendering_options` says most of them are present in `Sketchup::Style`
> and governed by the selected style, and *should not be changed from the scene*. The ones not
> related to Style — chiefly fog (`DisplayFog`, `FogColor`) — are safe to set per scene.
> Blasting a full dump back into a page fights the style and gives non-deterministic results.

From SketchUp **2024.0**, `RenderingOptions#[]=` raises `ArgumentError` on a bad value.

Colours need a tagged encoding for JSON — e.g. `{"type":"Color","value":[r,g,b,a]}` — rebuilt
with `Sketchup::Color.new`.

### 3.4 Shadow info

`Sketchup::ShadowInfo` exposes **23 string keys**: geo-location (`City`, `Country`,
`Latitude`, `Longitude`, `TZOffset`, `NorthAngle`), the shadow instant (`ShadowTime`,
`ShadowTime_time_t`, `DayOfYear`, `DaylightSavings`), computed solar output (`SunDirection`,
`SunRise`, `SunRise_time_t`, `SunSet`, `SunSet_time_t`), and display toggles
(`DisplayShadows`, `DisplayOnGroundPlane`, `DisplayOnAllFaces`, `EdgesCastShadows`,
`DisplayNorth`, `UseSunForAllShading`, `Dark`, `Light`).

`SunDirection`, `SunRise` and `SunSet` are annotated in the docs as generated from
`ShadowTime` — they are **computed and read-only**, as are their `_time_t` twins.

> **SketchUp 2026.1 made `ShadowInfo#[]=` strict.** It now raises `KeyError` for an invalid
> *or read-only* key and `TypeError` for a wrong-typed value, where older versions silently
> returned `false`. A naive "loop over the JSON hash and assign every key" restore loop that
> worked on 2023 **will now raise** on `SunDirection`, `SunRise`, `SunSet` and the `*_time_t`
> keys. Restoring must use an explicit writable-key allowlist.

**`ShadowTime` is a Ruby `Time`.** The only representation that round-trips exactly across
machines and time zones is the integer epoch in `ShadowTime_time_t`. Any string from
`Time#to_s`, `#inspect` or an unqualified `strftime` bakes the exporting machine's OS time
zone into the payload and drifts on import.

**Scope trap:** the docs state plainly that north angle, latitude and longitude are
**model-level and not page-specific**. Writing `page.shadow_info['NorthAngle']` /
`'Latitude'` / `'Longitude'` / `'City'` inside a per-scene import loop silently mutates the
whole target model, once per scene. Write geo/north keys **once**; write only the genuinely
per-page keys per scene.

### 3.5 Styles

`Sketchup::Style` is one of the thinnest classes in the API. As of SketchUp 2026.2 it has
exactly **six** own instance methods: `name`, `name=`, `description`, `description=`,
`path` (2025.0+) and `duplicate` (2026.2+).

> **`Style#save_as` does not exist.** Neither does `Style#save`, `#thumbnail`, any watermark
> accessor, or any edge-settings accessor. Verified against both `ruby.sketchup.com` and the
> canonical `SketchUp/ruby-api-stubs` source. Any plan built on `save_as` fails with
> `NoMethodError`.

The actual visual settings of a style live in `Sketchup::RenderingOptions`. SketchUp staff
confirm the coupling: the rendering options are not saved to the Scene but to the Style.

`Sketchup::Styles` has `[]`, `active_style`, `active_style_changed` (**no question mark** —
`active_style_changed?` does not exist), `add_style`, `count`, `each`, `length`, `parent`,
`purge_unused`, `remove_style` (2026.0+), `selected_style`, `selected_style=`, `size`,
`update_selected_style`.

**Can you CREATE a named style from Ruby? Until SketchUp 2026.2, no. Now, yes.**

This is the single most-requested gap in the Styles API — `SketchUp/api-issue-tracker` issue
**#1026**, "Create new style or duplicate style (to create scenes with distinct rendering)".
For most of the API's life the answer was that there was *no* way to duplicate a loaded style
or create one from scratch; the GUI's "Save as a new Style" had no API equivalent, and the
only workaround was to ship pre-made `.style` files and load them.

**SketchUp 2026.2 added `Sketchup::Style#duplicate`** — *"creates a copy of the style with all
its properties"*, returning the newly created `Sketchup::Style`. That is the missing
primitive. Two other recent changes matter alongside it:

| Change | Version |
| --- | --- |
| `Sketchup::Style#duplicate` added | **2026.2** |
| `Styles#add_style` now returns the added `Style` instead of a Boolean | 2026.0 |
| `Styles#remove_style` added | 2026.0 |
| `Style#path` added | 2025.0 |

**Active style vs selected style — the distinction the whole technique rests on.**
`Styles#selected_style` is the style selected in the Styles Browser. `Styles#active_style` is
a *temporary working copy* holding uncommitted edits. Writing `model.rendering_options` edits
the **active** style; nothing is persisted into a real style until
`Styles#update_selected_style` is called, and that commits into whichever style is
**currently selected**. (`selected_style=` raises `ArgumentError` if handed the `active_style`.)

**The four working tiers, in order of preference:**

1. **Bind existing** — a style of that name is already in the target model.
2. **Load from file** — `Style#path` (2025.0+) recorded where the source style came from. If
   that `.style` file still exists, `Styles#add_style(path, false)` creates a genuine style.
3. **Duplicate + commit** (2026.2+) — duplicate any existing style to get a new `Style`
   object, rename it, select it, replay the captured rendering options onto
   `model.rendering_options`, then `update_selected_style` to commit them into it. **Save and
   restore the original selection**, because selecting a style changes what the user sees.
4. **Options only** — older SketchUp. The scene still looks right because its own rendering
   options carry the appearance, but no named style exists.

You still can never *export* a style: no Ruby method writes a `.style` file, so tier 2 only
helps when the source style was itself loaded from a file that is still on disk.

**Not reachable from Ruby and will not transfer:** watermarks, sketchy-edge stroke sets,
background images, photo-match overlays. Only the `DisplayWatermarks` on/off flag and the
`EdgeType` standard-vs-sketchy switch cross the boundary.

`page.use_style = x` **expects a `Sketchup::Style` object, not a boolean**, despite matching
the `use_X=` naming of ten sibling methods. It returns `nil`, and it is the only writer for a
page's style — there is no `style=`.

### 3.6 Section planes

`Sketchup::SectionPlane` is a `Drawingelement` with only eight own methods: `activate`,
`active?`, `get_plane`, `set_plane`, `name`, `name=`, `symbol`, `symbol=`.

> **It has no `#transformation` and no `#transform!`.** Its position and orientation are
> expressed solely as a plane array, in the coordinate system of whichever `Entities`
> collection owns it.

A plane is either `[a, b, c, d]` satisfying `ax + by + cz + d = 0`, or `[Point3d, Vector3d]`.
`Entities#add_section_plane` and `SectionPlane#set_plane` accept **either** form, but
`get_plane` **always returns the 4-float coefficient form**, with a unit-length normal and
`d` the negated signed distance from the origin along that normal, in inches. That makes it
trivially JSON-safe. Rebuild with `add_section_plane(point, vector)` where
`point = ORIGIN.offset(n, -d)` and `vector = n`.

**Scenes do not own section plane entities.** The planes are model geometry that exists
regardless of scenes; a Page merely records which plane was active in each `Entities`
collection. Which plane is active is per-collection state, read/written via
`Entities#active_section_plane` / `#active_section_plane=` (nil deactivates).

> There is **no `Model#active_section_plane`** (singular) — that setter exists only on
> `Sketchup::Entities`. SketchUp 2026 added the read-only `Model#active_section_planes` and
> `Page#active_section_planes` (plural), which finally allow reading a page's section state
> without activating the page. `Page#active_section_planes` returns `nil` when
> `use_section_planes?` is false.

`Page#set_visibility` is for Layers and LayerFolders **only** and never takes a SectionPlane.
Whether the cut is drawn is a RenderingOptions/style matter (`DisplaySectionPlanes`,
`DisplaySectionCuts`, `SectionCutFilled`, `SectionCutWidth`, `SectionCutDrawEdges` and the
four `Section*Color` keys) and is stored per-page under `use_rendering_options`, **not** under
the section-planes flag.

### 3.7 Tags (Layers)

A tag's own `visible?` is the **current model state**, not scene state. Per-scene tag
visibility is stored as a **delta**.

> **`Page#layers` is badly named.** It does *not* return the visible layers, nor the hidden
> ones. It returns "layers that don't use their default visibility on this page" — an
> exception list relative to each `Layer#page_behavior`.

The docs' own helper spells out the inversion: a layer is visible in the scene iff
`scene.layers.include?(layer) == hidden_by_default?(layer)`, where `hidden_by_default?` is
`layer.page_behavior & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT == LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT`.

**Serialise absolute booleans per tag name, never the raw array**, or scenes will invert when
the target model's tags have different defaults.

Writing is far simpler than reading: `Page#set_visibility(layer_or_folder, absolute_bool)`
does the delta arithmetic for you, and works **without activating the page**.

Constant values: `LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT` 0, `LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT` 1,
`LAYER_USES_DEFAULT_VISIBILITY_ON_NEW_PAGES` 0, `LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES` **16**,
`LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES` **32** (note: 16 and 32, not 2 and 4).

The only durable cross-model identifier is `Layer#name`. Recreate missing tags with
`Layers#add` before applying visibility, and folder paths with `Layers#add_folder` /
`LayerFolder#add_folder` (SU2021+).

### 3.8 Environments (HDRI / IBL)

Landed in **SketchUp 2025.0**, not 2026: `Sketchup::Environment`, `Sketchup::Environments`,
`Sketchup::EnvironmentsObserver`, `Model#environments`, and four `Page` methods
(`use_environment?`, `use_environment=`, `environment`, `environment=`). SketchUp 2026.0 added
only an `Environments#add(path)` single-argument overload.

> **Environment parameters are not stored per-Page as independent copies.** The model holds
> one live `Environment` object per collection entry, and a Page stores only a use flag plus a
> reference. Reading `page.environment.rotation` on a non-active page returns the **active**
> page's values. Reading a page's true environment state requires making it active with
> `PageOptions/ShowTransition` disabled.

---

## Part 4 — Attribute dictionaries as a data store

**Permitted value types are a closed list:** `Boolean`, `Integer`, `Float`, `Length`, `nil`,
`String`, `Time`, `Array`, `Geom::Point3d`, `Geom::Vector3d`. Arrays may nest and may contain
supported types.

> **`Hash` is NOT supported. Symbols are not supported.** Anything structural must be
> JSON-serialised to a `String`.

**There is no documented maximum String length**, but the file-size cost is severe and
superlinear in practice. One measured report: a single 100,000-character string grew a file
from 8 kB to 200 kB. A worse forum anecdote: 20 kB of attributes took a 3 MB model to 30 MB.

**Chunking is advisable above ~32 KB.** Split into ~16 KB slices across zero-padded numbered
keys plus an explicit `chunk_count`. **Reassemble by index `0...chunk_count`, never by
iterating the dictionary** — `AttributeDictionary#each` gives no ordering guarantee. Always
delete stale chunk keys before rewriting, or an old longer payload leaves orphans that bloat
the file permanently. `Zlib::Deflate` + `Base64.strict_encode64` typically shrinks scene JSON
by 80–90%.

**Other dictionary traps:**

- `entity.attribute_dictionaries` returns **`nil`**, not an empty collection, when the entity
  has none. Prefer `attribute_dictionary(name)` / `get_attribute`, which nil-guard for you.
- `attribute_dictionary(name)` defaults `create` to **false**. Forgetting the second argument
  is the most common cause of a silently failed write. `set_attribute` auto-creates.
- Do not mutate a dictionary while iterating with `#each` — elements get skipped. Snapshot
  `dict.keys` first.
- `AttributeDictionary#delete_key` removes **one key**; `AttributeDictionaries#delete`
  destroys an **entire dictionary**. Confusing them silently destroys the wrong thing.
- Reserved names: `"SU_InstanceSet"` and `"SU_DefinitionSet"` cannot be deleted;
  `"dynamic_attributes"` belongs to Dynamic Components; and **never create a dictionary with
  the empty-string name `""`** — the DC extension uses one.

**Operations:** `Model#start_operation(name, disable_ui, next_transparent, transparent)`.
A *transparent* operation merges into whatever preceded it, so settings writes never add a
stray step to the user's undo stack — this tool uses `start_operation(name, true, false, true)`
for all preference writes.

---

## Part 5 — Version gates (2023 → 2026)

| Feature | Version | Guard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`Style#duplicate`** — the only way to create a style from Ruby | **2026.2** | `respond_to?` |
| `Page#active_section_planes`, `Model#active_section_planes` | 2026.0 | `respond_to?` |
| `Styles#add_style` returns the `Style` (was Boolean) | 2026.0 | check return class |
| `Pages#unique_name`, auto-uniquing in `Pages#add` / `Page#name=` | 2026.0 | `respond_to?` |
| `Styles#remove_style` | 2026.0 | `respond_to?` |
| Page property edits become **undoable** | 2026.0 | wrap in `start_operation` |
| `ShadowInfo#[]=` raises `KeyError` / `TypeError` | 2026.1 | writable-key allowlist |
| `Pages#reorder`, `Style#path`, `AttributeDictionary#empty?` | 2025.0 | `respond_to?` |
| Environments API, `PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT` | 2025.0 | `defined?` |
| `RenderingOptions#[]=` raises `ArgumentError` | 2024.0 | validate before writing |
| Ruby 3.2.2 | 2024.0 | — |
| `DefinitionList#remove` | 2018 | `respond_to?` |

> **SketchUp 2026.0 undo change, verbatim in the docs:** modifying the `Sketchup::Axes`,
> `Sketchup::Camera`, `Sketchup::RenderingOptions` and `Sketchup::ShadowInfo` properties of a
> page is an undoable operation and should be wrapped between `Model#start_operation` and
> `Model#commit_operation`. Trimble states extensions that do not will be rejected from the
> Extension Warehouse. A full scene-set import that ignores this floods the undo stack with
> one entry per property per scene.

SketchUp 2026 still ships **Ruby 3.2.x** (confirmed locally: `x64-ucrt-ruby320.dll`). A
Trimble announcement dated 3 August 2026 says Ruby moves to 3.4 in an upcoming release,
shipping to SketchUp Labs first. Nothing in 2023–2026 changed attribute dictionaries or the
`.skp` format in a way that affects cross-model transfer.

**Use `respond_to?` for methods and `defined?` / `Object.const_defined?` for constants.** A
bare reference to an undefined constant raises `NameError` when that line is reached and will
kill the whole import.

---

## Part 6 — Gotcha index

Every trap found during research, grouped by area. Numbering restarts per area.

### Sketchup::Page and Sketchup::Pages

1. ORDER OF OPERATIONS — set use_* flags BEFORE writing the property, and prefer passing the flag mask to Pages#add. Pages#add(name, flags, index) does not merely record intent: at the moment of creation it snapshots the CURRENT model/view state for every bit in `flags` and switches those use_* slots ON. Slots whose bit is absent are created OFF. So the reliable sequence is: (1) call model.pages.add(name, explicit_flags) with the bits you want; (2) for any additional slot, set page.use_X = true FIRST; (3) only THEN write the property (page.camera.set(...), page.axes.set(...), page.shadow_info[k]=v, page.rendering_options[k]=v, page.set_visibility(layer, bool), page.use_style = style_obj, page.environment = env). Writing a property while its use_X? is false is the classic silent failure — the value goes nowhere visible and is discarded/ignored when the scene is activated. The flags that MUST be true before their write are: use_camera (camera), use_axes (axes), use_shadow_info (shadow_info), use_rendering_options (rendering_options), use_hidden_layers (set_visibility / layers / layer_folders), use_hidden_geometry and use_hidden_objects (set_drawingelement_visibility), use_section_planes (active section plane), use_environment (environment). The properties that are INDEPENDENT of all flags and can be set any time, before or after, are: name, description, delay_time, transition_time, include_in_animation, and attribute dictionaries. Style is a special case: it has no flag and no bit — assigning page.use_style = style_object is what turns use_style? true.
2. PAGES#ADD EXACT SIGNATURE: `add(name = nil, flags = PAGE_USE_ALL, index = self.size) → Sketchup::Page`. Three positional arguments only — no keywords, no block. name is a String (nil auto-generates a default name); flags is an Integer bitmask built by OR-ing PAGE_USE_* constants (default PAGE_USE_ALL = 4095 = every property); index is the 0-based insertion position (default = append at the end, which is how you control scene ORDER on SketchUp 2024 and older, since Pages#reorder only exists from SU2025). On SketchUp 2026.0+ the name is made unique automatically; on 2025 and older it was NOT (documented @bug), so duplicate scene names were possible and silently broke `pages['name']` lookups. Always capture the returned Page and re-read `.name` into your source_name→actual_name map.
3. WHAT HAPPENS WHEN A use_* FLAG IS FALSE: the scene simply does not override that aspect of the model when it is activated — the model's current/global value is used instead, and the scene's own stored value for that slot is not applied. Three concrete consequences you must code for: (1) page.layers and page.layer_folders return **nil, not an empty array**, when use_hidden_layers? is false — this is documented explicitly and is the #1 NoMethodError source in scene-export code; (2) page.active_section_planes (SU2026) returns nil when use_section_planes? is false; (3) accessors like page.camera, page.rendering_options and page.shadow_info still return live objects even when their flag is false, so reading them gives you plausible-looking values that the scene will never actually apply — always gate your export on the use_* predicate rather than on whether the accessor returned something. Toggling a flag false and then true again is not a safe round-trip: treat the stored value as undefined after a false→true transition and re-write the property explicitly.
4. HOW Page#update(flags) REALLY WORKS, and whether you need it. `update(flags)` COPIES the CURRENT VIEW / CURRENT MODEL state into the page for each bit set in flags, and switches the corresponding use_* slots ON. It does not take values as arguments and it cannot: as Aerilius put it on the SketchUp forum, 'the API lacks a way to specify the values of these properties, but it can only take them from the current view, which requires changing the camera of the active view.' Bits you do not pass are left alone, so `page.update(PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY)` re-bakes only tag visibility and leaves the saved camera untouched — and `page.update(PAGE_NO_CAMERA)` (4094) re-bakes everything EXCEPT the camera. The page does NOT need to be the selected page for update to work (Aerilius, same thread). IS IT REQUIRED? No, not universally — and on SketchUp 2026 much less than it used to be. Tag/tag-folder visibility never needs it (use page.set_visibility(layer, bool) directly). The SU2026 class documentation explicitly demonstrates direct mutation of a page's axes, camera, shadow_info and rendering_options — `page.axes.set(origin, Y_AXIS, X_AXIS, Z_AXIS); page.camera.fov = 56.78; page.shadow_info['City'] = 'Brasov, Romania'; page.rendering_options['BackgroundColor'] = 'Pink'` — so on your 2026 target you can write those slots in place without touching the active view at all. You still NEED update when you want the page to capture live model state you cannot address directly: which section planes are active, per-entity hidden state that you set via the model rather than via set_drawingelement_visibility, or when you deliberately want 'make this scene look like what I'm looking at now'. TIMING TRAP if you do use it: after changing the view (assigning model.active_view.camera, calling zoom_extents, applying a standard view, or setting pages.selected_page=) SketchUp may not have applied the change when update runs, and the page bakes the OLD view. DanRathbun's fix is to defer — UI.start_timer(0.1, false) { page.update(flags) } — and to set model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition'] = false around bulk work so scene transitions do not animate the camera out from under you.
5. THERE IS NO Page#camera= SETTER. The class exposes `camera` (getter) but no `camera=`. Your two options are: (a) mutate the returned Camera in place — page.camera.perspective = bool, then page.camera.set(eye, target, up), then fov= (perspective) or height= (parallel), then aspect_ratio= — which the SU2026 docs officially sanction and which does not disturb the user's view; or (b) build a Sketchup::Camera, assign model.active_view.camera = cam, then page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA), which is the long-standing forum-recommended pattern ('reset the view's camera as you iterate the pages, just before updating each page'). Set `perspective` BEFORE fov/height, because fov= is ignored in parallel projection and height= is ignored in perspective. Route (b) visibly moves the user's viewport, so bracket it: save model.active_view.camera first and restore it at the end.
6. Page IS NOT A Drawingelement. It subclasses Sketchup::Entity directly, so `hidden?`, `hidden=`, `visible?`, `visible=`, `layer`, `material` and `bounds` DO NOT EXIST on Sketchup::Page — calling page.hidden? raises NoMethodError. There is no API to hide a scene tab. What Page does inherit from Entity is: add_observer, attribute_dictionaries, attribute_dictionary, delete_attribute, deleted?, entityID, get_attribute, inspect, model, parent, persistent_id, remove_observer, set_attribute, to_s, typename, valid?.
7. IDENTITY KEYS ARE NOT PORTABLE. `entityID` (note the capitalisation — entityID, not entity_id) is session-local and changes on save/reload. `persistent_id` IS supported on Sketchup::Page (since SketchUp 2018, per the Entity docs' support table) and survives save/reload, but it is still MODEL-LOCAL and therefore worthless as a key when reconstructing scenes in a DIFFERENT model. For cross-model work key everything by name — Page#name for scenes, Layer#name for tags, Style#name for styles, Environment#name for environments — and stamp your own GUIDs into attribute dictionaries when you need to match individual Drawingelements for per-entity visibility overrides.
8. Page#layers IS BADLY NAMED AND WILL BURN YOU. It does NOT return 'the visible layers'; it returns 'layers that don't use their default visibility on this page' — i.e. the EXCEPTION list relative to each Layer's page_behavior. The docs' own helper spells out the inversion: a layer is visible in the scene iff `scene.layers.include?(layer) == hidden_by_default?(layer)`, where hidden_by_default? is `layer.page_behavior & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT == LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT`. Serialise ABSOLUTE booleans per tag name, never the raw array, or your scenes will invert when the target model's tags have different defaults. Relevant constant values: LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT = 0, LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT = 1, LAYER_USES_DEFAULT_VISIBILITY_ON_NEW_PAGES = 0, LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES = 16, LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES = 32 (note: 16 and 32, NOT 2 and 4). Rebuild with page.set_visibility(layer, absolute_bool), which takes the absolute value and hides the inversion for you — and which works WITHOUT activating the page.
9. use_style= IS NOT A BOOLEAN SETTER. Despite matching the use_X= naming of ten sibling methods, `page.use_style = x` expects a **Sketchup::Style object**, not true/false, and it returns nil. It is the only writer for a page's style (there is no `style=`). There is also no PAGE_USE_STYLE constant and no style bit for Page#update — the style slot sits entirely outside the flag system. Corollary for cross-model rebuild: locate or import the equivalent style in the TARGET model (model.styles.find { |s| s.name == name }, or model.styles.add_style(path, false)) before assigning; Style#guid is not portable.
10. SHADOWINFO IS PARTLY MODEL-LEVEL, NOT PAGE-LEVEL. The official note on Page#shadow_info states that while the Shadows-panel settings are per-page, 'global settings like north angle, latitude, and longitude are managed at the model level and are not page-specific.' So writing page.shadow_info['NorthAngle'] / 'Latitude' / 'Longitude' / 'City' during a per-scene import loop silently mutates the whole target model, once per scene. Write the geo/north keys ONCE from model.shadow_info (and only if the user asked for it); write only the genuinely per-page keys per scene — ShadowTime, DisplayShadows, Light, Dark, UseSunForAllShading, DisplayOnAllFaces, DisplayOnGroundPlane. ShadowTime is a Ruby Time: encode as Integer epoch (t.to_i) and decode with Time.at(i), because JSON has no Time type.
11. RENDERING OPTIONS ARE MOSTLY OWNED BY THE STYLE. The official note on Page#rendering_options: 'Most rendering options of a scene are also present in Sketchup::Style and are governed by the selected style. Those options should not be changed from the scene. The ones not related to Sketchup::Style like fog (DisplayFog, FogColor) are safe to be changed from the scene.' If you blast a full rendering_options dump back into a page you will fight the style and get non-deterministic results. Restore the style by name and only write the style-independent keys per scene. Also: rendering_options values include Sketchup::Color objects, which JSON.generate cannot round-trip — encode as tagged arrays ({"type":"Color","value":[r,g,b,a]}) and rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new.
12. CONSTANT SPELLINGS THAT DO NOT MATCH THEIR METHODS (these are literal Ruby names — one wrong character is a NameError): axes → PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS (there is NO PAGE_USE_AXES); shadows → PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO (one word, no underscore between SHADOW and INFO, while the method is use_shadow_info?); tags → PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY (while the methods are use_hidden_layers?/use_hidden_layers=); and there is no PAGE_USE_STYLE at all. All PAGE_* constants live in the TOP-LEVEL namespace — write PAGE_USE_CAMERA, never Sketchup::PAGE_USE_CAMERA. Full exact list (12): PAGE_NO_CAMERA, PAGE_USE_ALL, PAGE_USE_CAMERA, PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS, PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY, PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS, PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES, PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO, PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS.
13. FLAG INTEGER VALUES — use the names, but here they are: PAGE_USE_CAMERA = 1 (0x0001), PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS = 2 (0x0002), PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO = 4 (0x0004), PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS = 8 (0x0008), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN = 16 (0x0010, deprecated), PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY = 32 (0x0020), PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES = 64 (0x0040), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY = 128 (0x0080), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS = 256 (0x0100), PAGE_USE_ALL = 4095 (0x0fff), PAGE_NO_CAMERA = 4094 (0x0ffe). PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT's integer is NOT published anywhere I could reach — by bit progression it is almost certainly 512 (0x0200) but treat that as UNCONFIRMED. The 1–256 and 4094/4095 values come from a live-session constant dump (MSP-Greg's Constants Guide) cross-checked against the SketchUp C SDK header scene.h, whose FLAG_USE_* #defines the Ruby constants mirror exactly. Trimble's own guidance is that constant VALUES are not contractual across versions — so persist the flag set in your JSON as an array of constant NAME strings and rebuild the mask with Object.const_get(name) on import, guarded by defined?(). That also gives you free forward-compatibility for PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT.
14. VERSION GUARDS you need if the plugin must load on anything below 2026: active_section_planes and Pages#unique_name are SU2026.0+; Pages#reorder, Page#environment/environment=/use_environment?/use_environment= and PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT are SU2025.0+; Page#layer_folders and the set_visibility(layer_folder, bool) overload are SU2021.0+; use_hidden_geometry?/=, use_hidden_objects?/= and their two constants are SU2020.1+; get_drawingelement_visibility/set_drawingelement_visibility are SU2020.0+; include_in_animation?/= are SU2018+; Page#axes is SU2016+; Pages#length is SU2014+. Use page.respond_to?(:x) for methods and defined?(CONST) for constants — a bare reference to an undefined constant raises NameError at parse-of-that-line time and will kill your whole import.
15. SU2026 UNDO BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: 'Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying the Sketchup::Axes, Sketchup::Camera, Sketchup::RenderingOptions, and Sketchup::ShadowInfo properties of a page is an undoable operation and should be wrapped between Sketchup::Model#start_operation and Sketchup::Model#commit_operation.' Since your target IS 2026, wrap the ENTIRE scene-reconstruction pass in one start_operation/commit_operation (with disable_ui = true) or you will generate one undo entry per property write — hundreds of them for a full scene set — and the import will be visibly slow. Use abort_operation on any exception so a half-built scene set is not left behind.
16. Pages#show_frame_at CRASHES SketchUp outright (not an exception you can rescue) when called on a model with zero pages, in versions prior to SketchUp 2019. Guard with pages.size > 0. Separately, Pages#slideshow_time's source @return tag wrongly says 'status - true if successful' — it actually returns a Float number of seconds; the rendered docs have the correct type. And Pages#each returns nil, not the collection, so it cannot be chained; never erase while iterating it — snapshot with model.pages.to_a first.

### Sketchup::Camera and Sketchup::View

1. THE #1 TRAP — there is NO `eye=` and NO `up=` setter on Sketchup::Camera. Verified against both ruby.sketchup.com and the official SketchUp/ruby-api-stubs Camera.rb source. Position and orientation change ONLY via `Camera#set(eye, target, up)` or the constructor. The docs are explicit about why: 'You have to set the camera eye, target and up parameters at the same time to make sure that you have a valid camera definition.'
2. THE #2 TRAP — there is NO `Sketchup::Page#camera=` setter. You cannot assign a Camera object onto a scene. Either mutate the live `page.camera` reference in place (the official SU2026 pattern), or set `view.camera=` first and then `pages.add(name, flags)` / `page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA)`.
3. ASSIGNMENT ORDER — `perspective=` MUST precede `fov=` and `height=`. The docs say fov is 'only valid on a perspective camera' and height is 'only valid if it is not a perspective camera'. The docs' own height example puts `camera.perspective = false` on the line before `camera.height = 20`. Setting height on a still-perspective camera does not survive the later mode flip.
4. ASSIGNMENT ORDER — `aspect_ratio=` MUST precede `fov=`. aspect_ratio drives the read-only `fov_is_height?` flag: aspect_ratio 0.0 (match viewport) means fov is the VERTICAL angle; a non-zero explicit aspect_ratio means fov is the HORIZONTAL angle. Assign aspect_ratio after fov and the identical number is silently reinterpreted as a different angle and the view visibly changes. Eneroth3's view-lib confirms this by caching fov, setting aspect_ratio, then re-applying fov. The SketchUp team acknowledged the underlying inconsistency on the SDK forum ('Geting wrong fov for cameras') — the SDK doc claiming FOV is always vertical is wrong when an aspect ratio is set.
5. ASSIGNMENT ORDER — `image_width=` MUST precede `focal_length=`, because focal_length= computes fov from the focal length and the CURRENT image_width. Simplest fix: never call focal_length= in a restore. Store and restore `fov` directly and the whole hazard disappears.
6. SILENT WRONG VALUES, NOT EXCEPTIONS — reading `fov` on a parallel camera or `height` on a perspective camera does NOT raise in Ruby. The official ruby-api-stubs Camera.rb source contains ZERO @raise tags for the entire class and the docs page carries no exception note anywhere. SketchUp keeps both frusta internally, so you get a stale/default number (a plausible-looking 35.0-ish fov, or a stale ortho height). The equivalent C API calls DO signal the problem — SUCameraGetPerspectiveFrustumFOV and SUCameraGetOrthographicFrustumHeight both return SU_ERROR_NO_DATA for the wrong camera type — but the Ruby layer swallows it. Consequence: your serialiser will happily write a wrong value and your rebuilt scene will be subtly wrong with no error anywhere. ALWAYS branch on `perspective?` before reading fov or height.
7. TWO-POINT PERSPECTIVE IS UNRECONSTRUCTABLE. `is_2d?`, `center_2d` and `scale_2d` are read-only with no setters. SketchUp/api-issue-tracker issue #88 ('Missing methods for 2 point perspective', Eneroth3, 24 April 2018) requests `center_2d=`, `scale_2d=` and `is_2d=`; it is still OPEN, and was re-confirmed unavailable on the forum in June 2023. A two-point-perspective scene will round-trip as an ordinary perspective scene with the same eye/target/up/fov but WITHOUT the lens shift — verticals that were parallel in the source will converge in the destination. Detect `is_2d?` on export, record the three values so nothing is lost, and warn the user explicitly rather than silently producing a wrong view.
8. UNITS — `height` is in INCHES (SketchUp internal units) and `eye`/`target` coordinates are raw inch Floats. `image_width` and `focal_length` are in MILLIMETRES. `fov` is in DEGREES. Never round-trip lengths through Length#to_s or the model's display-unit settings; use `.to_a` on Point3d/Vector3d to get bare Floats, and write those to JSON. A model whose display units differ between source and destination will otherwise silently rescale your camera.
9. `image_width` of 0 is a SENTINEL meaning 36 mm, not a real zero. 0 and 36.0 produce identical focal_length results but are different stored values — preserve the raw value rather than normalising, or your round-trip will not be exact.
10. `Camera#focal_length` is documented (and stubbed) as `focal_length(length)` taking an argument, but the docs' own example calls it with no arguments (`camera.focal_length`). This is a long-standing documentation/stub bug. Call the zero-arg form. Also, focal_length is purely derived from fov and image_width — do NOT serialise it as state.
11. `view.camera=` ANIMATION SYNTAX — `view.camera = [cam, 2.0]` works (a single Array argument). `view.send(:camera=, cam, 2.0)` FAILS with `ArgumentError: wrong number of arguments (2 for 1)`. More importantly the transition is ASYNCHRONOUS and does NOT block: a loop that assigns several cameras with transition times finishes in milliseconds while the first animation is still running, so every intermediate camera is discarded. Use the plain no-transition form for programmatic restore.
12. `pages.selected_page = page` also starts an asynchronous animated transition. Calling `page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA)` right after selecting a page can therefore capture a MID-TRANSITION camera and store garbage. Mutate `page.camera` directly instead and sidestep the whole race.
13. PERSISTENCE — DanRathbun's rule: in a model that contains scene pages, setting `view.camera=` alone does NOT survive save-and-reopen; SketchUp restores the selected page's camera on open. You must also call `model.pages.selected_page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA)` (or write the camera onto the page).
14. `page.use_camera?` must be TRUE or the scene ignores its stored camera entirely — the tab will not move the view, with no error raised. Capture and restore this flag, and set `page.use_camera = true` on rebuilt pages.
15. SKETCHUP 2026 SPECIFIC — modifying a Page's Axes, Camera, RenderingOptions or ShadowInfo is now an UNDOABLE operation and must be wrapped in `model.start_operation` / `model.commit_operation`, or you will flood the undo stack with one entry per property per scene. The official Page docs example demonstrates exactly this.
16. Cameras cannot be `clone`d or `dup`ed into an independent copy — described on the SketchUp Community forum as 'a very old known issue'. You must rebuild via the constructor from saved values. This is a point in favour of your JSON approach, not against it.
17. DEGENERATE CAMERA RISK — if the restored `up` vector ends up parallel to `(target - eye)`, the camera definition is invalid and SketchUp's behaviour is undefined. Best defence: serialise `cam.yaxis.to_a` INSTEAD of `cam.up.to_a`. yaxis is documented as 'equivalent to the up direction, but is computed to make sure that it is perpendicular to the direction', so it is guaranteed valid by construction.
18. `view.zoom_extents` and `view.zoom` DESTROY a restored camera (they overwrite eye, target, and ortho height). Never call them after applying a serialised camera — a very common copy-paste bug from tutorial code.
19. Setting `fov = 0` does NOT switch a camera to parallel projection (a documented SketchUCation dead end). Only `perspective = false` does. Also note that mutating a DETACHED camera object never repaints the viewport — you must still assign `view.camera = cam`.
20. `aspect_ratio` 0.0 is viewport-dependent, so a scene captured on a 16:9 viewport and restored on a 4:3 viewport will frame differently even with a perfect round-trip. Capture `view.vpwidth`/`view.vpheight` as context so your importer can warn about this. A NON-zero explicit aspect_ratio is actually the more portable choice — it reproduces identically regardless of window size.
21. Per Eneroth3's view-lib, ortho `height` is measured against the VIEWPORT aspect ratio (grey bars included), so visible width is `height * (view.vpwidth / view.vpheight.to_f)` — NOT `height * camera.aspect_ratio`. Getting this wrong produces ortho scenes that are correctly positioned but wrongly zoomed.
22. Beware the `perspective?` (getter, with `?`) versus `perspective=` (setter, no `?`) spelling. Likewise `fov_is_height?`, `is_2d?` and `use_camera?` all carry the `?`. `Sketchup::Camera#description` is the CAMERA's description and is a completely different value from `Sketchup::Page#description` (the scene description in the Scenes manager) — capture both.
23. `PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS` is the AXES flag, despite the name suggesting a coordinate-system-of-sketch. Reference the PAGE_USE_* constants by NAME, never by hard-coded integer — the numeric values are undocumented and the flag set grew in SU2020 (PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS) and later (PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT).
24. Prior to SketchUp 2026.0, `Pages#add` did NOT make a duplicate name unique (documented Known Bug). If you support older releases, call `pages.unique_name(base_name)` yourself before adding.
25. In a multi-model session (common on macOS), `Sketchup.active_model` follows the focused window and can change between your export and import steps. Since your whole workflow is 'read from model A, write into model B', hold explicit model references and go through `that_model.active_view`, never a cached `Sketchup.active_model`.
26. No new Camera methods have been added since SketchUp 2015 (which introduced center_2d, is_2d?, scale_2d and fov_is_height?). The SU2026 release notes contain no Camera additions — only the Page-property undoability change. So a serialiser written against this surface is stable, and the two-point-perspective gap is not fixed in 2026.

### Sketchup::RenderingOptions

1. KEY THAT DOES NOT EXIST — 'ExtendLinesAmount'. Your candidate list includes it, but it is NOT a RenderingOptions key in any SketchUp version. It appears in neither the Ruby doc comment, the C API SURenderingOptionsRef key table, nor any runtime dump. The edge-extension AMOUNT key is 'LineExtension' (Integer pixels); the on/off key is 'ExtendLines' (Boolean). Writing 'ExtendLinesAmount' silently no-ops on SketchUp 2023 and earlier and raises ArgumentError on 2024+.
2. KEY THAT DOES NOT EXIST HERE — 'DisplayNorth'. This is a Sketchup::ShadowInfo key, NOT a RenderingOptions key: Sketchup.active_model.shadow_info['DisplayNorth']. Confirmed by the bundled su_solarnorth extension, which reads and writes shadow_info['DisplayNorth']. Include it in your ShadowInfo serialiser, not your RenderingOptions one. Its companion is shadow_info['NorthAngle'] (Float degrees).
3. DOCUMENTATION BUG — 'ROPDrawHiddenGeometry' / 'ROPDrawHiddenObjects' are NOT keys. The official ruby.sketchup.com RenderingOptions page (and the ruby-api-stubs comment it is generated from) lists these ROP-prefixed names under 'Added in SketchUp 2020.0' in the rendering-KEY list. They are actually the integer OBSERVER CONSTANTS. The real dictionary keys are the un-prefixed 'DrawHiddenGeometry' and 'DrawHiddenObjects'. Verified against the SketchUp C API key table and a runtime observer dump on the SketchUp forum. Using the ROP-prefixed spelling as a key is silently ignored pre-2024 and raises ArgumentError on 2024+.
4. REMOVED KEY — 'FaceColorMode' was removed in SketchUp 2019.1 ('previously included but it was ineffective'). On a 2026 target, reading it returns nil and writing it raises ArgumentError. Do NOT include it in a hard-coded key list. Note the trap that the observer constant ROPSetFaceColorMode STILL EXISTS in 2026 — the presence of a ROP* constant is not evidence that the corresponding key exists.
5. BREAKING CHANGE IN SKETCHUP 2024.0 — RenderingOptions#[]= now raises ArgumentError when the key is invalid, the value is not settable, or the value is the wrong type/range. Before 2024.0 these all failed SILENTLY. This is the single biggest risk to a cross-model restore: one stale key aborts your entire loop. Every assignment in a restore routine MUST be individually wrapped: begin; ro[k] = v; rescue ArgumentError, TypeError, StandardError => e; log(k, e); end.
6. BREAKING CHANGE IN SKETCHUP 2026.0 — modifying Sketchup::Axes, Sketchup::Camera, Sketchup::RenderingOptions and Sketchup::ShadowInfo OF A Sketchup::Page is now an UNDOABLE operation. Scene changes must be made between model.start_operation and model.commit_operation or you flood the undo stack. The release notes state that extensions which do not comply 'will be rejected for updates or publication to the Extension Warehouse'. Wrap your whole scene-rebuild in a single start_operation('Restore Scenes', true) ... commit_operation.
7. MODEL vs PAGE — the crucial difference. model.rendering_options is the LIVE viewport state: fully readable and writable, repaints immediately, and is what the Styles dialog edits. page.rendering_options is a SEPARATE RenderingOptions instance holding that scene's SAVED snapshot — reading it does NOT require activating the scene, which makes it the correct, flicker-free source for a per-scene JSON dump. It is a distinct object, not an alias, and reading model.rendering_options after selecting a page gives you the applied result, not the stored record.
8. IS Page#rendering_options WRITABLE? Technically yes — it is a normal (non-frozen) RenderingOptions object and []= will accept writes. In practice it is UNRELIABLE and officially discouraged. The Page docs state: 'Most rendering options of a scene are also present in Style and are governed by the selected style. Those options should not be changed from the scene. The ones not related to Style like fog (DisplayFog, FogColor) are safe to be changed from the scene.' SketchUp staff on the forum confirm 'rendering options aren't saved directly to the Scene but to the Style'. So a page-level write to a Style-owned key can be reverted the instant the page's style reasserts itself. Only fog keys are genuinely safe to poke directly on a page.
9. THE CORRECT WRITE RECIPE for restoring scene rendering state into a different model — do NOT just assign to page.rendering_options. Sequence: (1) ensure the destination has a Style to own the settings — model.styles.selected_style = some_style, or model.styles.add_style(path, false); (2) write the values to model.rendering_options[key] = value; (3) model.styles.update_selected_style — THIS IS THE STEP EVERYONE MISSES, without it your writes are a transient override; (4) page.use_rendering_options = true and page.use_style = style; (5) page.update. Passing PAGE_USE_ALL to page.update without step (3) is the documented failure mode on the SketchUp forum.
10. ENUM — EdgeColorMode (Integer): 0 = ObjectColor (UI 'By material'), 1 = ForegroundColor (UI 'All same', uses the ForegroundColor key), 2 = DirectionColor (UI 'By axis'). Validate 0..2 before assigning.
11. ENUM — EdgeDisplayMode (Integer): 0 = EdgeDisplayNone (no edges at all), 1 = EdgeDisplayAll, 2 = EdgeDisplayStandalone (only loose/standalone edges). Validate 0..2. Restore this before DrawProfilesOnly / DrawSilhouettes.
12. ENUM — EdgeType (Integer): 0 = Standard edges, 1 = Sketchy edges (NPR). This is the ONE enum whose meanings are stated in the official Ruby docs. Caveat: the sketchy STROKE SET is Style data, not a RenderingOptions value, so EdgeType = 1 in a destination model gives sketchy mode with that model's default strokes.
13. ENUM — RenderMode (Integer): 0 = RenderWireframe, 1 = RenderHidden (Hidden Line), 2 = RenderFlat (Shaded), 3 = RenderSmooth (Shaded With Textures), 4 = RenderTextureObsolete (DO NOT WRITE — legacy), 5 = RenderNoMaterials (Monochrome), 6 = RenderPhysicallyBased (NEW in SketchUp 2025.0 / C API 13.0). Value 6 will raise ArgumentError on SketchUp 2024 and earlier — gate it and fall back to 3. X-ray is NOT a RenderMode value; it is the separate Boolean key ModelTransparency.
14. ENUM — FaceColorMode: removed in SketchUp 2019.1 and was documented as ineffective even before that. No usable integer meanings; do not serialise.
15. ENUM — TransparencySort (Integer): 0 = Faster, 1 = Medium, 2 = Nicer. The official docs add: 'SketchUp 2017 treats Medium transparency as Faster', so 0 and 1 render identically on any modern build. ARGUABLY NOT DOCUMENT STATE — it tracks an OpenGL performance preference, so consider excluding it from cross-model scene restore rather than stomping the destination user's setting.
16. TYPE TRAP — three keys look like Booleans or Floats but are Int32: GroundTransparency (0-100 percent, Integer), LineExtension (pixels, Integer), and all the *Width keys (DepthQueWidth, LineEndWidth, SectionCutWidth, SilhouetteWidth — all Integer pixels, NOT Lengths).
17. TYPE TRAP — JSON Integer/Float collapse. InactiveFade, InstanceFade, FogStartDist, FogEndDist, AmbientOcclusionDistance, AmbientOcclusionIntensity and AmbientOcclusionMultiplier are all Float (Double). If JSON gives you back a bare 0 or 1, Ruby produces an Integer and SketchUp 2024+ raises ArgumentError. ALWAYS coerce: ro[k] = v.to_f for the Float set, ro[k] = v.to_i for the Integer set. Store the Ruby class name in your JSON per key so the decoder knows which coercion to apply — do not infer from the JSON literal.
18. FOG DISTANCES ARE LENGTHS, NOT RATIOS. FogStartDist and FogEndDist are Floats in SketchUp's internal unit (inches) — confirmed by working plugin code (Eneroth Fog Tool) which calls ro['FogStartDist'].to_l. Because SketchUp's internal unit is always inches regardless of the document's display units, the raw Float transfers between models unchanged. But a fog range tuned for a 5 m house is meaningless in a 5 km site model — consider normalising against the model bounding box diagonal if the target model differs in scale. Also restore Start and End together, ordering the two writes so you never momentarily set End below Start (SketchUp clamps).
19. OBSERVER BLIND SPOTS — do NOT build a change-tracking serialiser on RenderingOptionsObserver alone. These keys are confirmed NOT to fire onRenderingOptionsChanged: BandColor, DisplaySectionCuts, FaceBackColor, FogEndDist, HorizonColor, InactiveFade, InactiveHidden, InstanceFade, SectionCutDrawEdges, ShowViewName. Always do a full ro.each / ro.keys sweep. Conversely, remove your observer before a bulk restore loop or ~69 writes each fire a callback.
20. NEVER HARD-CODE THE KEY LIST. Call ro.keys at dump time and store that array in your JSON, then on restore compute (stored_keys & destination_ro.keys) before writing. This single practice automatically neutralises FaceColorMode (gone 2019.1), the AmbientOcclusion trio (2024+), AmbientOcclusionColor/Multiplier (2026+), DrawHiddenGeometry/Objects (2020+), SectionCutFilled/SectionDefaultFillColor (2018+), DisplaySectionCuts/DrawBackEdges/SectionCutDrawEdges (2015+) and DisplaySectionPlanes (2014+).
21. ORDERING MATTERS ON RESTORE. Dependent pairs where the master flag can reset the amount: set RenderMode before Texture; set EdgeDisplayMode before DrawProfilesOnly and DrawSilhouettes; set DrawSilhouettes before SilhouetteWidth; set ExtendLines before LineExtension; set DrawLineEnds before LineEndWidth; set DrawDepthQue before DepthQueWidth; set DrawGround before GroundColor/GroundTransparency/DrawUnderground; set DisplayFog before FogColor/FogUseBkColor/FogStartDist/FogEndDist. Safest practical approach: apply the whole set TWICE in one operation — the second pass fixes anything the first pass' master flags reset.
22. SKIP DrawHidden ON RESTORE for 2020+ targets. It is the legacy combined toggle; writing it after DrawHiddenGeometry and DrawHiddenObjects will clobber both. Serialise all three for fidelity but only WRITE the two modern keys on a 2020+ destination.
23. STATE THAT IS *NOT* IN RenderingOptions and will silently be lost in a cross-model rebuild: watermark images and sketchy-edge stroke sets (both live in Sketchup::Style, transferable only by moving the .style file via Styles#add_style); Match Photo background/overlay images and opacities (ROPSetPhotomatch* constants exist but no Ruby keys); X-ray opacity amount (ROPSetXRayOpacity exists but no key); the environment / IBL setting (Sketchup::Page#environment, added SketchUp 2025); which section planes are active (Model#active_section_planes / Page#active_section_planes, added 2026); tag/layer colours behind DisplayColorByLayer; camera; and shadow settings (Sketchup::ShadowInfo, a separate dictionary).
24. Sketchup::RenderingOptions is a fixed-schema collection, not a Hash. There is no #merge, #update, #store, #delete, #has_key?, #fetch, #to_h or #include?. #each / #each_key / #each_pair all return nil (not self, not an Enumerator), so you cannot chain — build your Hash imperatively. Use ro.keys.include?(k) for existence checks. It also inherits from Sketchup::Entity, so it responds to attribute-dictionary methods, but do NOT store your JSON there — put it on the model via model.attribute_dictionary/set_attribute.
25. COLOUR ALPHA is real and must be preserved. Sketchup::Color#to_a returns FOUR Integers (R, G, B, A) each 0-255, and #alpha is documented as 0 = transparent, 255 = opaque. Round-trip all four. Do NOT use Sketchup::Color#to_i for serialisation: the docs warn that when assigning via a hexadecimal 'R and B will be flipped' and 'Alpha is not supported'. The Integer/hex path is lossy and channel-swapped — always use the RGBA Integer array.
26. The stubs declare each_key and keys as BOTH class methods (Sketchup::RenderingOptions.keys) and instance methods. Only the instance forms are real and usable; the class-method entries are a YARD/documentation artefact. Always call them on an instance.

### Sketchup::ShadowInfo

1. MISSING KEY IN YOUR LIST: DayOfYear. The official docs list 23 keys; your list of 22 omits DayOfYear. Full verified set, exact capitalisation: City, Country, Dark, DayOfYear, DaylightSavings, DisplayNorth, DisplayOnAllFaces, DisplayOnGroundPlane, DisplayShadows, EdgesCastShadows, Latitude, Light, Longitude, NorthAngle, ShadowTime, ShadowTime_time_t, SunDirection, SunRise, SunRise_time_t, SunSet, SunSet_time_t, TZOffset, UseSunForAllShading. Sketchup::ShadowInfo#length / #count / #size return 23 — assert this at load.
2. READ-ONLY SET CONFIRMED FROM THE DOCS: SunDirection, SunRise and SunSet are each annotated verbatim "(Generated based on ShadowTime)" on ruby.sketchup.com. Your suspicion is correct. Their epoch twins SunRise_time_t and SunSet_time_t are the same derived quantities and are equally read-only. DayOfYear is also derived from ShadowTime and should be treated as read-only even though the docs do not annotate it — this is the one genuine ambiguity in the whole key set, so probe it defensively rather than assuming.
3. SU2026 BREAKING CHANGE — THE BIG ONE. Release notes: "Modified Sketchup::ShadowInfo#[]= to be stricter and provide better error feedback. It will now raise a KeyError for invalid or read-only keys, and a TypeError for values of an incorrect type." Before 2026 the same call silently returned false. A restore loop of the form json.each { |k,v| shadow_info[k] = v } that worked for a decade will now RAISE on the first read-only key and abandon the scene half-restored. Filter to a writable whitelist and rescue per-key.
4. SU2026 BREAKING CHANGE #2 — UNDOABILITY. Release notes: "Modifying the properties Sketchup::Axes, Sketchup::Camera, Sketchup::RenderingOptions, and Sketchup::ShadowInfo of a Sketchup::Page is now an undoable operation. Scene changes are expected to be called between Sketchup::Model#start_operation and Sketchup::Model#commit_operation to not flood the undo stack. Extensions that don't follow this requirement will be rejected for updates or publication to the Extension Warehouse." This is an EW publication gate, not a suggestion. Restoring 30 scenes without an operation wrapper produces hundreds of undo entries.
5. LIVE 2026 DEFECT ON THE KEY YOU MOST NEED. SketchUp/api-issue-tracker issue #1086, "Sketchup::ShadowInfo#[]= now inconsistent in Sketchup 2026": VR Sketch collected ~5 automated crash reports from `@model.shadow_info["ShadowTime_time_t"] = new_time` raising `KeyError: Invalid key: 'ShadowTime_time_t'` on 2026.1. "Usually it works as expected" — the failure is sporadic and unexplained, with no SketchUp-staff response. Your importer MUST rescue KeyError on this write and fall back to setting ShadowTime from Time.utc(y,mo,d,h,mi,s). Do not ship without that fallback.
6. WHICH TIME REPRESENTATION ACTUALLY ROUND-TRIPS — THE ANSWER: the Integer epoch (ShadowTime_time_t), not a formatted string. A JSON Integer is exact and carries no zone semantics at all. A formatted string is corrupted at BOTH ends: producing it via Time#to_s / Time#inspect / strftime on a non-UTC Time renders in the EXPORTING process's OS zone, and consuming it via Time.parse / Time.local re-interprets in the IMPORTING process's OS zone. Export in London in August and import in Denver and you move the sun by 7 hours = 105 degrees of azimuth. If you are forced to use a string for human readability, the only safe form is t.utc.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S") reconstructed with Time.utc(*parts) — with NO %z/%Z token and NO Time.parse.
7. NEVER LET A RAW Time REACH JSON.generate. Ruby's JSON has no Time type and falls back to Time#to_s, silently emitting something like "2026-08-26 14:00:00 +0100". It looks fine in the payload and is wrong on the other machine. Same trap for Geom::Vector3d (SunDirection) and Geom::Point3d — convert with .to_a or explicit [x,y,z] floats before generating.
8. ShadowTime IS NOT PLAIN LOCAL TIME AND IS NOT PLAIN UTC — READ IT WITH .utc. The wall-clock shown in Window > Shadows is recovered by calling .utc on the Time you get back. Dan Rathbun documented on Sketchucation that SunRise/SunSet come back with "the hour ... off by 1 difference to UTC (GMT)" and that "the time returned by ShadowInfo['ShadowTime'] is currently (on DST,) always 4 hours less than the 'SliderTime' in the Shadow Settings inspector dialog" — exactly his OS offset, suggesting the code "subtracts twice instead of once". The accepted workaround from Wehby: "Try modifying your ShadowTime with '.utc' to fix the issue." Practical rule: construct with Time.utc, read with .utc, never touch Time.local / Time.parse / #localtime.
9. BECAUSE THE .utc SEMANTICS ARE A QUIRK AND NOT A CONTRACT, VERIFY THE ROUND TRIP. Store BOTH the Integer epoch AND the decomposed UTC parts [y,mo,d,h,mi,s]; after writing, read shadow_info["ShadowTime"].utc back, compare the six components, and if they disagree re-write via Time.utc(*parts). That verify-and-repair step is what makes the serialiser genuinely safe across SketchUp point releases rather than merely correct on your machine today.
10. TZOffset IS NOT THE EXPORTING MACHINE'S TIME ZONE. It is a per-model property describing the SITE's civil offset from UTC (in SketchUp's locations.dat it is the fifth field, "referenced to UTC"). Never derive it from Time.now.utc_offset. SketchUp's solar solver reads ShadowTime's instant TOGETHER with TZOffset (and DaylightSavings, Latitude, Longitude) — transfer the epoch without transferring TZOffset and the sun rotates 15 degrees per hour of mismatch even though the timestamp is byte-identical.
11. TZOffset MUST BE WRITTEN AS A FLOAT. The accepted forum answer is literally shadow_info["TZOffset"] = -5.0, with the .0. Integer -5 was historically unreliable, and half/quarter-hour zones (India 5.5, Nepal 5.75, Newfoundland -3.5) are unrepresentable as Integer anyway. Same reasoning applies to Latitude, Longitude and NorthAngle — coerce with .to_f. Conversely write Dark and Light as Integer, since 2026's "accepts any Numeric" leniency does not exist on older hosts you may still support.
12. ORDER OF WRITES MATTERS. Correct sequence on restore: (1) Country, (2) City, (3) Latitude, (4) Longitude, (5) TZOffset, (6) DaylightSavings, (7) NorthAngle — all on model.shadow_info — then (8) ShadowTime_time_t / ShadowTime on the page, then (9) UseSunForAllShading, Dark, Light, DisplayOnGroundPlane, DisplayOnAllFaces, EdgesCastShadows, and (10) DisplayShadows LAST. Writing DisplayShadows first forces a full shadow recompute and viewport redraw on every subsequent key, which is agonisingly slow across many scenes; and writing ShadowTime before the location keys means the derived SunDirection/SunRise/SunSet are computed against the wrong site and must be recomputed anyway.
13. MODEL-LEVEL vs PAGE-LEVEL IS EXPLICIT IN THE DOCS AND MUST BE ENCODED IN YOUR SCHEMA. Page#shadow_info carries the Note: "While certain shadow settings, such as those available in the Shadows panel, can be controlled on a per-page basis, global settings like north angle, latitude, and longitude are managed at the model level and are not page-specific." So structure your JSON as { "model_shadow": {...geo keys...}, "pages": [ { "name":..., "use_shadow_info":..., "shadow": {...per-scene keys...} } ] }. A flat per-page dump of all 23 keys will write geo keys 30 times and produce whichever scene happened to be last.
14. PER-PAGE TZOffset IS STORED BUT IGNORED. A Sketchucation thread demonstrated model.pages.each { |p| puts "#{p.name} - TZ: #{p.shadow_info['TZOffset']}" } returning genuinely different values per page (1.0 and 2.0), while the user observed "when I select that page and go into model info it still says GMT+2". Since SketchUp 7 the time zone is effectively model-global regardless of what the page object holds. Do not attempt per-scene time zones; if your scenes span zones, encode a single model TZOffset and shift the per-scene epoch instead.
15. page.use_shadow_info = true BEFORE WRITING PER-PAGE KEYS. A page that is not storing shadow info may discard or pass through your writes. And use_shadow_info? is itself a scene property that must be in your JSON — a scene with shadows disabled behaves entirely differently on activation from one with shadows saved-and-off.
16. MORE ROBUST ALTERNATIVE TO PER-PAGE WRITES: set model.shadow_info to the scene's target state, make the page current, then call page.update(PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO). This lets SketchUp snapshot its own state and sidesteps every per-page write quirk. Note the constant's spelling — PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO, one word, no underscore before INFO, unlike PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS.
17. EXACT CAPITALISATION IS NOW LOAD-BEARING IN A NEW WAY. Under SU2026, a typo no longer returns false — it raises KeyError and kills your restore. Real-world typos seen in the wild: "Displayshadows" (lowercase s) for DisplayShadows; "DisplayOnFaces" for DisplayOnAllFaces; "Sunset" for SunSet; "SunRise_Time_T" for SunRise_time_t; "ShadowTime_t" for ShadowTime_time_t; "UseSunForShading" for UseSunForAllShading. Validate every key in your whitelist against Sketchup::ShadowInfo.keys at plugin load and fail loudly in development rather than at a user's restore.
18. SETTING ShadowInfo Latitude/Longitude DOES NOT MAKE Model#georeferenced? TRUE. Georeferencing is driven by the separate, undocumented "GeoReference" model attribute dictionary. So a cross-model scene restore reproduces SUN POSITION faithfully while leaving the target model non-georeferenced — terrain, imagery alignment and Add Location state are NOT reproduced. Record model.georeferenced? in your payload as an informational flag and warn on import.
19. COPYING THE GeoReference DICTIONARY IS ALL-OR-NOTHING. Community report: "SketchUp will crash if you don't set all of the GeoReference attributes." A partial write is worse than no write. The reported keys (UsesGeoReferencing, Latitude, Longitude, GeoReferenceNorthAngle, ModelTranslationX/Y/Z, LocationSource, ZValueCentered, TimeStamp, ModelHereState, ModelHereZoom) are community-sourced and version-variable, so enumerate the live dictionary at runtime rather than hard-coding them. For a scene/shadow serialiser the recommended default is to skip GeoReference entirely.
20. TWO DIFFERENT NORTH ANGLES — DO NOT CONFLATE. ShadowInfo's "NorthAngle" is SOLAR north (degrees clockwise from the green axis) and drives shadow casting. The GeoReference dictionary's "GeoReferenceNorthAngle" is MAP north and drives imagery alignment. They can legitimately differ. Compounding this, both Model#point_to_latlong and Model#latlong_to_point carry an explicit doc Note that they "do not account for the model's North Angle (solar north)" — so geo conversions and shadow geometry disagree by NorthAngle degrees whenever it is non-zero.
21. AXIS-ORDER MINEFIELD ACROSS THE GEO API. Model#latlong_to_point takes [LONGITUDE, LATITUDE] (lon first — the doc example is [-105.28300, 40.01700] for Boulder CO). Model#point_to_latlong RETURNS a Geom::Point3d, not a LatLong, with .x = longitude, .y = latitude, .z = altitude in METRES. But Geom::LatLong.new takes (LATITUDE, LONGITUDE) — lat first — and #to_a returns [lat, long]. ShadowInfo itself uses named keys so it is immune, but every conversion between them is a chance to swap them silently and end up 90 degrees away.
22. ALTITUDE IS ALWAYS METRES in latlong_to_point / point_to_latlong regardless of the model's display units. Do not apply a Length conversion.
23. DO NOT ROUND Latitude/Longitude WHEN SERIALISING. Six decimal places is ~11 cm and, more importantly, quantises the solar altitude enough to be visible in low-sun / high-latitude studies. Ruby's JSON.generate emits full double precision for Floats — just pass the Float straight through and do not format it.
24. SunDirection IS YOUR BEST END-TO-END ASSERTION. Export it as [x,y,z] Floats (read-only, never written back). After a restore, read it again from the target model and compare component-wise to ~1e-6.
