If it matches, then the epoch, TZOffset, DaylightSavings, Latitude, Longitude and NorthAngle all transferred correctly — a single check that validates the entire time-and-place pipeline. If it does not match, the discrepancy tells you exactly what to hunt: a pure azimuth rotation implicates NorthAngle or TZOffset, an altitude error implicates Latitude or the date.
25. #[] IS PERMISSIVE WHILE #[]= IS STRICT. The reader returns nil for an unknown key instead of raising, so a misspelling in your EXPORT path fails silently (you write a null into the JSON) and only detonates on IMPORT. Validate spellings at load against Sketchup::ShadowInfo.keys rather than trusting the reader.
26. ShadowInfoObserver type codes are NOT contiguous — documented values are 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 9, 10; 5 and 6 are absent. Do not write a case statement assuming 0..10. Also keep the observer instance in a module variable so remove_observer receives the SAME object; passing a new instance removes nothing and returns false, leaking observers across every plugin reload.
27. Thomas Thomassen's standing advice on this exact class, from the Sketchucation SunDirection thread: "the API docs are incomplete" and "never trust the docs". Given issue #1086 is open and unanswered against ShadowTime_time_t on 2026.1, treat every ShadowInfo write as fallible: wrap per key, collect failures, verify by reading back, and surface a partial-restore report to the user rather than silently producing a scene with the sun in the wrong place.

### Sketchup::Style and Sketchup::Styles

1. ROUTE (a) IS HALF-DEAD — THE EXPORT HALF DOES NOT EXIST. Sketchup::Style#save_as is NOT a method. The Instance Method Summary on ruby.sketchup.com/Sketchup/Style.html lists exactly six methods (#description, #description=, #duplicate, #name, #name=, #path) and the canonical stub source SketchUp/ruby-api-stubs/.../Sketchup/Style.rb defines exactly those six and nothing more. There is no save, no save_as, no export, no write. NOTHING in the Ruby API writes a .style file. Styles are exported only by the user, in the Styles Browser, by right-clicking a style and choosing Save As. Any architecture that depends on programmatically dumping a style to disk is unbuildable.
2. THE IMPORT HALF OF ROUTE (a) DOES WORK, AND YES, add_style TAKES AN ABSOLUTE PATH. The official example is `filename = File.expand_path('./MyStyle.style'); styles.add_style(filename, true)` — File.expand_path yields an absolute path, so absolute paths are the documented usage. On Windows use forward slashes or File.join to avoid backslash escaping. The file must be a real .style archive; you cannot synthesise one because it is a ZIP with an UNPUBLISHED XML schema (SketchUcation, moderator: "A '.style' file is only a zip-file with a different suffix - any watermarks are defined in the 'document.xml' under the 'screenimages' section"; DanRathbun on the SketchUp forum: "The XML schemas for .style (and .skm, etc.) are not published.").
3. CONFIRMED: THE STYLE OBJECT EXPOSES ALMOST NOTHING; THE REAL SETTINGS LIVE IN RenderingOptions. Your understanding is correct, and it is corroborated at three levels. Official docs: Style has six methods, none of which touch a single visual property. SketchUp staff (ene_su) on the SketchUp Community: "The rendering options aren't saved directly to the Scene but to the Style." Official Page docs @note: "Most rendering options of a scene are also present in Sketchup::Style and are governed by the selected style." And from the field, a developer probing a style object reported "I don't see any useful methods on s, just name and description" with `s.attribute_dictionaries.nil? # => true`, and DanRathbun's summary: "styles are not fully exposed to the API (yet.)" SketchUcation puts it flatly: "access to loaded Style info is very limited - to name and description". Refutation: none found anywhere.
4. THE BRIEF'S METHOD LIST CONTAINS THREE NAMES THAT DO NOT EXIST. (1) Style#save_as — does not exist. (2) Styles#active_style_changed? — the real method has NO question mark; it is `active_style_changed` (verified in both the docs Instance Method Summary and the stub `def active_style_changed`). (3) Style#display_name — does not exist on Sketchup::Style (Layer and Material have it; Style does not). All three raise NoMethodError.
5. WATERMARKS ARE NOT REACHABLE FROM RUBY. PLAINLY: you cannot read a watermark, cannot create one, cannot position or scale one, cannot extract its image, and cannot transfer one between models. The entire watermark surface in the Ruby API is one boolean key, `DisplayWatermarks` (note the PLURAL — you will find the singular `DisplayWatermark` in forum posts and it is wrong), plus the matching observer constant ROPSetDisplayWatermarks. Watermark data lives inside the .style ZIP in document.xml under the 'screenimages' section with images in a ref/ subfolder, and there is no API into it. DanRathbun: "There is no way to import watermark images except by a Style, and styles are not fully exposed to the API (yet.)" The ONLY workaround is to ship pre-authored .style files containing the watermarks and select them with add_style — which is a distribution strategy, not a serialisation strategy.
6. SKETCHY EDGES (NPR) ARE ONLY HALF REACHABLE, AND THE HALF THAT MATTERS IS MISSING. Reachable: EdgeType (0 = Standard, 1 = Sketchy — the only officially value-documented key in the whole class), JitterEdges, ExtendLines, LineExtension, DrawSilhouettes, SilhouetteWidth, DrawProfilesOnly, DrawLineEnds, LineEndWidth. NOT reachable: WHICH sketchy stroke set is in use — the hand-drawn line texture that defines whether a style looks like 'Marker Loose', 'Pencil on Tracing Paper' or 'Brush Strokes'. That setting is proven to exist internally by the observer constant ROPSetLineStyleEdges, which has NO corresponding public key. So setting EdgeType = 1 in model A gives you sketchy mode with WHATEVER stroke set model A's target style already carries, which will not match model B. A sketchy style CANNOT be faithfully transferred by RenderingOptions replay.
7. THE ROP* CONSTANT SET IS THE HARD PROOF OF HOW MUCH IS HIDDEN. Sketchup::RenderingOptions defines ~74 ROP* observer constants but only 68 public keys, and several constants have no key at all: ROPSetLineStyleEdges (sketchy stroke set), ROPSetXRayOpacity (X-ray opacity amount), ROPSetModelingGrid, ROPSetHideSpaces, ROPSetFogHint, ROPSetAOColorEnabled, and the four ROPSetPhotomatch* constants (background/overlay draw and opacity). Every one of those is a real style setting SketchUp tracks internally and will notify you about, but which Ruby cannot read or write. Photo-match background images, in particular, are part of a scene's look and will simply not survive any Ruby-based transfer.
8. model.rendering_options WRITES TO THE ACTIVE STYLE, NOT TO THE SCENE AND NOT TO THE SAVED STYLE. This is the number-one cause of 'my style restore didn't stick'. The active_style is a scratch copy; its edits are "silently dropped once a new style is selected". You MUST call styles.update_selected_style to commit, and you must make sure the right style is selected first or you will silently overwrite the user's own style. Correct order: select/mint target style -> write all rendering options -> styles.update_selected_style -> page.use_style = styles.selected_style -> page.update(PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS).
9. DO NOT BULK-WRITE page.rendering_options. The official Page docs say it explicitly: "Most rendering options of a scene are also present in Sketchup::Style and are governed by the selected style. Those options should not be changed from the scene. The ones not related to Sketchup::Style like fog (DisplayFog, FogColor) are safe to be changed from the scene." Read from page.rendering_options for export (convenient — no scene navigation needed); write via model.rendering_options + update_selected_style for import. Fog keys are the documented exception you may set per-scene.
10. Page#use_style= TAKES A STYLE OBJECT, NOT A BOOLEAN — despite every one of its siblings (use_camera=, use_shadow_info=, use_section_planes=, use_rendering_options=, use_hidden=) taking a Boolean. Official example: `style = model.styles[0]; page.use_style = style`. Passing `true` will not work. Setting it also flips use_rendering_options? to true as a side effect. And use_style? and use_rendering_options? are documented to return IDENTICAL values — they are two views of the same 'Style and Fog' checkbox.
11. RenderingOptions#[]= BECAME FATAL IN 2024.0. "Raises ArgumentError if the given key is invalid, the key's value is not settable, or the given value is invalid. SketchUp versions earlier than 2024.0 will not raise this error." On 2023 and earlier a bad key was a silent no-op; on 2024+ it aborts. If you replay 68 keys in a bare loop, ONE unsupported key kills the whole scene restore. Mandatory defence: (1) build an allowlist from the TARGET model's own rendering_options.keys and skip anything not in it, AND (2) wrap each individual assignment in begin/rescue ArgumentError so a read-only or derived key logs and continues.
12. SPELLING TRAPS THAT WILL SILENTLY (or loudly) BREAK YOU. `DepthQueWidth` and `DrawDepthQue` are spelled Q-U-E, not 'Cue'. `DisplayWatermarks` is PLURAL. The Profiles toggle is `DrawSilhouettes` (plural) but its width is `SilhouetteWidth` (singular) — and `DrawProfilesOnly` is a completely different setting. `ROPDrawHiddenGeometry` and `ROPDrawHiddenObjects` are genuine rendering option KEYS despite carrying the ROP prefix used by observer constants. The observer constants use a different vocabulary from the keys in several places (ROPSetProfileEdges ↔ DrawSilhouettes; ROPSetExtendEdges ↔ ExtendLines).
13. FaceColorMode WAS REMOVED IN SketchUp 2019.1 ("This option was previously included but it was ineffective") but its observer constant ROPSetFaceColorMode still exists. Any archived JSON payload captured on an older SketchUp that contains FaceColorMode will raise ArgumentError on a 2024+ restore. Blacklist it explicitly in addition to the keys.include? allowlist.
14. Sketchup::Color IS NOT JSON-SERIALISABLE. Roughly twenty of the 68 keys return Sketchup::Color objects. Encode as {"__t":"Color","r":c.red,"g":c.green,"b":c.blue,"a":c.alpha} (all Integer 0-255) and rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). Do not call .to_s or JSON.generate on the raw object and expect anything useful back.
15. THE DOCS DO NOT DOCUMENT THE INTEGER VALUE MAPPINGS FOR RenderMode OR EdgeDisplayMode, AND ONLY COMMUNITY SOURCES COVER EdgeColorMode (0 = By material, 1 = All same, 2 = By axis, per the SketchUp forum — with the poster noting no API constants exist for them). EdgeType is the ONLY key with an official value table (0 = Standard, 1 = Sketchy). For a serialiser this is a non-problem provided you round-trip the raw Integer opaquely — read it, store it, write it back. Do NOT hard-code interpreted values; the mappings are undocumented and unstable.
16. KEY SETS DIFFER BY SKETCHUP VERSION — 2026 EXPORTS KEYS 2023 CANNOT ACCEPT. AmbientOcclusionColor and AmbientOcclusionMultiplier are 2026.0+; AmbientOcclusion/Distance/Intensity are 2024.0+; ROPDrawHiddenGeometry/ROPDrawHiddenObjects are 2020+; SectionCutFilled/SectionDefaultFillColor are 2018+. Store the source model's rendering_options.keys array AND the source Sketchup.version in your JSON so that on restore you can diff, filter, and honestly report to the user which settings could not be carried across.
17. Styles#selected_style= CAN CRASH SKETCHUP ON OLD VERSIONS. Official @bug: "Prior to SketchUp 2025.0 setting the #selected_style= to the #active_style would chrash SketchUp." (the typo is in the official source). On 2025.0+ it raises ArgumentError instead. Guard with an identity check before every assignment: `return if style == styles.active_style`. A crash here takes the user's unsaved model with it.
18. THE OFFICIAL remove_style EXAMPLE IS BUGGY — DO NOT COPY IT. The stub's @example writes `styles.remove(styles.first)` but the method is `remove_style`; there is no Styles#remove. It also raises ArgumentError if the style is not in the collection, and — easy to miss — if the collection contains only ONE style. Rescue both conditions.
19. Styles#purge_unused WILL DELETE A STYLE YOU HAVE JUST CREATED but not yet bound to a page, because an unbound style counts as unused. Never purge in the middle of a scene rebuild. It also invalidates any Style references you are holding — re-fetch by name afterwards, and check style.valid? before use.
20. STYLE NAMES GET BRACKETED ON IMPORT. The official selected_style= example does `styles.add_style(filename, true)` then `styles.selected_style = styles['[MyStyle]']` — note the square brackets around the name. A style loaded from MyStyle.style may be addressable as '[MyStyle]', not 'MyStyle'. Your name-based lookup must try both forms, or iterate with #each and normalise. There is also no documented uniqueness enforcement on style names, so styles[name] can be ambiguous — namespace your reconstructed styles (e.g. prefix 'NA_').
21. Style#path IS A HINT, NOT A CONTRACT. It returns the file path the style was loaded from — which means an empty string for styles authored in-model, and an authoring-machine-local path for the rest. Serialise it as an opportunistic fast path (if File.exist? then add_style gives you a perfect style including watermarks) but never depend on it. It also did not exist before SketchUp 2025.0, so wrap in respond_to?(:path).
22. Style#duplicate (2026.2) IS IN-MODEL ONLY. It takes no model argument and cannot clone across models — it is not a cross-model transfer mechanism. Its real value to you is minting a fresh style slot in the TARGET model so your restore does not overwrite the user's existing style. Also note it is documented on ruby.sketchup.com and in the 2026.2 release notes but was still ABSENT from ruby-api-stubs main when I checked, so guard with respond_to?(:duplicate) and have a fallback for 2026.0/2026.1.
23. Styles#add_style CHANGED ITS RETURN TYPE IN 2026.0 — Boolean before, Sketchup::Style-or-nil after. Version-safe idiom: `result = styles.add_style(path, true); style = result.is_a?(Sketchup::Style) ? result : styles.selected_style`. Also check File.exist?(path) first, because a missing file yields a falsey/nil result rather than a diagnostic error.
24. NEVER SERIALISE entityID OR persistent_id AS YOUR STYLE KEY. entityID is session-scoped; persistent_id survives save/reload but only WITHIN one model. Neither means anything in a different model. The style NAME is your only viable cross-model identifier — which is exactly why you must also handle the bracketed-name and non-uniqueness quirks above.
25. DO NOT STORE YOUR METADATA IN A STYLE'S ATTRIBUTE DICTIONARY. Style inherits set_attribute/get_attribute from Entity, but in practice `style.attribute_dictionaries` returns nil, persistence across save/reload is undocumented and untested, and a .style round-trip through the Styles Browser will not carry it. Keep your scene JSON in the MODEL attribute dictionary as you already plan, and reference the style by name.

### Sketchup::SectionPlane and section display

1. THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT FACT FOR ROUND-TRIPPING: SectionPlane#get_plane ALWAYS returns the 4-element coefficient form [a, b, c, d] as plain Ruby Floats, never the [Point3d, Vector3d] form. set_plane and add_section_plane accept BOTH forms. So the asymmetry is: read coefficients, write either. Store the coefficients in JSON (they are already Floats, lossless, no coercion) and rebuild with the point/vector form for clarity.
2. SIGN CONVENTION: the equation is a*x + b*y + c*z + d = 0 (Geom docs: 'Ax + By + Cz + D = 0'). Substituting the origin gives d, so d is the SIGNED distance of the ORIGIN from the plane along the unit normal — confirmed on the SketchUp forum: 'The distance d can be negative, indicating that the origin is behind the plane.' The point on the plane closest to the origin is therefore -d * n, i.e. ORIGIN.offset(n, -d). Getting this sign backwards puts your reconstructed plane the same distance on the WRONG side of the origin — a mirror-image error that looks plausible in a symmetric model and is easy to ship.
3. (a, b, c) from get_plane is UNIT length. If you ever rebuild a coefficient array by hand, all four numbers must be scaled together: multiplying [a,b,c,d] by any non-zero k describes the same plane, but normalising only [a,b,c] while leaving d alone MOVES the plane. This is the most common silent plane-maths bug.
4. Geom::Point3d#to_a returns an Array of Sketchup::Length objects, NOT Floats. JSON.generate turns a Length into a string like "50.0\"" which will not parse back into a number. ALWAYS .map(&:to_f) before serialising any point, vector or bounding box. get_plane is the exception — it already gives you Floats, which is another reason to store the coefficient form.
5. All lengths in the SketchUp API, including d, are INCHES regardless of the model's display units. There is no unit conversion to do between two models — but if you ever pretty-print or hand-edit the JSON, remember the numbers are inches.
6. Sketchup::SectionPlane has NO #transformation, NO #transformation=, and NO #transform!. The official page's full instance-method summary is exactly eight methods. If you were planning to store a transformation matrix per section plane the way you would for a ComponentInstance, that route does not exist — the plane array is the only placement representation. Use Entities#transform_entities(tr, [sp]) to move one, or compute the new plane and call set_plane.
7. Sketchup::Model#active_section_plane and #active_section_plane= (SINGULAR) DO NOT EXIST on any version. Model gained only the plural, read-only #active_section_planes in SketchUp 2026.0. The getter/setter pair lives exclusively on Sketchup::Entities. model.active_section_plane raises NoMethodError.
8. Section planes are per-ENTITIES-COLLECTION, not per-model. The model root's entities has one active plane; EVERY ComponentDefinition's entities has its own independent active plane. That is how SketchUp shows multiple simultaneous cuts. A complete capture must walk model.entities plus every model.definitions entry (or use Model#active_section_planes on 2026+).
9. SCENES DO NOT OWN SECTION PLANES. The SectionPlane objects are model geometry that exists whether or not any scene references them. A Page stores only (a) a snapshot of which plane was active in each Entities collection, gated by Page#use_section_planes?, and (b) separately, per-page visibility overrides on top-level drawing elements. Deleting a scene never deletes a section plane; deleting a section plane silently drops it out of every scene that referenced it.
10. Page#update SNAPSHOTS THE CURRENT MODEL STATE — it does not accept the state as an argument. The mandatory sequence is: model.pages.selected_page = page → mutate the model into the desired state → page.use_section_planes = true → page.update(PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES). Skipping the selected_page step bakes whatever scene happens to be current into the page you named.
11. Confirmed on the SketchUp forum (Dan Rathbun): 'a page update saves the settings for ALL of the entities objects that have section planes.' PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES is model-wide, not scoped. If you set only the root collection's active plane and call update, you also bake whatever every nested definition's active plane currently is — which may be leftover state from a previously-selected scene. Set EVERY collection explicitly before every update.
12. Page#set_visibility takes ONLY a Sketchup::Layer or Sketchup::LayerFolder. It will not accept a SectionPlane. Its relevance to section planes is entirely indirect: assign the plane to a tag, then hide that tag on the scene, and the plane is hidden and stops cutting. The API for per-page visibility of the section-plane ENTITY is the differently-named Page#set_drawingelement_visibility (SketchUp 2020.0+).
13. Page#set_drawingelement_visibility is documented as working for 'drawing elements on the root of the model, as well as nested instances of components, groups, and images'. A SectionPlane nested INSIDE a group or component is therefore NOT individually controllable per scene — only its enclosing Group/ComponentInstance is. If your design needs per-scene control of nested planes, flatten them to the model root on import or drive them via tags.
14. Page#hidden_entities and Page#layers return nil, not an empty Array, when the page stores no overrides. Every call site needs `|| []` or `&.`. This is a routine NoMethodError source in scene-walking code.
15. Page#layers is a DELTA list — 'layers that don't use their default visibility on this page' — not a list of visible layers. Reading it as an absolute list inverts the visibility of every layer whose model default happens to be hidden. For a robust JSON round-trip, store the absolute per-page visibility of every layer, plus each layer's model default.
16. Whether a section CUT is drawn is a RenderingOptions matter (DisplaySectionCuts), which rides on the page's 'Style and Fog' checkbox (use_rendering_options?), NOT on the 'Active Section Planes' checkbox (use_section_planes?). A perfectly reconstructed active plane will show no cut if the destination model or the destination page's style has DisplaySectionCuts false. Serialise and restore both groups of state.
17. When a page has use_rendering_options? == true, SELECTING that page OVERWRITES model.rendering_options with the page's stored copy. So setting model.rendering_options['DisplaySectionCuts'] = true and then selecting a style-carrying scene silently reverts it. Either set the key on each page's own rendering_options, or set it after selecting the page and then re-update the page.
18. RenderingOptions#[]= RAISES ArgumentError for an invalid key in SketchUp 2024.0 and later; earlier versions silently ignored unknown keys. A JSON payload authored on 2026 containing AmbientOcclusionColor will abort an import into an older model mid-operation. Always guard writes with `next unless ro.keys.include?(key)` and capture ro.keys in the export payload for diffing.
19. RenderingOptions colour keys (SectionActiveColor, SectionInactiveColor, SectionDefaultCutColor, SectionDefaultFillColor) return Sketchup::Color objects, which are NOT JSON-safe. Encode as color.to_a => [r, g, b, a] Integers 0..255, and decode with Sketchup::Color.new(r, g, b, a). Do not round-trip via a hex string — alpha is lost.
20. Exact key spellings, all case-sensitive, all confirmed against the ruby-api-stubs verbatim list: DisplaySectionPlanes (plural Planes), DisplaySectionCuts (plural Cuts), SectionCutWidth (singular Cut), SectionCutFilled ('Filled' past tense, not 'Fill'), SectionCutDrawEdges, SectionDefaultCutColor, SectionDefaultFillColor, SectionActiveColor (no 'Default'), SectionInactiveColor (no 'Default'). Note the asymmetry: the CUT and FILL colours carry 'Default' in their names, the ACTIVE and INACTIVE marker colours do not.
21. SectionCutDrawEdges (SketchUp 2015) was not in the original brief but MUST be captured for visual fidelity — omitting it produces imported scenes with missing or unexpected cut linework.
22. Version gates for the section rendering keys: DisplaySectionPlanes is 2014; DisplaySectionCuts, SectionCutDrawEdges and DrawBackEdges are 2015; SectionCutFilled and SectionDefaultFillColor are 2018. The four Section*Color/Width keys other than the fill colour date to SketchUp 6. Probe with rendering_options.keys.include? rather than rescuing.
23. Version gates for the methods: SectionPlane#get_plane and #set_plane are SketchUp 6.0; #activate, #active?, Entities#add_section_plane, #active_section_plane, #active_section_plane= and EntitiesObserver#onActiveSectionPlaneChanged are all SketchUp 2014; #name, #name=, #symbol, #symbol= are 2018; Page#get_drawingelement_visibility and #set_drawingelement_visibility are 2020.0; Model#active_section_planes and Page#active_section_planes are 2026.0. Use respond_to? guards throughout if you support anything below 2026.
24. SectionPlane#symbol= requires three characters or fewer. Clamp defensively — sp.symbol = value.to_s[0, 3] — because the failure mode varies by version between ArgumentError and silent truncation.
25. Sketchup::Pages#add auto-uniquifies names in SketchUp 2026.0 ('if the specified name is already taken, it automatically modifies the name to ensure it is unique'), and Page#name= has always done so. ALWAYS read back page.name after creating or renaming, and record the {requested_name => actual_name} mapping, or cross-references in your JSON will dangle.
26. Reading which planes a scene uses required SELECTING the scene before SketchUp 2026 ('The plane cannot be active until the scene is set as the current view'). That is slow and visually disruptive. Guard it with model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition'] = false, restore the previous value in an ensure block, and record + restore model.pages.selected_page and model.active_path so you leave the user where you found them. On 2026+, use Page#active_section_planes and skip all of this.
27. entityID is session-scoped and worthless as a cross-model or cross-session key. persistent_id is stable within one model across saves but is still NOT valid in a different model. For cross-model reconstruction the durable identity is the GEOMETRY (the world plane coefficients) plus name/symbol plus a deterministic ordering — write your own mapping table into the destination model's attribute dictionary if you need repeat-import idempotency.
28. A section plane inside a ComponentDefinition that has N instances is ONE entity with N world placements. Walking model.definitions finds it once (definition-local); walking the instance tree finds it N times (world). Decide deliberately which you want. For a JSON scene exporter that rebuilds into a different model, flattening every placement to a world-space plane at the model root is the simplest correct choice — but it loses the ability to toggle a whole component's cut from one place.
29. Transforming a plane by transforming its normal with Vector3d#transform is only correct for rigid and uniform-scale transformations. Normals transform by the inverse-transpose. Component instances with non-uniform scale are common in real models and will give you a normal that is no longer perpendicular to the transformed plane. Use the three-point refit via Geom.fit_plane_to_points instead — and then re-orient, because fit_plane_to_points does not guarantee which of the two normal directions it returns.
30. Reversing a section plane means negating ALL FOUR coefficients: sp.set_plane(sp.get_plane.map { |i| -i }). Negating only the normal leaves d inconsistent and moves the plane to a mirrored position.
31. Entities#add_section_plane returns nil on failure (e.g. a zero-length normal vector from corrupt JSON). Always nil-check the result before calling .activate on it, or you get NoMethodError on nil deep inside an undo operation.
32. Entities#active_section_plane= requires the SectionPlane to be a member of THAT SAME Entities collection. Passing a plane belonging to a different collection is an error. When rebuilding, build your {collection => plane} map keyed by the collection you actually created the plane in.
33. Entities#transform_entities interprets the transformation as WORLD if the entities are in the active drawing context or one of its parents, and as LOCAL otherwise — a documented behaviour that silently changes meaning depending on what the user has open. For deterministic imports, create planes in the right place with add_section_plane rather than creating-then-transforming.
34. Wrap all reconstruction in model.start_operation(name, true) / commit_operation with abort_operation in a rescue. The `true` (disable_ui) makes bulk imports dramatically faster. But do page SELECTION outside the operation where practical — model.pages.selected_page= inside an operation can interact badly with the undo stack.
35. Never erase entities while iterating a Sketchup::Entities collection. Collect into an Array first, then call ents.erase_entities(array) for a single undo step. This matters when clearing pre-existing section planes from the destination model before import.
36. SketchUp attribute dictionary values must be one of a limited set of primitives (Boolean, String, Float, Integer, Length, Array, Time, Point3d, Vector3d). A Ruby Hash is NOT supported. Since your design stores JSON in a model attribute dictionary, JSON.generate to a String and store the String — and be aware very large strings in attribute dictionaries can bloat the .skp and slow saves, so consider chunking or compressing if the payload is megabytes.
37. PAGE_USE_* constant integer values documented in the SketchUp constants reference: PAGE_USE_CAMERA=1, PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS=2, PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO=4, PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS=8, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN=16, PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY=32, PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES=64, PAGE_USE_ALL=4095, PAGE_NO_CAMERA=4094. Values for PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS and PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT are NOT published. SketchUp's own documentation warns that constant values have changed between versions — ALWAYS use the constant name, never the literal, and guard newer constants with defined?() since referencing an undefined constant raises NameError.
38. Watch the constant spelling PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO (one word, no underscore) versus the method Page#shadow_info (with underscore), and PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS (SketchUp Coordinate System) for the axes flag. Both are easy to mistype into a NameError.
39. The observer callback is spelled onActiveSectionPlaneChanged — camel case, capital S, capital P. A misspelled observer method is never called and never warns, producing a 'my observer does nothing' bug that is invisible in testing.
40. Do not use Sketchup::Model#active_entities in exporter code — it returns whatever group/component the user currently has open, making your output depend on UI state. Use model.entities plus model.definitions. (Note that the official SectionPlane doc examples all use active_entities, which is fine for a console snippet and wrong for a plugin.)
41. When enumerating model.definitions, skip image definitions (defn.image?) and consider skipping unused ones (defn.count_used_instances == 0) — a section plane inside an unplaced definition exists in the file, is returned by a naive scan, and is never drawn anywhere.

### Sketchup::Axes, Layer, Layers and LayerFolder

1. TERMINOLOGY: SketchUp 2020 renamed 'Layers' to 'Tags' in the USER INTERFACE ONLY. The Ruby API classes are still Sketchup::Layer, Sketchup::Layers, Sketchup::LayerFolder, and the constants are still LAYER_*. There is no Sketchup::Tag class and no alias. Only Layer#display_name reflects the rename, and only for the default layer: #name is always 'Layer0' while #display_name is 'Untagged' in 2020+. ALWAYS key your JSON on #name — `model.layers['Untagged']` returns nil, `model.layers['Layer0']` works.
2. Page#layers DOES NOT return hidden layers. It returns "layers that don't use their default visibility on this page" — a DELTA set relative to each layer's LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT bit. Older SketchUp docs described it as hidden layers and were wrong; this was corrected after the forum study 'Layers vs Pages visibility study >> mistakes in documentation'. The decode is `visible_on_page = page.layers.include?(layer) == hidden_by_default?(layer)`. If you assume it is a hidden list, every tag whose page_behavior has the HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT bit will come out inverted.
3. Page#layers and Page#layer_folders BOTH RETURN nil (not an empty array) when Page#use_hidden_layers? is false. This is not in the official docs. Always write `(page.layers || [])`. A NoMethodError on nil.include? is the classic symptom.
4. Page#layer_folders is documented differently from Page#layers: it retrieves the HIDDEN layer folders, not a delta set. Folders have no page_behavior bitfield (only the Boolean visible_on_new_pages?), so folder membership in that array means simply 'hidden on this page'. Do not apply the delta logic to folders.
5. LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT and LAYER_USES_DEFAULT_VISIBILITY_ON_NEW_PAGES are both ZERO (0x0000). You cannot test for them with a bitwise AND — `pb & LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT` is always 0 for every layer. Test the negative: visible-by-default is `(pb & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT) == 0`, and uses-default-on-new-pages is `(pb & (16|32)) == 0`.
6. The LAYER_* and PAGE_USE_* constants are TOP-LEVEL GLOBAL constants defined on Object, even though the docs list the LAYER_* ones under Sketchup::Layer. Write `LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT`, not `Sketchup::Layer::LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT` (which raises NameError). Inside a nested module they resolve fine via normal Ruby constant lookup.
7. Spelling traps in the constant names: LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT has NO 'IS', but LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES and LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES DO. LAYER_USES_DEFAULT_VISIBILITY_ON_NEW_PAGES uses 'VISIBILITY' (noun), not 'VISIBLE'. PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO is one word with no underscore. PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS is the AXES flag despite the name ('sketch coordinate system').
8. There is NO Sketchup::Model#axes= and NO Sketchup::Page#axes=. The Axes object is mutated in place via #set. Attempting `model.axes = something` raises NoMethodError. Similarly there are no #origin=, #xaxis=, #yaxis=, #zaxis= setters on Sketchup::Axes.
9. Sketchup::Axes#set argument order is (origin, xaxis, yaxis, zaxis) — ORIGIN FIRST. Getting this wrong (e.g. passing the three vectors then the point) fails with a confusing type error, or worse, silently produces garbage if your point happens to duck-type as a vector.
10. Sketchup::Axes#set THROWS if the three vectors are not mutually orthogonal. Floats round-tripped through JSON can drift enough to trip this. Re-orthogonalise defensively before calling: `z = x_vec * y_vec; y = z * x_vec` (Geom::Vector3d#* is the cross product), then normalize.
11. Axes values are in INCHES — SketchUp's internal unit is always inches regardless of the model's display units. Point3d#to_a returns Length objects that behave as Floats; call .to_f explicitly before JSON.generate or you may serialise a Length and get inconsistent output. On restore, plain Floats are interpreted as inches, so no conversion is needed as long as both ends stay in inches.
12. SKETCHUP 2026 BREAKING CHANGE: 'Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying the Axes, Camera, RenderingOptions, and ShadowInfo properties of a page is an undoable operation and should be wrapped between Model#start_operation and Model#commit_operation.' Unwrapped page.axes.set / page.camera= calls in 2026 can behave differently from 2025 and earlier. Wrap the whole scene restore in one operation.
13. Page#use_axes? and Page#use_hidden_layers? are the ON/OFF gates. On a NEWLY CREATED page you must set `page.use_axes = true` BEFORE `page.axes.set(...)` and `page.use_hidden_layers = true` BEFORE any `page.set_visibility(...)`, or the values silently do not stick. Order matters in both cases.
14. ORDERING WITHIN TAG RESTORE: set `layer.page_behavior = captured_integer` BEFORE calling `page.set_visibility(layer, bool)`. set_visibility stores a delta computed against the current default-visibility bit, so flipping page_behavior afterwards INVERTS every scene visibility you already wrote.
15. KNOWN BUG (SketchUp/api-issue-tracker#457, confirmed in 16.1.1449, 17.2.2555, 18.0.16975, 20.0.363 and still reported since): setting Layer#page_behavior= on a freshly added layer does not propagate to existing pages until a scene refresh. Documented workaround: `pages = model.pages; pages.selected_page = pages.selected_page`. Run this once after creating all tags and before/after applying set_visibility, and verify by reading back.
16. Sketchup::Layers#each is RECURSIVE (all layers, including those inside folders) but Sketchup::Layers#layers and #each_layer are NOT (top-level only). On Sketchup::LayerFolder the polarity is reversed: #each is an alias of #each_layer and covers direct children only. Using Layers#layers when you meant Layers#each silently drops every foldered tag from your export.
17. There is NO method named Sketchup::Layers#each_layer_folder. The correct spelling is #each_folder (and #folders for the array). Sketchup::Page#layer_folders is a separate, unrelated method on Page.
18. Sketchup::Layer has NO #visible_on_new_pages? / #visible_on_new_pages= — those exist ONLY on Sketchup::LayerFolder. For layers the equivalent information lives in the LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES / LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES bits of #page_behavior. Calling visible_on_new_pages? on a Layer raises NoMethodError.
19. LayerFolder#visible= FAILS SILENTLY (no exception) if the folder contains the model's active tag. Set `model.active_layer = model.layers['Layer0']` first, and read the value back to confirm the write landed.
20. Layer#visible= on the model's active layer both hides it AND reassigns model.active_layer to a different layer (SketchUp/api-issue-tracker#945). Save and restore model.active_layer around bulk visibility work.
21. Layer#visible? is CURRENT MODEL STATE, not scene state — it reflects whichever scene is presently active. Serialising Layer#visible? and calling it 'the scene's tag visibility' is the single most common mistake in this area. Per-scene state only comes from Page#layers / Page#set_visibility.
22. Sketchup::Layers#purge_unused (alias #purge_unused_layers) and #purge_unused_folders will DELETE tags and folders that carry no geometry. In a cross-model scene restore many tags exist purely to hold scene visibility state and have no geometry yet — purging destroys exactly the state you just rebuilt. Never purge during or immediately after reconstruction.
23. Sketchup::Layers#remove defaults `remove_geometry` to FALSE, which reassigns that tag's geometry to the default layer rather than deleting it. Silently re-tagging a user's model is worse than raising. Never call remove as part of an import.
24. Layers#add IS idempotent by name (it returns the existing layer if the name is taken), but Layers#add_folder is NOT — calling it twice with the same String can create two same-named sibling folders. Always search `model.layers.folders.find { |f| f.name == n }` before creating.
25. Folder names are NOT unique within a model, unlike layer names. The only reliable cross-model key for a folder is its full ancestor PATH as an Array<String>, e.g. ['Site','Existing','Below Ground'].
26. LayerFolder is SketchUp 2021.0+, Layer#folder / #folder= are 2021.0+, Layer#display_name is 2020.0+, Layer#line_style is 2019+, Layer#color is 2014+, Sketchup::Axes is 2016+. Even targeting 2026, guard with respond_to? if the plugin might load in an older host, and NEVER let a nil folder API crash the whole scene import.
27. Layer#entityID is session-scoped and Layer#persistent_id is model-scoped. NEITHER is usable as a cross-model key. Only #name transfers. Do not be tempted by persistent_id because it sounds durable — a same-named tag in model B has a different one.
28. Pages#add snapshots the CURRENT model state at creation time (current axes, current tag visibility), and its `flags` argument defaults to PAGE_USE_ALL (4095) so every use_* switch is on. After creating a page you must explicitly overwrite every property you care about; do not assume a fresh page is blank.
29. Pages#selected_page= ACTIVATES the scene, applying its axes and tag visibility to the live model and running the transition animation. Setting it in the middle of a restore loop will clobber in-progress state. Do it once, at the end. (Reassigning the already-selected page is the documented cache-refresh idiom and is safe.)
30. Photomatch scenes (Pages#add_matchphoto_page) have axes bound to the photo. Detect and skip or specially handle them — reconstructing one as a plain page with axes.set will not reproduce the match.
31. Page#hidden_entities is documented as returning drawing elements but older docs claimed a Sketchup::Entities object; it actually returns an Array. It is also not cross-model transferable, as with get/set_drawingelement_visibility.
32. Page#use_style= is NOT a boolean setter despite the name pattern — the docs describe it as setting the Style object to be used by the page. Do not blindly loop `page.send("#{flag}=", true)` over every use_* method.
33. The integer values of PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS and PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT are not published anywhere in the Ruby API docs or the community constants guides (only the SU6-era values 1/2/4/8/16/32/64/4095 are). Never hardcode a guess — resolve at runtime with Object.const_defined?/Object.const_get and fall back to 0.

### Attribute dictionaries, Model and operations

1. NAME COLLISION, HIGH SEVERITY: AttributeDictionary#delete_key removes ONE key; AttributeDictionaries#delete removes an ENTIRE dictionary. There is no #delete on AttributeDictionary and no #delete_key on AttributeDictionaries. Calling the wrong one silently destroys the whole payload.
2. AttributeDictionary#each yields TWO values (key, value). AttributeDictionaries#each yields ONE value (the dictionary object). This asymmetry between the singular and plural classes causes block-arity bugs that fail silently.
3. Enumerable#include? on an AttributeDictionary tests the yielded [key, value] PAIR, not the key. `dict.include?('mykey')` returns false even when the key exists. Use `dict.keys.include?('mykey')`. AttributeDictionary also does NOT define has_key?, key?, member?, fetch, merge, store, update or to_h — it is NOT a Hash and does not subclass Hash (DanRathbun states this explicitly on the SketchUp forum).
4. Entity#attribute_dictionaries can return nil OR an empty collection — the official @note says both are possible. Always nil-guard before calling #each or #[].
5. Entity#attribute_dictionary's second argument `create` defaults to FALSE. Forgetting `true` on the write path gives you nil and a NoMethodError one line later.
6. nil is listed as a legal attribute value, but the set_attribute docs warn that prior to SketchUp 2015 an empty-string key — "This also includes values that will evaluate to empty strings, such as nil" — could CORRUPT THE MODEL. Never write nil and never write an empty-string key.
7. Hash is NOT a storable value type: written via set_attribute it reads back as nil, silently losing data with no exception. Symbol is not on the whitelist either and has no confirmed behaviour. Custom classes fail. DanRathbun's canonical hash-flattening idiom wraps each assignment in `rescue`, showing some unsupported values DO raise rather than fail silently — so the failure mode is genuinely undefined. Validate types yourself before writing.
8. Sketchup.platform returns a SYMBOL (:platform_win / :platform_osx). Symbols are not storable — call .to_s before writing platform metadata into an attribute.
9. There is NO documented maximum length for a String attribute value. The community-established practical ceiling is ~20,000 characters per value (TIG, Sketchucation), and it is probably measured in BYTES not characters — so UTF-8 multibyte content hits it sooner. Chunk at 16,000 to be safe.
10. Large attribute payloads visibly bloat the .skp and slow SketchUp: a forum report states "20k of attributes was found to be increasing file size ten fold on a 3mb model", and another user reports lag from storing all their JSON on the model. Compress (Zlib + Base64) and/or distribute data onto the Pages it describes rather than one giant model-level blob.
11. Length values are Floats in INCHES and Point3d/Vector3d coordinates are in INCHES. `to_json` on a Length emits a bare number with no unit, so the inch convention MUST be documented in your schema and re-applied with `.to_l` on reconstruction, or the destination model gets silently wrong geometry.
12. Time values do NOT round-trip cleanly through JSON. Store epoch Integers (`time.to_i`) and rebuild with `Time.at(n)`. ShadowInfo already exposes 'ShadowTime_time_t' as the epoch companion to 'ShadowTime' — use it.
13. Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d and Geom::Transformation do NOT serialise usefully via to_json. Call `.to_a` first ([x,y,z] for points/vectors, 16 Floats column-major for transformations) and reconstruct with `Geom::Point3d.new(*a)` / `Geom::Transformation.new(a)`. Geom::Transformation is not even a storable attribute type — only its 16-Float Array is.
14. Nested Arrays inside an attribute value are NOT confirmed to round-trip. Community type lists only ever demonstrate flat arrays. Do not rely on nesting — JSON-encode structured data into a String instead.
15. OPERATIONS CANNOT NEST. The official note: "If you start a new Ruby operation while another is still open, you will implicitly close the first one." Failing to commit_operation leaves your op open, and the next extension's start_operation absorbs your changes into their undo step.
16. NEVER call abort_operation on an operation started with transparent = true. The official note: "Never abort a transparent operation. Doing so would abort the operation it chains to" — i.e. it rolls back the USER'S previous action. In a transparent observer write, rescue and still commit.
17. A transparent operation (4th arg true) does not create its own Undo entry and its op_name is NOT displayed — it merges into the previous step. It is still on the undo stack; it is not a way to make changes undo-free. Use it only for observer bookkeeping, never for a user-initiated export the user expects to undo separately.
18. next_transparent (3rd arg) is DEPRECATED and labelled "Use extreme caution". It exists only because DefinitionList#load and Model#place_component commit their own internal operations and operations cannot nest. Do not use it in new code; call those methods outside your operation instead.
19. start_operation raises RuntimeError if called inside a Sketchup::Entities#build block. Never mix build with your own operations.
20. Model#delete_attribute (and Entity#delete_attribute) returned ALWAYS true prior to SketchUp 2019.0 — a documented @bug. Do not branch on its return value unless you have gated on version_number >= 1_900_000_000.
21. The dictionaries named exactly "SU_InstanceSet" and "SU_DefinitionSet" (SketchUp 2018+) raise ArgumentError if you try to delete them via AttributeDictionaries#delete or Entity#delete_attribute. Their key/value pairs can be deleted safely. Skip these names in any dictionary-clearing routine.
22. There is NO Sketchup::Model#title= — confirmed absent from the official instance method summary. title is derived from the filename and is read-only. Use Model#name= for the user-visible Model Info name.
23. Model#path and Model#title return an EMPTY STRING (not nil) for an unsaved model. Test with .empty?, never .nil?.
24. Model#guid CHANGES every time the model is modified and saved. It is not a stable identity for a model and must not be used as a cache key or a 'same source' test.
25. Sketchup.active_model is documented as returning "active model object if successful, false if unsuccessful" — false, not nil. On macOS several models can be open and the focused one can change between calls, so capture it once into a local. Inside observers use entity.model instead.
26. Sketchup.version returns a STRING; comparing it numerically is a classic bug ("9.0" > "26.0" is true as String comparison). Always gate on Sketchup.version_number (Integer). Note the packing scheme CHANGED at SketchUp 2016: XXYYYZZZ (8 digits) for SU6–SU2015, XXYZZZZZZZ (10 digits) for SU2016+, so the value jumps from ~15003000 to 1600000000.
27. The newest Model VERSION_* save constant is VERSION_2021 — there is no VERSION_2022 through VERSION_2026. Never build the constant name dynamically from a year; it will NameError. On SketchUp 2026 omit the version argument to save in the current native format.
28. AttributeDictionary#empty? was added only in SketchUp 2025.0. AttributeDictionary#count is SketchUp 2014+. On the PLURAL AttributeDictionaries class, #count, #length AND #size are all SketchUp 2014+, whereas on the SINGULAR class #length and #size go back to SketchUp 6.0. The classes are NOT symmetric — gate accordingly. `dict.length.zero?` is the universally safe emptiness test.
29. Do not remove keys while iterating with #each — the official @note says elements get SKIPPED as indices shift. Copy with `to_a` first, then iterate the array.
30. Parameter names differ between the Entity and Model documentation for the same underlying methods (Entity: dict_name / default_value; Model: attrdictname / dictname / defaultvalue). They are positional so call sites are unaffected — but never attempt keyword arguments.
31. Sketchup::Entity#entityID is NOT persistent between sessions and must never be serialised as an identity. Sketchup::Entity#persistent_id IS persistent within one model across sessions but is NOT stable across DIFFERENT models — so for cross-model scene reconstruction, match by NAME (page name, layer name, style name, material name) and treat PIDs as provenance metadata only.
32. Attribute writes live only in memory until the .skp is saved. If the user closes without saving, the payload is lost. Model#close(true) discards changes silently. Consider flushing from a ModelObserver#onPreSaveModel hook (using a transparent operation) and checking Model#modified? before prompting.
33. Styles, Materials and Environments are binary assets that cannot be reconstructed from JSON. Only their names (and, for Style/Material, persistent_id on SketchUp 2020.1+) can be transferred; resolve by name in the destination model and fall back gracefully when absent.
34. When opening a donor model to read its stored scenes, use `Sketchup.open_file(path, with_status: true)` (SketchUp 2021.0+) and check for Sketchup::Model::LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS_MORE_RECENT — a model saved by a newer SketchUp may have silently lost features on load, making its scene payload untrustworthy.

### API changes, SketchUp 2023 to 2026

1. ENVIRONMENTS LANDED IN 2025.0, NOT 2026.0. Several secondary sources (and search summaries) claim the Environments API is a SketchUp 2026 feature. The official release notes and the class documentation both say SketchUp 2025.0 for Sketchup::Environment, Sketchup::Environments, Sketchup::EnvironmentsObserver, Sketchup::Model#environments and all four Sketchup::Page environment methods. The ONLY Environments addition in 2026.0 is the single-argument Environments#add(path) overload. Gate on 2025.0, not 2026.0, or you will needlessly disable the feature for a whole release.
2. THE PER-SCENE ENVIRONMENT VALUES CANNOT BE READ WITHOUT ACTIVATING THE PAGE. This is the single most important behavioural trap. SketchUp keeps ONE Environment object per entry in the model's Environments collection and one viewport; a Page stores only 'whether to use' flags plus a reference, not its own copy of rotation/exposure. So `Sketchup.active_model.pages[3].environment.rotation` returns the ACTIVE page's rotation, not page 3's. SketchUp staff confirmed on the forum that this is architectural, not a bug: 'at some time, you'll need to make the page active'. Two scenes sharing one HDRI at different rotations will silently serialise identically unless you activate each page. The community workaround is to disable scene transitions, iterate pages setting `pages.selected_page`, read, then restore the original page and the transition setting.
3. ACTIVATING PAGES TO HARVEST DATA IS DESTRUCTIVE IF YOU FORGET TO RESTORE. Before the activate-each-page loop, save `model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition']` and `pages.selected_page.name`, set ShowTransition to false, and restore BOTH in an ensure block. Also save `model.environments.current` and `model.active_section_planes` (2026.0+) so you can put the model back exactly as you found it. Without ShowTransition disabled, each page switch animates and the loop takes minutes and fires a storm of observer callbacks.
4. SKETCHUP 2026.0 MADE PAGE PROPERTY EDITS UNDOABLE — THIS IS AN EXTENSION WAREHOUSE COMPLIANCE REQUIREMENT, NOT JUST A NICETY. Modifying a Page's Axes, Camera, RenderingOptions or ShadowInfo now pushes undo entries. The release notes state plainly: 'Extensions that don't follow this requirement will be rejected for updates or publication to the Extension Warehouse.' An importer that restores 40 scenes without wrapping will create hundreds of undo entries and be rejected. Wrap the ENTIRE restore in one start_operation(name, true) / commit_operation, with abort_operation in a rescue.
5. THE ROUND-TRIP SHADOWINFO LOOP NOW RAISES. `shadow_info.each_pair { |k,v| h[k]=v }` on export then `h.each { |k,v| si[k]=v }` on import was the standard idiom and it BREAKS on SketchUp 2026.1, which made ShadowInfo#[]= raise KeyError for invalid or read-only keys and TypeError for wrong types. The exported hash contains derived read-only keys — SunDirection, SunRise, SunSet, SunRise_time_t, SunSet_time_t — that will now raise. Use an explicit writable allow-list and rescue per key.
6. THE SAME TRAP EXISTS ONE VERSION-FAMILY EARLIER FOR RENDERINGOPTIONS. SketchUp 2024.0 made RenderingOptions#[]= raise ArgumentError 'if the given value cannot be set for the given key'. Code written against 2023 that assigned silently-ignored keys now aborts mid-restore. Rescue per key AND filter your JSON keys against the running version's `rendering_options.keys` — a 2026-authored JSON containing AmbientOcclusionColor will raise when restored into 2025.
7. JSON NUMBER TYPES vs SKETCHUP FLOAT EXPECTATIONS. JSON.parse returns Integer for `1` and Float for `1.0`. With the strict 2024.0 RenderingOptions and strict 2026.1 ShadowInfo setters, handing an Integer where a Float is expected can now raise TypeError/ArgumentError where it previously coerced. Explicitly `.to_f` every numeric that the API documents as Float (Dark, Light, Latitude, Longitude, NorthAngle, TZOffset, rotation, skydome_exposure, reflection_exposure, AmbientOcclusionDistance, AmbientOcclusionIntensity, AmbientOcclusionMultiplier, transition_time, delay_time) and `.to_i` those documented as Integer (DayOfYear).
8. Environment#path IS A FILENAME, NOT A PATH. The official example proves it: adding 'path/to/environment.hdr' then calling #path outputs 'environment.hdr'. You cannot reopen it and it is useless in a target model. The ONLY supported extraction route is `Environment#write_hdr(directory)`, which takes a DIRECTORY (not a file path) and returns the full written path. Compare with Sketchup::Style#path (2025.0), which genuinely is a file path — the two methods share a name and behave differently.
9. HDRI PAYLOAD SIZE vs ATTRIBUTE DICTIONARIES. Attribute values may be Strings, so base64-embedding an HDR is technically possible, but HDR/EXR files routinely run 5–100 MB and every byte inflates the SKP and its save/load time, in a String that must also survive UTF-8 handling. Prefer writing the HDR as a sidecar file beside the .skp and storing only a relative filename plus a content hash in the JSON, falling back to a user prompt if the sidecar is missing at import.
10. PAGE NAMES ARE NO LONGER GUARANTEED TO STICK ON 2026.0+. Both Pages#add and Page#name= now silently rename on collision to enforce uniqueness. `pages.add('Scene 1').name` may return 'Scene 1-1'. Always capture the returned Page and read back #name, build an old→new name map, and rewrite any references (including LayOut viewport scene bindings and your own JSON cross-references) using that map.
11. Styles#add_style CHANGED ITS RETURN TYPE IN 2026.0, from Boolean to the added Style (or nil). Truthiness-based code survives; `== true` comparisons break. Also, its second parameter gained a default only in 2026.0 — always pass `select` explicitly for portability across 2023–2026.
12. Page#style HAS NO SETTER ON ANY VERSION, and Page#active_section_planes (new in 2026.0) HAS NO SETTER either. Both are read-only. Restoring style and section-plane state still requires the activate-page-then-Page#update dance, exactly as it did in 2023. The 2026.0 addition improves READING only.
13. PAGE_USE_* CONSTANT VALUES ARE UNPUBLISHED — NEVER HARDCODE THEM. The official stubs define them as nil placeholders and the docs list only names and meanings. Reference the constants symbolically and build masks at runtime. Note the non-obvious spellings: PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO (one word, no underscore, unlike the method shadow_info), PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS (not PAGE_USE_AXES), PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY (singular LAYER). PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT exists only from 2025.0 and referencing it unguarded raises NameError on 2024 — guard with defined?. Do not assume PAGE_USE_ALL includes PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT; OR it in explicitly.
14. Environments#current = nil WAS BROKEN ON FIRST RELEASE. Unsetting the current environment did not work until SketchUp 2025.0.2 ('Fixed a bug where developers were unable to unset the current environment with the Ruby API'). respond_to? cannot detect this because the method exists in all 2025 builds. Either gate on Sketchup.version_number for the 2025.0.2 build, or set it and verify with a read-back of #current.
15. DO NOT CALL Environments#purge_unused DURING AN IMPORT. An environment you have added but not yet assigned to a page counts as unused and will be destroyed. Add → assign to pages → only then consider purging.
16. THE 2025.0 PER-MONITOR DPI CHANGE SILENTLY CHANGED SCREEN COORDINATE SEMANTICS. From 2025.0 all screen coordinates in the Ruby API are LOGICAL pixels, scaled by UI.scale_factor(view), and UI.scale_factor with no argument now always returns 1.0 as a compatibility shim. This affects View#center, #corner, #draw2d, #inputpoint, #pick_helper, #pickray, #screen_coords, #vpwidth, #vpheight, PickHelper#do_pick/#init/#window_pick, InputPoint#pick, and Tool/Overlay mouse events. If your scene tool exports viewport images at a specific pixel size, or draws a preview overlay, the output changed at non-100% DPI between 2024 and 2025. Use the new View#device_width/#device_height for physical pixels and pass Sketchup.resize_viewport(..., logical_pixels: false) explicitly on 2025+.
17. Sketchup::Model#drawing_element_visible? IS BROKEN FROM 2021.1 TO 2025.x when the leaf of the InstancePath is a Group or ComponentInstance — precisely the common case for per-scene entity visibility. Fixed only in 2026.0. Rescue the exception and degrade to 'unknown' on earlier versions.
18. NON-INVERTIBLE TRANSFORMS NOW RAISE. From 2026.0 Geom::Transformation#inverse/#invert! and ComponentInstance/Group#transformation= raise ArgumentError on a degenerate matrix; 2026.1 extended this to #transform!, Image#transform!, Image#transformation=, Entities#transform_entities and Entities#add_instance. If you serialise transformation matrices alongside scenes, JSON precision loss or a zero-scale axis that used to fail silently now aborts your import. Validate before applying.
19. Sketchup.send_action WAS DEPRECATED IN 2026.2 for being asynchronous and fragile. Legacy scene tools often call it around scene capture (e.g. zoom extents); because it is asynchronous it can execute AFTER your Page#update and capture the wrong view. Replace with direct API calls now.
20. MDI AWARENESS (Sketchup.mdi?, 2026.2). In MDI mode Sketchup.active_model can change between calls. For a cross-model transfer tool this is a real hazard: capture the source and target Model objects into locals at the start and never re-call Sketchup.active_model inside the loop.
21. ENCODING WHEN READING BACK ATTRIBUTE STRINGS. A String returned from Model#get_attribute on Windows can carry a non-UTF-8 external encoding, and JSON.parse will raise on non-ASCII scene names or descriptions. Call .force_encoding('UTF-8') (and validate with .valid_encoding?) before parsing.
22. NOTHING CHANGED FOR LAYERS/TAGS IN 2023–2026. No Layer, Layers or LayerFolder API additions appear in any release note across the whole window. The 2026.0 attribute-dictionary additions are LAYOUT-only (Layout::Entity/Document/Page plus the new Layout::Dictionary class) and do not affect Sketchup:: model attributes or cross-model SKP transfer.

### Cross-model data retrieval

1. THE HEADLINE ANSWER: model-level attribute dictionaries (model.set_attribute) do NOT travel to the ComponentDefinition produced by definitions.load in another model. DanRathbun's statement is definitive in the write direction — you must hand-copy model dicts onto a definition before saving it out — and he explicitly confirms the read direction too: on insertion the dictionaries "remain as definition-level attributes" and "will NOT be automatically recopied up to the model object level". The corroborating symptom is that model B's description also comes back nil after definitions.load. Architect on the assumption that model-level storage is invisible cross-model.
2. DEFINITION-LEVEL dictionaries DO survive the round trip. eneroth3: "A component instance doesn't correspond to a model, but a component definition do. If you store the attributes to the definition, they'll be exported with the model." And the documented workaround in the 'Load definition from file will lost information' thread reads d.attribute_dictionaries['dynamic_attributes'] straight off a loaded definition. This is your data channel.
3. INSTANCE-level dictionaries are the classic trap and are LOST on export/re-import. Never use instance.set_attribute for the payload; always instance.definition.set_attribute.
4. definitions.load PLACES NO INSTANCE. It adds the definition to the DefinitionList only — nothing appears in the viewport. Good for a silent probe; but it means the loaded definition is 'unused' and is therefore a purge_unused casualty and also invisible to the user except in the In-Model component browser.
5. THE PATH CACHE IS THE BIGGEST FOOTGUN. DefinitionList#load associates the definition with the source path and "refuses to load components from paths associated with existing components that haven't been modified" — on a second read of the same file you silently get a STALE definition and therefore stale scene JSON. Mitigation: FileUtils.cp the file to a unique filename in Sketchup.temp_dir for every single read, load from there, then delete the temp file. This is the same trick the api-issue-tracker #50 reporter describes ("save out files to temporary locations just to disassociate them with the load path").
6. Inside an open start_operation, definitions.load of the same file twice reuses the existing definition, and component GUIDs do not update until the operation commits. Never attempt two reads of one path inside a single operation.
7. abort_operation is the documented tool for temporary model manipulation with no undo-stack clutter, but there is NO documentation or forum confirmation that it unwinds definitions.load, and it certainly will not clear the internal path-association cache. Belt and braces: definitions.remove(defn) in an ensure block, THEN abort_operation, AND load from a unique temp path.
8. NEVER do start_operation / definitions.remove / commit_operation / Sketchup.undo. api-issue-tracker #75 documents model corruption and crash from exactly that sequence when the definition contains a CPoint. Marked 'logged' with no fix. Use abort_operation, not commit-then-undo.
9. DefinitionList#remove only exists in SketchUp 2018+. On 2015-2017 it raises NoMethodError. Universal fallback: definition.entities.clear! — SketchUp does not allow empty definitions and auto-purges them.
10. NEVER use purge_unused for cleanup. It is indiscriminate and silently destroys every unused definition in the user's model. The docs themselves warn "Silently purging can cause data loss."
11. Loading model B as a definition drags model B's ENTIRE geometry, all nested definitions, and all materials into model A's memory and lists. For a large architectural model this is slow and pollutes the material list with renamed duplicates. This is the single strongest argument for a sidecar JSON file as the primary read path, with the definition probe as fallback.
12. Sketchup.open_file is DESTRUCTIVE on Windows: one document window, so model A is closed and replaced. "on the PC, this is the only model that one can have access to via the API." There is no non-destructive variant and no multi-model Ruby support on Windows Desktop. On macOS it opens an extra window and active_model returns whatever the user focused — non-deterministic. Do not build a background-read on open_file on any platform.
13. Sketchup::Model#close (2021.0) does not rescue the Windows situation. On Mac "only the active model can be closed"; on Windows there is only ever one model to close.
14. The ONLY Ruby API that reads anything from an unopened .skp is Sketchup::Skp.read_guid (2021.0), and it returns only a GUID. Sketchup.save_thumbnail(skp_path, img_path) also reads a file on disk but yields only a raster image. Nothing in 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025 or 2026 added attribute, page, style or metadata reading for files on disk. Full model-level reading requires the C API (SUModelCreateFromFile + SUModelGetAttributeDictionary) via a compiled Ruby C extension.
15. ATTRIBUTE VALUE TYPES are a closed list: Boolean, Integer, Float, Length, nil, String, Time, Array, Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d. HASH IS NOT SUPPORTED — JSON-serialise to a String. Symbols are not supported. Arrays may contain supported types and may nest.
16. NO DOCUMENTED MAXIMUM STRING LENGTH exists for an attribute value, but the file-size cost is severe and superlinear in practice. Measured report: a single 100,000-character string grew a file from 8 kB to 200 kB. Worse anecdote from the forum: "20k of attributes was found to be increasing file size ten fold on a 3mb model [i.e. it was 30mb with the 20kb of attributes added]". Treat several hundred KB of JSON in a dictionary as a serious file-bloat and save-time risk.
17. CHUNKING IS ADVISABLE for anything over ~32KB: split into ~16KB String slices across zero-padded numbered keys (chunk_0000, chunk_0001, ...) plus an explicit chunk_count key. Reassemble by index 0..chunk_count-1 — NEVER by iterating the dictionary, because AttributeDictionary#each does not guarantee insertion order. Always delete stale chunk keys before rewriting, or an old longer payload leaves orphan chunks that bloat the file permanently. Zlib::Deflate + Base64.strict_encode64 typically shrinks scene JSON by 80-90% and is worth doing before chunking.
18. RESERVED / DANGEROUS DICTIONARY NAMES, exact spelling: "SU_InstanceSet" and "SU_DefinitionSet" cannot be deleted (AttributeDictionaries#delete raises ArgumentError); "dynamic_attributes" belongs to Dynamic Components; and DanRathbun warns never to create a dictionary with the EMPTY STRING name "" because the DC extension uses one and you will interfere with it. Namespace yours as AuthorNamespace_SpecificPlugin_DictionaryName.
19. entity.attribute_dictionaries returns nil (not an empty collection) when the entity has no dictionaries — "the collection is not instantiated when it is not needed". Every read must nil-guard, or prefer entity.attribute_dictionary(name) / entity.get_attribute which handle it.
20. attribute_dictionary(name) defaults create to FALSE. Forgetting the second argument is the most common cause of a silently failed write. set_attribute auto-creates; attribute_dictionary does not.
21. Do not mutate a dictionary or a collection while iterating it with #each — the docs warn elements will be skipped. Safe idiom: snapshot with dict.keys (a plain Array) first, then delete_key inside that loop.
22. delete_key vs delete: AttributeDictionary#delete_key removes ONE key; AttributeDictionaries#delete destroys an ENTIRE dictionary. Confusing them silently destroys the wrong thing.
23. SKETCHUP 2026.0 BREAKING CHANGE FOR SCENE RECONSTRUCTION: modifying properties of Sketchup::Axes, Sketchup::Camera, Sketchup::RenderingOptions and Sketchup::ShadowInfo ON A Sketchup::Page is now UNDOABLE and therefore MUST be wrapped in start_operation/commit_operation. Code that wrote page.camera or page.shadow_info outside an operation and worked on 2025 will misbehave or warn on 2026. Wrap all scene restoration in a single operation.
24. SKETCHUP 2026.0 CHANGE: Pages#add and Page#name= now auto-adjust duplicate names to be unique. The Page you get back may not carry the name you requested — always capture the return value and read page.name back rather than assuming, and re-key your JSON mapping accordingly. Pages#unique_name (2026.0) lets you pre-compute it.
25. Pages#reorder only exists from 2025.0. On 2021-2024 the only ordering control is the index argument to Pages#add, so scenes must be created in the intended order.
26. Setting Pages#selected_page= triggers an ANIMATED transition, so reading the camera immediately afterwards gives the previous camera. Zero the transition time or drive the camera directly.
27. Sketchup::Model#guid "changes after modification and save" — capture it AFTER model.save when stamping a sidecar file, or your Sketchup::Skp.read_guid validity check will always mismatch. Its volatility makes it a good cache token and a terrible permanent identity; generate your own UUID for stable identity.
28. ComponentDefinition#save_as mutates definition#path and that change is NOT recorded on the undo stack (api-issue-tracker #74), so abort_operation will not restore it. Prefer #save_copy (2022.0+), which writes the file without touching #path.
29. DefinitionList#load(path, allow_newer: false) — the default really is false despite documentation to the contrary (api-issue-tracker #997). Pass allow_newer: true when probing user files of unknown vintage, and then never write back to that file.
30. Model#import for a .skp does NOT put entities into model.entities — "They are packaged in a new component" — and it returns only true/false, plus it renames colliding materials and definitions. Always use definitions.load for .skp, never import.
31. persistent_id and guid are stable within one model but are NOT preserved across definitions.load into a different model. Do not use them as cross-model foreign keys for scene tag/layer visibility state; key off names plus your own generated identifiers.
32. PRIOR ART CONFIRMS THE ARCHITECTURE: every existing extension that moves scenes between models does it through an EXTERNAL INTERMEDIARY FILE, not by reading the other .skp. PageExIm.rb (Smustard) exports/imports scenes with camera, shadow settings and behaviours via an export file; the SketchUcation Camera Settings Export/Import plugin writes a .txt (e.g. D:\\SU_camera_export.txt) and matches scenes by identical scene name on import. Nobody has solved it by reading the foreign .skp directly, which is strong evidence that no clean Ruby route exists.
33. eneroth3's warning applies directly to this design: "Model attributes should be kept to a minimum" because they bloat files and travel with the document. Reserve model-level storage for genuinely project-specific data, and put global plugin preferences in an external JSON under AppData/Application Support (per DanRathbun) rather than in the model or in read_default/write_default, which has documented macOS problems since the 2018 JSON format change.
34. A carrier definition with NO instance in model B's root is vulnerable to purge_unused (run by the user or by any other extension) and may not be reconstructed by definitions.load at all, since load rebuilds model B's ROOT ENTITIES. Always place a hidden, locked instance. Prefer a ComponentInstance over a Group as the carrier — Groups get their definitions silently made unique on edit, which orphans the attributes.

---

## Part 7 — Generated API reference

Every method, constant and dictionary key found, with exact spelling and capitalisation.
These are literal Ruby identifiers and string keys — one wrong character is a `NameError`
or a silently ignored write.

`Ser.` column: **direct** = JSON-safe as-is; **encode** = needs a tagged encoding;
**READ-ONLY** = computed by SketchUp, never assign it; **no** = not transferable.

### Sketchup::Page and Sketchup::Pages  —  95 entries

| Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `active_section_planes` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::SectionPlane> or nil | SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | The #active_section_planes method is used to retrieve the active section plane for the Sketchup::Page. Returns nil if the page does not use section planes. | Read-only; there is no active_section_planes= setter. Do NOT store the SectionPlane objects. Serialise each plane's identity portably: {"persistent_id": sp.persistent_id, "name": sp.name, "plane": sp.get_plane.to_a} — but on rebuild in a different model you must match section planes by name (SectionPlane#name, SU2018+) or by plane coefficients, since entityID/persistent_id are model-local. To reconstruct: create/locate the equivalent SectionPlane in the target model, set sp.active = true while the target page is the selected page, then page.update(PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES). Only present in SU2026.0+ — guard with page.respond_to?(:active_section_planes). |
| `axes` | instance_method | Sketchup::Axes | SketchUp 2016 | encode | The axes method returns the drawing axes for the page. Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying the axes of a scene is an undoable operation (wrap in start_operation/commit_operation). Documented example: page.axes.set([10,0,0], xaxis, yaxis, Z_AXIS) followed by page.update(PAGE_USE_ALL). | Live object — mutate it, do not replace it (there is no axes= setter). Encode as {"origin": page.axes.origin.to_a, "xaxis": page.axes.xaxis.to_a, "yaxis": page.axes.yaxis.to_a, "zaxis": page.axes.zaxis.to_a} (four 3-element Float arrays; origin is in inches). Rebuild with page.axes.set(Geom::Point3d.new(*origin), Geom::Vector3d.new(*xaxis), Geom::Vector3d.new(*yaxis), Geom::Vector3d.new(*zaxis)). Only meaningful when use_axes? is true. |
| `camera` | instance_method | Sketchup::Camera | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The #camera method retrieves the camera for a particular page. Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying the camera properties of a scene is an undoable operation. The class-level SU2026 documentation shows page.camera.fov = 56.78 inside a start_operation/commit_operation block. | THERE IS NO Page#camera= SETTER — this is the single biggest trap. Serialise the scalars: {"eye": cam.eye.to_a, "target": cam.target.to_a, "up": cam.up.to_a, "perspective": cam.perspective?, "fov": cam.fov, "aspect_ratio": cam.aspect_ratio, "height": cam.height, "is_2d": cam.is_2d?, "focal_length": cam.focal_length, "image_width": cam.image_width, "description": cam.description}. All lengths are Float inches; fov is Float degrees; height is the parallel-projection extent in inches. Rebuild EITHER by mutating in place — page.camera.perspective = persp; page.camera.set(Geom::Point3d.new(*eye), Geom::Point3d.new(*target), Geom::Vector3d.new(*up)); page.camera.fov = fov (perspective) or page.camera.height = height (parallel); page.camera.aspect_ratio = ar — OR by the historically bulletproof route: build a fresh Sketchup::Camera, assign model.active_view.camera = cam, then page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA). Set perspective BEFORE fov/height, because fov= is ignored in parallel projection and height= is ignored in perspective. aspect_ratio 0.0 means 'match the viewport'. |
| `delay_time` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The delay_time method retrieves the amount of time, in seconds, that a page will be displayed before transition to another page during a tour. The default delay time can be modified in the Model Info > Animation panel of the SketchUp User Interface. If this method returns -1, the default delay time is used. | Plain Float, JSON-safe. The sentinel value -1 means 'inherit the model's default delay'. Round-trip it verbatim; do not normalise -1 to 0 or you will silently change animation behaviour. |
| `delay_time=` | instance_method | Float (returns the number of seconds of delay) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The delay_time= method sets the amount of time, in seconds, that a page will be displayed before transitioning to another page during a tour. If you set the delay for a page to be -1, the default delay time will be used. | page.delay_time = json['delay_time'].to_f. Accepts -1 to restore the model default. Safe to call at any time, before or after Pages#add; unaffected by any use_* flag. |
| `description` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The description method retrieves the description for a page as found in the Scenes manager dialog. | Plain String (may be empty). May contain newlines and non-ASCII — ensure UTF-8-clean JSON encoding. Store verbatim. |
| `description=` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The description method sets the description for a page as found in the Scenes manager dialog. | page.description = json['description'].to_s. Independent of every use_* flag; set it any time after the page exists. |

If the HDR asset is unavailable in the target model, skip and set page.use_environment = false. Guard the whole branch with page.respond_to?(:environment) — SU2025.0+ only. |
| `environment=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environment | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | The #environment= method is used to set the Sketchup::Environment for the scene. Cross-model reconstruction requires importing/creating the environment in the target model first. SU2025.0+ only. |
| `get_drawingelement_visibility` | instance_method | Boolean (true if visible, false if not) | SketchUp 2020.0 | encode | The #get_drawingelement_visibility method is used to get the visibility of a drawing element on a particular page. The docs include a helper example: for ComponentInstance/Group return unless page.use_hidden_objects?; otherwise return unless page.use_hidden_geometry? and return unless element.parent == Sketchup.active_model; then page.get_drawingelement_visibility(element). | Per-entity override, keyed on a live Drawingelement — the hardest part to port across models. Serialise as a map of element persistent_id (SU2017+) or a stable custom GUID you stamp into each element's attribute dictionary → Boolean. entityID is NOT stable across save/reload and NEVER portable across models; persistent_id is stable within a model across sessions but still not portable. For cross-model work, write your own key (e.g. entity.set_attribute('NA_SceneExport','guid', SecureRandom.uuid)) on the source side and match on it in the target. The result is only meaningful when use_hidden_geometry? / use_hidden_objects? is true — the doc example checks those first. |
| `hidden_entities` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::Drawingelement> or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Ruby docs (class page): 'Returns the drawing elements whose visibility is overridden by this page.' Stub text: 'The hidden_entities method retrieves all hidden entities within a page.' @return: an array of drawing elements that are *hidden* on the page. | NOTE the two doc sources disagree in wording: the rendered docs say 'visibility is overridden by this page' while the stub says 'all hidden entities'. Treat it as the override list and verify each element with get_drawingelement_visibility(el) rather than assuming every returned element is hidden. Serialise using your own portable per-element key (see get_drawingelement_visibility notes), never entityID. Reconstruct with page.set_drawingelement_visibility(el, false). |
