Returns the Page, not a Boolean — do not test its return value. Independent of the PAGE_USE_* flag system; safe to set at any time. |
| `include_in_animation?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2018 | direct | The #include_in_animation? method determines whether the page should be included when exporting an animation from the model. Documented example: model.pages.select { |page| page.include_in_animation? }. | Plain true/false → JSON boolean. Guard with respond_to? if you must support SU2017 and older. |
| `label` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The label method retrieves the label for a page from the page tab. @return label - a string label for the page tab. | READ-ONLY — there is no label= setter. In practice label mirrors #name (the text drawn on the scene tab) and can differ from name only in SketchUp-internal display situations. Do NOT round-trip it: capture #name for reconstruction and store label only as diagnostic metadata. |
| `layer_folders` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::LayerFolder> or nil | SketchUp 2021.0 | encode | The #layer_folders method retrieves the hidden layer folders associated with a page. @return: Returns nil if #use_hidden_layers? returns false. | RETURNS nil (not []) when use_hidden_layers? is false — always nil-guard. These are Tag Folders in the UI. Serialise by full folder path/name: LayerFolder#name plus its parent chain (folder.parent walks up to Sketchup::Layers or another LayerFolder), because names are only unique within a parent. Rebuild by locating the equivalent folder in the target model and calling page.set_visibility(folder, false). Requires SU2021.0+ — guard with page.respond_to?(:layer_folders). |
| `layers` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::Layer> or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The #layers method retrieves layers that don't use their default visibility on this page. @return: Returns nil if #use_hidden_layers? returns false. Documented test-helper example: def visible_in_scene?(layer, scene); scene.layers.include?(layer) == hidden_by_default?(layer); end / def hidden_by_default?(layer); layer.page_behavior & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT == LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT; end | BADLY NAMED — this is NOT 'the visible layers'. It is the EXCEPTION list: layers whose visibility on this page differs from their model-level default (Layer#page_behavior). A layer in this array is visible on the page iff it is hidden by default, and hidden on the page iff it is visible by default — exactly the doc's visible_in_scene? formula. Returns nil when use_hidden_layers? is false. For a portable JSON payload, do NOT store this raw array: resolve every tag to an absolute boolean, e.g. model.layers.map { |l| [l.name, page.layers ? (page.layers.include?(l) == ((l.page_behavior & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT) == LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT)) : l.visible?] }.to_h. Rebuild with page.set_visibility(target_layer, bool) per tag, matched by Layer#name (tag names are unique per model and are the only portable key). Relevant constants: LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT=0, LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT=1, LAYER_USES_DEFAULT_VISIBILITY_ON_NEW_PAGES=0, LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES=16, LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES=32. |

Store verbatim. Note that on rebuild the name may be altered for uniqueness (SU2026 makes both Pages#add and Page#name= uniquify), so always read page.name BACK after creating it and keep a source_name → actual_name map in your import result. |
| `name=` | instance_method | String (returns the name that you have set) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #name= method sets the name for a page's tab. If the name is already used by another page, a unique name is created. @bug Prior to SketchUp 2026.0 this method did not make the name unique. | BEHAVIOUR CHANGE IN SU2026.0: names are now auto-uniquified. On SU2025 and older this method would happily create duplicate scene names, which then broke Pages#['name'] lookups. Always re-read page.name after assignment. On SU2026 you can pre-compute a safe name with model.pages.unique_name(base). |

Those options should not be changed from the scene. The ones not related to Sketchup::Style like fog (DisplayFog, FogColor) are safe to be changed from the scene. | Live keyed collection, no setter for the collection itself. Serialise with page.rendering_options.each_pair { |k, v| ... } into a {String => value} hash. Values are mixed types: Integer, Float, Boolean, String, and Sketchup::Color — encode Color as v.to_a ([r,g,b,a] Integers 0-255) and tag the type, e.g. {"type":"Color","value":[255,0,255,255]}, otherwise JSON.generate will emit something you cannot decode. Rebuild with page.rendering_options[key] = value, converting colour arrays back with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). HEED THE OFFICIAL NOTE: only write the non-Style options (DisplayFog, FogColor, etc.) from the page; let use_style=/Style ownership drive the rest, or the style and the scene will fight. Only meaningful when use_rendering_options? is true. |
| `set_drawingelement_visibility` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.0 | READ-ONLY | The #set_drawingelement_visibility method is used to change the visibility of a drawing element on a particular page. Only drawing elements on the root of the model, as well as nested instances of components, groups, and images are controlled by Page visibility. Documented example: page.set_drawingelement_visibility(constpoint, false). | This is the WRITE path for per-entity scene overrides — the reconstruction counterpart of get_drawingelement_visibility/hidden_entities. Scope limit is explicit in the docs: root-level drawing elements plus nested instances of components/groups/images. Loose nested geometry (edges/faces inside a group) cannot be overridden per-scene. For it to take effect on activation the page needs use_hidden_geometry? (loose root geometry) and/or use_hidden_objects? (instances/groups) enabled. |
| `set_visibility` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page (the page whose visibility was set) | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #set_visibility method sets the visibility for a layer or layer folder on a page. Overload 1 (SU6.0): (Sketchup::Layer layer, Boolean visible_for_page). Overload 2 (SU2021.0): (Sketchup::LayerFolder layer_folder, Boolean visible_for_page). Documented example: layer = model.layers.add('My Layer'); page.set_visibility(layer, false). | THE method for rebuilding tag visibility in a target model, and per Aerilius on the SketchUp forum it works WITHOUT activating the page — unlike camera/shadow/rendering state, tag visibility does not need the view-then-update dance. Takes an ABSOLUTE boolean (true = visible on this page), so it hides the page_behavior/default-visibility inversion from you. The layer or folder must belong to the same model as the page. Returns the Page, so it chains but is not a success Boolean. Requires use_hidden_layers = true on the page for the setting to have any effect when the page is activated. |
| `shadow_info` | instance_method | Sketchup::ShadowInfo | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The #shadow_info method retrieves the ShadowInfo object for the page. Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying shadow_info of a scene is an undoable operation. @note While certain shadow settings, such as those available in the Shadows panel, can be controlled on a per-page basis, global settings like north angle, latitude, and longitude are managed at the model level and are not page-specific. | Live keyed collection; no setter for the collection. Serialise with page.shadow_info.each_pair into a {String => value} hash. Value types to watch: 'ShadowTime' and 'ShadowTime_time_t' are Time — encode as Integer epoch seconds (t.to_i) and decode with Time.at(i); 'City' is String; 'Latitude'/'Longitude'/'NorthAngle'/'TZOffset'/'Light'/'Dark'/'SunRise'/'SunSet' are Float; 'DisplayShadows'/'UseSunForAllShading'/'DisplayOnAllFaces'/'DisplayOnGroundPlane' are Boolean. CRITICAL per the official note: NorthAngle, Latitude, Longitude and City are MODEL-level, not page-level — writing them via page.shadow_info changes the whole target model. When porting scenes into a different model, write the geo/north keys ONCE from model.shadow_info (and only if you intend to), and write only the true per-page keys (ShadowTime, DisplayShadows, Light, Dark, UseSunForAllShading, DisplayOnAllFaces, DisplayOnGroundPlane) per page. Only meaningful when use_shadow_info? is true. |
| `style` | instance_method | Sketchup::Style | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The style method retrieves the style associated with the page. @return style - the Style object if successful. | Read-only getter; the writer is the oddly-named use_style= (which takes a Style object, NOT a boolean). Serialise identity only: {"style_name": page.style && page.style.name, "style_description": page.style && page.style.description, "style_guid": page.style && page.style.guid}. On rebuild, find the matching style in the target model by name (model.styles.find { |s| s.name == name }); if absent, either import the .style file first via model.styles.add_style(path, false) or leave use_style? false. Style#guid is NOT portable across models. Can return nil if the page stores no style. |
| `transition_time` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Get the amount of time that it takes to transition to this page during a slideshow or animation export. If this value is -1, it means to use the default transition time. | Plain Float, JSON-safe. -1 is the 'use model default' sentinel — preserve it exactly. |
| `transition_time=` | instance_method | Float (returns the new transition time) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The transition_time= method is used to set the transition time. @param trans_time The transition time in seconds. | page.transition_time = json['transition_time'].to_f. Accepts -1 for the model default. Note that when you are bulk-rebuilding scenes it is worth setting model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition'] = false first so SketchUp does not animate between every scene you touch (standard forum practice), then restore it afterwards. |
| `update` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #update method performs an update on the page properties based on the current view that the user has. What properties of the Page get updated are controlled via an integer whose bits corresponds to different properties. These flags can be used individually or combined using bitwise OR. Documented flag list verbatim: PAGE_USE_CAMERA # Camera Location; PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS # Drawing Style; PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO # Shadow Setting; PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS # Axes Location; PAGE_USE_HIDDEN # Hidden Geometry & Objects (Up 2019 and older); PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY # Hidden Geometry (SU 2020 and later); PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS # Hidden Objects (SU 2020 and later); PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY # Visible Layers; PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES # Active Section Planes; PAGE_USE_ALL # All possible scene properties; PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT # Environment settings. Documented example: status = page.update  /  flags = PAGE_USE_CAMERA | PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO | PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY; status = page.update(flags). | THE BAKE OPERATION. It COPIES the current model/view state into the page for each bit set, and switches the corresponding use_* slot ON. It reads from the CURRENT VIEW, never from arguments — Aerilius on the SketchUp forum: 'the API lacks a way to specify the values of these properties, but it can only take them from the current view, which requires changing the camera of the active view.' Bits you do NOT pass are left untouched. The stub signature is update(flags) (required arg) but the documented example calls page.update with no argument, which works and defaults to PAGE_USE_ALL — pass the flag explicitly anyway. The page does NOT need to be the selected page (Aerilius, same thread). TIMING TRAP (DanRathbun, forums.sketchup.com/t/problem-with-page-update/40414): if you have just changed the view (set a camera, called zoom_extents, toggled a standard view), SketchUp may not have applied it when update runs — defer with UI.start_timer(0.1, false) { page.update(flags) } or the page bakes the OLD view. |
| `use_axes=` | instance_method | Boolean (returns true if you are saving the property) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_axes= method sets the page's axes property. @param pagesettings: true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property. Documented example: status = page.use_axes=false. | Boolean → JSON boolean. Note the parameter is named 'pagesettings' here but 'setting' on every sibling — cosmetic only. Corresponds to bit PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS (8), NOT a 'PAGE_USE_AXES' constant, which does not exist. Order: set this true BEFORE writing page.axes.set(...), otherwise the axes write is stored against a disabled slot. |
| `use_axes?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_axes? method determines whether you are storing the axes property with the page. @return status - true if you are storing this property with the page, false if you are not storing this property with the page. | Plain Boolean. Serialise as "use_axes": page.use_axes?; only bother capturing the axes payload when this is true. |
| `use_camera=` | instance_method | Boolean (returns true if you are saving the property) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_camera= method sets the page's camera property. @param setting: true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property. Documented example: status = page.use_camera = true. | Boolean → JSON boolean. Corresponds to bit PAGE_USE_CAMERA (1). MUST be true before the page will store or apply any camera. Set it true FIRST, then write the camera (mutate page.camera, or set the view camera and call page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA) which sets this flag for you as a side-effect). |
| `use_camera?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_camera? method determines whether you are storing the camera property with the page. | Plain Boolean. A scene with use_camera? == false does not move the view at all when clicked — it is a 'settings-only' scene. Capture this faithfully or you will turn property-only scenes into camera scenes on import. |
| `use_environment=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | The #use_environment= method is used to set if the Sketchup::Environment settings are used in the scene. Documented example shows the default: page.use_environment? # => true, then page.use_environment = false, then page.use_environment? # => false. | Boolean → JSON boolean. DEFAULT IS true on a freshly added page (per the doc example). Corresponds to bit PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT. SU2025.0+ only — always guard with page.respond_to?(:use_environment=) so the exporter/importer still runs on older SketchUp. |
| `use_environment?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | The #use_environment? method is used to determine if the Sketchup::Environment settings are used in the scene. | Plain Boolean, default true. SU2025.0+ only; emit null/omit the key on older versions and treat a missing key as 'leave at default' on import. |
| `use_hidden=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_hidden= method sets the page's hidden property. @deprecated The functionality is replaced by #use_hidden_geometry= and #use_hidden_objects= in SketchUp 2020.1. @see #use_hidden_geometry= @see #use_hidden_objects= | DEPRECATED since SU2020.1 but still present and still functional in SU2026. Corresponds to legacy bit PAGE_USE_HIDDEN (16). Do NOT write this in a 2026-targeted plugin — write use_hidden_geometry= and use_hidden_objects= instead. Capture it on export only as a legacy fallback for models produced by SU2019 and older. |
| `use_hidden?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_hidden? method determines whether you are storing the hidden property with the page. @deprecated The functionality is replaced by #use_hidden_geometry? and #use_hidden_objects? in SketchUp 2020.1. | DEPRECATED. On SU2020.1+ this reports the merged legacy state; prefer use_hidden_geometry?/use_hidden_objects?. Safe to record in JSON as a legacy field but never use it as the authoritative value when both modern flags exist. |
| `use_hidden_geometry=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.1 | direct | Sets the page's use hidden geometry property. @param [Boolean] setting: true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property. | Boolean → JSON boolean. Corresponds to bit PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY (0x0080 = 128). Governs per-scene hidden/unhidden state of LOOSE ROOT-LEVEL geometry (edges, faces, cpoints directly in model.entities). Guard with respond_to? for SU2020.0 and older. |
| `use_hidden_geometry?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.1 | direct | Returns the use hidden geometry property from the page. | Plain Boolean. The docs' element_visible_in_page? example checks this (plus element.parent == model) before trusting get_drawingelement_visibility for non-instance elements. |
| `use_hidden_layers=` | instance_method | Boolean (returns true if you are saving the property) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Sets the page's hidden layers property. @param setting: true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property. Documented example: status = page.use_hidden_layers = false. | Boolean → JSON boolean. NAMING TRAP: the flag constant is PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY (32) — there is no PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_LAYERS constant. This is the 'Visible Tags' checkbox in the Scenes panel. MUST be set true BEFORE calling page.set_visibility(layer, bool) has any effect on activation, and while it is false both page.layers and page.layer_folders return nil. |
| `use_hidden_layers?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_hidden_layers? method determines whether you are storing the hidden layers property with the page. | Plain Boolean. It is the documented gate for #layers and #layer_folders — both return nil when this is false. Always test it before iterating either. |
| `use_hidden_objects=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.1 | direct | Sets the page's use hidden objects property. @param [Boolean] setting: true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property. | Boolean → JSON boolean. Corresponds to bit PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS (0x0100 = 256). Governs per-scene hidden state of OBJECTS — ComponentInstances, Groups and Images (including nested ones). Guard with respond_to? for SU2020.0 and older. |
| `use_hidden_objects?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.1 | direct | Returns the use hidden objects property from the page. | Plain Boolean. Per the docs' element_visible_in_page? example, this is the gate to check for ComponentInstance and Group elements before calling get_drawingelement_visibility. |
| `use_rendering_options=` | instance_method | Boolean (returns true if you are saving the property) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Stub text (note the typo in the source comment: 'The use_rendering_optoins= method sets the page's display settings property.'). @param setting: true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property. Documented example: status = page.use_rendering_options = false. | Boolean → JSON boolean. Corresponds to bit PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS (2). The METHOD name is correctly spelled use_rendering_options= — only the doc comment contains the 'optoins' typo. Must be true before page.rendering_options[...] = ... has any effect on activation. |
| `use_rendering_options?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_rendering_options? method determines whether you are storing the rendering options property with the page. (Rendered docs phrase it: determines if the page saves its own unique rendering options.) | Plain Boolean. When false, page.rendering_options still returns a RenderingOptions object but the page will not override the model's on activation — so a value read here can be misleading. Gate your export on this flag. |
| `use_section_planes=` | instance_method | Boolean (returns true if you are saving the property) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_section_planes= method sets the page's section planes property. @param setting: true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property. Documented example: status = page.use_section_planes=false. | Boolean → JSON boolean. Corresponds to bit PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES (64). Must be true for the page to remember which SectionPlane is active; while false, Page#active_section_planes (SU2026) returns nil. |
| `use_section_planes?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_section_planes? method determines whether you are storing the section planes property with the page. | Plain Boolean, and the documented gate for active_section_planes returning nil. |
| `use_shadow_info=` | instance_method | Boolean (returns true if you are saving the property) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_shadow_info= method sets the page's shadow info property. @param setting: true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property. Documented example: status = page.use_shadow_info=false. | Boolean → JSON boolean. Corresponds to bit PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO (4) — note the constant spells it SHADOWINFO with no underscore between SHADOW and INFO, while the method uses use_shadow_info. Must be true before per-page shadow writes take effect on activation. |
| `use_shadow_info?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_shadow_info? method determines whether you are storing the shadow info property with the page. | Plain Boolean. Remember that even when true, the geo keys (NorthAngle/Latitude/Longitude/City) are model-level and are NOT actually per-page — see the shadow_info note. |
| `use_style=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Style in, nil out | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The use_style= method sets the style to be used by the page. @param style: The Style object to use. @return nil. Documented example: style = model.styles[0]; page.use_style = style. | THE BIGGEST NAMING TRAP ON THIS CLASS. Despite the use_* naming, this does NOT take a Boolean — it takes a Sketchup::Style OBJECT, and it is the ONLY writer for the page's style (there is no style= method). Passing true/false here is wrong. It returns nil, not a status. There is also no PAGE_USE_STYLE constant and no style bit for Page#update — style is outside the flag system entirely. To reconstruct: find/import the style in the TARGET model by name and call page.use_style = that_style. To turn the style slot off there is no documented Boolean path; simply leave it unset on a fresh page (use_style? is false until you assign one). |
| `use_style?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_style? method determines whether storing a style with the page. (Rendered docs: determines if the page saves style properties.) @return status - true if you are storing this property with the page, false if you are not. | Plain Boolean — asymmetric with its 'setter' use_style=, which takes a Style. Read it to decide whether to emit the style_name field at all. |
| `add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Adds an EntityObserver to this Page. | Runtime only. Useful during an import to watch for onChangeEntity, but nothing to serialise. Always pair with remove_observer to avoid leaks across model loads. |
| `attribute_dictionaries` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Retrieves the AttributeDictionaries collection attached to the entity. Returns nil if the entity has no dictionaries. | RETURNS nil, NOT an empty collection, when the page has no dictionaries — always nil-guard. Iterate to snapshot custom per-scene metadata your own plugin (or a third-party one) wrote: (page.attribute_dictionaries || []).each { |d| d.each_pair { |k,v| ... } }. Values can be Length/Color/Point3d/Vector3d/Time as well as plain scalars, so tag types when encoding. This is also exactly the mechanism you will use to stash your scene JSON on the model (model.set_attribute(dict, key, json_string)). |
| `attribute_dictionary` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionary or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Retrieves an attribute dictionary by name; if create is true and the dictionary does not exist, it is created. | Second argument defaults to FALSE — page.attribute_dictionary('MyDict') returns nil when absent. Pass true explicitly to create: page.attribute_dictionary('NA_SceneExport', true). Use a namespaced dictionary name for your plugin's per-scene bookkeeping (e.g. the source scene's original name/GUID) so re-imports can be matched idempotently. |
| `delete_attribute` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. If only the dictionary_name is given, deletes the entire AttributeDictionary. Otherwise deletes the attribute with the given key from the given dictionary. @bug Prior to SketchUp 2019.0 the return value was always true. @note In SketchUp 2018 special attribute dictionaries 'SU_InstanceSet' and 'SU_DefinitionSet' were added; these cannot be deleted via Ruby and an ArgumentError will be raised, though their key/value pairs can be deleted safely. | Two overloads (whole dictionary vs single key). Return value is untrustworthy before SU2019.0 (always true). Use it to clear stale scene-export metadata on re-import. |
| `deleted?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Determines if the entity is still valid (not deleted by another script, for example). | Guard every cached Page reference with this (or valid?) before touching it — a Pages#erase elsewhere, or an undo, invalidates your handle and further calls raise. Essential in observer callbacks and deferred UI.start_timer blocks. |
| `entityID` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Retrieves a unique id for the entity within the current SketchUp session. | NOTE THE CAPITALISATION: entityID, capital I capital D — not entity_id and not entityId. SESSION-LOCAL ONLY: it changes on save/reload and is meaningless in a different model. NEVER use it as a JSON key for cross-model scene reconstruction. Emit it only as debug metadata, if at all. |
| `get_attribute` | instance_method | Object (whatever was stored) or default_value | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Retrieves the value of the given key from the given dictionary; returns default_value (nil unless supplied) if the dictionary or key does not exist. | Exact argument order is (dict_name, key, default_value). Both dict_name and key are Strings and are case-sensitive. Returned values keep their SketchUp type (Length, Color, Point3d, Time, Array...), so normalise before JSON.generate. This is the read side of your model-level scene store: model.get_attribute('NA_SceneExport', 'scenes_json', '{}'). |
| `inspect` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Formats the entity as a string for inspection/debugging. | Debug only; the exact format is not contractual and differs between SketchUp versions. Never parse it. |
| `model` | instance_method | Sketchup::Model | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Retrieves the model that contains the entity. | Runtime only. Vital for correctness in a two-model workflow: always resolve layers, styles, environments and section planes through page.model, never through Sketchup.active_model, or you will hand objects from the wrong model to set_visibility/use_style= and SketchUp will reject them or crash. |
| `parent` | instance_method | Sketchup::Model (for a Page) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. The parent will be a ComponentDefinition, a Group, a Model, or whatever the entity is contained within. For a Page this is the Sketchup::Model. | Runtime only. Note Sketchup::Pages also defines its own #parent returning the Model. |
| `persistent_id` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2017 (Sketchup::Page support added in SketchUp 2018) | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Retrieves a unique persistent id assigned to an entity. The persistent id is persistent between sessions. The documented support table lists Sketchup::Page under [SketchUp 2018]. | Sketchup::Page DOES support persistent_id, since SU2018 (confirmed in the Entity docs' support table). Unlike entityID it survives save/reload — but it is still MODEL-LOCAL and therefore NOT a valid key for reconstructing a scene in a different model. Use it as the stable key for round-tripping within the SAME model (re-import/update-in-place); use Page#name plus your own stamped GUID for cross-model matching. |
| `remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Removes an observer from the current object. | Runtime only. Must be passed the same observer instance that was added. |
| `set_attribute` | instance_method | Object (the value that was set) | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Sets the value of a key in the given attribute dictionary, creating the dictionary if needed. | Argument order (dict_name, key, value); both names are case-sensitive Strings. Storable value types are limited to Ruby scalars plus SketchUp value types (Boolean, Integer, Float, String, Length, Array, Color, Point3d, Vector3d, Time) — you cannot store a Hash. For your scene payload, JSON.generate the whole structure to ONE String and store that String, e.g. model.set_attribute('NA_SceneExport', 'scenes_v1', JSON.generate(data)). Very large Strings are fine but bloat the .skp; consider compressing/base64 if the payload is huge. Wrap writes in model.start_operation/commit_operation so the write is undoable and does not spam the undo stack. |
| `to_s` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Formats the entity as a String. | Debug only. For a Page this is NOT the scene name — use #name. |
| `typename` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Retrieves the type of the entity, e.g. 'Page'. | Returns the String 'Page' for a Sketchup::Page. Cheap sanity check when reading back heterogeneous entity collections; prefer is_a?(Sketchup::Page) in new code. |
| `valid?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Determines if the entity is valid (i.e. not deleted). The inverse of deleted?. | Use page.valid? as the guard in any deferred block (UI.start_timer, observer callback) before calling anything on a cached Page. |
| `Sketchup::Pages.add_frame_change_observer` | class_method | Integer (a unique id of the observer) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The .add_frame_change_observer method is used to add a new frame change observer that is called with each frame of an animation, meaning the end user has clicked on a Scene tab (aka Page) inside SketchUp and the camera is animating to that scene. The method returns an integer id that can be stored and later used to remove the observer with Sketchup::Pages.remove_frame_change_observer. @param [#frameChange] object — An object that implements the Sketchup::FrameChangeObserver#frameChange method. Documented example: class MyFrameChangeObserver; def frameChange(from_page, to_page, percent_done); ...; end; end; id = Sketchup::Pages.add_frame_change_observer(MyFrameChangeObserver.new). | CLASS method on Sketchup::Pages, not an instance method — call it as Sketchup::Pages.add_frame_change_observer(obj), never model.pages.add_frame_change_observer. The observer object must implement frameChange(from_page, to_page, percent_done) — note the camelCase 'frameChange'. KEEP A REFERENCE to both the observer object and the returned Integer id, or Ruby GC may collect the observer. Not part of scene serialisation; useful only if you want to react while SketchUp animates to a restored scene. |
| `Sketchup::Pages.remove_frame_change_observer` | class_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The .remove_frame_change_observer method is used to remove a frame change observer. @param [Integer] observer_id — The unique id returned by .add_frame_change_observer. Documented example: Sketchup::Pages.remove_frame_change_observer(id). | Takes the INTEGER ID returned by add_frame_change_observer, NOT the observer object — this is different from every other remove_observer in the API. Class method. |
| `[]` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The [] method retrieves a page by either name or index. @param index_or_name — The index or the string name of the specific page. @return page - a Page object if successful. Documented example: pages.add 'Page 1'; pages.add 'Page 2'; page = pages['Page 2']. | Dual-typed argument: Integer index (0-based) OR String name. Returns nil when not found (does not raise). Negative indices are not documented — do not rely on them. Because duplicate scene names were possible before SU2026, a String lookup on a legacy model may return only the first match; prefer iterating with #each and comparing names yourself when correctness matters. |
| `add` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #add method is used to add a new Page object to the collection. If no name is given, then a new name is generated using the default name for new Pages. If a name is given, then a new Page with that name is added. If the name is already used by another page, a unique name is created. If the flags parameter is given, it controls which properties are saved with the Page. See the Page#update method for a description of the flags that can be set. If index is given, it specifies the position in the page list that the new page is added. Otherwise the new page is added to the end. @bug Prior to SketchUp 2026.0 this method didn't make the name unique. @param [String] name; @param [Integer] flags — Bit flags in integer form; @param [Integer] index — Index of where to inset. | EXACT SIGNATURE: add(name = nil, flags = PAGE_USE_ALL, index = self.size) — three positional args, no keyword args, no block. The default flags value is PAGE_USE_ALL (0x0fff = 4095), so a bare pages.add('Name') creates a scene that captures EVERYTHING from the current view/model state at that instant. The flags argument decides which use_* slots are ON and which current-state values get baked in AT CREATION TIME — it is not merely a hint. To create a scene that stores only what you intend, pass an explicit mask, e.g. model.pages.add('S1', PAGE_USE_CAMERA | PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY). Since SU2026 the name is uniquified automatically (use pages.unique_name(base) to predict it); before SU2026 duplicates were allowed and silently broke Pages#[] lookups. Always capture the return value and re-read .name. |
| `add_matchphoto_page` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page | SketchUp 7.0 | READ-ONLY | The #add_matchphoto_page method is used to add a photomatch page to the model. This is an advanced feature that was added to support internal SketchUp work, so it is unlikely to be useful to you. @param [String] image_filename — The image file to use as the background. @param [Sketchup::Camera] camera; @param [String] page_name. Documented example: page = pages.add_matchphoto_page('/path/to/image.png'). | THE ONLY Pages method that accepts a Camera object directly — but it always creates a photo-match scene with a background image, so it is not a general-purpose 'add scene with this camera' route. For scene serialisation: if a source scene is a match-photo scene you cannot faithfully rebuild it from Page methods alone; detect it (the model will have a matching Sketchup::Image / matchphoto data) and either skip it, or re-create with add_matchphoto_page and copy the image file alongside your JSON. Officially discouraged for third-party use. |
| `add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean (true if successful, false if unsuccessful) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The add_observer method is used to add an observer to the Pages object. See the PagesObserver interface for more details. Documented example: status = pages.add_observer observer. | Takes a Sketchup::PagesObserver (callbacks include onContentsModified, onElementAdded, onElementRemoved). Suppress your own observer during a bulk import or you will recurse. Keep a strong reference to the observer instance. |
| `count` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | @note Since SketchUp 2014 the count method is inherited from Ruby's Enumerable mix-in module. Prior to that the #count method is an alias for #length. Documented example: pages.add('Page 1'); pages.add('Page 2'); number = pages.count. | Integer → JSON number. Since SU2014 it is Enumerable#count, so it also accepts a block or an argument (pages.count { |p| p.use_camera? }) — which #size and #length do not. For a plain total prefer #size (O(1)) over #count (may iterate). |
| `each` | instance_method | nil (yields Sketchup::Page) | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #each method is used to iterate through pages in the model. @yieldparam [Sketchup::Page] page. @return [nil]. Documented example: pages.add('Page 1'); pages.add('Page 2'); pages.each { |page| puts page.name }. | Returns nil, NOT the collection — do not chain off it. Sketchup::Pages includes Enumerable, so map/select/find/sort_by/to_a all work. NEVER erase pages while iterating with #each — snapshot first (model.pages.to_a.each { |p| model.pages.erase(p) }) or you will skip entries. |
| `erase` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #erase method is used to remove a page from the collection. @param [Sketchup::Page] page. Documented example: page = Sketchup.active_model.pages.add('Hello World'); Sketchup.active_model.pages.erase(page). | Takes the Page OBJECT, not an index or name. Invalidates the handle — any cached reference becomes deleted? == true. When replacing a whole scene set on import, iterate over model.pages.to_a (a snapshot) and wrap the lot in one start_operation/commit_operation so it is a single undo step. |
| `length` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2014 | direct | The #length method is an alias for #size. Documented example: number = pages.length. @see #size | Exact alias of #size. Added to the docs in SketchUp 2014 (the underlying behaviour predates it). |
| `parent` | instance_method | Sketchup::Model | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The parent method is used to determine the model for the Pages collection. @return model - the model that contains the pages if successful. | Runtime only. Use model.pages.parent (or page.model) rather than Sketchup.active_model whenever you are writing into a second, non-active model. |
| `remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean (true if successful, false if unsuccessful) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The remove_observer method is used to remove an observer from the current object. See the PagesObserver interface for more details. Documented example: status = pages.remove_observer observer. | Pass the same observer instance that was added. Unlike remove_frame_change_observer, this takes the object, not an id. |
| `reorder` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | The #reorder method is used to reorder an existing Sketchup::Page object inside collection. new_index specifies the new position of the page. It should be a value between 0 and the max index of the Sketchup::Pages collection. Negative indices can be used to specify an index from the end of the list. @param [Sketchup::Page] page — The page to be reordered. @param [Integer] new_index — Index of where to replace the page. @raise [IndexError] if the given new_index is out of range. Documented example: pages.reorder(model.pages[0], 2). | SU2025.0+ ONLY — guard with model.pages.respond_to?(:reorder). RAISES IndexError (does not return false) when new_index is out of range, so rescue it. Negative indices ARE supported here (unlike Pages#[]). This is the clean way to restore original scene ORDER on import; on SU2024 and older your only option is to add the pages in the correct sequence, or pass the third `index` argument to Pages#add. |
| `selected_page` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The selected_page method is used to retrieve the currently selected page. @return page - the currently selected Page object if successful. | Returns nil when no scene tab is active (which is the normal state in a model with no scenes, and also right after adding scenes programmatically). Record the source model's selected scene by NAME if you want to restore it after import. Always nil-guard. |
| `selected_page=` | instance_method | Boolean (true if successful) | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The selected_page method is used to set the currently selected page. Once you set this, SketchUp will animate to that page as if the user had clicked on its scene tab. @param [Sketchup::Page] page. Documented example: pages.selected_page = model.pages[1]. | SIDE EFFECT: SketchUp ANIMATES to the page, exactly as if the user clicked the tab — the camera move is asynchronous and takes transition_time seconds. This is the main cause of flicker and of Page#update baking the wrong camera during bulk work. Standard mitigation (forums.sketchup.com/t/how-to-set-all-pages-on-scenes-to-the-same-view/12203): save and clear the transition first — old = model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition']; model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition'] = false; ...work...; model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition'] = old. Note you do NOT need to select a page in order to call Page#update or Page#set_visibility on it. |
| `show_frame_at` | instance_method | Array(Sketchup::Page, Float) or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #show_frame_at method is used to show a frame in animation (of the slide show) at a given time in seconds. @param [Float] seconds — The time in seconds. @return Upon success it returns the page that is displayed and a parameter between 0.0 and 1.0 that tells how far along the transition to next page you are at. @note In versions prior to SketchUp 2019 this method will crash if called when there are no pages in the model. Documented example: page, ratio = pages.show_frame_at(1.8). | Returns a two-element Array that destructures as `page, ratio = pages.show_frame_at(t)`; ratio is a Float 0.0..1.0. HARD CRASH (not an exception) on SU2018 and older when the model has zero pages — always check pages.size > 0 first if you support legacy versions. Purely a viewport/animation utility; nothing to serialise. |
| `size` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #size method is used to retrieve the number of pages. Documented example: pages.add('Page 1'); pages.add('Page 2'); number = pages.size. @see #length | Integer → JSON number. This is also the default value of the third argument of Pages#add (index = self.size, i.e. append at the end). |
| `slideshow_time` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The slideshow_time method is used to get the amount of time that a slideshow of all of the pages will take. This takes into account the transition time for each Page and the amount of time that each Page is displayed. Documented example: time = pages.slideshow_time. | NOTE THE DOC BUG: the @return tag in the source reads 'status - true if successful', which is wrong — the method returns a Float number of seconds (the rendered docs correctly type it Float). Derived value; do NOT serialise it, it recomputes from the per-page delay_time/transition_time you restore. |
| `unique_name` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2026.0 | READ-ONLY | The #unique_name method is used to generate a unique name for the page based on a base_name string. For example, a base name of 'Joe' might return 'Joe 2' if 'Joe' already exists. @param [String] base_name — The base name to use for the unique name. @return [String] The unique name. Documented example: page = pages.add('Joe'); page.unique_name('Joe'). | SU2026.0+ ONLY — guard with model.pages.respond_to?(:unique_name). NOTE the doc example is itself sloppy: it calls page.unique_name (on the Page) when the method is defined on Sketchup::Pages — call it as model.pages.unique_name('Joe'). Use it to pre-resolve name collisions when importing a scene set into a model that already has scenes, so your JSON→page name map is known before you call add. |
| `PAGE_USE_CAMERA` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Camera Location'. Controls the page's stored camera (eye/target/up/fov/projection). Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_CAMERA. | VALUE: 1 (0x0001). Top-level constant (no Sketchup:: prefix). Pairs with use_camera?/use_camera=. Trimble explicitly recommends using the constant NAME rather than the literal, since values are not contractual across versions — store the flag set in JSON as an array of NAME strings (e.g. ["PAGE_USE_CAMERA","PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY"]) and rebuild the mask with Object.const_get on import; that also makes your JSON readable and future-proof. |
| `PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Drawing Style'. Controls the page's stored RenderingOptions. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS. | VALUE: 2 (0x0002). Pairs with use_rendering_options?/use_rendering_options=. |
| `PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Shadow Setting'. Controls the page's stored ShadowInfo. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_SHADOWINFO. | VALUE: 4 (0x0004). SPELLING TRAP: SHADOWINFO is one word with no underscore, even though the method is use_shadow_info?. Pairs with use_shadow_info?/use_shadow_info=. |
| `PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Axes Location' (SketchCS = SketchUp Coordinate System). Controls the page's stored drawing axes. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_AXES. | VALUE: 8 (0x0008). SPELLING TRAP: there is NO 'PAGE_USE_AXES' constant in Ruby — the axes bit is PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS, while the matching methods are use_axes?/use_axes= and the C API calls it FLAG_USE_AXES. Three different names for the same slot. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Hidden Geometry & Objects (Up 2019 and older)'. The legacy combined hidden flag. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_HIDDEN, which is marked deprecated since SketchUp 2020.1. | VALUE: 16 (0x0010). DEPRECATED in favour of PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY | PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS since SU2020.1, but still defined and still inside PAGE_USE_ALL. For a 2026-targeted plugin write the two modern bits; keep this one only to interpret data produced by SU2019 and older. |
| `PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Visible Layers' (Visible Tags in modern UI). Controls the page's stored per-tag and per-tag-folder visibility. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY. | VALUE: 32 (0x0020). NAMING TRAP: the constant says LAYER_VISIBILITY but the methods say use_hidden_layers?/use_hidden_layers=. Same slot. |
| `PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Active Section Planes'. Controls which section plane(s) the page activates. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_SECTION_PLANES. | VALUE: 64 (0x0040). Pairs with use_section_planes?/use_section_planes= and (SU2026) active_section_planes. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2020.1 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Hidden Geometry (SU 2020 and later)'. Controls per-scene hidden state of loose root-level geometry. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY. | VALUE: 128 (0x0080), confirmed from the SketchUp C SDK header scene.h (#define FLAG_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY 0x0080). Defined in Ruby from SU2020.1 onwards — referencing the constant name on an older SketchUp raises NameError, so guard with defined?(PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY). Pairs with use_hidden_geometry?/use_hidden_geometry=. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2020.1 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Hidden Objects (SU 2020 and later)'. Controls per-scene hidden state of component instances, groups and images. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS. | VALUE: 256 (0x0100), confirmed from the SketchUp C SDK header scene.h (#define FLAG_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS 0x0100). Defined in Ruby from SU2020.1 onwards — guard with defined?(PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS). Pairs with use_hidden_objects?/use_hidden_objects=. |
| `PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'Environment settings'. Controls the page's stored Sketchup::Environment. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_ENVIRONMENT. | VALUE: NOT PUBLISHED. It is the next free bit after FLAG_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS (0x0100), so it is almost certainly 0x0200 (512), but the only public scene.h copy I could reach predates it and neither the Ruby docs nor the C API doc page print values — treat the integer as UNCONFIRMED and use the constant NAME. It is inside PAGE_USE_ALL (0x0fff) either way. Defined in Ruby only from SU2025.0 — guard with defined?(PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT). Pairs with use_environment?/use_environment=. |
| `PAGE_USE_ALL` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — 'All possible scene properties'. The default value of the flags argument to Sketchup::Pages#add. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_USE_ALL. | VALUE: 4095 (0x0fff) — all twelve low bits set, so it covers current and future property bits including PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT. This is the DEFAULT for Pages#add(name, flags = PAGE_USE_ALL, index = self.size), which is why a naive pages.add('X') snapshots the entire current model/view state. Also the safe argument for a full rebake: page.update(PAGE_USE_ALL). |
| `PAGE_NO_CAMERA` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Scene update flag — every scene property EXCEPT the camera. Corresponds to the C API's FLAG_NO_CAMERA. | VALUE: 4094 (0x0ffe) — i.e. PAGE_USE_ALL with bit 0 (PAGE_USE_CAMERA) cleared. Undocumented on the Page.html flag list but present in the top-level namespace and in the stubs. THE flag to use when you want a property-only scene, or when re-baking every scene without disturbing saved viewpoints: page.update(PAGE_NO_CAMERA). Equivalent to writing PAGE_USE_ALL & ~PAGE_USE_CAMERA. |

### Sketchup::Camera and Sketchup::View  —  51 entries

| Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `new` | class_method | Sketchup::Camera | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Returns a new camera with eye (where the camera is) and target (where the camera is looking). Two documented overloads: zero-arg (an arbitrary default camera) and the 3-to-5 arg form. Docs example: `eye = Geom::Point3d.new(20, 5, 30); target = Geom::Point3d.new(20, 60, 25); up = Z_AXIS; camera = Sketchup::Camera.new(eye, target, up)`. Note the documented default fov here is 30.0, which is NOT SketchUp's interactive default (35.0). | This is your reconstruction entry point. Accepts Geom::Point3d or plain 3-element Arrays for eye/target/up (the docs' own class-level example passes bare arrays: `Sketchup::Camera.new [1000,1000,1000], [0,0,0], [0,0,1]`). DO NOT rely on the 4th/5th args to set projection mode and fov during a restore — pass only eye/target/up and then assign perspective=/fov=/height= explicitly in the documented order, because the fov argument is meaningless when perspective is false and you still have to set height afterwards anyway. From JSON: `Sketchup::Camera.new(Geom::Point3d.new(*h['eye']), Geom::Point3d.new(*h['target']), Geom::Vector3d.new(*h['up']))`. |
| `aspect_ratio` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #aspect_ratio method is used to retrieve the aspect ratio of the Camera. `0.0` is the default value and denotes an aspect ratio matching that of the View. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup.active_model.active_view.camera; aspect_ratio = camera.aspect_ratio`. | Store as a JSON number under key "aspect_ratio". 0.0 is the portable, viewport-matching value and is what you get for a normal scene. A non-zero value is an EXPLICIT locked ratio (SketchUp draws grey letterbox bars) and reproduces identically in any model regardless of viewport size — so a non-zero ratio is actually the MORE deterministic thing to transfer. Critically, this value governs how `fov` is interpreted (see fov_is_height?), so it must be restored BEFORE fov. |
| `aspect_ratio=` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #aspect_ratio= method is used to set the aspect ratio for a Camera. Changing this value will cause SketchUp to show gray bars over the screen to show the resulting view. If you set the value to `0.0`, then the aspect ratio of the Camera will match the aspect ratio of its View. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; camera.aspect_ratio = 1.85`. | ORDER-CRITICAL. Assign this BEFORE `fov=`. Toggling between 0.0 and a non-zero ratio flips `fov_is_height?`, which reinterprets the stored fov number from a vertical angle to a horizontal one (or vice versa) without changing the number — the view visibly changes. Eneroth3's view-lib proves this: its View.set_aspect_ratio caches `full_fov_v`, assigns aspect_ratio, then re-applies the cached fov. Restore as `cam.aspect_ratio = h['aspect_ratio'].to_f`. |
| `center_2d` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d | SketchUp 2015 | READ-ONLY | The #center_2d method returns a point with the x and y offset of the camera when it's in two-point perspective or match photo mode. When the camera is in two-point perspective and the user pans around, the x and y values will change. These values are in normalized device coordinates, so for instance, the range [-1.0, 1.0] spans the full width or height of the screen. The z value is unused and it is always zero. Docs example: `Sketchup.active_model.active_view.camera.center_2d`. | READ-ONLY — there is NO `center_2d=`. Serialise defensively as `cam.center_2d.to_a` -> [Float, Float, 0.0] under key "center_2d" so the data is not lost, but understand it CANNOT be pushed back into a rebuilt camera. This is api-issue-tracker issue #88, open since April 2018. For a non-2D camera this returns [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]; only meaningful when is_2d? is true. |
| `description` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #description method is used to retrieve the description for a Camera. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; description = camera.description`. | Store as a JSON string under key "description". This is the CAMERA's description (e.g. Advanced Camera Tools writes a lens/camera-model name here such as "35 mm Camera") and is entirely separate from Sketchup::Page#description, which is the scene description shown in the Scenes manager. Capture both if you are serialising scenes. May be "" or, on some builds, nil — guard with `.to_s`. |
| `description=` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #description= method is used to set the description for the Camera. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; description = camera.description = "35 mm Camera"`. | Order-independent — has no effect on projection geometry. Restore with `cam.description = h['description'].to_s`. Safe to assign at any point in the rebuild. |
| `direction` | instance_method | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #direction method is used to retrieve a Vector3d object in the direction that the Camera is pointing. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; direction = camera.direction`. | DERIVED, read-only — no `direction=`. It is the normalised vector from eye toward target, and is identical to #zaxis. DO NOT serialise it as primary state: it is fully implied by eye and target. Store it at most as a diagnostic/checksum field. Reconstruct implicitly by passing eye and target to Camera#set. |
| `eye` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The #eye method is used to retrieve the eye Point3d object for the Camera. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; eye = camera.eye`. | MANDATORY state. Encode as `cam.eye.to_a` -> [Float, Float, Float] under key "eye". The values are in SketchUp INTERNAL UNITS (inches) as raw Floats — do NOT round-trip them through Length#to_s or the model's display units, or you will lose precision and inject unit strings. Decode with `Geom::Point3d.new(*h['eye'])`. Coordinates are absolute global model coordinates and transfer directly to another model (they are unaffected by Sketchup::Model#axes, which is only a drawing-axes display setting). |
| `focal_length` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The focal_length method is used to get the focal length in millimeters of perspective Camera. This value is computed on the fly based on the camera #fov value and the camera #image_width value. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; focal_length = camera.focal_length` — note the example itself passes no argument, contradicting the published `focal_length(length)` signature. The docs also give the 35 mm-equivalent formula: `focal_length_35_eqv = 36.0 / (2 * Math.tan(camera.fov.degrees / 2))`. | DO NOT SERIALISE THIS AS STATE. It is a pure derived read of (fov, image_width) — it holds no information that fov + image_width do not already hold, and storing it invites a restore that fights the fov value. Both ruby.sketchup.com and the official ruby-api-stubs source declare `def focal_length(length)`, which is a long-standing documentation/stub bug; the working call is the zero-arg `camera.focal_length`. Only applicable to perspective cameras. If your JSON must be human-inspectable, write it to a separate "_derived" sub-hash that your importer never reads back. |
| `focal_length=` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The #focal_length= method allows you to set the field of view by specifying a focal length in millimeters. Focal length must be between 1 and 3000, inclusive. The camera #fov value is set based on the provided focal_length and the current #image_width for the camera. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup.active_model.active_view.camera; camera.focal_length = 120`. | This is a WRITE-THROUGH ALIAS for fov= — it does not store anything of its own, it computes and assigns fov using the CURRENT image_width. Two consequences for a rebuild: (1) if you ever use it, `image_width=` MUST be assigned first or you will get the wrong fov; (2) prefer restoring `fov=` directly and never calling focal_length= at all, which removes the ordering hazard entirely. Documented valid range 1..3000 mm. |
| `fov` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #fov method retrieves the field of view of the Camera. This is only applicable to perspective cameras. Returns: (Float) — Field of view in degrees. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; fov = camera.fov`. | Store as a JSON number (DEGREES, not radians) under key "fov", but ONLY trust it when perspective? is true. The angle is vertical or horizontal depending on fov_is_height? — capture that flag alongside it. FAILURE MODE ON A PARALLEL CAMERA: the Ruby API does NOT raise. The official ruby-api-stubs source for Sketchup::Camera contains ZERO @raise tags for the entire class, and there is no exception note anywhere on the docs page. SketchUp's Camera keeps the perspective and ortho frusta side by side internally, so reading `fov` on a parallel camera returns a stale/default perspective angle (commonly 35.0) rather than erroring. The corresponding C API call SUCameraGetPerspectiveFrustumFOV DOES report SU_ERROR_NO_DATA for a non-perspective camera, which tells you the value is formally undefined — the Ruby layer just swallows that and hands you a plausible-looking number. This is the dangerous case: the failure is SILENT and yields wrong output, not an exception. Always gate on `perspective?` before reading. |
| `fov=` | instance_method | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #fov= method sets the field of view for a Camera. Field of view must be between 1 and 120 degrees, inclusive. This is only valid on a perspective camera. Parameter fov is (Numeric) — Field of view in degrees. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; fov = camera.fov = 56.78`. | ORDER-CRITICAL, TWO WAYS. (1) Assign only AFTER `perspective = true` — it is documented as 'only valid on a perspective camera'. (2) Assign only AFTER `aspect_ratio=`, because aspect_ratio drives fov_is_height? and therefore whether your number is read as the vertical or horizontal angle. Documented valid range 1..120 inclusive; the docs carry no @raise tag, so out-of-range behaviour (clamp vs ArgumentError) is unspecified — clamp defensively yourself: `cam.fov = h['fov'].to_f.clamp(1.0, 120.0)`. Note that setting fov to 0 does NOT switch a camera to parallel projection (a documented SketchUCation dead end); use `perspective = false`. |
| `fov_is_height?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2015 | READ-ONLY | The #fov_is_height? method indicates whether the field of view is measured vertically, as opposed horizontally. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup.active_model.active_view.camera; if camera.fov_is_height?; fov_vertical = camera.fov; # Compute the horizontal FOV.; else; fov_horizontal = camera.fov; # Compute the vertical FOV.; end`. | READ-ONLY DERIVED FLAG — there is NO `fov_is_height=`. You cannot set it directly; it follows from aspect_ratio. Observed and officially-acknowledged rule (SketchUp SDK forum thread 'Geting wrong fov for cameras', where the team conceded the SDK doc claiming FOV is always vertical is wrong): aspect_ratio == 0.0 (match viewport) => fov_is_height? is TRUE, fov is the VERTICAL angle; a non-zero explicit aspect_ratio => fov_is_height? is FALSE, fov is the HORIZONTAL angle. Serialise it under key "fov_is_height" as an integrity check: on restore, after setting aspect_ratio and fov, assert `cam.fov_is_height? == h['fov_is_height']`. If it mismatches, convert the angle rather than shipping a wrong view, using the view-lib formula `Math.atan(Math.tan(angle.degrees / 2) * ratio).radians * 2`. Restoring aspect_ratio faithfully makes the flag match automatically — this is the whole reason aspect_ratio must precede fov. |
| `height` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #height method retrieves the height of a Camera. This is only valid if it is not a perspective camera. Returns: (Float) — height in inches. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; camera.perspective = false; height = camera.height`. | This IS the zoom level of a parallel-projection camera and is the single most commonly-dropped field in scene serialisers. Store as a JSON number under key "height" — the units are INCHES (SketchUp internal units), a raw Float, NOT model display units. It is the model-space length of a line parallel to the screen and parallel to the screen's vertical axis; `camera.height = camera.height * 2` zooms OUT by 2x. Moving eye/target further away does NOT zoom an ortho camera — height is the only zoom control. Per Eneroth3's view-lib, height is measured against the VIEWPORT ratio (grey bars included), so the visible width is `height * (view.vpwidth / view.vpheight.to_f)`, not `height * camera.aspect_ratio`. FAILURE MODE ON A PERSPECTIVE CAMERA: same as fov in reverse — NO exception is raised (no @raise tags in the official stub, no exception note in the docs). You get the stale/default ortho frustum height. The C API counterpart SUCameraGetOrthographicFrustumHeight reports SU_ERROR_NO_DATA for a non-orthographic camera; Ruby silently returns a number instead. Gate on `!perspective?` before reading. |
| `height=` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #height= method is used to set the height for the Camera in inches. This is only valid if it is not a perspective camera. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; camera.perspective = false; camera.height = 20`. | ORDER-CRITICAL: the docs' own example sets `camera.perspective = false` on the line BEFORE `camera.height = 20`. Assigning height to a still-perspective camera is 'not valid' and will not survive the later flip to parallel. Restore as `cam.height = h['height'].to_f` (inches) and only in the `!perspective` branch. There is no width= — width is implied by height and the viewport ratio. |
| `image_width` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #image_width method returns the width of the image, as used to calculate the #focal_length. This value has no effect on how the view is displayed in SketchUp. The default value, 0, is interpreted by SketchUp as 36 mm (the width of traditional 35 mm film). Returns: (Float) — Width in mm. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; width = camera.image_width`. | Store as a JSON number (MILLIMETRES) under key "image_width". Note the sentinel: 0 means 'use 36 mm', so 0 and 36.0 are visually identical but are DIFFERENT stored values — preserve the raw value rather than normalising it, or a round-trip will not be byte-identical. Since it has no effect on display, it is order-independent with respect to fov — but it MUST precede any `focal_length=` call. |
| `image_width=` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #image_width= method is used to set the width of the image, as used to calculate the #focal_length. This value has no effect on how the view is displayed in SketchUp. The default value, 0, is interpreted by SketchUp as 36 mm (the width of traditional 35 mm film). Parameter image_width is (Float) — Width in mm. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; camera.image_width = 1.0; focal_length = camera.focal_length`. | Assign BEFORE `focal_length=` (which reads it) and it is otherwise order-free relative to fov/height because it does not affect the displayed view. Restore as `cam.image_width = h['image_width'].to_f`. Safe slot in the recipe: immediately after aspect_ratio=, before the fov/height branch. |
| `is_2d?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2015 | READ-ONLY | The #is_2d? method indicates whether the camera mode is two-point perspective or match photo mode, as opposed to a normal perspective or parallel projection camera. Docs example: `Sketchup.active_model.active_view.camera.is_2d?`. | READ-ONLY — there is NO `is_2d=`. THIS IS A HARD BLOCKER for faithful cross-model scene transfer: a two-point-perspective or match-photo camera cannot be reconstructed from Ruby at all. Serialise it under key "is_2d" and, when it is true, have your exporter emit an explicit WARNING and your importer refuse to claim fidelity — the rebuilt camera will come back as an ordinary perspective camera with the same eye/target/up/fov but without the 2D lens-shift, so verticals will converge where the original kept them parallel. Open enhancement request: SketchUp/api-issue-tracker issue #88 (Eneroth3, 24 Apr 2018), still open; re-confirmed unavailable on the forum in June 2023. Match-photo pages are a partial exception — see Sketchup::Pages#add_matchphoto_page, which accepts a camera argument. |
| `perspective?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #perspective? method is used to determine whether a camera is a perspective or orthographic camera. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; status = camera.perspective?; if (status); puts "Perspective"; else; puts "Orthographic"; end`. | MANDATORY state. Store as a JSON boolean under key "perspective" (note: the getter is `perspective?` with a question mark, the setter is `perspective=` without — a classic source of typo bugs). This flag is the switch that decides whether `fov` or `height` is the meaningful field, so your exporter must read it FIRST and branch on it, and your importer must write it BEFORE fov/height. |
| `perspective=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The #perspective= method is used to set whether or not this is a perspective camera or an orthographic camera. Parameter perspective is (Boolean). Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; camera.perspective = false`. | ORDER-CRITICAL AND FIRST OF THE MODE GROUP. Assign this before fov=, before height=, and before focal_length=. Setting `fov = 0` is NOT a way to get parallel projection (a documented SketchUCation dead end) — only `perspective = false` does it. Gotcha reported on SketchUCation: mutating a camera object you are holding does not repaint the viewport by itself; on a detached camera you must still assign `view.camera = cam` afterwards (on a live `view.camera` reference it takes effect, with a `view.invalidate` to force the redraw). Restore as `cam.perspective = !!h['perspective']`. |
| `scale_2d` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 2015 | READ-ONLY | The #scale_2d method returns a float indicating the scaling factor of 2 point perspective cameras. Docs example: `Sketchup.active_model.active_view.camera.scale_2d`. | READ-ONLY — there is NO `scale_2d=`. Same blocker as center_2d and is_2d?. Capture it under key "scale_2d" so no information is destroyed and a future SketchUp release (or a manual fix-up) can use it, but the importer cannot apply it. Only meaningful when is_2d? is true; returns 1.0 for ordinary cameras. Part of api-issue-tracker issue #88. |
| `set` | instance_method | Sketchup::Camera | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The #set method sets the camera orientation. You have to set the camera eye, target and up parameters at the same time to make sure that you have a valid camera definition. Parameters: eye (Geom::Point3d), target (Geom::Point3d), up (Geom::Vector3d). Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; eye = Geom::Point3d.new(20, 5, 30); target = Geom::Point3d.new(20, 60, 25); up = Z_AXIS; camera.set(eye, target, up)`. | THE ONLY WAY to change position/orientation on an existing camera — there is no eye= and no up=. The docs are explicit that all three must go in together to guarantee a valid definition. Use it as the LAST geometric step of a rebuild (after perspective=/aspect_ratio=/fov=/height=) to re-assert eye and target, because flipping projection mode can nudge the internal eye distance. It returns the camera, so it chains. Guard against a degenerate camera: if `up` is parallel to `(target - eye)` the definition is invalid — check `(target - eye).parallel?(up)` and substitute Z_AXIS (or Y_AXIS for a plan view) if so. |
| `target` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The #target method retrieves Point3d that the camera is pointing at. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; target = camera.target`. | MANDATORY state. Encode as `cam.target.to_a` -> [Float, Float, Float] under key "target", raw INCHES. Decode with `Geom::Point3d.new(*h['target'])`. Together with eye it defines direction and the eye-target distance. For a PERSPECTIVE camera the eye-target distance affects nothing optically (fov is the zoom) but it does set the orbit pivot the user will feel, so preserve it. For a PARALLEL camera the distance affects nothing at all optically — height is the zoom — but a target that ends up behind geometry in the destination model will cause clipping, so keep it. Never recompute target from direction alone: you would lose the distance. |
| `up` | instance_method | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The #up method is used to retrieve the up vector for the camera. This is the direction that the top of the camera is facing. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; up = camera.up`. | MANDATORY state. Encode as `cam.up.to_a` -> [Float, Float, Float] under key "up". This is a unitless direction vector, normally already unit length. Decode with `Geom::Vector3d.new(*h['up'])`. Do not substitute a hard-coded Z_AXIS on restore — that silently destroys any rolled or plan-view camera. NOTE the constructor's parameter table documents `up` as (Geom::Point3d) while Camera#set documents it as (Geom::Vector3d); this is a docs inconsistency, and in practice a Vector3d or a 3-element Array works in both. |
| `xaxis` | instance_method | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #xaxis method is used to retrieve the x axis of the camera coordinate system. This value is computed from the cross product between the camera direction and the up vector. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; xaxis = camera.xaxis`. | DERIVED, read-only — no setter. Fully implied by eye/target/up. DO NOT serialise as state; at most keep it in a diagnostic sub-hash. Useful at runtime for building a screen-aligned transformation, not for persistence. |
| `yaxis` | instance_method | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #yaxis method retrieves the y axis of the camera coordinate system. This value is computed to be perpendicular the camera x and z axes. It is equivalent to the up direction, but is computed to make sure that it is perpendicular to the direction. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; # 0.0, 1.0, 0.0; yaxis = camera.yaxis`. | DERIVED, read-only — no setter. This is the ORTHOGONALISED up vector: it equals #up only when the supplied up was already perpendicular to direction. Handy trick for a robust exporter — serialise `cam.yaxis.to_a` INSTEAD of `cam.up.to_a` and your restored camera is guaranteed never to be degenerate, since yaxis is by construction perpendicular to the view direction. This is the single best defensive choice for cross-model transfer. |
| `zaxis` | instance_method | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | The #zaxis method retrieves the z axis of the camera coordinate system. This value is computed. It is the same as Camera.direction. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new; # 0.0, 1.0, 0.0; vector = camera.zaxis`. | DERIVED, read-only — no setter, and explicitly identical to #direction. Do not serialise as state. Note the docs' example comment '# 0.0, 1.0, 0.0' is copy-pasted from yaxis and is wrong for zaxis on a default camera — ignore it. |
| `eye=` | absent_method | N/A |  | no | There is NO `eye=` setter. Verified against both ruby.sketchup.com/Sketchup/Camera.html (Instance Method Summary lists no eye=) and the official SketchUp/ruby-api-stubs Camera.rb source (no `def eye=`). Calling it raises NoMethodError. | Use `Camera#set(eye, target, up)` instead — the docs state you must set all three at once to guarantee a valid camera. To move only the eye: `cam.set(new_eye, cam.target, cam.up)`. Listing this explicitly because assuming an eye= setter exists is the most common cause of a silently-failing camera restore (NoMethodError swallowed inside a rescue, leaving the default camera in place). |
| `up=` | absent_method | N/A |  | no | There is NO `up=` setter. Verified against both ruby.sketchup.com/Sketchup/Camera.html and the official SketchUp/ruby-api-stubs Camera.rb source. Calling it raises NoMethodError. | Use `cam.set(cam.eye, cam.target, new_up)`. Same failure-mode warning as eye=. |
| `clone / dup` | absent_method | N/A |  | no | Reported on the SketchUp Community thread 'How can I save and restore the camera?' as 'a very old known issue' — camera objects cannot be cloned using standard Ruby clone() or dup(). You must manually reconstruct via the constructor from saved property values. | This is precisely why a JSON serialiser is the right approach here rather than caching Camera objects. Always rebuild with `Sketchup::Camera.new(eye, target, up)` plus explicit property assignment. |
| `Sketchup::Model#active_view` | instance_method | Sketchup::View | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The #active_view method returns the active View object for this model. In a multi-model session (SketchUp on macOS can have several open models) always go through the SPECIFIC model's active_view, not a cached one — this matters directly for your use case, since you are writing scenes into a DIFFERENT model than the one you read them from. `Sketchup.active_model` follows the focused window and can change between the export and import halves of your workflow. |
| `Sketchup::View#camera` | instance_method | Sketchup::Camera | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The camera method is used to retrieve the camera for the view. Returns: (Sketchup::Camera) — a Camera object. Docs example: `camera = view.camera`. | Returns a LIVE reference to the view's camera — mutating it (e.g. `view.camera.perspective = false`) changes the view, as confirmed by thomthom on SketchUCation. Follow a mutation with `view.invalidate` to force the repaint. For EXPORT, read the properties off it immediately into a Hash; do not hold the object. |
| `Sketchup::View#camera=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Camera or Array(Sketchup::Camera, Float) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The #camera= method is used to set the camera for the view. If a transition time is given, then it will animate the transition from the current camera to the new one. Overload 1 parameter: camera (Sketchup::Camera) — The new camera object. Overload 2 parameter: camera_and_transition (Array(Sketchup::Camera, Float)) — The second item in the array represents the transition time from the existing camera to the new one. Docs example: `camera = Sketchup::Camera.new([5, 5, 9], [5, 10, 0], Z_AXIS); view = Sketchup.active_model.active_view; view.camera = camera`. | ANIMATION ARGUMENT — exact syntax matters. `view.camera = [cam, 2.0]` WORKS (the assignment operator receives a single Array argument). `view.camera = cam, 2.0` also parses as an Array and works. `view.send(:camera=, cam, 2.0)` FAILS with `ArgumentError: wrong number of arguments (2 for 1)` — verified on the SketchUp Community thread 'Setter #view.camera= Testing with Second Transition Time Argument'. CRITICAL FOR BATCH RESTORE: the transition is ASYNCHRONOUS and does NOT block; a loop assigning cameras with transition times completes in milliseconds while the animation is still running, so every intermediate assignment is discarded. Use the plain single-argument form (no transition) for programmatic scene reconstruction, and reserve the Array form for user-facing single jumps. If you genuinely need sequenced animation, drive it from UI.start_timer or a Sketchup::Pages FrameChangeObserver. Always assign the camera AFTER it is fully configured. |
| `Sketchup::View#field_of_view` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The field_of_view method is used get the view's field of view setting, in degrees. Docs example: `fov = Sketchup.active_model.active_view.field_of_view`. | A convenience shortcut to the active camera's fov. Do NOT use this as your capture path — it bypasses the camera object and gives you no way to know whether the value is vertical or horizontal (no fov_is_height? at this level) or whether the camera is even perspective. Read `view.camera.fov` with a `perspective?` guard instead. |
| `Sketchup::View#field_of_view=` | instance_method | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The field_of_view= method is used set the view's field of view setting, in degrees. Docs example: `my_view.field_of_view = 45`. | Shortcut for `view.camera.fov =`. Avoid on the restore path for the same reason — it gives you no control over assignment order relative to perspective= and aspect_ratio=. |
| `Sketchup::View#vpwidth` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Retrieves the width of the viewport in pixels. | Needed to reason about aspect ratio: the implicit viewport ratio is `view.vpwidth / view.vpheight.to_f` (Eneroth3 view-lib's vp_aspect_ratio).
