| `Sketchup::View#vpheight` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Retrieves the height of the viewport in pixels. | See vpwidth. Note the `.to_f` — both are Integers, so integer division will silently give you 1 or 2 if you forget. |

| `Sketchup::View#refresh` | instance_method | Sketchup::View | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Forces an immediate view redraw. Docs warning: 'This method might impact performance and if used incorrectly cause instability or crashes. Don't use this unless you have verified that you cannot use #invalidate instead.' | Avoid in a scene-restore loop — the docs explicitly warn about instability and crashes. Use #invalidate. |
| `Sketchup::View#zoom_extents` | instance_method | Sketchup::View | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The zoom_extents method is used to zoom to the extents about the entire model, as if the user has selected the zoom extents command from the menu. Docs example: `new_view = view.zoom_extents`. | DESTROYS a restored camera — it overwrites eye, target and (for ortho) height. Never call it after applying a serialised camera. It is only useful as a fallback when the incoming JSON is invalid or the destination model's geometry is somewhere entirely different from the source model's. |
| `Sketchup::View#zoom` | instance_method | Float or Array<Sketchup::Entity> | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The zoom method is used to zoom in or out by some zoom factor. | `Sketchup::Page#camera` | instance_method | Sketchup::Camera | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The #camera method retrieves the camera for a particular page. Docs example: `camera = page.camera`. | Returns a LIVE reference to the page's stored camera — the official SU2026 Page class overview example mutates it directly (`page.camera.fov = 56.78`) inside a start_operation/commit_operation block to change the scene. THIS IS YOUR PRIMARY WRITE PATH for reconstructing a scene camera, because Page has no camera= setter. Apply the same assignment order as for a detached camera: perspective= -> aspect_ratio= -> image_width= -> fov=/height= -> set(eye, target, up). |
| `Sketchup::Page#camera=` | absent_method | N/A |  | no | The Page Instance Method Summary on ruby.sketchup.com lists `#camera` but no `#camera=`. You cannot swap a whole Camera object onto a Page. Calling page.camera = cam raises NoMethodError. | Three supported alternatives, in order of preference for programmatic scene reconstruction: (1) MUTATE `page.camera` in place inside model.start_operation/commit_operation — the official SU2026 pattern, deterministic, no viewport dependency, no animation race. (2) Set `view.camera = cam` first, then create the scene with `model.pages.add(name, PAGE_USE_CAMERA | ...)` — the page captures the current view. (3) Set `view.camera = cam` first, then call `page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA)` on an existing page. Option 3 is the one DanRathbun points at for persistence, but it captures whatever the view currently shows, so it is unsafe if a scene transition is mid-flight. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_camera?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_camera? method determines whether you are storing the camera property with the page. If this is false the scene ignores its stored camera entirely and clicking the tab will not move the view — a restored camera will appear to 'not work' with no error. Store under key "use_camera". |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_camera=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The use_camera= method sets the page's camera property. Docs example: `flags = PAGE_USE_CAMERA | PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO | PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY; status = page.update(flags)`. | PULL-model, not push: it copies FROM the current view INTO the page. So `view.camera = cam` must come first. DanRathbun's key point: in a model that has scene pages, setting view.camera alone does NOT persist across save/reopen — you must call `model.pages.selected_page.update(PAGE_USE_CAMERA)`. Danger for batch restores: `pages.selected_page = page` starts an ASYNCHRONOUS animated transition, so calling update() immediately after selecting a page can capture a mid-transition camera. | `PAGE_USE_CAMERA` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 (PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY / PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS: SketchUp 2020; PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT: later) | READ-ONLY | Documented on Sketchup::Page#update as '# Camera Location'. Note PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS is the AXES flag despite the unintuitive name — exact spelling matters. These are top-level constants (Object), not namespaced under Sketchup::Page. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#add` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The #add method is used to add a new Page object to the collection. If no name is given, then a new name is generated using the default name for new Pages. If a name is given, then a new Page with that name is added. If the name is already used by another page, a unique name is created. If the flags parameter is given, it controls which properties are saved with the Page. See the Sketchup::Page#update method for a description of the flags that can be set. If index is given, it specifies the position in the page list that the new page is added. Otherwise the new page is added to the end. Known Bugs: 'Prior to SketchUp 2026.0 this method didn't make the name unique.' | There is no camera parameter — the new page captures the CURRENT active view. So the pattern is `view.camera = cam; pages.add(name, PAGE_USE_ALL)`. The `index` parameter is how you restore scene ORDER when rebuilding a whole set of scenes into a new model. Version gotcha for a cross-version tool: on SU2025 and earlier, adding a page whose name already exists does NOT auto-uniquify — check `pages.unique_name(base_name)` yourself before adding if you must support older releases. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#add_matchphoto_page` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page | SketchUp 7.0 | no | The #add_matchphoto_page method is used to add a photomatch page to the model. 'This is an advanced feature that was added to support internal SketchUp work, so it is unlikely to be useful to you.' Parameters: image_filename (String) — The image file to use as the background; camera (Sketchup::Camera, defaults to nil); page_name (String, defaults to nil). Docs example: `page = pages.add_matchphoto_page("/path/to/image.png")`. | The ONLY API that accepts a Camera object when constructing a Page, and therefore the only partial route to a 2D/match-photo scene — but it requires an image file on disk and is officially discouraged. There is a long-standing forum complaint ('Why Pages.add_matchphoto_page ignores camera properties?') that the camera argument is not honoured reliably. Do not build your scene restore on this; treat is_2d? scenes as unsupported and warn the user. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#selected_page=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The selected_page method is used to set the currently selected page. | Assigning this triggers SketchUp's animated scene transition, which is ASYNCHRONOUS. Do not assume the view has arrived at the page's camera on the next line — that is the same non-blocking behaviour proven for View#camera= with a transition time. Avoid inside a restore loop. |
| `Sketchup::Model#start_operation / #commit_operation` | instance_method | String, Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 (Page camera undoability: SketchUp 2026.0) | no | Wraps model changes into a single undoable operation. Required since SketchUp 2026.0 for Page camera edits: 'Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying the Axes, Camera, RenderingOptions, and ShadowInfo properties of a page is an undoable operation and should be wrapped between Model#start_operation and Model#commit_operation.' | MANDATORY on SU2026 for your use case. Without it, restoring N scenes floods the undo stack with N x (number of camera properties) separate undo entries, which is both a terrible user experience and a performance problem. The official Page docs example is exactly this pattern. Use `model.start_operation('Restore Scenes', true)` (the true disables UI updates for speed) and always `commit_operation` — or `abort_operation` in a rescue. |

### Sketchup::RenderingOptions  —  164 entries

| Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `AmbientOcclusion` | rendering_option_key | Boolean (TrueClass/FalseClass) | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Master on/off for the Ambient Occlusion screen-space shading effect. Styles dialog > Edit > Background/AO settings. | Write straight to JSON as true/false. Rebuild: ro['AmbientOcclusion'] = json_value ? true : false. GUARD: key does not exist before SketchUp 2024.0 — test ro.keys.include?('AmbientOcclusion') before reading or writing, or []= raises ArgumentError on 2024+. |
| `AmbientOcclusionColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | Tint colour applied to the ambient occlusion contact shading. | Encode: c = ro['AmbientOcclusionColor']; [c.red, c.green, c.blue, c.alpha] (four Integers 0-255). Rebuild: ro['AmbientOcclusionColor'] = Sketchup::Color.new(r, g, b, a). GUARD: SketchUp 2026.0+ only. |
| `AmbientOcclusionColorEnabled` | rendering_option_key | Boolean (TrueClass/FalseClass) | SketchUp 2026.0 (C API 14.0) | direct | Whether the AmbientOcclusionColor tint is applied at all (vs. a neutral default). Documented in the SketchUp C API rendering-options key table for SketchUp 2026 / API 14.0. | Listed in the C API SURenderingOptionsRef key table but NOT in the Ruby doc comment. VERIFY AT RUNTIME with ro.keys.include?('AmbientOcclusionColorEnabled') before use. If present, plain boolean round-trip. Paired observer constant Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetAOColorEnabled exists in the 2026 Ruby stubs, which strongly implies the Ruby key is exposed too. |
| `AmbientOcclusionDistance` | rendering_option_key | Float (Double) | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Radius / falloff distance over which ambient occlusion darkening is sampled. | MUST be written back as a Float, not an Integer. | `AmbientOcclusionIntensity` | rendering_option_key | Float (Double) | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Strength of the ambient occlusion darkening. | Always coerce with .to_f on rebuild. GUARD: SketchUp 2024.0+ only. |
| `AmbientOcclusionMultiplier` | rendering_option_key | Float (Double) | SketchUp 2026.0 | direct | Scalar multiplier applied on top of the ambient occlusion intensity. | Always coerce with .to_f on rebuild. GUARD: SketchUp 2026.0+ only. |
| `BackgroundColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The viewport background colour. Styles > Edit > Background Settings > Background. | Encode as [c.red, c.green, c.blue, c.alpha]. Rebuild: ro['BackgroundColor'] = Sketchup::Color.new(r, g, b, a). Note the []= setter also accepts a String colour name ('white') or a hex String ('#ff0000'), but for round-tripping always use the RGBA Integer form — it is lossless. Interacts with SkyColor/GroundColor: if DrawGround or DrawHorizon is true the sky/ground gradient paints over this. |
| `BandColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of the dead 'letterbox' bands drawn outside the image area when the view has a fixed aspect ratio (e.g. camera.aspect_ratio set, or an export preview). | Encode/rebuild exactly like BackgroundColor. | `DisplayDims` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 7.0 | direct | Show or hide all Sketchup::DimensionLinear / DimensionRadial entities. | `DisplayFog` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Master on/off for the fog effect. Window > Fog, or Styles. | Plain boolean. | `DisplayText` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 7.0 | direct | Show or hide all Sketchup::Text entities (screen text and leader text). | Plain boolean. GUARD: SketchUp 7.0+. |
| `DisplayWatermarks` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Show or hide the style's watermark image layers. Styles > Edit > Watermark Settings. | Plain boolean. | `DrawGround` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Paint the ground plane below the horizon. Styles > Edit > Background Settings > Ground. | Plain boolean. Pairs with GroundColor, GroundTransparency, DrawUnderground. |

| `DrawHiddenGeometry` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.0 | direct | Show hidden EDGES and FACES (View > Hidden Geometry) as dashed/mesh ghosts. | EXACT KEY SPELLING IS 'DrawHiddenGeometry' — NOT 'ROPDrawHiddenGeometry'. | `DrawProfilesOnly` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Draw ONLY the profile (silhouette) edges and suppress all interior edges — the 'Profiles only' style look. | Plain boolean. | `DrawSilhouettes` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Enable thickened profile/silhouette edges. Styles > Edit > Edge Settings > Profiles checkbox. | Plain boolean. | `DrawUnderground` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether geometry below the ground plane shows through the painted ground (the 'Show ground from below' / transparency-from-underneath behaviour). | Plain boolean. Only meaningful when DrawGround is true. |
| `EdgeColorMode` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) — ENUM | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | How edges are coloured. Styles > Edit > Edge Settings > Color dropdown ('All same' / 'By material' / 'By axis'). | ENUM INTEGER. 0 = ObjectColor (By material — edge takes its material colour), 1 = ForegroundColor (All same — uses the ForegroundColor key), 2 = DirectionColor (By axis — red/green/blue by alignment). Source: SketchUp C API SURenderingOptionsRef. Rebuild with .to_i and CLAMP/VALIDATE to 0..2 before assigning, because SketchUp 2024+ raises ArgumentError on an out-of-range value. |
| `EdgeDisplayMode` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) — ENUM | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Which edges are drawn at all. | ENUM INTEGER. 0 = EdgeDisplayNone (no edges drawn), 1 = EdgeDisplayAll (all edges drawn), 2 = EdgeDisplayStandalone (only standalone/loose edges drawn). Source: SketchUp C API SURenderingOptionsRef. Rebuild with .to_i, validate 0..2. Restore this BEFORE DrawProfilesOnly/DrawSilhouettes so the final edge appearance settles correctly. |
| `EdgeType` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) — ENUM | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Standard vector edges vs. NPR 'sketchy' hand-drawn stroke edges. | ENUM INTEGER. 0 = Standard edges (EdgeStandard), 1 = Sketchy edges (EdgeNPR). This enum is documented explicitly in the official Ruby doc comment. Rebuild with .to_i, validate 0..1. MAJOR CROSS-MODEL CAVEAT: setting EdgeType = 1 selects sketchy rendering, but the actual sketchy stroke TEXTURE is a property of the Sketchup::Style, not of RenderingOptions. Reconstructing into a different model with only the integer will give you sketchy mode with the destination's default stroke set, not the source's. |
| `ExtendLines` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Enable the 'Extension' edge decoration where edges overshoot their endpoints. Styles > Edit > Edge Settings > Extension checkbox. | Plain boolean. Controls whether LineExtension has any effect. NOTE: the amount key is called 'LineExtension', NOT 'ExtendLinesAmount' — see gotchas. |
| `FaceBackColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Default back-face colour (the blue-grey). Styles > Edit > Face Settings > Back color. | Encode as [c.red, c.green, c.blue, c.alpha]; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). Does NOT fire onRenderingOptionsChanged — must be captured via ro.keys rather than by observer. |
| `FaceColorMode` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) — REMOVED | SketchUp 6.0 (removed SketchUp 2019.1) | no | Historic face-colouring mode selector. Officially documented as 'previously included but it was ineffective'. | REMOVED IN SKETCHUP 2019.1. On a SketchUp 2026 target this key DOES NOT EXIST: reading returns nil, and ro['FaceColorMode'] = x raises ArgumentError on 2024+. Do NOT include it in a serialiser key list. If your JSON was written by an older tool and contains it, skip it on restore. The observer constant Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetFaceColorMode still exists as a constant, which is misleading — the constant surviving does not mean the key survived. Use RenderMode + Texture + FaceFrontColor/FaceBackColor to express face appearance instead. |
| `FaceFrontColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Default front-face colour (the off-white). Styles > Edit > Face Settings > Front color. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). |
| `FogColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The fog colour. Window > Fog > Color. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). Only visible when FogUseBkColor is false — if FogUseBkColor is true, SketchUp uses BackgroundColor instead and this value is stored but unused. Serialise both. |
| `FogEndDist` | rendering_option_key | Float (Double) — LENGTH IN INCHES | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Distance from the camera at which fog reaches full opacity. Window > Fog > Distance, right-hand slider. | This is a MODEL-SPACE LENGTH in SketchUp's internal unit (inches), NOT a 0-1 ratio. Confirmed by real plugin code (Eneroth Fog Tool) which does ro['FogEndDist'].to_l to turn it into a Length. Because SketchUp's internal unit is always inches regardless of the document's display units, the raw Float transfers between models unchanged — write it to JSON as-is and restore with .to_f. Does NOT fire onRenderingOptionsChanged. Restore FogStartDist and FogEndDist TOGETHER; if you set End below the current Start, SketchUp may silently clamp. |
| `FogStartDist` | rendering_option_key | Float (Double) — LENGTH IN INCHES | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Distance from the camera at which fog begins. Window > Fog > Distance, left-hand slider. | Model-space length in inches, same as FogEndDist. Round-trip as a Float (.to_f on restore — an Integer will raise ArgumentError on SketchUp 2024+). Set FogStartDist before FogEndDist when the new range is larger, and after when it is smaller, to avoid clamping. |
| `FogUseBkColor` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | When true, fog is drawn in the BackgroundColor and the FogColor value is ignored. Window > Fog > 'Use background color' checkbox. | Plain boolean. Note the abbreviated spelling 'Bk', not 'Back' or 'Background'. Serialise alongside FogColor and BackgroundColor. |
| `ForegroundColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The single colour used for all edges when EdgeColorMode is 1 (All same). Styles > Edit > Edge Settings > Color. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). Serialise together with EdgeColorMode — the colour is meaningless unless EdgeColorMode == 1. |
| `GroundColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of the painted ground plane. Styles > Edit > Background Settings > Ground. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). Only visible when DrawGround is true. |
| `GroundTransparency` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) — PERCENT | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Transparency of the painted ground plane, as a percentage (UI slider 0-100). | INTEGER, NOT BOOLEAN and NOT a Float — the SketchUp C API declares it SUTypedValueType_Int32. This is a very common serialisation bug because the name reads like a float. Rebuild with .to_i and clamp to 0..100. |
| `HideConstructionGeometry` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Hide all construction geometry (guides). Equivalent to View > Guides off. | Plain boolean. INVERTED SENSE: true HIDES guides. Do not confuse with a 'show' flag when mapping to a UI checkbox. |
| `HighlightColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour used to draw selected entities (the selection highlight). Styles > Edit > Modeling Settings > Selected. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). |
| `HorizonColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of the horizon line drawn between sky and ground. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). Does NOT fire onRenderingOptionsChanged — capture via ro.keys. Only visible when DrawHorizon is true. |
| `InactiveFade` | rendering_option_key | Float (Double) — 0.0 to 1.0 | SketchUp 8.0 | direct | How much the rest of the model fades out while you are inside a group/component edit context. Styles > Edit > Modeling Settings > Rest of model 'Fade' slider. | FLOAT in the range 0.0-1.0. Must be restored as a Float (.to_f) or SketchUp 2024+ raises ArgumentError when JSON gives you an Integer 0 or 1. Does NOT fire onRenderingOptionsChanged. Pairs with InactiveHidden. |
| `InactiveHidden` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Completely hide the rest of the model while editing a group/component. Styles > Edit > Modeling Settings > Rest of model 'Hide' checkbox. | Plain boolean. Does NOT fire onRenderingOptionsChanged — capture via ro.keys. When true, InactiveFade is irrelevant. |
| `InstanceFade` | rendering_option_key | Float (Double) — 0.0 to 1.0 | SketchUp 8.0 | direct | How much OTHER instances of the component being edited are faded. Styles > Edit > Modeling Settings > Similar components 'Fade' slider. | FLOAT 0.0-1.0. Coerce with .to_f on restore. Does NOT fire onRenderingOptionsChanged. |
| `InstanceHidden` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Completely hide other instances of the component being edited. Styles > Edit > Modeling Settings > Similar components 'Hide' checkbox. | Plain boolean round-trip. |
| `JitterEdges` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Draw each edge with a slight random wobble/overdraw for a hand-sketched look. Styles > Edit > Edge Settings > Jitter. | Plain boolean round-trip. |
| `LineEndWidth` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Pixel width of the thickened endpoint decoration. Styles > Edit > Edge Settings > Endpoints value. | Integer pixels. Rebuild with .to_i. Only has effect when DrawLineEnds is true. |
| `LineExtension` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Pixel amount by which edges overshoot their endpoints. Styles > Edit > Edge Settings > Extension value. | THIS is the edge-extension amount key. There is NO key named 'ExtendLinesAmount' — see gotchas. Integer pixels; rebuild with .to_i. Practical clamp used by working plugins is 0..100. Only has effect when ExtendLines is true. |
| `LockedColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour used to draw locked groups and component instances (the red highlight). Styles > Edit > Modeling Settings > Locked. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). |
| `MaterialTransparency` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether material alpha is honoured when rendering faces. Styles > Edit > Face Settings > 'Enable transparency'. | Plain boolean. Distinct from ModelTransparency (the global X-ray toggle). Serialise both. |
| `ModelTransparency` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Global X-ray mode — renders the whole model semi-transparent. View > Face Style > X-ray. | Plain boolean. This is the X-ray toggle; it is a Boolean, NOT part of the RenderMode enum. There is a related observer constant ROPSetXRayOpacity but no corresponding public Ruby key for the X-ray opacity amount. |
| `RenderMode` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) — ENUM | SketchUp 6.0 (value 6 added SketchUp 2025.0) | direct | The Face Style. View > Face Style (Wireframe / Hidden Line / Shaded / Shaded With Textures / Monochrome). | ENUM INTEGER. 0 = RenderWireframe, 1 = RenderHidden (Hidden Line), 2 = RenderFlat (Shaded), 3 = RenderSmooth (Shaded With Textures), 4 = RenderTextureObsolete (DO NOT USE — legacy, may raise on 2024+), 5 = RenderNoMaterials (Monochrome), 6 = RenderPhysicallyBased (added SketchUp 2025.0 / C API 13.0). Source: SketchUp C API SURenderingOptionsRef. Rebuild with .to_i. VALIDATE on restore: reject 4, and reject 6 unless the destination is SketchUp 2025.0 or newer (fall back to 3). Interacts with the Texture boolean. |
| `SectionActiveColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of the ACTIVE section plane marker rectangle. Styles > Edit > Modeling Settings > Section Planes 'Active'. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). |
| `SectionCutDrawEdges` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2015 | direct | Draw the section-cut outline edges where a section plane slices geometry. | Plain boolean. GUARD: SketchUp 2015+. Does NOT fire onRenderingOptionsChanged — capture via ro.keys. Serialise together with SectionCutFilled, SectionCutWidth, SectionDefaultCutColor and SectionDefaultFillColor as one coherent group. |
| `SectionCutFilled` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2018 | direct | Fill the cut region of a section with a solid colour rather than leaving it hollow. | Plain boolean. GUARD: SketchUp 2018+. Controls whether SectionDefaultFillColor has any visible effect. |
| `SectionCutWidth` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Pixel line width of the section-cut outline edges. | Integer pixels; rebuild with .to_i. Note this is Int32 despite the 'Width' name suggesting a Length. |
| `SectionDefaultCutColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Default colour of the section-cut outline edges. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). 'Default' in the name refers to the model-wide default; individual SectionPlane entities can override it in later SketchUp versions. |
| `SectionDefaultFillColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 2018 | encode | Default fill colour for the cut region when SectionCutFilled is true. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). GUARD: SketchUp 2018+. |
| `SectionInactiveColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of INACTIVE section plane marker rectangles. Styles > Edit > Modeling Settings > Section Planes 'Inactive'. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). |
| `ShowViewName` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Draw the current scene's name as an overlay in the top-left of the viewport. View > Scene name / Styles watermark area. | Plain boolean. Does NOT fire onRenderingOptionsChanged — capture via ro.keys. Highly relevant to a scene-image exporter: leave it false for clean plate renders. |
| `SilhouetteWidth` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Pixel width of profile/silhouette edges. Styles > Edit > Edge Settings > Profiles value. | Integer pixels; rebuild with .to_i. Practical UI clamp is 1..15. Only has effect when DrawSilhouettes is true. |
| `SkyColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of the sky gradient above the horizon. Styles > Edit > Background Settings > Sky. | Encode as RGBA Integer array; rebuild with Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a). Only visible when the sky is enabled (DrawHorizon / DrawGround interplay); otherwise BackgroundColor shows. |
| `Texture` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether material TEXTURES are displayed (as opposed to just the material's average colour). Styles > Edit > Face Settings > 'Display shaded using all same'/textures. | Plain boolean. Serialise together with RenderMode: 'Shaded With Textures' in the UI is effectively RenderMode 3 with Texture true; 'Shaded' is RenderMode 2 (or 3 with Texture false). Restore RenderMode FIRST, then Texture, because changing RenderMode can reset Texture. |
| `TransparencySort` | rendering_option_key | Integer (Int32) — ENUM | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Transparency rendering quality / sorting method. Window > Preferences > OpenGL 'Transparency quality' (Faster / Medium / Nicer). | ENUM INTEGER: 0 = Faster, 1 = Medium, 2 = Nicer. The official Ruby doc adds the note: 'SketchUp 2017 treats Medium transparency as Faster', so a stored value of 1 renders identically to 0 on modern SketchUp. Rebuild with .to_i, validate 0..2. NOTE: this is arguably a machine/OpenGL preference rather than true document state — consider excluding it from cross-model scene reconstruction so you do not stomp the destination user's performance setting. |
| `#[]` | instance_method | Object or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Reads the value of one rendering option. Returns nil for an unknown key rather than raising. | THE SAFE PROBE. Because it returns nil (never raises) for an unrecognised key, `ro[k].nil?` is a cheap capability test for version-gated keys such as AmbientOcclusion. Caution: nil is also a legitimate 'not set' answer in theory, so prefer ro.keys.include?(k) when you need certainty. Example: Sketchup.active_model.rendering_options['DisplayInstanceAxes'] |
| `#[]=` | instance_method | Object (the value set) | SketchUp 6.0 (raises ArgumentError since SketchUp 2024.0) | READ-ONLY | Writes the value of one rendering option and returns the value that was set. This is the ONLY way to mutate rendering options — there is no #merge, #update or #store. | BREAKING CHANGE IN SKETCHUP 2024.0: raises ArgumentError if the key is invalid, the key's value is not settable, or the value is of the wrong type/range. Versions before 2024.0 failed SILENTLY, which is why old serialisers appear to work and then break on modern SketchUp. Your restore loop MUST wrap each assignment in begin/rescue ArgumentError => e and log-and-continue, otherwise one stale key aborts the whole scene rebuild. Type strictness matters: Float keys need .to_f, Integer keys need .to_i. Colour keys accept a Sketchup::Color, a colour-name String ('white'), a hex String ('#ff0000'), or an RGB(A) Array — use Sketchup::Color.new(r,g,b,a) for lossless round-trips. Since SketchUp 2026.0, writing to a PAGE's rendering options is undoable and must sit inside model.start_operation/commit_operation. |
| `#each` | instance_method | nil (returns nil, not self and not an Enumerator) | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Iterates every key/value pair. Yields a String key and the raw Ruby value. | THE PRIMARY SERIALISATION ENTRY POINT. Returns nil, so you cannot chain (no `.map`, no `.to_h` directly off it) — build a Hash yourself: h = {}; ro.each { |k, v| h[k] = encode(v) }; h. Note it does NOT return an Enumerator when called without a block on older versions, so avoid ro.each.to_a. Values arrive as the live Ruby objects (Sketchup::Color instances, not copies of their RGBA), so encode immediately. |
| `#each_key` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Iterates every rendering option key name. Yields a String. | Returns nil. Useful for building a version-appropriate key whitelist at runtime: valid = []; ro.each_key { |k| valid << k }. Note the stubs oddly declare this both as a class method (Sketchup::RenderingOptions.each_key) and an instance method — only the INSTANCE form is real and usable; the class-method entry is a documentation artefact. |
| `#each_pair` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Alias for #each. Iterates every key/value pair. | Functionally identical to #each. Use whichever reads better; no behavioural difference. |
| `#keys` | instance_method | Array<String> | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Returns an Array of every rendering option key name available in this SketchUp version. | THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT METHOD FOR CROSS-VERSION SAFETY. Never hard-code a key list into a serialiser — call ro.keys at dump time and store the key set alongside the values, then on restore intersect the stored keys with the DESTINATION model's ro.keys before writing. This automatically handles FaceColorMode (removed 2019.1) and AmbientOcclusion* (added 2024/2026). Like #each_key, the stubs list a spurious class-method form; use the instance method. |
| `#count` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2014 | READ-ONLY | Number of rendering options in the collection. Provided by the Enumerable mix-in. | Handy as a cheap version fingerprint to store in your JSON (e.g. a 2026 model reports more options than a 2019 one), but do NOT branch behaviour on it — branch on ro.keys.include?(name). Because it comes from Enumerable it also accepts a block/argument form (count { |k,v| ... }). |
| `#length` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2014 | READ-ONLY | Number of options in the rendering options collection. | Native implementation (unlike #count which comes via Enumerable) so it is cheaper. Same use and same caveat: informational only. |
| `#size` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2014 | READ-ONLY | Alias for #length. | Identical to #length. |
| `#add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Attaches a Sketchup::RenderingOptionsObserver so you are notified when any rendering option changes. Returns true on success. | The observer's callback is onRenderingOptionsChanged(rendering_options, type) where `type` is an Integer matching one of the 74 Sketchup::RenderingOptions ROP* constants — it tells you WHICH option changed, not the new value; you must read ro[key] yourself. CRITICAL WARNING FOR A SERIALISER: the observer does NOT fire for every key. Confirmed non-firing keys are BandColor, DisplaySectionCuts, FaceBackColor, FogEndDist, HorizonColor, InactiveFade, InactiveHidden, InstanceFade, SectionCutDrawEdges and ShowViewName. Never build a 'dirty tracking' serialiser on the observer alone — always do a full ro.each sweep. To map a `type` Integer back to a readable name: LOOKUP = Sketchup::RenderingOptions.constants.map { |c| [Sketchup::RenderingOptions.const_get(c), c.to_s.delete_prefix('ROP')] }.to_h |
| `#remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Detaches a previously added observer. Returns true on success. | You must pass the SAME observer object instance you added. Always remove observers before a bulk restore loop, otherwise each of your ~69 writes fires a callback and you get a re-entrancy storm (and, on SketchUp 2026 page writes, undo-stack flooding). |
| `Sketchup::Model#rendering_options` | instance_method | Sketchup::RenderingOptions | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Returns the LIVE, model-level RenderingOptions object — the settings currently painting the viewport. This is what the Styles dialog edits. | THIS IS WHERE YOU WRITE. Fully readable AND writable. Changing a value here repaints the viewport immediately. Values here are driven by whatever Sketchup::Style is currently selected — if the user then re-selects a style, your writes are overwritten. To make a change stick as a scene, write here, then styles.update_selected_style, then page.use_rendering_options = true (and/or page.use_style = style), then page.update. NOT undoable at model level in the same way page writes are, but still wrap bulk restores in start_operation/commit_operation for a single clean undo step. |
| `Sketchup::Page#rendering_options` | instance_method | Sketchup::RenderingOptions | SketchUp 6.0 (undoable since SketchUp 2026.0) | READ-ONLY | Returns the RenderingOptions object holding the display settings SAVED WITH THAT SCENE — i.e. what the viewport will look like when that scene is activated. | THE CRUCIAL DIFFERENCE. This is a SEPARATE RenderingOptions instance from model.rendering_options — reading page.rendering_options does NOT give you the live viewport state, it gives you that scene's stored snapshot. That makes it the correct source for a per-scene JSON dump WITHOUT having to activate every scene (no pages.selected_page = page, no camera thrash, no screen flicker). WRITABILITY: it is technically writable via []= — it is not a frozen Ruby object — but writing to it is unreliable and is explicitly warned against in the official docs: 'Most rendering options of a scene are also present in Style and are governed by the selected style. Those options should not be changed from the scene. The ones not related to Style like fog (DisplayFog, FogColor) are safe to be changed from the scene.' SketchUp staff confirmed on the forum that 'rendering options aren't saved directly to the Scene but to the Style', so a direct page-level write to a Style-owned key can be silently reverted the moment the page's style reasserts itself. SINCE SKETCHUP 2026.0 writing here is an UNDOABLE OPERATION and MUST be wrapped in model.start_operation / model.commit_operation — extensions that don't are rejected from the Extension Warehouse. RECOMMENDED PATTERN: read from page.rendering_options; write via model.rendering_options + Styles#update_selected_style + Page#update. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_rendering_options?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether this scene saves and restores its own rendering options (the 'Style and Fog' checkbox in the Scenes dialog's Properties to save list). | MUST BE SERIALISED alongside the option values — a perfectly restored option set is inert if this flag is false, because activating the scene will not apply it. Plain boolean in JSON. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_rendering_options=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Sets whether the scene saves its rendering options. Note the official docs contain the typo 'use_rendering_optoins=' in the method description text; the actual method name is correctly spelled. | Set this to true BEFORE calling page.update, otherwise the update will not capture the style/fog state. Order on rebuild: apply options to model.rendering_options → styles.update_selected_style → page.use_rendering_options = true → page.update. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_style?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether the page stores a Style with it. Tightly coupled to use_rendering_options?. | Serialise as a boolean. In practice use_style? and use_rendering_options? move together, because most rendering options ARE style properties. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_style=` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Assigns a Sketchup::Style to the page and enables style/rendering-option saving for it. | Takes a Sketchup::Style OBJECT, not a name String. For cross-model reconstruction: in the destination model, find or create the style (model.styles), select it, push your restored rendering options into model.rendering_options, call model.styles.update_selected_style, then page.use_style = that_style, then page.update. Returns nil, so do not chain off it. |
| `Sketchup::Page#style` | instance_method | Sketchup::Style | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Retrieves the Style object associated with the page. | For a JSON scene record, serialise style.name (and optionally style.description and style.display_name) as a hint, since the Style object itself cannot cross models. On rebuild, look up model.styles[name]; if absent, either add the .style file via model.styles.add_style(path, false) or fall back to applying the raw rendering options onto the destination's current style. |
| `Sketchup::Page#update` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Re-captures the current model state into the page according to the page's use_* flags (or the explicit PAGE_USE_* bit flags). | THE COMMIT STEP. After writing to model.rendering_options and updating the selected style, call page.update to bake it in. Passing PAGE_USE_ALL alone is NOT sufficient to move rendering options into a page if the style was not updated first — this is the exact failure reported on the SketchUp forum. |
| `Sketchup::Styles#update_selected_style` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Pushes the current model.rendering_options values into the currently selected Style, so they become persistent style state rather than a transient override. | THE MISSING LINK most scene-serialisers get wrong. Without this, rendering option writes are a temporary override that the next style refresh discards. Sequence: model.rendering_options[...] = ... ; model.styles.update_selected_style ; page.use_rendering_options = true ; page.update. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPAssign` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Observer `type` code: a wholesale assignment of the rendering options set (e.g. a style change) occurred. | Observer notification code only — NOT a dictionary key. When you see this in onRenderingOptionsChanged, re-read ALL keys rather than one. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPDrawHidden` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Observer `type` code for a change to the legacy DrawHidden option. | Observer code only. Corresponds to key 'DrawHidden'. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPDrawHiddenGeometry` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2020.0 | READ-ONLY | Observer `type` code for a change to the DrawHiddenGeometry option. | OBSERVER CONSTANT, NOT A KEY. The Ruby documentation mistakenly lists this name in the rendering-KEY list for SketchUp 2020.0. The dictionary key is the un-prefixed 'DrawHiddenGeometry'. Never pass 'ROPDrawHiddenGeometry' to ro[] — it returns nil, and ro[]= raises ArgumentError on 2024+. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPDrawHiddenObjects` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2020.0 | READ-ONLY | Observer `type` code for a change to the DrawHiddenObjects option. | OBSERVER CONSTANT, NOT A KEY. Same documentation trap as ROPDrawHiddenGeometry. The dictionary key is 'DrawHiddenObjects'. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPEditComponent` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Observer `type` code: the component-edit-context display settings (fade/hide of rest of model and similar components) changed. | Observer code only. Relates to InactiveFade, InactiveHidden, InstanceFade, InstanceHidden. |
