### Sketchup::ShadowInfo  —  52 entries

| Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `City` | shadowinfo_key | String |  | direct | Location label. Official docs: "City (in Model Info > Geo-location > Set Manual Location…) Note that 'City' is called 'Location' in the UI". Via the API it is a free-form string — the doc's own example is shadowinfo["City"]="Denver, CO". It is purely a label; it does NOT geocode or move Latitude/Longitude. | Encode: h["City"] = si["City"].to_s. Decode: si["City"] = h["City"].to_s. Guard against nil (fresh models can return ""). Model-level, not per-page — write to model.shadow_info only. May contain non-ASCII; ensure the JSON is written/read as UTF-8 (JSON.generate handles this, but force_encoding('UTF-8') on the string retrieved from the attribute dictionary if you see mojibake). |
| `Country` | shadowinfo_key | String |  | direct | Country label. Docs: "Country (in Model Info > Geo-location > Set Manual Location…)". Free-form string via the API; matches the Country field of SketchUp's locations.dat (comma-delimited: Country, City, Long, Lat, TZOffset). | Encode: h["Country"] = si["Country"].to_s. Decode: si["Country"] = h["Country"].to_s. Set Country BEFORE City so the UI's location list resolves sensibly. Model-level. |
| `Dark` | shadowinfo_key | Integer (reads back Integer; accepts Integer or Float since 2026) |  | direct | Docs: "Dark (in Window > Shadows)". The Shadows panel 'Dark' slider, 0–100. The #[]= docs explicitly call out Dark as one of the "numeric properties" that "accepts any Numeric value (Integer or Float)". | Encode: h["Dark"] = si["Dark"].to_i. Decode: si["Dark"] = h["Dark"].to_i. Write an Integer even though 2026 tolerates Float — pre-2026 hosts raise/ignore on Float. Clamp to 0..100 before writing. Per-page (Window > Shadows panel setting). |
| `DayOfYear` | shadowinfo_key | Integer (1..366) |  | READ-ONLY | Ordinal day of the year corresponding to ShadowTime. NOT in the user's original key list — this is the key that was missed. Confirmed present in the official 23-key list on ruby.sketchup.com and in the C API SUShadowInfoRef key list; a real Ruby Console dump shows "DayOfYear - 312". It is derived from ShadowTime. | TREAT AS DERIVED / DO NOT WRITE. It is not annotated "(Generated based on ShadowTime)" in the docs the way SunDirection/SunRise/SunSet are, so its writability is genuinely ambiguous and it is a prime candidate to raise KeyError under the stricter SU2026 #[]= . Export it for diagnostics only (h["DayOfYear"] = si["DayOfYear"].to_i) and NEVER include it in your import whitelist — setting ShadowTime regenerates it. If you must probe it, wrap in begin/rescue KeyError, TypeError. |
| `DaylightSavings` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean (true/false) |  | direct | Docs list it plainly as "DaylightSavings". Whether a one-hour DST shift is applied on top of TZOffset when SketchUp maps ShadowTime to a solar position. Writable. | Encode: h["DaylightSavings"] = (si["DaylightSavings"] ? true : false)  — coerce, because SketchUp booleans can come back as true/false but some hosts historically returned 1/0. Decode: si["DaylightSavings"] = !!h["DaylightSavings"]. CRITICAL for cross-model fidelity: DaylightSavings + TZOffset together determine the solar position for a given epoch. Copying ShadowTime without copying both of these moves the sun by up to an hour. Set it BEFORE ShadowTime. |
| `DisplayNorth` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean |  | direct | Docs list it plainly as "DisplayNorth" (no UI location annotated). Toggles drawing of the solar-north indicator line in the viewport. Writable. | Encode: h["DisplayNorth"] = !!si["DisplayNorth"]. Decode: si["DisplayNorth"] = !!h["DisplayNorth"]. Pairs with NorthAngle. Because the docs group north angle with the model-level settings, treat DisplayNorth as model-level too — write it to model.shadow_info, and only mirror it per-page if your round-trip verification proves the page stores it. |
| `DisplayOnAllFaces` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean |  | direct | Docs: "DisplayOnAllFaces (in Window > Shadows)". The 'On faces' checkbox — faces receive cast shadows. Writable. Per-page. | Encode/decode as plain JSON boolean with !! coercion. Per-page: include in the page payload. Note the trap that bit this in a public forum thread — the key is DisplayOnAllFaces, not DisplayOnFaces; and separately "Displayshadows" (lowercase s) is a real reported typo bug. Exact capitalisation is mandatory: under SU2026 a misspelling now raises KeyError instead of silently returning false. |
| `DisplayOnGroundPlane` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean |  | direct | Docs: "DisplayOnGroundPlane (in Window > Shadows)". The 'On ground' checkbox — the invisible ground plane receives shadows. Writable. Per-page. | Encode/decode as plain JSON boolean with !! coercion. Note the singular 'GroundPlane', not 'GroundPlanes'. |
| `DisplayShadows` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean |  | direct | Docs: "DisplayShadows (in Window > Shadows)". Master shadow on/off. Writable. Per-page — this is the single most important per-scene shadow flag. | Encode/decode as plain JSON boolean with !! coercion. ORDERING: set DisplayShadows LAST, after time and location. Turning shadows on first causes SketchUp to recompute and redraw shadows on every subsequent key write, which is slow across many scenes. Capitalisation: capital D, capital S — "Displayshadows" is a documented real-world typo that breaks scripts. |
| `EdgesCastShadows` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean |  | direct | Docs: "EdgesCastShadows (in Window > Shadows)". The 'From edges' checkbox — standalone edges cast shadows. Writable. Per-page. | Encode/decode as plain JSON boolean with !! coercion. Note the plural 'Edges' and plural 'Shadows' — EdgesCastShadows, not EdgeCastsShadow. |
| `Latitude` | shadowinfo_key | Float (degrees, north-positive, -90.0..90.0) |  | direct | Docs: "Latitude (in Model Info > Geo-location > Set Manual Location…)". Named explicitly in the #[]= docs as a numeric property that "accepts any Numeric value (Integer or Float)". This is the latitude that Model#point_to_latlong and Model#latlong_to_point actually consult — the docs for both state "This method uses the location information set in ShadowInfo." MODEL-LEVEL: Page#shadow_info docs state "global settings like north angle, latitude, and longitude are managed at the model level and are not page-specific." | Encode: h["Latitude"] = si["Latitude"].to_f. Decode: si["Latitude"] = h["Latitude"].to_f. JSON round-trips IEEE-754 doubles exactly at 17 significant digits — use JSON.generate (Ruby emits full precision by default); do NOT round to 6dp, that is ~11 cm of positional error and visibly shifts the sun for high-latitude low-sun studies. Write to model.shadow_info ONCE, never per-page. Sign convention is north-positive (note locations.dat stores "latitude negative southward"). |
| `Light` | shadowinfo_key | Integer (reads back Integer; accepts Integer or Float since 2026) |  | direct | Docs: "Light (in Window > Shadows)". The Shadows panel 'Light' slider, 0–100. Named in the #[]= docs alongside Dark as a flexible-Numeric property. | Encode: h["Light"] = si["Light"].to_i. Decode: si["Light"] = h["Light"].to_i. Write Integer for pre-2026 compatibility. Clamp 0..100. Per-page. |
| `Longitude` | shadowinfo_key | Float (degrees, east-positive, -180.0..180.0) |  | direct | Docs: "Longitude (in Model Info > Geo-location > Set Manual Location…)". Named in the #[]= docs as a flexible-Numeric property. Consumed by Model#point_to_latlong / #latlong_to_point. MODEL-LEVEL, not page-specific (explicit in the Page#shadow_info note). | Encode: h["Longitude"] = si["Longitude"].to_f. Decode: si["Longitude"] = h["Longitude"].to_f. Full double precision — do not round. East-positive: Boulder CO is -105.28300 in the official Model#latlong_to_point example. Beware the axis-order trap elsewhere in the API: Model#latlong_to_point takes [longitude, latitude] (LON FIRST), and Model#point_to_latlong returns a Point3d whose .x is longitude and .y is latitude — but Geom::LatLong.new takes (lat, long) LAT FIRST. ShadowInfo itself uses named keys so it is unambiguous; the confusion arises when you convert between them. |
| `NorthAngle` | shadowinfo_key | Float (degrees, 0.0..360.0) |  | direct | Docs list it plainly as "NorthAngle". Solar north measured in degrees clockwise from the model's green (Y) axis; 0.0 means green axis points north (a real console dump shows "NorthAngle - 0.0"). MODEL-LEVEL — the Page#shadow_info note names north angle explicitly as a model-level global. Writable. | Encode: h["NorthAngle"] = si["NorthAngle"].to_f. Decode: si["NorthAngle"] = h["NorthAngle"].to_f. DEGREES, not radians — do not pass a Ruby Float from .degrees/.radians helpers. Two separate north angles exist and must not be conflated: this ShadowInfo NorthAngle (solar north used for shadow casting) and the GeoReference dictionary's "GeoReferenceNorthAngle" (map north used for imagery alignment). Both Model#point_to_latlong and #latlong_to_point carry an explicit doc Note: "This method does not account for the model's North Angle (solar north)." So if you rotate NorthAngle on import, geo conversions do NOT follow. Set NorthAngle BEFORE ShadowTime. |
| `ShadowTime` | shadowinfo_key | Time (Ruby Time object) |  | encode | Docs: "ShadowTime (in Window > Shadows)". The date+time instant the sun position is computed for. Returns a Ruby Time. Writable — accepts a Time (community code also assigns Time.local(...) and even a DateTime successfully, though Time is the documented/contract type and the only one you should rely on). Setting ShadowTime regenerates DayOfYear, SunDirection, SunRise, SunRise_time_t, SunSet, SunSet_time_t. | NEVER PUT A RAW Time INTO JSON. JSON has no Time type; JSON.generate falls back to Time#to_s, producing e.g. "2026-08-26 14:00:00 +0100" — that string embeds the EXPORTING machine's OS zone and re-parsing it on a machine in another zone shifts the sun. Correct encode, both forms, and store both:   t = si["ShadowTime"]   u = t.utc                      # <- the wall-clock the Shadows dialog shows   h["ShadowTime_time_t"] = t.to_i                       # absolute epoch, Integer, TZ-free   h["ShadowTime_utc_parts"] = [u.year, u.month, u.day, u.hour, u.min, u.sec] Correct decode (epoch first, verified, with a decomposed fallback):   begin     si["ShadowTime_time_t"] = h["ShadowTime_time_t"].to_i   rescue KeyError, TypeError, ArgumentError     y,mo,d,hh,mi,ss = h["ShadowTime_utc_parts"]     si["ShadowTime"] = Time.utc(y,mo,d,hh,mi,ss)   end   # verify and repair:   got = si["ShadowTime"].utc   want = h["ShadowTime_utc_parts"]   if [got.year,got.month,got.day,got.hour,got.min,got.sec] != want     si["ShadowTime"] = Time.utc(*want)   end Why .utc: the shadow instant is stored so that the wall-clock shown in Window > Shadows is recovered by calling .utc on the returned Time. Dan Rathbun documented on Sketchucation that the hour in the returned Time "are off by 1 difference to UTC (GMT)" and that ShadowTime read back "is currently (on DST,) always 4 hours less than the 'SliderTime' in the Shadow Settings inspector dialog" — exactly his OS offset; Wehby's accepted fix was "Try modifying your ShadowTime with '.utc' to fix the issue." So: Time.utc to construct, .utc to read. Never Time.local, never Time.parse, never Time#localtime, never Time#to_s. PER-PAGE: yes — ShadowTime is a Window > Shadows setting and is stored per scene when Page#use_shadow_info? is true. |
| `ShadowTime_time_t` | shadowinfo_key | Integer (Unix epoch seconds) |  | direct | Docs: "ShadowTime_time_t (ShadowTime in Epoch time)". The same instant as ShadowTime expressed as seconds since 1970-01-01. WRITABLE — confirmed by production code (VR Sketch does `@model.shadow_info["ShadowTime_time_t"] = new_time`) and by community usage `Sketchup.active_model.shadow_info["ShadowTime_time_t"]=Time.now.to_i`. This is THE key to serialise the shadow instant with. | THIS IS THE ONE THAT ROUND-TRIPS. An Integer through JSON is lossless and carries zero time-zone semantics; a formatted string does not, because producing it (Time#to_s / strftime without .utc) and consuming it (Time.parse / Time.local) both consult the *process's* TZ, which differs between the exporting and importing machine. Encode: h["ShadowTime_time_t"] = si["ShadowTime"].to_i (Time#to_i is the absolute epoch and is itself TZ-independent, so reading via ShadowTime.to_i and via ShadowTime_time_t should agree — assert this in a debug build). Decode: si["ShadowTime_time_t"] = value.to_i, wrapped in `rescue KeyError`. SU2026 HAZARD — MUST HANDLE: SketchUp api-issue-tracker issue #1086 ("Sketchup::ShadowInfo#[]= now inconsistent in Sketchup 2026") reports ~5 automated crash reports from VR Sketch where `shadow_info["ShadowTime_time_t"] = new_time` raised `KeyError: Invalid key: 'ShadowTime_time_t'` sporadically in 2026.1. "Usually it works as expected" — it fails randomly. The issue is unresolved with no SketchUp-staff answer. Therefore your importer MUST rescue KeyError on this write and fall back to `si["ShadowTime"] = Time.utc(y,mo,d,h,mi,s)` from decomposed parts. This is not defensive paranoia — it is a known live 2026 defect. Note the exact spelling: lowercase t, underscore before time and before t: ShadowTime_time_t. |
| `SunDirection` | shadowinfo_key | Geom::Vector3d (unit vector, model coordinates) |  | READ-ONLY | Docs annotate it explicitly: "SunDirection (Generated based on ShadowTime)". A unit vector pointing FROM the model TOWARD the sun, expressed in model coordinates (so it already has NorthAngle folded in). Real console output: SunDirection - (-0.423824071310226, -0.772439983723215, 0.472979521886207) and (0.9712478375635323, -0.23406079968387353, -0.04351068925572213). Community code reads it as `direction_towards_sun = info["SunDirection"]`. Updates automatically the moment ShadowTime is assigned. | READ-ONLY / COMPUTED — CONFIRMED by the official "(Generated based on ShadowTime)" annotation. DO NOT WRITE IT. Under SketchUp 2026 the release notes state #[]= "will now raise a KeyError for invalid or read-only keys", so an import loop that blindly replays every exported key WILL raise KeyError here and abort your scene restore. Exclude it from the import whitelist. Export for verification only: h["SunDirection"] = [v.x.to_f, v.y.to_f, v.z.to_f] (Geom::Vector3d is not JSON-serialisable — .to_a also works and yields [x,y,z] Floats). On import, do not set it; instead set ShadowTime/Latitude/Longitude/TZOffset/DaylightSavings/NorthAngle correctly, then read SunDirection back and compare against the stored triple with a tolerance of ~1e-6 per component. If it matches, your time+location transfer is provably correct — this is the best end-to-end assertion available for a cross-model scene serialiser. Elevation/azimuth are not exposed by the API and must be derived from this vector. |
| `SunRise` | shadowinfo_key | Time |  | READ-ONLY | Docs annotate it explicitly: "SunRise (Generated based on ShadowTime)". The sunrise Time for the date in ShadowTime at the current Latitude/Longitude/TZOffset. | READ-ONLY / COMPUTED — CONFIRMED by the docs annotation. Do NOT write; under SU2026 it raises KeyError. Exclude from the import whitelist. If exporting for diagnostics use the epoch: h["SunRise"] = si["SunRise"].to_i. Same UTC caveat as ShadowTime — Dan Rathbun's report is specifically that SunRise and SunSet come back with "the hour ... off by 1 difference to UTC (GMT)" (his example: API said 2:07 AM, actual local sunrise in Melbourne FL was 6:07 AM at GMT-4 DST); read the wall-clock via .utc, never via .localtime. |
| `SunRise_time_t` | shadowinfo_key | Integer (Unix epoch seconds) |  | READ-ONLY | Docs: "SunRise_time_t (SunRise in Epoch time)". Epoch twin of SunRise, therefore equally computed from ShadowTime. | READ-ONLY / COMPUTED — it is the same derived quantity as SunRise, so it is read-only for the same reason and will raise KeyError under SU2026's strict setter. Exclude from the import whitelist. Export as a plain Integer for diagnostics only: h["SunRise_time_t"] = si["SunRise_time_t"].to_i. Exact spelling: SunRise_time_t (capital R). |
| `SunSet` | shadowinfo_key | Time |  | READ-ONLY | Docs annotate it explicitly: "SunSet (Generated based on ShadowTime)". The sunset Time for the date in ShadowTime at the current location. | READ-ONLY / COMPUTED — CONFIRMED by the docs annotation. Do NOT write; SU2026 raises KeyError. Exclude from the import whitelist. Diagnostics: h["SunSet"] = si["SunSet"].to_i. Same .utc reading rule as SunRise/ShadowTime. Exact spelling: SunSet, capital S in the middle — not Sunset. |
| `SunSet_time_t` | shadowinfo_key | Integer (Unix epoch seconds) |  | READ-ONLY | Docs: "SunSet_time_t (SunSet in Epoch time)". Epoch twin of SunSet; computed from ShadowTime. | READ-ONLY / COMPUTED. Do NOT write. Exclude from the import whitelist. Export as Integer for diagnostics only. Exact spelling: SunSet_time_t. |
| `TZOffset` | shadowinfo_key | Float (hours relative to UTC; supports half/quarter-hour zones, e.g. 5.5, 5.75, -3.5) |  | direct | Docs: "TZOffset (in Window > Shadows)". Time-zone offset from UTC in hours, used together with ShadowTime and Latitude/Longitude to compute the solar position. Writable — the accepted forum answer is literally `Sketchup.active_model.shadow_info["TZOffset"] = -5.0`. A real console dump shows "TZOffset - -7.0". In SketchUp's locations.dat the fifth field is TZOffset, "referenced to UTC". | Encode: h["TZOffset"] = si["TZOffset"].to_f. Decode: si["TZOffset"] = h["TZOffset"].to_f (write a FLOAT — -5.0 not -5; pre-2026 hosts were fussy, and the accepted forum answer uses -5.0 explicitly). Must be Float to represent India (5.5) and Nepal (5.75). THE TZOFFSET INTERACTION — this is the crux of cross-model fidelity: TZOffset is NOT the exporting machine's OS time zone and has nothing to do with it. It is a per-model property describing the site's civil zone. ShadowTime's epoch and TZOffset are read together by SketchUp's solar solver; transferring one without the other rotates the sun by 15 degrees per hour of mismatch. So ALWAYS write TZOffset (and DaylightSavings, Latitude, Longitude) to the target model BEFORE writing ShadowTime, and never derive TZOffset from Time.now.utc_offset on the exporting machine. SCOPE: officially model-level in effect. A Sketchucation thread demonstrated `model.pages.each { |p| puts "#{p.name} - TZ: #{p.shadow_info['TZOffset']}" }` returning genuinely different per-page values (1.0 and 2.0), yet "when I select that page and go into model info it still says GMT+2" — i.e. per-page TZOffset is stored but IGNORED by SketchUp since v7. Write it once to model.shadow_info; do not try to vary it per scene. |
| `UseSunForAllShading` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean |  | direct | Docs: "UseSunForAllShading (in Window > Shadows)". The 'Use sun for shading' checkbox — when true, faces are shaded by the sun even with DisplayShadows off. Writable. Corresponds to ShadowInfoObserver type code 7. Per-page. | Encode/decode as plain JSON boolean with !! coercion. Exact spelling — UseSunForAllShading, note the 'All' in the middle; there is no such key as 'UseSunForShading'. This dramatically changes viewport appearance, so a scene serialiser that drops it produces visibly wrong scenes even when shadows are off. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo.keys` | class_method | Array<String> |  | no | "The keys method is a class method that returns an array with all of the attribute keys." Example from docs: keys = Sketchup::ShadowInfo.keys. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | USE THIS TO FUTURE-PROOF YOUR EXPORTER. Rather than hard-coding 23 strings, export by iterating the live keys and import against a hard-coded WRITABLE whitelist. That way a future SketchUp that adds a 24th key still exports it (forward compatible) while your importer never attempts a read-only or unknown key (KeyError-safe). Belt and braces: the writable whitelist is the 15 keys City, Country, Dark, DaylightSavings, DisplayNorth, DisplayOnAllFaces, DisplayOnGroundPlane, DisplayShadows, EdgesCastShadows, Latitude, Light, Longitude, NorthAngle, TZOffset, UseSunForAllShading — plus the time via ShadowTime_time_t / ShadowTime. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo.each_key` | class_method | nil |  | no | "The #each_key method iterates through all of the shadow information keys." Documented as BOTH a class method and an instance method. Example: shadowinfo.each_key { |key| puts key }. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Class-method form yields the schema (key names only), instance form yields the same names for a live object. Useful once at load time to assert your whitelist still matches the host's schema and to log a warning if SketchUp 2027 adds a key. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#[]` | instance_method | Object or nil |  | no | "The [] method retrieves a value from the array of keys." Example: value = shadowinfo["key"]. Parameter key (String) — "The key of the shadowinfo value to retrieve." Returns (Object, nil) — "the value that is retrieved." Version: SketchUp 6.0. | The reader is NOT strict — an unknown key returns nil rather than raising, in contrast to #[]= which raises KeyError since 2026. Therefore a nil return is ambiguous (bad key vs genuinely unset) and you should validate key spelling against Sketchup::ShadowInfo.keys rather than relying on the reader to complain. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#[]=` | instance_method | Object (the value set), or false on failure |  | no | "The set value []= method is used to set the value in the array of shadow info options." Docs add: "For numeric properties like “Dark”, “Light”, “Latitude”, and “Longitude”, this method is flexible and accepts any Numeric value (Integer or Float)." Example: model = Sketchup.active_model; shadowinfo = model.shadow_info; shadowinfo["City"]="Denver, CO". Returns "the value that was set if successful, or false if unsuccessful." Raises: "A KeyError is raised if the key is invalid or read-only." and (TypeError) "if the value is not the correct type for the key." Version: SketchUp 6.0. | THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT BEHAVIOURAL CHANGE FOR A 2026 PLUGIN. Release notes (2026.1 section): "Modified {Sketchup::ShadowInfo#[]=} to be stricter and provide better error feedback. It will now raise a KeyError for invalid or read-only keys, and a TypeError for values of an incorrect type." Pre-2026 the same code silently returned false. Consequence: a naive `json_hash.each { |k,v| si[k] = v }` restore loop that worked for years will now RAISE and abort halfway through, leaving the target model in a half-restored state — and because Page property edits are undoable in 2026, a half-restore inside start_operation must be aborted with Model#abort_operation. Always: (a) filter to the writable whitelist, (b) coerce types explicitly (.to_f / .to_i / !! / .to_s), (c) wrap each individual write in begin/rescue KeyError, TypeError and collect failures rather than letting one bad key kill the whole scene restore. Note the return value is `false` on failure, not nil — but you cannot rely on it any more since failures now raise. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean |  | no | "The add_observer method is used to add an observer to the current object." Example: status = object.add_observer observer. Parameter observer (Object) — "An observer." Returns true if successful, false if unsuccessful. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Attach a Sketchup::ShadowInfoObserver to detect user edits and invalidate a cached JSON export. MUST call remove_observer on unload/reload or you leak observers across plugin reloads. Do not perform model edits inside the observer callback without Model#start_operation(name, true, false, true) (transparent), or you will corrupt the undo stack. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#count` | instance_method | Integer |  | no | "The count method is inherited from the Enumerable mix-in module." Example: shadow_info = Sketchup.active_model.shadow_info; number = shadow_info.count. Version: SketchUp 2014. | Returns 23 on SketchUp 2026. Use as a cheap schema-version assertion at load; if it is not 23, log and fall back to iterating Sketchup::ShadowInfo.keys rather than assuming your hard-coded list is complete. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#each` | instance_method | nil |  | no | "The #each method iterates through all of the shadow information key/value pairs." Example: model = Sketchup.active_model; shadow_info = model.shadow_info; shadow_info.each { |key, value| puts "#{key} : #{value}" }. Yields key (String), value (Object). Returns nil. See Also: #each_pair. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | THE EXPORT PRIMITIVE. Iterate this, coerce each value by key-specific rule (Time -> .to_i, Geom::Vector3d -> .to_a, everything else .to_f/.to_i/!!/.to_s), and you have a complete, forward-compatible snapshot. Because ShadowInfo includes Enumerable you also get map/select/to_a/find etc. — but note #to_a yields [key, value] pairs, and a naive shadow_info.to_a.to_h still contains raw Time and Vector3d objects that JSON.generate will mangle. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#each_key` | instance_method | nil |  | no | "The #each_key method iterates through all of the shadow information keys." Example: shadowinfo.each_key { |key| puts key }. Yields key (String). Returns nil. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Instance-level twin of the class method. Use to enumerate the live schema before writing, so you never send a key the host does not know. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#each_pair` | instance_method | nil |  | no | "The #each_pair method is an alias for #each." Example: model = Sketchup.active_model; shadow_info = model.shadow_info; shadow_info.each_pair { |key, value| puts "#{key} : #{value}" }. Yields key (String), value (Object). Returns nil. See Also: #each. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Exact alias for #each; no behavioural difference. Prefer #each_pair in export code for readability. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#keys` | instance_method | Array<String> |  | no | "The keys method is a class method that returns an array with all of the attribute keys" (the doc text is copied from the class method; this is the instance form). Example shown in docs: keys = Sketchup::ShadowInfo.keys. Returns (Array<String>) — "an array of keys". Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Instance form returns the same 23 strings. Safe to call on a Page's shadow_info too. Use `si.keys` to drive export; use a hard-coded whitelist to drive import. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#length` | instance_method | Integer |  | no | "The #length method returns the number of options in the shadow options collection." Example: shadow_info = Sketchup.active_model.shadow_info; number = shadow_info.length. See Also: #size. Version: SketchUp 2014. | 23 on SketchUp 2026. Same use as #count. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean |  | no | "The remove_observer method is used to remove an observer from the current object." Example: status = object.remove_observer observer. Parameter observer (Object). Returns true if successful, false if unsuccessful. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Keep the observer instance in a module-level variable so you can pass the SAME object back to remove_observer — passing a fresh instance removes nothing and silently returns false. Essential in a dev reload loop. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#size` | instance_method | Integer |  | no | "The #size method is an alias for #length." Example: shadow_info = Sketchup.active_model.shadow_info; number = shadow_info.size. See Also: #length. Version: SketchUp 2014. | Alias of #length; returns 23. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo (inherited from Sketchup::Entity)` | instance_method | various |  | no | ShadowInfo inherits from Sketchup::Entity, so it carries the full Entity attribute API. Documented in the "Methods inherited from Entity" section of the ShadowInfo page. | #model is handy in a serialiser to get back to the owning Sketchup::Model from a ShadowInfo you were handed. Do NOT stash your JSON on the ShadowInfo entity's own attribute dictionary — a Page's ShadowInfo is a transient-ish sub-object; put the payload on the Model via Model#set_attribute so it survives save/reopen predictably. |
| `Sketchup::Model#shadow_info` | instance_method | Sketchup::ShadowInfo |  | no | "This method is used to retrieve the shadow information for the Model." Example: model = Sketchup.active_model; shadowinfo = model.shadow_info. Version: SketchUp 6.0. This is the model-wide/current shadow state — the state the viewport is rendering right now, and the state the geo-location conversion methods consult. | THIS is where you write Latitude, Longitude, City, Country, TZOffset, NorthAngle, DaylightSavings, DisplayNorth — exactly once per import, not per scene. Model#shadow_info is also what Model#point_to_latlong / #latlong_to_point / #point_to_utm read ("This method uses the location information set in ShadowInfo"). Editing model.shadow_info is not itself covered by the 2026 page-undoability change, but wrap it in start_operation/commit_operation anyway for a clean single undo of the whole scene import. |
| `Sketchup::Page#shadow_info` | instance_method | Sketchup::ShadowInfo |  | no | "The #shadow_info method retrieves the ShadowInfo object for the page." Carries an explicit doc Note: "While certain shadow settings, such as those available in the Shadows panel, can be controlled on a per-page basis, global settings like north angle, latitude, and longitude are managed at the model level and are not page-specific." And: "Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying shadow_info of a scene is an undoable operation." Example: model = Sketchup.active_model; pages = model.pages; page = pages.add "My Page"; shadowinfo = page.shadow_info. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | MODEL vs PAGE — THE SCOPE SPLIT YOUR SERIALISER MUST ENCODE. Model-level (write once to model.shadow_info): Latitude, Longitude, NorthAngle, City, Country, TZOffset, DaylightSavings, DisplayNorth. Per-page (write to page.shadow_info): ShadowTime / ShadowTime_time_t, DisplayShadows, Dark, Light, UseSunForAllShading, DisplayOnGroundPlane, DisplayOnAllFaces, EdgesCastShadows. Always set page.use_shadow_info = true BEFORE writing to page.shadow_info, or the page may not retain what you wrote. SU2026 MANDATE: the Page class overview states the 2026.0 breaking change verbatim — "Modifying the properties Sketchup::Axes, Sketchup::Camera, Sketchup::RenderingOptions, and Sketchup::ShadowInfo of a Sketchup::Page is now an undoable operation. Scene changes are expected to be called between Sketchup::Model#start_operation and Sketchup::Model#commit_operation to not flood the undo stack. Extensions that don't follow this requirement will be rejected for updates or publication to the Extension Warehouse." Not optional if you plan to publish. The official 2026 pattern, verbatim from the Page overview: model.start_operation("Set Page Properties"); page = pages.add("My Page"); page.axes.set(...); page.camera.fov = 56.78; page.shadow_info["City"] = "Brasov, Romania"; page.rendering_options["BackgroundColor"] = "Pink"; model.commit_operation. ALTERNATIVE, MORE RELIABLE RESTORE PATH: set model.shadow_info to the target state, select the page, then page.update(PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO) to capture it. This avoids all per-page write quirks. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_shadow_info?` | instance_method | Boolean |  | direct | "The use_shadow_info? method determines whether you are storing the shadow info property with the page." Example: status = page.use_shadow_info?. Returns "true if you are storing the this property with the page, false if you are not storing this property with the page." Version: SketchUp 6.0. | MUST BE PART OF YOUR SCENE JSON — it is a genuine scene property, and a scene that does not save shadows behaves completely differently on activation. Encode: h["use_shadow_info"] = page.use_shadow_info?. Decode: page.use_shadow_info = !!h["use_shadow_info"] BEFORE writing any per-page shadow keys. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_shadow_info=` | instance_method | Object |  | no | "The use_shadow_info= method sets the page's shadow info property." Example: status = page.use_shadow_info=false. Parameter setting (Boolean) — "true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property." Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Order of operations on restore: model.start_operation -> page = pages.add(name) -> page.use_shadow_info = true -> write per-page shadow keys -> ... -> model.commit_operation. |
| `Sketchup::Page#update` | instance_method | Boolean |  | no | "The #update method performs an update on the page properties based on the current view that the user has. What properties of the Page get updated are controlled via an integer whose bits corresponds to different properties. These flags can be used individually or combined using bitwise OR." Example from docs: flags = PAGE_USE_CAMERA | PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO | PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY; status = page.update(flags). Version: SketchUp 6.0. | The robust alternative restore path for shadows: set model.shadow_info to the scene's target state, make the page current, then page.update(PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO). This makes SketchUp itself snapshot the state, sidestepping every per-page write quirk. Must still be inside start_operation/commit_operation on 2026. |
| `PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO` | constant | Integer |  | no | Bit flag for Page#update meaning "Shadow Setting". The full documented set is PAGE_USE_CAMERA (Camera Location), PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS (Drawing Style), PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO (Shadow Setting), PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS (Axes Location), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN (Hidden Geometry & Objects, SU2019 and older), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY (SU2020+), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS (SU2020+), PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY (Visible Layers), PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES (Active Section Planes), PAGE_USE_ALL (All possible scene properties), PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT (Environment settings). | Note the spelling: PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO is one word SHADOWINFO with no underscore, unlike PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS. Do not serialise the numeric value across models — it is a host constant; serialise the booleans from the use_*? predicates instead. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfoObserver#onShadowInfoChanged` | instance_method | nil |  | no | "The #onShadowInfoChanged method is invoked whenever the user alters a setting inside the Shadows and Model Info dialogs. The type parameter contains a number identifying which option was altered." Documented type codes verbatim: 0 = Time/Date sliders; 1 = Display Shadows checkbox; 2 = Light/Dark sliders; 3 = Geographic Location (in Model Info > Location); 4 = Solar Orientation (in Model Info > Location); 7 = Use Sun for Shading checkbox; 8 = Display from Edges checkbox; 9 = Display on Ground checkbox; 10 = Display on Faces checkbox. Parameters: shadow_info (Sketchup::ShadowInfo), type (Integer). Returns nil. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Note the gaps: 5 and 6 are undocumented/unused — do not assume a contiguous range. Attach with Sketchup.active_model.shadow_info.add_observer(MyShadowInfoObserver.new). Use type 3 and 4 to invalidate a cached model-level geo block, and 0/1/2/7/8/9/10 to mark the current scene dirty. Callback fires for USER edits via the dialogs; do not rely on it firing for your own Ruby writes. |
| `Sketchup::Model#georeferenced?` | instance_method | Boolean |  | direct | "This methods determines if the model is georeferenced." Example: model = Sketchup.active_model; model.georeferenced?. Version: SketchUp 7.1. | CRITICAL DISTINCTION FOR YOUR PLUGIN: setting shadow_info["Latitude"] and ["Longitude"] does NOT make georeferenced? return true. Georeferencing is a separate state driven by the undocumented "GeoReference" model attribute dictionary (populated by Add Location / imported terrain). So a scene serialiser that copies ShadowInfo lat/long gets correct SUN POSITION in the target model but leaves georeferenced? == false there. Store the flag (h["georeferenced"] = model.georeferenced?) so the importer can warn the user that geo-dependent features (terrain, imagery alignment, accurate point_to_latlong on a non-geo model) will not be reproduced. Read-only — there is no georeferenced= setter. |
| `Sketchup::Model#point_to_latlong` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d |  | encode | "The point_to_latlong method converts a point in the model to geographic longitude/latitude. This method uses the location information set in ShadowInfo." Two explicit doc Notes: (1) "Return value is a Geom::Point3d, not a Geom::LatLong. Components: x: longitude in degrees (east-positive); y: latitude in degrees (north-positive); z: altitude in meters". (2) "This method does not account for the model's North Angle (solar north). The result is in the unrotated georeference frame." Example: lonlat = model.point_to_latlong(local_point) # Point3d[lon, lat, meters]; puts "lon=#{lonlat.x}, lat=#{lonlat.y}, z=#{lonlat.z}m". Docs also show: latlong = model.point_to_utm(local_point).to_latlong. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | RETURNS A Point3d, NOT A LatLong — and the ordering is LON in .x, LAT in .y. This trips everyone. Encode a result as [p.x.to_f, p.y.to_f, p.z.to_f] = [lon_deg, lat_deg, alt_m]. Because it reads ShadowInfo, it silently returns garbage-but-plausible values if the target model's Latitude/Longitude have not been restored yet — always restore geo BEFORE calling it. NorthAngle is NOT applied, so if your scenes rely on a rotated solar north, model-space geometry and this conversion disagree by NorthAngle degrees. |
| `Sketchup::Model#latlong_to_point` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d |  | encode | "The latlong_to_point method converts geographic coordinates to a model Geom::Point3d. It does not take a Geom::LatLong object; pass numeric values instead." Doc Note: "This method does not account for the model's North Angle (solar north)." Two overloads: lnglat_array (Array(Numeric, Numeric)) — "A 2-element array: [longitude_deg, latitude_deg]"; and (Array(Numeric, Numeric, Numeric)) — "A 3-element array: [longitude_deg, latitude_deg, altitude_m] where altitude is in meters and defaults to 0 if omitted." Examples: lnglat_array = [-105.28300, 40.01700]; local_point = model.latlong_to_point(lnglat_array) — and with altitude, model.latlong_to_point([-105.28300, 40.01700, 1655.0]). Version: SketchUp 6.0. | LONGITUDE FIRST. It explicitly REFUSES a Geom::LatLong — pass a plain Array of numbers. Altitude is METRES regardless of the model's display units. Inverse of #point_to_latlong; both read ShadowInfo Latitude/Longitude, so restore those first. |
| `Sketchup::Model#point_to_utm` | instance_method | Geom::UTM |  | encode | "This method converts a Point3d object in the Model to UTM coordinates. This method uses the location information set in ShadowInfo. See also UTM." Example: point = Geom::Point3d.new(10, 10, 10); utm = model.point_to_utm(point). Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Also driven by ShadowInfo location. The docs show model.point_to_utm(local_point).to_latlong as the supported route to an actual Geom::LatLong object. Encode a Geom::UTM as its four components (zone_number, zone_letter, x, y) — see the Geom::UTM.new([+1, "A", 0.12333333, 0.12321321]) form in the utm_to_point example. |
| `Sketchup::Model#utm_to_point` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d |  | encode | "The utm_to_point method converts a position given in UTM coordinates to a Point3d in the Model." Example: utm = Geom::UTM.new([+1, "A", 0.12333333, 0.12321321]); point = model.utm_to_point(utm). Parameter utm (Geom::UTM). Returns a Point3d. Version: SketchUp 6.0. | Geom::UTM constructor takes [zone_number, zone_letter, easting, northing]. Serialise as that 4-element array; reconstruct with Geom::UTM.new(array). |
| `Geom::LatLong#latitude` | instance_method | Float |  | encode | "retrieves the latitude coordinate from a LatLong object". Version: SketchUp 6.0. Constructors: Geom::LatLong.new (→ 0,0), .new(latlong), .new([lat, long]), .new(lat, long). | NOTE THE AXIS ORDER INVERSION: Geom::LatLong.new takes (LAT, LONG) but Model#latlong_to_point takes [LONG, LAT] and Model#point_to_latlong returns a Point3d with LONG in .x. Serialise a LatLong as ll.to_a → [lat, long] and reconstruct with Geom::LatLong.new(array); convert to the Model method order explicitly with [ll.longitude, ll.latitude]. |
| `Geom::LatLong#longitude` | instance_method | Float |  | encode | "retrieves the longitude coordinate from a LatLong object". Version: SketchUp 6.0. | See #latitude for the axis-order warning. Other Geom::LatLong members: #to_a ⇒ Array(Float, Float) [lat, long], #to_s ⇒ String, #to_utm ⇒ Geom::UTM. |
| `GeoReference (model attribute dictionary)` | attribute | mixed (see notes) |  | encode | UNDOCUMENTED / community-sourced. The model-level attribute dictionary named "GeoReference" that SketchUp writes when a model is geolocated via Add Location, and which drives Model#georeferenced?. Reported member keys (from the sketchupruby Google Group and SketchUp Community threads): "UsesGeoReferencing" (Boolean), "Latitude" (Float, degrees north-positive), "Longitude" (Float, degrees east-positive), "GeoReferenceNorthAngle" (Float degrees — the angle between the green axis and map-north, distinct from ShadowInfo's solar NorthAngle), "ModelTranslationX", "ModelTranslationY", "ModelTranslationZ" (Floats, world-coordinate offsets — Dan Rathbun notes SketchUp "does not actually use the Z translation. It assumes sea level (it seems) for all shadow calculations"), "LocationSource" (String), "ZValueCentered" (Boolean), "TimeStamp", "ModelHereState", "ModelHereZoom". | LOWER CONFIDENCE — these key names are community-reported, NOT in the official Ruby API docs, and the set has changed between SketchUp versions. Verify at runtime on 2026 with: d = model.attribute_dictionaries && model.attribute_dictionaries["GeoReference"]; d && d.each_pair { |k,v| puts "#{k} (#{v.class}) = #{v.inspect}" } — and serialise WHATEVER YOU ACTUALLY FIND rather than a hard-coded list. DANGER, EXPLICITLY REPORTED: "SketchUp will crash if you don't set all of the GeoReference attributes" — a partial write is worse than no write. So the safe policy for a cross-model scene exporter is: copy the GeoReference dictionary ALL-OR-NOTHING (every key you found, verbatim, with types preserved) or copy NONE of it and rely on ShadowInfo Latitude/Longitude alone for sun position. Given that your stated goal is scene reconstruction (sun/shadow fidelity), NOT terrain reconstruction, the recommended default is to skip GeoReference entirely, set ShadowInfo lat/long/TZOffset, and record h["source_georeferenced"] = model.georeferenced? as an informational flag. Encoding: all values are String/Float/Boolean/Integer and go through JSON directly; on restore use model.set_attribute("GeoReference", k, v) with the type coerced back to exactly what was read (a Float that JSON round-trips to Integer 0 instead of 0.0 is a plausible crash vector here). |

| `duplicate` | instance_method | Sketchup::Style | SketchUp 2026.2 | no | Creates a copy of the style with all its properties inside the SAME model. The duplicated style is given a unique name derived from the original style's name. Returns the newly created Style. | CRITICAL for your use case: this is an IN-MODEL copy only — it takes no model argument and cannot clone a style across models. It is however the cleanest way, on SketchUp 2026.2+, to mint a FRESH style slot in the target model before you pour the deserialised RenderingOptions into it, so that you do not overwrite an existing style: new_style = model.styles.selected_style.duplicate; model.styles.selected_style = new_style; new_style.name = data["style_name"]. Note this method is present on ruby.sketchup.com and in the 2026.2 release notes but was still ABSENT from the ruby-api-stubs main branch at time of checking, so respond_to?(:duplicate) guard it. |
| `name` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Gets the name for a Style. | Store as {"style_name": style.name}. Beware that styles imported from a .style file are often bracketed by SketchUp, e.g. the official example does styles['[MyStyle]'] after add_style — so a name-based lookup can miss. Prefer to iterate styles.each and compare both name and "[#{name}]". |

Even when populated, the path points at a location on the AUTHORING machine — it is meaningless on a different workstation. Useful fallback strategy: on import, if File.exist?(data['style_path']) then styles.add_style(data['style_path'], true) gives you a pixel-perfect style including watermarks; otherwise fall back to the RenderingOptions replay. | `size` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2014 | direct | An alias for #length. | Same as #length. |
| `BackgroundColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The style's background colour. | Sketchup::Color is NOT JSON-serialisable. Encode as {"__t":"Color","r":c.red,"g":c.green,"b":c.blue,"a":c.alpha} (all Integer 0-255). Decode with Sketchup::Color.new(r, g, b, a). Do not use c.to_a on older versions without checking; the four named readers are safest. |
| `BandColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of the horizon band (the haze band between sky and ground). | Encode/decode as Color, per BackgroundColor. Only visible when DrawGround/DrawHorizon are enabled. |
| `ConstructionColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour used for construction geometry (guide lines and guide points). | Encode/decode as Color. Pairs with HideConstructionGeometry. |
| `DepthQueWidth` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Width of depth-cue edges. NOTE THE SPELLING: SketchUp spells it "Que", not "Cue". | SPELLING TRAP — "DepthQueWidth" with Q-U-E. Get this wrong and on 2024+ you get an ArgumentError, on 2023 and earlier a silent no-op. The docs type it as Numeric; capture the runtime class rather than assuming Integer, and write back the raw JSON number. Only takes effect when DrawDepthQue is true. |
| `DisplayColorByLayer` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether geometry is coloured by its layer/tag colour. | true/false, JSON-native. Safe to round-trip directly. |
| `DisplayFog` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether fog is displayed. | SPECIAL STATUS: the Page docs explicitly name fog as one of the few options that IS safe to set per-scene rather than via the style — "The ones not related to Sketchup::Style like fog (DisplayFog, FogColor) are safe to be changed from the scene." So DisplayFog/FogColor/FogStartDist/FogEndDist/FogUseBkColor can go on page.rendering_options directly. |
| `DisplayInstanceAxes` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether component/group instance axes are drawn. This is the key used in the official RenderingOptions examples. | JSON-native boolean. |
| `DisplayWatermarks` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether the style's watermarks are displayed. NOTE: plural — "DisplayWatermarks", not "DisplayWatermark". | SPELLING TRAP — the plural form is correct; you will find "DisplayWatermark" (singular) in forum posts and it is WRONG. This boolean is the ONLY watermark-related thing Ruby can touch. It toggles visibility of watermarks that already exist in the style; it cannot read, create, position, or transfer a watermark image. Serialising it is nearly pointless cross-model because the target style will have no watermarks to show. |
| `DrawDepthQue` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether depth-cue edge rendering is enabled. Again spelled "Que". | SPELLING TRAP — "DrawDepthQue". Pairs with DepthQueWidth. |
| `DrawGround` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether the ground plane is drawn. | JSON-native. Related: GroundColor, GroundTransparency, DrawUnderground. |
| `DrawHidden` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Legacy hidden-geometry display flag. | SUPERSEDED but still present: since SketchUp 2020 the split keys ROPDrawHiddenGeometry and ROPDrawHiddenObjects are the ones the UI drives. Serialise all three and, on import, write DrawHidden first then the two ROPDraw* keys so the newer values win. Also note Page has its own PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY / PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS flags which are a separate mechanism. |
| `DrawHorizon` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether the horizon line is drawn. | JSON-native. Related: HorizonColor, BandColor. |
| `DrawLineEnds` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether edge endpoints are drawn. | JSON-native. Pairs with LineEndWidth. |
| `DrawProfilesOnly` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether only profile edges are drawn (interior edges suppressed). | JSON-native. Interacts with DrawSilhouettes and SilhouetteWidth — restore all three together or the result will not match. |
| `DrawSilhouettes` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether profile/silhouette edges are drawn thicker. This is the API name for the UI's "Profiles" checkbox. | NAMING TRAP — the UI says "Profiles" but the key says "Silhouettes" (and the width key is SilhouetteWidth, singular). Meanwhile DrawProfilesOnly is a DIFFERENT setting (the UI's "Profiles only"). Do not conflate them. |
| `DrawUnderground` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether geometry below the ground plane is drawn. | JSON-native. Only meaningful when DrawGround is true. |
| `EdgeColorMode` | rendering_option_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | How edges are coloured. Community-documented values (NOT in the official docs): 0 = By material, 1 = All same, 2 = By axis. | Integer, JSON-native. The API defines NO named constants for these values — a forum respondent noted "I would have expected there to be a constant defined in the API for each of these values, but if there is, it does not seem to be mentioned in the documentation." The 0/1/2 mapping above is community-sourced, so do not hard-code decisions on it: for a serialiser, just round-trip the raw Integer opaquely, which is correct regardless of what the numbers mean. |
| `EdgeDisplayMode` | rendering_option_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Edge display mode. The official docs give no value table. | Round-trip the raw Integer opaquely. Do not attempt to interpret it — the mapping is undocumented and has changed presentation between versions. |
| `EdgeType` | rendering_option_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | THE ONLY officially value-documented key. Accepted values (Integer): 0 = Standard edges, 1 = Sketchy edges. | This is the full extent of Ruby's reach into sketchy-edge (NPR) rendering: you can switch sketchy mode ON or OFF, and that is all. WHICH sketchy stroke set is in use — the hand-drawn line texture that defines the look of styles like 'Marker Loose' or 'Pencil on Tracing Paper' — is stored inside the .style archive and has NO Ruby accessor whatsoever. Setting EdgeType = 1 in the target model gives you whatever stroke set that target style already carries, which will almost certainly not match the source. Combine with JitterEdges, LineExtension, ExtendLines for the parts that ARE reachable. |
| `ExtendLines` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether edges are drawn overshooting their endpoints (the "Extension" effect). | Boolean master switch; LineExtension carries the amount. Restore both. |
| `FaceBackColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Default colour of back faces. | Encode/decode as Color. This is the one people notice immediately when a style transfer fails — the classic blue-grey back-face colour. |
| `FaceFrontColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Default colour of front faces. | Encode/decode as Color. |
| `FogColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Fog colour. | Encode/decode as Color. Explicitly named in the Page docs as safe to set per-scene. Ignored when FogUseBkColor is true. |
| `FogEndDist` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Distance at which fog reaches full density. | A model-space distance in INCHES (SketchUp's internal unit). Because it is an absolute distance, a value transferred from a small model into a large one will look wrong. Consider recording model bounds alongside it if fidelity matters. Round-trip as a raw JSON number. |
| `FogStartDist` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Distance at which fog begins. | Same inches/scale caveat as FogEndDist. |
| `FogUseBkColor` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether fog uses the background colour instead of FogColor. | Order matters on restore: set FogColor first, then FogUseBkColor, so the stored colour survives even when the flag is true. |
| `ForegroundColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The style's foreground (edge) colour. | Encode/decode as Color. Only visible when EdgeColorMode selects "All same". |
| `GroundColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Ground plane colour. | Encode/decode as Color. |
| `GroundTransparency` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Transparency of the ground plane. | Round-trip the raw number. The documented type is Numeric; the docs do not state the range or whether it is Integer percent or Float 0..1, so do not clamp or rescale it — write back exactly what you read. |
| `HideConstructionGeometry` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether construction geometry (guides) is hidden. | JSON-native. Pairs with ConstructionColor. |
| `HighlightColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Selection highlight colour. | Encode/decode as Color. Purely an editing aid; arguably not worth transferring, but it IS part of the style. |
| `HorizonColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Horizon line colour. | Encode/decode as Color. |
| `InactiveHidden` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether geometry outside the active edit context is hidden while editing a group/component. | JSON-native. Pairs with InactiveFade. |
| `InstanceHidden` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether similar instances are hidden while editing a component. | JSON-native. Pairs with InstanceFade. |
| `JitterEdges` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether edges are drawn with a hand-drawn jitter/wobble. | One of the few genuinely NPR-related keys Ruby can reach. Boolean only — no amount, no seed. Restore alongside EdgeType, ExtendLines and LineExtension for the closest achievable approximation of a sketchy style. |
| `LineEndWidth` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Width of drawn endpoints. | Round-trip the raw number. Only effective when DrawLineEnds is true. |
| `LineExtension` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Amount by which edges overshoot their endpoints. | Round-trip the raw number. Only effective when ExtendLines is true — set ExtendLines first on restore. |
| `LockedColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour used to draw locked entities. | Encode/decode as Color. |
| `MaterialTransparency` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether material transparency is honoured in rendering. | JSON-native. Distinct from ModelTransparency (X-ray) — restore both. |
| `ModelTransparency` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Global model transparency — the X-ray face style toggle. | JSON-native. Note there is a ROPSetXRayOpacity observer constant but NO corresponding public key for the X-ray opacity AMOUNT — you can turn X-ray on and off but not set how transparent it is. Another data point for the central question. |
| `RenderMode` | rendering_option_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The face rendering mode (SketchUp's Wireframe / Hidden Line / Shaded / Shaded With Textures / Monochrome family). The official docs give NO value table for this key. | IMPORTANT: I could not find an authoritative published mapping of RenderMode integers to face styles — the SketchUp docs do not document them and I will not guess. For a serialiser this does not matter: read the Integer, store the Integer, write the Integer back. Round-tripping opaquely is both correct and version-proof. Only if you need to SET a specific mode from a UI should you determine the value empirically on the target version (set the mode in the UI, then read the key). |
| `SectionActiveColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of the active section plane. | Encode/decode as Color. |
| `SectionCutWidth` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Line width of section cut edges. | Round-trip the raw number. |
| `SectionDefaultCutColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Default colour of section cut lines. | Encode/decode as Color. |
| `SectionInactiveColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Colour of inactive section planes. | Encode/decode as Color. |
| `ShowViewName` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether the scene/view name is displayed in the viewport. | JSON-native. Directly relevant to a scene exporter — this is the on-screen scene-name overlay. |
| `SilhouetteWidth` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Width of profile/silhouette edges. Note singular "Silhouette" here versus plural "DrawSilhouettes" for the toggle. | SPELLING TRAP — toggle is DrawSilhouettes (plural), width is SilhouetteWidth (singular). Round-trip the raw number. |
| `SkyColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Sky colour. | Encode/decode as Color. There is no separate 'DrawSky' key in the documented list; sky visibility rides with DrawGround/DrawHorizon and the background settings. |
| `Texture` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Whether textures are displayed. | JSON-native. Interacts with RenderMode — a shaded-with-textures RenderMode plus Texture=false gives plain shaded. Restore RenderMode first, then Texture. |
| `TransparencySort` | rendering_option_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Transparency sorting quality. Official note: "SketchUp 2017 treats Medium transparency as Faster." | Round-trip the raw Integer. The documented behaviour change in 2017 means a stored value may render differently on different versions even when set identically — do not treat a mismatch as a bug. |
| `DisplayDims` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 7 | direct | Whether dimension entities are displayed. | JSON-native. Added in SketchUp 7 — guard with keys.include? if you support anything older (you will not, for 2026, but the guard costs nothing). |
| `DisplaySketchAxes` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 7 | direct | Whether the model axes are displayed. | JSON-native. Note this is DISPLAY of axes; the axes POSITION is a separate scene property carried by PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS. |
| `DisplayText` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 7 | direct | Whether text/label entities are displayed. | JSON-native. |
| `InactiveFade` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 8 | direct | Fade amount applied to geometry outside the active edit context. | Round-trip the raw number; range is undocumented, so do not rescale. Pairs with InactiveHidden. |
| `InstanceFade` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 8 | direct | Fade amount applied to similar component instances during editing. | Round-trip the raw number. Pairs with InstanceHidden. |
| `DisplaySectionPlanes` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2014 | direct | Whether section plane markers are displayed. | JSON-native. Distinct from DisplaySectionCuts (whether the CUT is active). Both belong to the style, but section-plane ACTIVATION per scene is carried separately by PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES / Page#use_section_planes=. |
| `DisplaySectionCuts` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2015 | direct | Whether section cuts are displayed (section cut effect on/off). | JSON-native. |
| `DrawBackEdges` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2015 | direct | Whether back edges (edges hidden behind faces) are drawn dashed. | JSON-native. |
| `SectionCutDrawEdges` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2015 | direct | Whether edges are drawn at section cuts. | JSON-native. |
| `SectionCutFilled` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2018 | direct | Whether section cuts are filled with a solid colour. | JSON-native. Pairs with SectionDefaultFillColor. Highly relevant to architectural scene exports. |
| `SectionDefaultFillColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 2018 | encode | Fill colour used for filled section cuts. | Encode/decode as Color. Set SectionCutFilled = true as well or it will not show. |
| `FaceColorMode` | rendering_option_key | Integer (formerly) | removed in SketchUp 2019.1 | no | REMOVED in SketchUp 2019.1. Official note: "This option was previously included but it was ineffective." | Do NOT write this key. On 2024.0+ attempting to set an invalid key raises ArgumentError, so a legacy JSON payload containing FaceColorMode will blow up a restore unless you filter against rendering_options.keys. If you are importing archived data captured on an old SketchUp, explicitly blacklist this key. (The ROPSetFaceColorMode observer constant still exists, which is a nice illustration of the observer surface outliving the public key.) |
| `ROPDrawHiddenGeometry` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.0 | direct | Whether hidden GEOMETRY (hidden edges/faces) is drawn. Introduced when SketchUp 2020 split the old single hidden-geometry toggle in two. | CONFUSING NAME — this is a rendering option KEY that happens to share the ROP prefix used by the observer constants. It really is a key: it appears in the official key list under "Added in SketchUp 2020.0". Serialise it. Together with ROPDrawHiddenObjects it supersedes the legacy DrawHidden key. |
| `ROPDrawHiddenObjects` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.0 | direct | Whether hidden OBJECTS (hidden groups/components) are drawn. The second half of the SketchUp 2020 hidden-geometry split. | Same ROP-prefix caveat as above. Note the parallel Page flags PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY and PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS (both SketchUp 2020+) which control whether the SCENE remembers these — a scene serialiser should handle both layers. |
| `AmbientOcclusion` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Master toggle for ambient occlusion rendering. | JSON-native. SketchUp 2024.0+ only — MUST be guarded with keys.include?, because a 2026-authored scene restored into 2023 will otherwise raise nothing on 2023 (silent ignore) but a 2023-authored payload is simply missing the key. Set this before the other AmbientOcclusion* keys. |
| `AmbientOcclusionDistance` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Ambient occlusion sampling distance. | Round-trip the raw number. SketchUp 2024.0+ — guard with keys.include?. Being a distance, it is scale-sensitive between models. |
| `AmbientOcclusionIntensity` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Ambient occlusion intensity. | Round-trip the raw number. SketchUp 2024.0+ — guard with keys.include?. |
| `AmbientOcclusionColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | Colour of the ambient occlusion shading. | Encode/decode as Color. SketchUp 2026.0+ ONLY — this is one of the two newest keys and will be absent on 2025 and earlier. Guard with keys.include? on both export and import. |
| `AmbientOcclusionMultiplier` | rendering_option_key | Numeric | SketchUp 2026.0 | direct | Ambient occlusion multiplier. | Round-trip the raw number. SketchUp 2026.0+ ONLY. Guard with keys.include?. |
| `style` | instance_method | Sketchup::Style | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Retrieves the style associated with the page. Official example: style = page.style. | YOUR EXPORT HOOK. For each page: page.style.name is the style identity to record in your scene JSON. Do not serialise the object itself — capture name, description and (2025+) path. |
| `use_style=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Style (parameter) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Sets the style to be used by the page. Official example: style = model.styles[0]; page.use_style = style. | SIGNATURE TRAP — despite the use_* naming convention (where every sibling such as use_camera=, use_shadow_info=, use_section_planes= takes a Boolean), use_style= takes a Sketchup::Style OBJECT. Passing true will not do what you expect. Setting it also flips use_rendering_options? to true. The Style must belong to the SAME model as the page. |
| `use_style?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Determines whether the page saves style properties. Corresponds to the "Style and Fog" checkbox in the Scenes panel. Returns the same value as #use_rendering_options?. | Serialise as a boolean. Note it is documented to be linked to use_rendering_options? — they return identical values, so store one and restore both, or just store both for clarity. |
| `use_rendering_options?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Determines whether you are storing the rendering options property with the page. Corresponds to the "Style and Fog" checkbox. | JSON-native boolean. Must be true (directly, or as a side effect of use_style=) for the reconstructed style to stick to the scene. |
| `use_rendering_options=` | instance_method | Boolean (parameter and return) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Sets whether the page saves its own rendering options. Setting it to false also sets use_style? to false. Unlike use_style=, this one DOES take a Boolean. | Restore order that works: apply rendering options -> styles.update_selected_style -> page.use_style = style (which sets this true) -> page.update(PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS). Setting this false is how you make a scene NOT remember a style. |
| `rendering_options` | instance_method | Sketchup::RenderingOptions | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Retrieves a RenderingOptions object for the page. Official @note: "Most rendering options of a scene are also present in Sketchup::Style and are governed by the selected style. Those options should not be changed from the scene. The ones not related to Sketchup::Style like fog (DisplayFog, FogColor) are safe to be changed from the scene." Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying a scene's rendering_options is an undoable operation. | READ from this for export (it gives you the page's own snapshot without having to navigate to the page). Do NOT bulk-WRITE to it on import — the official note is explicit that style-governed options should not be changed from the scene. Write to model.rendering_options + update_selected_style instead. The documented exception is fog, which you may set directly on page.rendering_options. |
| `update` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Commits the current model state into the page for the given property flags. | page.update(PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS) is the step that actually bakes your restored style into the scene. Without it the scene will snap back to its old style the moment the user clicks another scene tab. Combine flags with bitwise OR when restoring several properties at once. |
| `PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page#update flag meaning "Drawing Style" — i.e. the style/rendering options of the scene. | Top-level constant (not namespaced under Sketchup::Page). This is the flag your style-restore needs. Value is an Integer bitmask; OR it together with other PAGE_USE_* flags for a combined update. |
| `PAGE_USE_CAMERA` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page#update flag for Camera Location. | Include in your combined update mask when rebuilding a full scene. |
| `PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page#update flag for Shadow Settings. | ShadowInfo is a separate serialisation area from Style, but note that shadow settings and rendering options together determine the visual result. Restore both. |
| `PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page#update flag for Axes Location. | Distinct from the DisplaySketchAxes rendering option, which controls whether axes are DRAWN. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2020 | direct | Page#update flag for Hidden Geometry. | SketchUp 2020+. Pairs conceptually with the ROPDrawHiddenGeometry rendering option. Guard with defined? on older targets. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2020 | direct | Page#update flag for Hidden Objects. | SketchUp 2020+. Pairs with ROPDrawHiddenObjects. |
| `PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page#update flag for Visible Layers (tags). | Separate serialisation area, but part of any full scene rebuild. |
| `PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page#update flag for Active Section Planes. | Distinct from the DisplaySectionPlanes / DisplaySectionCuts rendering options, which are style-owned. |
| `PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT` | constant | Integer |  | direct | Page#update flag for Environment settings. | Newer flag; guard with defined?(PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT) before use on older versions. |
| `PAGE_USE_ALL` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page#update flag meaning all possible scene properties. | Convenient but blunt — page.update(PAGE_USE_ALL) captures the CURRENT model state for every property, which during a staged rebuild may capture half-restored state. Prefer explicit ORed flags in a serialiser. |
| `ROPAssign` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | RenderingOptionsObserver change-type constant: a bulk assignment of rendering options occurred. | Observer flag, not a key. Useful to detect a wholesale style switch (as opposed to a single tweak) — e.g. to invalidate a cache when the user changes style. |
| `ROPDrawHidden` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for the legacy hidden-geometry setting. | Observer flag. Note the name collides conceptually with the DrawHidden key — the constant is the notification id, the key is the setting. |
| `ROPEditComponent` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for component-edit display settings (InactiveHidden/InstanceHidden/fades). | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSectionDisplayTurnedOff` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type: section display was turned off. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetAOColor` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2026.0 | no | Observer change-type for the AmbientOcclusionColor setting. | Observer flag; corresponds to the AmbientOcclusionColor key (2026.0+). |
| `ROPSetAOColorEnabled` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2026.0 | no | Observer change-type for whether AO colour is enabled. | Observer flag. NOTE: there is no documented public KEY for an 'AO colour enabled' boolean — another example of an internal setting visible to the observer but not to the key API. |
| `ROPSetAODistance` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2024.0 | no | Observer change-type for AmbientOcclusionDistance. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetAOEnabled` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2024.0 | no | Observer change-type for the AmbientOcclusion master toggle. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetAOIntensity` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2024.0 | no | Observer change-type for AmbientOcclusionIntensity. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetAOMultiplier` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2026.0 | no | Observer change-type for AmbientOcclusionMultiplier. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetBackgroundColor` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for BackgroundColor. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetConstructionColor` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for ConstructionColor. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDepthQueEdges` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for the DrawDepthQue setting. | Observer flag; note the same 'Que' spelling. |
| `ROPSetDepthQueWidth` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for DepthQueWidth. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDisplayColorByLayer` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for DisplayColorByLayer. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDisplayDims` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 7 | no | Observer change-type for DisplayDims. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDisplayFog` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for DisplayFog. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDisplayInstanceAxes` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for DisplayInstanceAxes. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDisplaySketchAxes` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 7 | no | Observer change-type for DisplaySketchAxes. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDisplayText` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 7 | no | Observer change-type for DisplayText. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDisplayWatermarks` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for DisplayWatermarks. | Observer flag. This is the ONLY watermark-related identifier in the entire Ruby API besides the DisplayWatermarks key — and it only tells you the visibility toggle changed, never anything about the watermark itself. |
| `ROPSetDrawBackEdges` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2015 | no | Observer change-type for DrawBackEdges. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDrawGround` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for DrawGround. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDrawHorizon` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for DrawHorizon. | Observer flag. |
| `ROPSetDrawUnderground` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for DrawUnderground. | Observer flag. |

This is precisely why sketchy-edge styles cannot be reproduced across models via RenderingOptions. |
