| `ROPTransparencySortMethod` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Observer change-type for TransparencySort. | Observer flag. |
| `Sketchup.temp_dir` | class_method | String | SketchUp 2014 | READ-ONLY | Retrieves the OS temporary directory for the current user. "You can use this directory to write temporary files that are not required to persist between SketchUp sessions." | THE answer to 'where can a .style file be written'. It is the correct, permission-safe scratch location on both Windows and macOS, and it avoids the Program Files write-permission trap. Relevant only for staging a .style file that you already have (e.g. one shipped with your plugin, or one the user exported by hand) — you still cannot GENERATE one from Ruby. |
| `Sketchup.find_support_file` | class_method | String or false | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Finds a file in SketchUp's support folders. Params: filename (String) and directory (String) "directory relative to the SketchUp installation directory". Returns the entire path if successful, or FALSE if unsuccessful. | Use to locate SketchUp's shipped Styles collections, e.g. Sketchup.find_support_file('Styles'). RETURN-VALUE TRAP: it returns false, not nil, on failure — so `path = Sketchup.find_support_file(...) or raise` works but `path&.length` does not behave as you might expect. Always test truthiness explicitly. |
| `Sketchup.get_resource_path` | class_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Retrieves the directory where "resource" files are stored by SketchUp. Official example: directory = Sketchup.get_resource_path("Styles.strings"). | Read-only discovery helper. Do not attempt to write .style files into SketchUp's install tree — on Windows that path is under Program Files and is not writable without elevation. The user's own writable Styles collection on Windows is %APPDATA%\SketchUp\SketchUp 2026\SketchUp\Styles (and on macOS ~/Library/Application Support/SketchUp 2026/SketchUp/Styles). |

### Sketchup::SectionPlane and section display  —  143 entries

| Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `activate` | instance_method | Sketchup::SectionPlane (self) | SketchUp 2014 | no | Makes the section plane the active one of its parent component/group (i.e. of the Sketchup::Entities collection that owns it). Equivalent to entities.active_section_plane = self. Only one section plane per Entities collection can be active at a time; activating one deactivates any other in the SAME collection but does not touch other collections. | Not a value — it is the action you replay on rebuild. Serialise the boolean from #active? as "active": true/false, then on rebuild call sp.activate when true. Do NOT rely on activate alone to make a cut appear: rendering_options['DisplaySectionCuts'] must also be true and the plane must not be hidden. |
| `active?` | instance_method | Boolean (TrueClass/FalseClass) | SketchUp 2014 | direct | Indicates whether the section plane is the active one of its parent Entities collection. Returns true if active. | Store as JSON boolean: {"active": sp.active?}. Reconstruct with sp.activate if true, or leave alone / set ents.active_section_plane = nil for the collection if no plane in it was active. Note this reflects the CURRENT model state, which for scene serialisation means the state of whatever page is currently selected — see the Page#active_section_planes / selected_page notes in gotchas. |
| `get_plane` | instance_method | Array of 4 Float (plain Ruby Floats, NOT Length objects) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Retrieves the plane that the section plane is on. Official docs say only 'a plane. See the Geom module and Array class for further information on planes.' In practice it ALWAYS returns the 4-element coefficient form [a, b, c, d] — never the [Point3d, Vector3d] form. (a, b, c) is the UNIT normal, which is the direction the section plane's arrow points (the side that is kept / the direction the eye looks from). d is the plane constant for a*x + b*y + c*z + d = 0, i.e. the negated signed distance of the origin from the plane measured along the normal, expressed in INCHES (SketchUp internal units). d may be negative, meaning the origin is behind the plane. The plane is expressed in the coordinate system of the Entities collection that owns the SectionPlane — for a plane nested inside a group/component that is DEFINITION-local, not world. | THIS IS THE SAFE ROUND-TRIP VALUE. sp.get_plane.to_a returns four plain Floats and can go straight into JSON with no coercion: {"plane_abcd": sp.get_plane.to_a}. Units are inches — if the target model uses different display units that is irrelevant, inches is the storage unit in both. To rebuild: n = Geom::Vector3d.new(a,b,c); pt = ORIGIN.offset(n, -d); entities.add_section_plane(pt, n). Do NOT round the floats; d for a plane 40 m out is ~1574.8 inches and a 1e-6 error is invisible but a 1e-2 error is a visible shift. Never call .to_s on the array for JSON — you will lose precision. |
| `set_plane` | instance_method | Sketchup::SectionPlane (the updated section plane) | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Sets the plane that the section plane is on. Documented parameter is only 'plane — An array representing the new plane'. It accepts BOTH plane forms: an Array of 4 numeric coefficients [a, b, c, d], and an Array of [Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d]. It moves and re-orients an EXISTING section plane in place, in the coordinate system of the Entities collection that owns it. There is no transform!/transformation route — set_plane is the only way to reposition a SectionPlane directly. | On rebuild you normally use Entities#add_section_plane instead (it creates and positions in one call). Use set_plane when updating an existing plane. If you pass the 4-coefficient form, a, b, c and d MUST be mutually consistent: scaling all four by the same factor is fine (the plane is unchanged), but changing only the normal's length while leaving d alone silently moves the plane. Passing [Point3d, Vector3d] is the safer form because the point unambiguously fixes the position. Reversing a plane = negate ALL FOUR values: sp.set_plane(sp.get_plane.map { |i| -i }). |
| `name` | instance_method | String (may be empty string) | SketchUp 2018 | direct | Retrieves the name of the section plane, as shown in the Outliner / Entity Info. Distinct from #symbol. | Plain String, JSON-direct. Guard the call for pre-2018 hosts: name = sp.respond_to?(:name) ? sp.name.to_s : ''. On rebuild: sp.name = data['name'] if sp.respond_to?(:name=) && !data['name'].to_s.empty?. Names are NOT unique and are not identity — do not use them as a matching key across models; use the recorded plane geometry plus symbol plus ordering instead. |
| `name=` | instance_method | String (the assigned name) | SketchUp 2018 | encode | Sets the name of a section plane. Parameter: name (String) — the new name for the section plane object. | Write side of the round-trip. Assigning nil or a non-String may raise; coerce with .to_s. Empty string is accepted and clears the name. |
| `symbol` | instance_method | String, three characters or fewer | SketchUp 2018 | direct | Retrieves the symbol of the section plane — the short tag (e.g. 'A', 'AB1') shown on the section plane marker and used to label section cuts in LayOut. | Plain String, JSON-direct. Guard with respond_to?(:symbol) for pre-2018. On rebuild the value MUST be three characters or fewer — truncate defensively: sp.symbol = data['symbol'].to_s[0, 3]. |
| `symbol=` | instance_method | String (three characters or less) | SketchUp 2018 | encode | Sets the symbol of a section plane. Documented note: 'The symbol must be three characters or less.' Parameter: symbol (String) — the new symbol for the section plane object. | Always clamp to 3 chars before assigning or you risk an ArgumentError / silent truncation depending on version. sp.symbol = (data['symbol'] || '').to_s[0, 3]. |
| `transformation` | instance_method | n/a |  | no | IMPORTANT NEGATIVE FINDING: Sketchup::SectionPlane does NOT define #transformation, #transformation=, or #transform!. Those live on Sketchup::ComponentInstance / Sketchup::Group / Sketchup::Image only. The full instance-method summary on the official SectionPlane page is exactly eight methods (activate, active?, get_plane, name, name=, set_plane, symbol, symbol=) plus inherited Drawingelement and Entity members. A section plane's placement is expressed ONLY through its plane array. | To 'transform' a section plane you have two options. (1) Compute the transformed plane yourself and call set_plane — decompose into point + normal, transform each, reform. (2) Use Sketchup::Entities#transform_entities(tr, [section_plane]), which does work on SectionPlane objects and is the undo-friendly route. To record WORLD placement of a nested section plane, multiply the transformations of every enclosing instance (or use Sketchup::InstancePath#transformation) and apply that to the definition-local plane — see code_examples. |
| `bounds` | instance_method | Geom::BoundingBox | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Returns the bounding box of the section plane's visual marker (the rectangular grip that SketchUp draws). This is a display artefact sized by SketchUp, not part of the mathematical plane. | DO NOT serialise this as state — there is no API to set the marker size, and SketchUp recomputes it. It is useful only for diagnostics (e.g. bounds.center gives you a rough anchor point of the marker). Serialise get_plane instead. If you must record it: [bb.min.to_a.map(&:to_f), bb.max.to_a.map(&:to_f)] — note .to_a returns Length objects, so .map(&:to_f) is mandatory before JSON. |
| `hidden?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Returns true if the section plane's own hidden flag is set. A hidden section plane does not cut, even if it is the active plane of its Entities collection. | {"hidden": sp.hidden?}. On rebuild: sp.hidden = !!data['hidden']. NOTE the distinction from #visible? — hidden? is the element's own flag; visible? additionally accounts for the element's Layer/Tag visibility. For scene work, hidden? is the one that is captured by Page#update's hidden flags, and per-page overrides go through Page#set_drawingelement_visibility. |
| `hidden=` | instance_method | Boolean — true if the element has been hidden, false if not | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Sets the section plane's hidden flag. | Write side. Pass a real Boolean; JSON null must be coerced (!!value). |
| `visible?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Effective visibility — false if the element is hidden OR its Layer/Tag is not visible. | Derived value; do not round-trip it as authoritative state. Serialise hidden? plus the layer/tag name, and let visible? fall out on rebuild. Recording it anyway is useful as a validation checksum after reconstruction. |
| `visible=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Sets visibility. In practice this sets the same underlying flag as hidden= (inverted); it does not change the layer's visibility. | Prefer hidden= for clarity so you are not mixing two spellings of the same flag. |
| `layer` | instance_method | Sketchup::Layer (a Tag in modern UI terminology) | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. The Layer/Tag the section plane is assigned to. Never nil — an unassigned element returns the 'Layer0'/'Untagged' layer. | Serialise as {"layer_name": sp.layer.display_name, "layer_persistent_id": sp.layer.persistent_id, "layer_visible": sp.layer.visible?}. Use display_name (2020+) rather than name where available — for the default tag, name returns 'Layer0' but display_name returns the localised 'Untagged'. On rebuild, resolve by name in the TARGET model: lyr = model.layers[data['layer_name']] || model.layers.add(data['layer_name']); sp.layer = lyr. persistent_id is NOT portable between models — use it only to disambiguate duplicates within the SOURCE model. |
| `layer=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Layer or String (layer name) | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Assigns the section plane to a Layer/Tag. Accepts either a Sketchup::Layer object or the layer's name as a String. | Accepting a String means you can do sp.layer = data['layer_name'] directly IF that layer already exists in the target model — otherwise it raises. Always create-or-fetch first: model.layers[name] || model.layers.add(name). This is the hinge between section planes and per-scene tag visibility: if the page hides that tag (via Page#set_visibility), the plane is hidden and stops cutting. |
| `material` | instance_method | Sketchup::Material or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Present on SectionPlane by inheritance but semantically meaningless — a section plane's colour comes from the SectionActiveColor / SectionInactiveColor rendering options, not from a material. Normally returns nil. | Skip it. Do not attempt to round-trip a material for a SectionPlane; the appearance you actually want to preserve lives in RenderingOptions. |
| `material=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Material, String or Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Setter counterpart to #material; no visible effect on a SectionPlane. | Do not call during reconstruction. |
| `casts_shadows?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Whether the element casts shadows. Present by inheritance; effectively inert on a SectionPlane since the plane marker is not shadow-casting geometry. | Cheap Boolean, safe to capture for fidelity: {"casts_shadows": sp.casts_shadows?}. On rebuild sp.casts_shadows = !!v. Wrap in begin/rescue — on some element types the setter returns false rather than raising, but defensive code costs nothing. |
| `casts_shadows=` | instance_method | Boolean — true if successful, false if unsuccessful | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Sets the casts-shadows flag. Note the return value is a SUCCESS flag, not the value you assigned. | Do not trust the return value as an echo of the input — it reports whether the assignment succeeded. |
| `receives_shadows?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Whether the element receives shadows. Inert on a SectionPlane. | {"receives_shadows": sp.receives_shadows?}; restore with sp.receives_shadows = !!v. |
| `receives_shadows=` | instance_method | Boolean — true if successful, false if unsuccessful | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Sets the receives-shadows flag; returns a success flag. | Same caveat as casts_shadows= — the return is success, not the assigned value. |
| `erase!` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Drawingelement. Deletes the section plane from its Entities collection. | Useful when rebuilding: clear pre-existing section planes in the destination model before importing, so you do not end up with duplicates. Always collect the planes into an Array FIRST, then erase — never erase while iterating an Entities collection. Prefer model.entities.erase_entities(array) for a single undo step. |
| `entityID` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. A session-scoped integer id, unique within the open model instance. It is NOT stable across save/reopen and is NOT portable to another model. | NEVER use entityID as the cross-model identity key. It is valid only for in-session bookkeeping (e.g. building a Hash of plane -> serialised record inside one export run). Store it if you like for debugging, but the reconstruction in the other model must key off the geometry + name + symbol + ordering. |
| `persistent_id` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2017 | direct | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. An id that IS stable across save/reopen within the same model (and across sessions), unlike entityID. Still not portable to a different model. | This is the right key to use for correlating your serialised JSON with the SOURCE model on a later run in that same model. It is a plain Integer, JSON-direct. It is NOT valid in the destination model — after rebuilding, write a mapping {source_persistent_id => new_persistent_id} into your attribute dictionary if you need traceability. Available SketchUp 2017+. |
| `parent` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition or Sketchup::Model | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. This is the DEFINITION, not an instance — a section plane inside a component that has five instances has exactly one parent definition and five world placements. | Critical for recursive enumeration. Serialise as {"parent": sp.parent.is_a?(Sketchup::ComponentDefinition) ? sp.parent.name : '<model>'} plus the definition's GUID if you need stronger identity (defn.guid). Use sp.parent.entities to get the Entities collection that owns the plane — that is the collection whose #active_section_plane governs whether this plane cuts. |

Always `next unless sp.valid?` before touching a cached SectionPlane reference. |
| `deleted?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Inverse of #valid? — true if the entity has been deleted. | Equivalent guard to !valid?. Present in older versions where valid? was unavailable. |

Prefer `is_a?(Sketchup::SectionPlane)` / `entities.grep(Sketchup::SectionPlane)` in code — typename string comparison is slower and locale-safe but brittle. |

| `inspect` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Ruby inspect form. | Debug only. |

SketchUp attribute values must be one of the supported primitives — Hashes are NOT supported, so store JSON as a String. |
| `set_attribute` | instance_method | Boolean, String, Float, Integer, Length, Array, Time, Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. | `delete_attribute` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Deletes a key, or the whole dictionary when key is omitted. | Use when re-importing to clear stale mapping attributes before rewriting them. |
| `add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Attaches a Sketchup::EntityObserver. | Runtime only. If you observe section planes to invalidate a cache, remember observers fire during model close — always guard with valid?. |
| `remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Detaches a previously attached observer. | Runtime only. |
| `add_section_plane` | instance_method | Sketchup::SectionPlane or nil | SketchUp 2014 | encode | Adds a section plane object to the entities. Three documented overloads, verbatim from the API stubs: (1) add_section_plane(point, vector) where point is a Geom::Point3d and vector is a Geom::Vector3d — TWO SEPARATE ARGUMENTS; (2) add_section_plane(plane) where plane is Array(Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d) — ONE argument, a two-element array; (3) add_section_plane(plane) where plane is Array(Integer, Integer, Integer, Integer) — the four plane coefficients [a, b, c, d]. The docs say 'Refer to the Geom module for information on how planes are represented.' The new plane is created in the coordinate system of the receiving Entities collection and is NOT activated automatically. Returns nil on failure (e.g. a zero-length normal vector). | THIS IS THE RECONSTRUCTION ENTRY POINT. Overload 1 with an explicit Point3d and Vector3d is the safest form for JSON round-trip because it makes the position unambiguous and avoids any question about the a/b/c/d scaling convention. Recommended rebuild: a,b,c,d = data['plane_abcd'].map(&:to_f); n = Geom::Vector3d.new(a,b,c); n.normalize!; pt = ORIGIN.offset(n, -d); sp = ents.add_section_plane(pt, n). ALWAYS nil-check the result. Coordinate arguments are interpreted as INCHES when given as bare numbers. The documented example passes plain numbers ([50, 50, 0], [1.0, 1.0, 0]) — those 50s are inches, not millimetres. Note overload 3's declared Integer types in the stubs are a documentation artefact; Floats are accepted and are what get_plane hands back. |
| `active_section_plane` | instance_method | Sketchup::SectionPlane or nil | SketchUp 2014 | encode | Accesses the currently active section plane in this Entities object. Returns nil if none is active in this collection. Each Entities collection (the model root's entities and each ComponentDefinition's entities) has its OWN independent active plane — this is why a model can show several simultaneous cuts. | Serialise as the identity of the plane, not the object: {"active_section_plane_pid": ents.active_section_plane&.persistent_id}. This is the single most important piece of section state to capture, because it is what a scene actually stores. To capture the FULL model cut state you must walk model.entities plus every model.definitions entry — see code_examples 'enumerate all section planes recursively' and 'read the whole active state'. |
| `active_section_plane=` | instance_method | Sketchup::SectionPlane or nil | SketchUp 2014 | encode | Sets the active section plane in this Entities object. Parameter sec_plane (Sketchup::SectionPlane, nil) — 'the SectionPlane object to be set active. Pass nil to deactivate any active section plane.' Returns the SectionPlane object that was set active. Setting nil deactivates any active plane in THIS collection only, not globally. | THE key write API for reconstructing scene section state. The SectionPlane you pass MUST be a member of this same Entities collection — passing a plane from a different collection is an error. Sequence for rebuilding a scene: (a) model.pages.selected_page = page; (b) set ents.active_section_plane for the model root and for EVERY definition; (c) page.use_section_planes = true; (d) page.update(PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES). Sketchup::Model#active_section_plane= does NOT exist — this setter is only on Entities. |
| `transform_entities` | instance_method | Boolean — false if the entities array was empty | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Applies a Geom::Transformation to several sub-entities at once. Works on SectionPlane objects (which have no transform! of their own). Documented coordinate-space rule, verbatim: 'If you are transforming entities in the active drawing context or any of its parent drawing contexts, the transformation will be interpreted as relative to the global coordinate system. Otherwise the transformation will be interpreted as being on the local coordinate system.' | The undo-friendly way to move/rotate an existing section plane without recomputing plane maths: ents.transform_entities(Geom::Transformation.new([100, 0, 0]), [sp]). Beware the active-drawing-context rule — it silently changes whether your transformation is world or local. For a deterministic import, prefer creating the plane fresh in world coordinates with add_section_plane rather than creating-then-transforming. |
| `erase_entities` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Erases one or more entities from the collection in a single operation. | Use to clear existing section planes in the destination model before import, in one undo step: ents.erase_entities(ents.grep(Sketchup::SectionPlane)). Collect first, erase second. |
| `grep` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::SectionPlane> | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Enumerable#grep, available because Sketchup::Entities includes Enumerable. The canonical, fast idiom for finding section planes in one collection. Sketchup::Entities#grep is optimised natively in modern SketchUp and is considerably faster than entities.select { |e| e.is_a?(...) }. | entities.grep(Sketchup::SectionPlane) is your per-collection primitive. It does NOT recurse into groups/components — you must walk model.definitions or recurse yourself. |
| `parent` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition or Sketchup::Model | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Returns the ComponentDefinition or Model that owns this Entities collection. The inverse of definition.entities / model.entities. | Use it to label which collection an active_section_plane record belongs to: key = ents.parent.is_a?(Sketchup::Model) ? '<model>' : ents.parent.name. Group#entities.parent returns the group's ComponentDefinition (groups are components under the hood). |
| `active_section_planes` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::SectionPlane> | SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | NEW IN SKETCHUP 2026.0. 'Returns all of the active section planes in the model.' A single call that replaces the pre-2026 idiom of walking model.entities plus every definition's entities and collecting each collection's active_section_plane. Read-only — there is no corresponding setter. | THIS IS YOUR 2026 FAST PATH for capturing model-wide cut state. Documented legacy equivalent for SketchUp 2024 and older, verbatim from the docs: entities_collections = [Sketchup.active_model.entities] + model.definitions.map(&:entities); section_planes = entities_collections.map(&:active_section_plane).compact. Serialise as an array of persistent_ids. Since there is no setter, reconstruction still goes through Entities#active_section_plane= per collection. |
| `active_section_plane (Model)` | instance_method | n/a |  | no | NEGATIVE FINDING, verified against the official Sketchup::Model docs page and the ruby-api-stubs Model class: Sketchup::Model defines NEITHER #active_section_plane NOR #active_section_plane= (singular). The only section-related member on Model is the plural, read-only #active_section_planes added in SketchUp 2026.0. The singular getter/setter pair exists only on Sketchup::Entities. | If your code calls model.active_section_plane it will raise NoMethodError on every SketchUp version. Use model.entities.active_section_plane (root collection) or model.active_entities.active_section_plane (currently-open context) instead. For the setter always use some_entities.active_section_plane = sp. |
| `rendering_options (Model)` | instance_method | Sketchup::RenderingOptions | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The model-level RenderingOptions object. This is where the global (current-view) values of DisplaySectionPlanes, DisplaySectionCuts, SectionCutFilled, SectionCutWidth, SectionCutDrawEdges and the four Section*Color keys live. | Read/write with ['Key'] and ['Key'] = value. This is the object you must set to make imported section cuts actually visible. Note that when a page has use_rendering_options? == true, selecting that page OVERWRITES the model rendering options with the page's own — so setting model.rendering_options and then selecting a style-carrying page silently reverts your change. Set section display on the PAGE's rendering_options too, or after selecting the page. |
| `pages` | instance_method | Sketchup::Pages | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The scenes collection. Sketchup::Pages#add(name) creates a page; as of SketchUp 2026.0, 'when adding a new Sketchup::Page, if the specified name is already taken, it automatically modifies the name to ensure it is unique.' Pages#selected_page / #selected_page= read and set the current scene. | model.pages.selected_page = page is REQUIRED before Page#update, because update snapshots the CURRENT view state. Wrap page iteration with model.options['PageOptions']['ShowTransition'] = false to suppress animated transitions (restore the previous value afterwards) — otherwise iterating pages is extremely slow and visually jarring. Note the 2026 auto-rename means the page name you get back may differ from the name you asked for; always read page.name after add. |
| `definitions` | instance_method | Sketchup::DefinitionList | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | All component definitions in the model, INCLUDING the auto-generated definitions that back every Group and every Image. Section planes nested inside groups/components live in definition.entities. | The flat-scan idiom for finding every section plane regardless of nesting: model.definitions.flat_map { |d| d.entities.grep(Sketchup::SectionPlane) } plus model.entities.grep(Sketchup::SectionPlane). This is O(definitions) rather than O(instances) and finds every plane exactly once, but it gives you DEFINITION-LOCAL planes with no world transform. Skip image definitions (d.image?) and consider skipping unused definitions (d.count_used_instances == 0) — a section plane inside an unplaced definition exists but is never drawn. |
| `entities (Model)` | instance_method | Sketchup::Entities | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The root/model-level Entities collection. Its #active_section_plane is the top-level cut. | Distinct from #active_entities. For serialisation you almost always want #entities (the deterministic root), not #active_entities (whatever the user happens to have open). |
| `active_entities` | instance_method | Sketchup::Entities | SketchUp 6.0 | no | The Entities collection of the group/component currently being edited, or the model's root entities if nothing is open. | DANGEROUS for export code — the result depends on user state. The SectionPlane doc examples use active_entities, but for a deterministic exporter use model.entities and walk model.definitions. Close open contexts first with model.close_active (repeatedly) if you need a clean root. |
| `active_path` | instance_method | Array of instances, or nil | SketchUp 2016 | READ-ONLY | The chain of instances the user currently has open. Settable (SketchUp 2020+) to programmatically open a nesting. | Record it so you can restore the user's editing context after your export/import operation. nil means nothing is open. |
| `start_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Opens a single undoable operation. Everything between start_operation and commit_operation becomes one undo step. | MANDATORY around any section-plane rebuild. Pass disable_ui = true (second arg) for a large import — it suppresses redraw and makes the operation dramatically faster. Always pair with commit_operation, and abort_operation in a rescue. Note: model.pages.selected_page= inside an operation can behave oddly; do page selection OUTSIDE the operation where practical. |
| `use_section_planes?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Verbatim: 'The use_section_planes? method determines whether you are storing the section planes property with the page.' Returns 'true if you are storing the this property with the page, false if you are not storing this property with the page.' This corresponds to the 'Active Section Planes' checkbox in the Scenes panel. | Plain Boolean, JSON-direct. This is the FLAG, not the data. When false, the page does not override section state at all — selecting it leaves whatever cuts were already active untouched, and Page#active_section_planes returns nil. Always serialise this alongside the plane ids, and always set it true BEFORE calling page.update(PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES) or the update has nothing to write into. |
| `use_section_planes=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Verbatim: 'The use_section_planes= method sets the page's section planes property.' Parameter setting (Boolean) — 'true if you want your page to save this property, false if you do not want your page to save this property.' | Order matters on reconstruction: (1) select the page, (2) set each Entities collection's active_section_plane, (3) page.use_section_planes = true, (4) page.update(PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES). Setting the flag to false does NOT erase the stored planes in all versions — it just stops the page applying them; re-enabling can resurrect stale state, so always follow a true with an explicit update. |
| `active_section_planes (Page)` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::SectionPlane> or nil | SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | NEW IN SKETCHUP 2026.0. Verbatim: 'The #active_section_planes method is used to retrieve the active section plane for the Sketchup::Page.' Returns nil if the page does not use section planes (i.e. use_section_planes? is false). This is the method that finally lets you read a page's stored section state WITHOUT selecting the page. | THE headline 2026 feature for this task. Pre-2026 you had to do model.pages.selected_page = page and then read every Entities collection's active_section_plane, which is slow and visually disruptive. Serialise as an array of persistent_ids. Reverse lookup — which scenes use a given section plane — becomes: section.model.pages.select { |p| p.active_section_planes&.include?(section) }. Mind the nil: use &.include? or `(p.active_section_planes || [])`. There is no setter; writing still goes through Entities#active_section_plane= plus Page#update. |
| `update` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Verbatim: 'The #update method performs an update on the page properties based on the current view that the user has. What properties of the Page get updated are controlled via an integer whose bits corresponds to different properties. These flags can be used individually or combined using bitwise OR.' Parameter flags (Integer) — 'The bitwise OR of the bit flags.' It SNAPSHOTS the model's current state into the page — it does not take the state as an argument. | THIS IS THE BAKE STEP. Because update snapshots the CURRENT model state, the mandatory sequence is: model.pages.selected_page = page → mutate the model into the state you want → page.update(flags). Documented example verbatim: flags = PAGE_USE_CAMERA | PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO | PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY; status = page.update(flags). CRITICAL GOTCHA confirmed on the SketchUp forum: a page update with PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES 'saves the settings for ALL of the entities objects that have section planes' — it is model-wide, not scoped to one plane, so you must have EVERY collection's active plane set correctly before you call it, or you will bake the wrong state for the planes you were not thinking about. |

The LayerFolder overload was added in SketchUp 2021.0. | ANSWERING THE DIRECT QUESTION: Page#set_visibility has NO direct role for section planes — it accepts ONLY a Sketchup::Layer or Sketchup::LayerFolder and will not accept a SectionPlane. Its relevance is INDIRECT but real: a SectionPlane is a Drawingelement with a #layer, so if you assign the plane to a tag and then call page.set_visibility(that_tag, false), the plane becomes invisible on that scene and stops cutting — even if it is still that collection's active_section_plane. This is the standard production technique for driving section cuts from tags. The API for per-page visibility of the section plane ENTITY itself is Page#set_drawingelement_visibility, which is a different method. |
| `get_drawingelement_visibility` | instance_method | Boolean — true if visible, false if not | SketchUp 2020.0 | direct | Verbatim: 'The #get_drawingelement_visibility method is used to get the visibility of a drawing element on a particular page.' Serialise as a per-page map: {"element_visibility": { "<section_plane_pid>": page.get_drawingelement_visibility(sp) }}. Returns a plain Boolean, JSON-direct. |
| `set_drawingelement_visibility` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.0 | encode | Verbatim: 'The #set_drawingelement_visibility method is used to change the visibility of a drawing element on a particular page. Parameters: element (Sketchup::Drawingelement), visibility (Boolean). | THE CORRECT API for per-scene section-plane visibility (not set_visibility). Read the scope restriction carefully: it works for a SectionPlane sitting at the MODEL ROOT. A section plane nested inside a group/component is NOT individually controllable per page — only the enclosing Group/ComponentInstance is. That is a hard architectural constraint on your exporter: if you need per-scene control of a nested plane, either flatten it to the root on import, or put it on its own tag and drive it with Page#set_visibility. Requires the page's corresponding use_hidden_* flag to be enabled, otherwise the override is not stored. |
| `hidden_entities` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::Drawingelement> or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Verbatim: 'Returns the drawing elements whose visibility is overridden by this page. | `layers` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::Layer> or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Verbatim: 'The #layers method retrieves layers that don't use their default visibility on this page.' For a robust JSON round-trip do not store this delta; store the ABSOLUTE map instead: model.layers.map { |l| [l.display_name, page.get_visibility(l)] } if available, or reconstruct by iterating all layers and calling set_visibility for each. | `layer_folders` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::LayerFolder> or nil | SketchUp 2021.0 | encode | Retrieves the hidden layer folders associated with a page. | Same delta semantics and nil-check as #layers. | `use_hidden_layers?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Determines whether you are storing the hidden layers property with the page. | `use_hidden_layers=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Sets the page's hidden layers property. | Write side of the 'Visible Tags' checkbox. |
| `use_hidden_geometry?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.1 | direct | Returns the use-hidden-geometry property from the page — whether the page stores the hidden state of loose geometry (edges, faces, and other non-instance drawing elements). | A root-level SectionPlane is a loose (non-instance) drawing element, so this is the flag that most plausibly governs whether a per-page hidden override on a section plane is stored. | `use_hidden_geometry=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.1 | encode | Sets the page's use-hidden-geometry property. | Pair with PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY in the update flags. |
| `use_hidden_objects?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.1 | direct | Returns the use-hidden-objects property from the page — whether the page stores the hidden state of ComponentInstance, Group and Image objects. | This is the flag that matters when a section plane is nested inside a group/component: the plane itself cannot be hidden per page, but its enclosing Group/ComponentInstance can, which hides the plane with it. |
| `use_hidden_objects=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.1 | encode | Sets the page's use-hidden-objects property. | Pair with PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS in the update flags. |
| `use_hidden?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | DEPRECATED. 'The functionality is replaced by use_hidden_geometry? and use_hidden_objects? in SketchUp 2020.1.' | `use_hidden=` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | DEPRECATED. Replaced by use_hidden_geometry= and use_hidden_objects= in SketchUp 2020.1. | Legacy write path only. |

This is the default when Page#update is called with no argument. | page.update with no arguments == page.update(PAGE_USE_ALL). Convenient but BLUNT: it re-snapshots camera, style, shadows, axes, hidden state, tags, section planes and environment all at once from the current view. In an importer that is usually what you want (you have just set every one of those). In a surgical update it is dangerous — pass explicit flags instead. Value 4095 = 0b111111111111, i.e. twelve bits set, which is why newer flags fit inside it. |
| `PAGE_NO_CAMERA` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Bit flag combination — everything in PAGE_USE_ALL except the camera bit (4095 - 1 = 4094). | Useful when you want to bake section state without disturbing a carefully-set camera. Not listed in the current Page#update doc block but present as a top-level constant. |
| `DisplaySectionPlanes` | rendering_option_key | Boolean (TrueClass/FalseClass) | SketchUp 2014 | direct | Controls whether the section plane MARKERS (the grey/orange rectangular grips with the symbol label) are drawn. The documented add_section_plane example sets it: model.rendering_options['DisplaySectionPlanes'] = true. | Plain Boolean, JSON-direct both ways. Exact spelling: capital D, capital S, capital P, plural 'Planes'. Set on model.rendering_options for the live view, and on page.rendering_options (with use_rendering_options = true) for per-scene. Typical presentation scenes set this FALSE and DisplaySectionCuts TRUE — you get the cut without the marker clutter. |
| `DisplaySectionCuts` | rendering_option_key | Boolean (TrueClass/FalseClass) | SketchUp 2015 | direct | Controls whether the active section planes actually CUT the model. THE most common reason an imported scene shows no section: the plane is active but this key is false in the destination model or in the destination page's stored style. Set it on BOTH model.rendering_options and every reconstructed page.rendering_options. |
| `SectionCutWidth` | rendering_option_key | Integer (screen pixels; SketchUp's UI exposes roughly 1–5) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The line weight, in pixels, used to draw the section cut outline. | `SectionCutFilled` | rendering_option_key | Boolean (TrueClass/FalseClass) | SketchUp 2018 | direct | Whether the section cut face is filled with a solid colour (the 'Section Fill' feature). | `SectionDefaultCutColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The colour used to draw the section cut LINE (the outline where geometry is sliced). Decode with ro['SectionDefaultCutColor'] = Sketchup::Color.new(r, g, b, a), or pass the array directly (SketchUp coerces [r,g,b] / [r,g,b,a] arrays to Color). Do NOT round-trip via the hex string form — alpha is lost. Exact spelling: SectionDefaultCutColor. |
| `SectionDefaultFillColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 2018 | encode | The fill colour applied to the section cut face when SectionCutFilled is true. | Same encoding as SectionDefaultCutColor: store .to_a as [r,g,b,a] Integers. Exact spelling: SectionDefaultFillColor. |
| `SectionActiveColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The colour of the section plane MARKER when that plane is the active (cutting) one of its Entities collection. Exact spelling: SectionActiveColor (no 'Default' in this one — contrast with SectionDefaultCutColor). |
| `SectionInactiveColor` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The colour of the section plane MARKER when that plane is NOT active. | `plane (4-coefficient form)` | constant | Array of 4 Numeric | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | One of the two plane representations the API accepts. Documented example: plane2 = [0, 0, 1, 0] — the XY plane with normal +Z through the origin. The equation form is Ax + By + Cz + D = 0. This is the form SectionPlane#get_plane and Geom.fit_plane_to_points return. | THE FORM TO STORE IN JSON. Four plain Floats, no coercion needed, fully lossless. Sign convention, confirmed on the SketchUp forum: substituting the origin into the equation gives d, so d is the SIGNED distance of the origin from the plane along the normal; 'the distance d can be negative, indicating that the origin is behind the plane.' Therefore the point on the plane closest to the origin is -d * n. Worked check against the official example add_section_plane([50, 50, 0], [1.0, 1.0, 0]): n normalises to (0.70711, 0.70711, 0), d = -(n · p) = -70.711, and indeed the origin lies behind that plane. Units for d are INCHES. |
| `plane (point-and-vector form)` | constant | Array of [Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d] | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | The other accepted plane representation. Accepted by SectionPlane#set_plane and by Entities#add_section_plane (both as a single two-element array and as two separate arguments). NEVER returned by get_plane. | NOT JSON-safe as-is — Point3d#to_a returns Length objects, which JSON.generate serialises as strings like "50.0\"" and which will NOT parse back into numbers. RECOMMENDATION: store the 4-coefficient form (which is already Floats) and reconstruct the point/vector form at build time — that is both lossless and unambiguous. Use this form for the actual add_section_plane call because it makes position explicit. |
| `Geom.fit_plane_to_points` | class_method | Array of 4 Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Computes the best-fit plane through three or more points and returns it in the 4-coefficient form. | THE ROBUST WAY TO TRANSFORM A PLANE. Instead of transforming the normal directly (which is only correct for rigid and uniform-scale transformations, because normals transform by the inverse-transpose), pick three non-collinear points on the plane, transform each with Point3d#transform, and refit. CAVEAT: fit_plane_to_points does not guarantee which of the two normal directions you get — always re-orient the result to match the transformed normal's direction (dot product < 0 ⇒ negate all four coefficients), or your section will cut the wrong half of the model. |

### Sketchup::Axes, Layer, Layers and LayerFolder  —  129 entries

| Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |

| `Sketchup::Model#axes` | instance_method | Sketchup::Axes | SketchUp 2016 | encode | Returns the drawing axes for the model. Official example: `tr = Sketchup.active_model.axes.transformation`. | READ-ONLY ACCESSOR — there is NO Sketchup::Model#axes= setter. To change the model axes you mutate the returned object: `model.axes.set(origin, xaxis, yaxis, zaxis)`. Serialise via `model.axes.to_a` -> encode as {"origin":[x,y,z],"xaxis":[x,y,z],"yaxis":[x,y,z],"zaxis":[x,y,z]} with Float inches. Restore in model B with `model_b.axes.set(Geom::Point3d.new(*o), Geom::Vector3d.new(*xa), Geom::Vector3d.new(*ya), Geom::Vector3d.new(*za))`. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#origin` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d | SketchUp 2016 | encode | "The #origin method returns the origin of the axes." Official example: `point = Sketchup.active_model.axes.origin`. | Geom::Point3d is not JSON-native. Encode with `pt.to_a.map(&:to_f)` -> [x, y, z] Floats in INCHES (SketchUp's internal unit). Decode with `Geom::Point3d.new(*arr)`. There is NO #origin= — origin can only be changed through #set. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#xaxis` | instance_method | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 2016 | encode | "The #xaxis method returns the x axis of the axes." | Encode with `vec.to_a.map(&:to_f)` -> [x, y, z]. Decode with `Geom::Vector3d.new(*arr)`. Read-only; no #xaxis= setter. Vectors returned are unit-length. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#yaxis` | instance_method | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 2016 | encode | "The #yaxis method returns the y axis of the axes." | Encode/decode identically to #xaxis. Read-only; no setter. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#zaxis` | instance_method | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 2016 | encode | "The #zaxis method returns the z axis of the axes." | Encode/decode identically to #xaxis. Read-only; no setter. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#axes` | instance_method | Array of three Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 2016 | encode | "The axes method returns the vectors representing the directions of the axes." Official example: `xaxis, yaxis, zaxis = Sketchup.active_model.axes.axes`. | Convenience triple. Encode as `axes.axes.map { |v| v.to_a.map(&:to_f) }`. Note the confusing name — Sketchup::Axes#axes. It does NOT include the origin; use #to_a for that. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#to_a` | instance_method | Array of one Geom::Point3d then three Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 2016 | encode | "The axes method returns the origin and vectors representing the axes." Official example: `point, xaxis, yaxis, zaxis = Sketchup.active_model.axes.to_a`. | THE method to use for capture — it returns exactly the four arguments #set consumes, in the same order. Encode: `axes.to_a.map { |e| e.to_a.map(&:to_f) }` -> [[ox,oy,oz],[xx,xy,xz],[yx,yy,yz],[zx,zy,zz]]. Restore: `o, x, y, z = json; target.axes.set(Geom::Point3d.new(*o), Geom::Vector3d.new(*x), Geom::Vector3d.new(*y), Geom::Vector3d.new(*z))`. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#set` | instance_method | Sketchup::Axes (returns self) | SketchUp 2016 | no | "The #set method allows the axes to be manipulated. The axes must always be orthogonal, otherwise an error is thrown." Official example: `xaxis = Geom::Vector3d.new(3, 5, 0); yaxis = xaxis * Z_AXIS; Sketchup.active_model.axes.set([10,0,0], xaxis, yaxis, Z_AXIS)`. | ARGUMENT ORDER IS STRICTLY (origin, xaxis, yaxis, zaxis) — origin FIRST, then X, Y, Z. This is the ONLY mutator on Sketchup::Axes and the only way to write model or page axes. Accepts duck-typed arrays too (`[10,0,0]` works for the Point3d, and Geom::Vector3d for the axes; the official example mixes an Array origin with Vector3d axes). The three vectors MUST be mutually orthogonal or an ArgumentError is raised — if your captured floats have drifted, re-orthogonalise before calling: `z = x * y; y = z * x` (Vector3d#* is cross product). Works on both `model.axes` and `page.axes`. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#transformation` | instance_method | Geom::Transformation | SketchUp 2016 | encode | "The transformation method returns the transformation of the axes. This is useful when creating tools that respect the model's drawing axes." | Read-only, derived from origin + the three vectors. Encode as `tr.to_a` -> a flat Array of 16 Floats (column-major 4x4). Reconstruct with `Geom::Transformation.new(array_of_16)`. For scene serialisation prefer #to_a — storing the transformation as well is redundant, though it is a useful cross-check/validation field. |
| `Sketchup::Axes#sketch_plane` | instance_method | Array of 4 Floats (plane equation coefficients a, b, c, d) | SketchUp 2016 | direct | "The sketch_plane method returns a plane representing the ground plane of the axes." Official example: `plane = Sketchup.active_model.axes.sketch_plane`. | Returns "an array of 4 numbers that give the coefficients of a plane equation" — plain Floats, so it serialises to JSON directly. READ-ONLY and DERIVED: there is no way to set it, and restoring the axes via #set regenerates it automatically. Store it only as a diagnostic; never try to restore from it. |
| `Sketchup::Page#axes` | instance_method | Sketchup::Axes | SketchUp 6.0 (Axes object return since 2016) | encode | "The axes method returns the drawing axes for the page." Official example: `page.axes.set(origin, Y_AXIS, X_AXIS, Z_AXIS)`. Docs carry the note: "Since SketchUp 2026.0, modifying the Axes, Camera, RenderingOptions, and ShadowInfo properties of a page is an undoable operation and should be wrapped between Model#start_operation and Model#commit_operation." | THIS is where per-scene axes live. There is NO Page#axes= — you get the page's Axes object and call #set on it. Capture with `page.axes.to_a`; restore with `page_b.axes.set(...)`. CRITICAL FOR 2026: wrap the restore in model.start_operation / model.commit_operation or the change may not register correctly in the undo stack. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_axes?` | instance_method | Boolean (true/false) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "Determines whether you are storing the axes property with the page." (Stub comment: "Determines whether axes property is stored with the page.") | This is the ON/OFF switch that binds axes to a scene — it corresponds to the 'Axes Location' checkbox in the Scenes manager. If false, the scene does NOT restore axes when activated and `page.axes` is meaningless for restoration purposes. Serialise as a plain JSON boolean under e.g. "use_axes". On restore, set `page.use_axes = json["use_axes"]` and only call `page.axes.set(...)` when it is true. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_axes=` | instance_method | Boolean parameter | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Sets whether the axes property is stored with the page. (Stub comment: "Sets the page's axes property.") | Set this to true BEFORE calling `page.axes.set(...)` on a newly created page, otherwise the page will not retain the axes. Order matters: `page.use_axes = true; page.axes.set(o, x, y, z)`. |
| `Sketchup::Page#update` | instance_method | Integer bitfield parameter, Boolean return | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The {#update} method performs an update on the page properties based on the current view that the user has. What properties of the Page get updated are controlled via an integer whose bits corresponds to different properties. These flags can be used individually or combined using bitwise OR." | An ALTERNATIVE restore path that is usually WRONG for cross-model reconstruction: it copies the CURRENT model/view state onto the page, so you would have to set model.axes and every tag's visible? first, then call page.update(PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS | PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY). This is actually a robust technique when direct setters misbehave. Full documented flag list: PAGE_USE_CAMERA (Camera Location), PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS (Drawing Style), PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO (Shadow Setting), PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS (Axes Location), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN (Hidden Geometry & Objects, SU 2019 and older), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY (SU 2020 and later), PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS (SU 2020 and later), PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY (Visible Layers), PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES (Active Section Planes), PAGE_USE_ALL (All possible scene properties), PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT (Environment settings). |
| `PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Axes Location'. Note the historic name — SKETCHCS = 'sketch coordinate system' — it is the AXES flag, not a style flag. | Value 8 (0x0008). Top-level global constant (defined on Object), so reference it bare as PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS, not Sketchup::Page::PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS. Used with Page#update and as the `flags` argument to Pages#add. Do NOT hardcode 8 in shipped code if you can avoid it; reference the constant. |
| `PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Visible Layers' (i.e. per-scene tag visibility). | Value 32 (0x0020). Top-level global constant. Pair with Page#update to snapshot the current model tag visibility onto a page. |
| `PAGE_USE_CAMERA` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Camera Location'. | Value 1 (0x0001). Top-level global constant. |
| `PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Drawing Style'. | Value 2 (0x0002). Top-level global constant. |
| `PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Shadow Setting'. | Value 4 (0x0004). Note the spelling — SHADOWINFO is one word, no underscore between SHADOW and INFO. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Hidden Geometry & Objects (SU 2019 and older)'. | Value 16 (0x0010). Superseded in SU 2020 by PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY and PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS but still defined. |
| `PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Active Section Planes'. | Value 64 (0x0040). Top-level global constant. |
| `PAGE_USE_ALL` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'All possible scene properties'. Also the DEFAULT value of the `flags` argument to Sketchup::Pages#add. | Value 4095 (0x0FFF). This is the default second argument of Pages#add, so a page created with `pages.add('Name')` has every use_* flag switched on. |
| `PAGE_NO_CAMERA` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Page flag meaning all properties except camera (PAGE_USE_ALL with bit 1 cleared). | Value 4094 (0x0FFE). Useful as `pages.add(name, PAGE_NO_CAMERA)` when you intend to write the camera yourself. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2020.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Hidden Geometry (SU 2020 and later)'. | Integer value is NOT published in the Ruby API docs or the 2014 constants guide. Resolve it at runtime rather than hardcoding: `Object.const_defined?(:PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY) ? PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY : 0`. Top-level global constant. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2020.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Hidden Objects (SU 2020 and later)'. | Integer value not published; resolve at runtime via Object.const_defined?. Top-level global constant. |
| `PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | Page update flag meaning 'Environment settings'. | Integer value not published; resolve at runtime. Guard its use — it does not exist before SU 2025. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#name` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The #name method is used to retrieve the internal name of the layer which is handled by the model." | THE PRIMARY CROSS-MODEL KEY. Names are unique within a model's Layers collection. Serialise as a plain JSON string. When reconstructing in another model, look up with `model.layers[name]` and create with `model.layers.add(name)` if nil. Encoding note: names may contain any UTF-8; JSON handles this, but be careful with any code that round-trips through attribute dictionaries. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#name=` | instance_method | String parameter | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The #name= method is used to set the name of a layer." | Renaming to a name already used by another layer is not silently unique-ified in the way Page#name= is — check with Layers#[] first, or use Layers#unique_name. Renaming the default 'Layer0'/'Untagged' layer should be avoided. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#display_name` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2020.0 | READ-ONLY | "The #display_name method is used to retrieve the display name of the layer. This is the name shown to the user in the SketchUp UI." | READ-ONLY, no display_name= setter. Differs from #name only for the default layer: internally it is always 'Layer0' but SketchUp 2020+ displays it as 'Untagged'. SERIALISE #name, NEVER #display_name — using display_name as the lookup key will fail to match the default layer in a target model (`model.layers['Untagged']` returns nil; `model.layers['Layer0']` works). |
| `Sketchup::Layer#visible?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The #visible? method is used to determine if the layer is visible." | This is the CURRENT MODEL-WIDE visibility, NOT a per-scene value. It reflects whatever scene is presently active. Do not mistake it for scene state — for that use Page#layers / Page#set_visibility. Serialise as a boolean if you want to also restore the model's live state; per-scene data must come from the Page. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#visible=` | instance_method | Boolean parameter | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The #visible= method is used to set if the layer is visible." | Sets the live model-wide visibility. Known API bug (api-issue-tracker #945): setting `model.active_layer.visible = false` hides the active layer AND changes which layer is active. Guard by never hiding model.active_layer, or reset model.active_layer afterwards. Useful in the Page#update restore strategy: set every layer's visible= to the desired scene state, then `page.update(PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY)`. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#page_behavior` | instance_method | Integer (bitfield) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The {#page_behavior} method is used to retrieve the visibility behavior of the layer for new pages and existing pages. For example, you may want your layer to be visible or hidden by default in any new pages (aka Scenes) created by the user." The docs add: "The default visibility for a layer is set by either LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT or LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT. This is what will be used when a page does not contain the visibility state of a layer." | A PLAIN INTEGER — serialises to JSON directly, no encoding needed. It carries TWO independent pieces of information ORed together: (a) the DEFAULT-VISIBILITY bit, tested as `(pb & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT) == LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT` i.e. `(pb & 1) == 1`; and (b) the NEW-PAGE behaviour bits, tested as `(pb & LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES) != 0` (16) or `(pb & LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES) != 0` (32); if neither of those bits is set the layer 'uses default visibility on new pages' (LAYER_USES_DEFAULT_VISIBILITY_ON_NEW_PAGES == 0). YOU MUST CAPTURE THIS to decode Page#layers correctly, because Page#layers is a delta against the default-visibility bit. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#page_behavior=` | instance_method | Integer parameter (bitfield) | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The {#page_behavior=} method is used to control the layer's visibility behavior on existing and new pages. When you Update a page (as opposed to creating a new page) the current visibility of the layer is used." Docs note: "The behavior is composed of a combination of these flags" combined with bitwise OR. | Restore with the exact integer you captured: `layer.page_behavior = json['page_behavior']`. Typical constructed values: `LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT | LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES` == 33; `LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT | LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES` == 16; plain 0 means visible-by-default and uses-default-on-new-pages. IMPORTANT ORDERING: set page_behavior= BEFORE calling Page#set_visibility on that layer, because set_visibility computes its stored delta relative to the default bit. KNOWN BUG (api-issue-tracker #457, confirmed in 16/17/18/20 and still reported since): setting page_behavior= on a freshly created layer does not propagate to existing pages until a scene refresh; workaround is `pages = model.pages; pages.selected_page = pages.selected_page`. |
| `LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | page_behavior bit: the layer is visible by default — this is what is used when a page does not contain the visibility state of the layer. | VALUE IS 0 (0x0000). Because it is zero you CANNOT test for it with a bitwise AND — `pb & LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT` is always 0. Test the negative instead: `visible_by_default = (pb & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT) == 0`. Top-level global constant (bare name, defined on Object); the docs list it under Sketchup::Layer but you reference it unqualified. |
| `LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | page_behavior bit: the layer is hidden by default — used when a page does not contain the visibility state of the layer. | VALUE IS 1 (0x0001). THE most important constant for decoding Page#layers. Test: `hidden_by_default = (layer.page_behavior & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT) == LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT`. Top-level global constant. |
| `LAYER_USES_DEFAULT_VISIBILITY_ON_NEW_PAGES` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | page_behavior bit: on newly created pages the layer takes whatever its default visibility bit says. | VALUE IS 0 (0x0000). Note the exact spelling — VISIBILITY (not VISIBLE) and ON_NEW_PAGES. Again zero-valued, so detect it as the absence of the other two: `uses_default_on_new = (pb & (LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES | LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES)) == 0`. Top-level global constant. |
| `LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | page_behavior bit: the layer will be VISIBLE on any newly created page regardless of its default visibility bit. | VALUE IS 16 (0x0010). Note spelling: LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES — 'IS' is present here but absent from LAYER_VISIBLE_BY_DEFAULT. Test: `(pb & LAYER_IS_VISIBLE_ON_NEW_PAGES) != 0`. Top-level global constant. |
| `LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | page_behavior bit: the layer will be HIDDEN on any newly created page regardless of its default visibility bit. | VALUE IS 32 (0x0020). Test: `(pb & LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES) != 0`. Combined idiom from the docs and forums: `layer.page_behavior = (LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT | LAYER_IS_HIDDEN_ON_NEW_PAGES)` (== 33). Top-level global constant. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#folder` | instance_method | Sketchup::LayerFolder or nil | SketchUp 2021.0 | encode | "The #folder method is used to return the parent layer folder of a layer." Returns nil if the layer is a direct child of the layer manager. | Do NOT serialise the LayerFolder object. Serialise the FOLDER PATH as an Array<String> by walking up: `path = []; f = layer.folder; while f; path.unshift(f.name); f = f.folder; end` -> ["Site", "Existing"] or [] for top level. Rebuild in the target model by walking that array with Layers#folders / LayerFolder#folders lookups and Layers#add_folder / LayerFolder#add_folder creates. Guard the whole thing with `layer.respond_to?(:folder)` for pre-2021 compatibility. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#folder=` | instance_method | Sketchup::LayerFolder or nil parameter | SketchUp 2021.0 | no | "The #folder= method is used to set the parent layer folder of a layer." | Pass nil to move the layer to the top level of the layer manager. Equivalent to LayerFolder#add_layer(layer). Use this when re-parenting a freshly created layer during reconstruction: `layer.folder = resolved_folder`. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#color` | instance_method | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 2014 | encode | "The #color method is used to retrieve the color of the layer." | Sketchup::Color is not JSON-native. Encode with `layer.color.to_a` -> "The returned array will contain 4 integer values (RGBA) between 0 and 255", e.g. [255, 128, 0, 255]. Decode with `Sketchup::Color.new(r, g, b, a)` or by assigning the array directly (`layer.color = [r,g,b,a]` is accepted). Purely cosmetic for scene restoration but worth carrying so recreated tags look right. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#color=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Color parameter | SketchUp 2014 | encode | "The #color= method is used to set the color of a layer." | Accepts a Sketchup::Color, an RGB/RGBA Array of Integers 0-255, a colour-name String (see Sketchup::Color.names), or a hex Integer such as 0xFF0000. Restore with `layer.color = Sketchup::Color.new(*json['color'])`. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#line_style` | instance_method | Sketchup::LineStyle or nil | SketchUp 2019 | encode | "The #line_style method retrieves the line style on this layer." Returns nil if no line style is set. | Serialise `layer.line_style && layer.line_style.name` -> a String or null. Sketchup::LineStyle only exposes #name. Restore by name via the model's line style collection: `ls = model.line_styles[json['line_style']]; layer.line_style = ls if ls`. Line style names are built-in and identical across models (e.g. "Solid Basic"), so name-based transfer is safe. Guard with `respond_to?(:line_style)` for pre-2019. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#line_style=` | instance_method | Sketchup::LineStyle or nil parameter; returns Sketchup::Layer | SketchUp 2019 | no | "The #line_style= method sets a specific line style to a layer." | Pass nil to clear the line style. Look the object up first with `Sketchup.active_model.line_styles['Solid Basic']` — you cannot assign a String. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#==` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The #== method is used to determine if two layers are the same." | Identity comparison within one model. Meaningless across models — never rely on it for cross-model matching; match on #name. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#<=>` | instance_method | Integer (-1, 0, or 1) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The #<=> method is used to compare two layers based on their names." | `Sketchup::Layer#persistent_id` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2017 | READ-ONLY | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. A per-entity id that is stable across save/load WITHIN one model. | TRAP: stable within a model only. It is NOT usable as a cross-model key — a layer with the same name in model B will have a different persistent_id. Store it only as an in-model back-reference if you also serialise the source model; the reconstruction key must be #name. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#entityID` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. A session-scoped id. | Changes every time the model is loaded. NEVER serialise this as an identity key. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#model` | instance_method | Sketchup::Model | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. The model the layer belongs to. | Useful assertion during cross-model work: verify `layer.model == target_model` before applying Page#set_visibility, otherwise you will silently operate on the wrong model. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#valid?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2014 | no | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. False once the layer has been deleted from the model. | Guard every cached Layer reference with `layer.valid?` before use — Layers#purge_unused or #remove invalidates references you may still be holding across a long serialisation loop. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#set_attribute` | instance_method | varies | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Stores an attribute on the layer itself. | An alternative to a model-level dictionary if you want per-tag metadata to travel with a tag. For your use case the model-level dictionary is the right home; note that layer attribute dictionaries do NOT survive a copy-paste of geometry between models — only the layer name does. |
| `Sketchup::Layer#get_attribute` | instance_method | varies | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Reads an attribute from the layer. | Attribute values are limited to a subset of types (String, Integer, Float, Boolean, Array of those, Length, Time, Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d, Sketchup::Color). A JSON String is the safest carrier — serialise your whole scene payload with JSON.generate and store one String. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#[]` | instance_method | Sketchup::Layer or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Retrieves a layer by zero-based Integer index or by String name. Returns nil if not found. | THE lookup used during reconstruction: `layer = model.layers[name] || model.layers.add(name)`. Name lookup matches the INTERNAL #name, so the default layer is found as 'Layer0', not 'Untagged'. Returns nil rather than raising when the name is absent — always handle nil. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#at` | instance_method | Sketchup::Layer or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Alias for #[]. | Identical behaviour to #[]. Present so the collection reads like a Ruby Array. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#add` | instance_method | Sketchup::Layer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The #add method is used to add a new layer." Also known as: #add_layer. | IDEMPOTENT BY NAME — if a layer with that name already exists, add returns the EXISTING layer rather than creating a duplicate. That makes `model.layers.add(name)` safe to call unconditionally during reconstruction. New layers are created at the top level of the layer manager (no folder); re-parent afterwards with Layer#folder=. See gotcha about page_behavior= on freshly added layers. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#add_layer` | instance_method | Sketchup::Layer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Documented alias of #add. | Exactly the same method. Older code uses this spelling; #add is the canonical name in current docs. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#add_folder` | instance_method | Sketchup::LayerFolder | SketchUp 2021.0 | no | Two overloads: given a String it creates a new top-level folder with that name; given an existing Sketchup::LayerFolder it MOVES that folder to be a top-level child of the layer manager. | NOT idempotent the way #add is — calling it twice with the same String can create two folders with the same name (folder names are not required unique the way layer names are). | `Sketchup::Layers#remove_folder` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2021.0 | no | "Removes the folder from the model." | `Sketchup::Layers#folders` | instance_method | Array of Sketchup::LayerFolder | SketchUp 2021.0 | encode | "Returns the folders of the layer manager." | `Sketchup::Layers#each_folder` | instance_method | yields Sketchup::LayerFolder | SketchUp 2021.0 | no | Iterates the DIRECT child folders of the layer manager. | EXACT SPELLING IS #each_folder. Non-recursive. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#layers` | instance_method | Array of Sketchup::Layer | SketchUp 2021.0 | encode | "Retrieves the layers not in a folder" — i.e. only the direct top-level children of the layer manager. | TRAP: this is NOT all layers. | `Sketchup::Layers#each` | instance_method | yields Sketchup::Layer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Iterates ALL layers in the model, including those nested inside folders. | `Sketchup::Layers#each_layer` | instance_method | yields Sketchup::Layer | SketchUp 2021.0 | no | Iterates only the layers NOT in a folder (the direct top-level children). | NOT an alias of #each — subtle and easy to get wrong. #each = everything; #each_layer = top-level only. | `Sketchup::Layers#count` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The number of layers in the collection." | Counts ALL layers including those in folders (it comes via Enumerable over #each). Because Enumerable is mixed in, `count` also accepts a block or argument form. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#length` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "Retrieves the number of layers." | Same total as #count. Cheaper than #count because it does not go through Enumerable. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#size` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Alias for #length. | Identical to #length. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#count_layers` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2021.0 | direct | "Number of layers not in a folder" — counts only direct top-level child layers. | Different from #count / #length / #size, which count everything. Matches #layers.size. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#count_folders` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2021.0 | direct | "Counts the number of folders which are direct children" of the layer manager. | Non-recursive. Matches #folders.size. A cheap 0-check to decide whether to emit folder data at all. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#remove` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Removes a layer from the model. Also known as: #remove_layer. The `layer` argument accepts a Sketchup::Layer, an Integer index, or a String name. If `remove_geometry` is true the entities on that layer are erased as well; if false (the default) they are moved to the default layer. | SECOND ARGUMENT DEFAULTS TO false — geometry is preserved. Do not call this during reconstruction unless you explicitly intend destructive tag cleanup; it silently reassigns geometry to Layer0. Cannot remove the default layer. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#remove_layer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Documented alias of #remove. | Identical to #remove. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#unique_name` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "Get a string that will be a unique layer name" in this model. Optional base_name argument; without it the base defaults to the localised default ('Layer' pre-2020, 'Tag' in 2020+). | Useful only if you deliberately want to avoid colliding with an existing tag. For scene reconstruction you normally want the OPPOSITE — to reuse the existing same-named tag — so use `layers[name] || layers.add(name)` instead. Note the default base string changed with the Layer->Tag rename, so do not depend on the no-argument form producing a predictable prefix. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#purge_unused` | instance_method | Integer (the Layers collection / count) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "Remove unused layers." Also known as: #purge_unused_layers. | DANGEROUS during scene reconstruction. A tag that exists only to carry per-scene visibility (no geometry assigned) counts as unused and WILL be purged, silently destroying the scene state you just rebuilt. Never call this between creating tags and applying Page#set_visibility. Also invalidates any Layer references you hold. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#purge_unused_layers` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Documented alias of #purge_unused. | Identical to #purge_unused. Same warning applies. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#purge_unused_folders` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2021.0 | no | "Remove all layer folder with no children." (Docs wording verbatim, including the grammar slip.) | Removes empty folders only. Same warning as purge_unused — do not run it mid-reconstruction, since folders are created before their layers are moved in. |
| `Sketchup::Layers#add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "Add an observer to the layers collection." Used with Sketchup::LayersObserver. | Relevant callbacks for tag work: onLayerAdded, onLayerRemoved, onLayerChanged, onCurrentLayerChanged, onRemoveAllLayers, and (2021+) onLayerFolderAdded, onLayerFolderRemoved, onLayerFolderChanged, onParentChanged. Decode with: `visible_on_page = page.layers.include?(layer) == hidden_by_default?(layer)` where `hidden_by_default?(l) = (l.page_behavior & LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT) == LAYER_HIDDEN_BY_DEFAULT`. PRECONDITIONS: (1) the layer must belong to the same model as the page — create it with Layers#add first; (2) `page.use_hidden_layers = true` must be set or the setting will not be retained; (3) set `layer.page_behavior=` BEFORE calling this, since changing the default bit afterwards flips the meaning of the stored delta. IMPORTANT: a newly added page snapshots the CURRENT model state (current axes, current tag visibility) — so after creating it you must explicitly overwrite: set use_axes/use_hidden_layers, then page.axes.set(...), then loop page.set_visibility(...). | Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |

| `attribute_dictionaries` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Retrieves the AttributeDictionaries collection attached to the entity. Documented @note: "The return value may be either +nil+ or an empty Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries collection if this entity has no Sketchup::AttributeDictionary s." Defined on Sketchup::Entity, therefore available on Sketchup::Model, Sketchup::Page, Sketchup::Layer, materials, edges, faces, groups and component definitions alike. | ALWAYS nil-guard: `dicts = model.attribute_dictionaries; return {} if dicts.nil?`. Do not assume a collection object exists. To enumerate every dictionary on the source model for export: `(model.attribute_dictionaries || []).each { |d| ... }`. Not itself serialisable to JSON — you walk it and build a Ruby Hash of {dict_name => {key => value}} then JSON-encode that. |
| `attribute_dictionary` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionary or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Retrieves an attribute dictionary with a given name attached to an Entity. @param [String] name — the name of the attribute dictionary. @param [Boolean] create — "boolean, if set to true then the attribute dictionary will be created if it does not exist." Returns an AttributeDictionary object if successful, or nil if there is no attribute dictionary. | Exact arg name is `create` and it defaults to FALSE. Read path: `model.attribute_dictionary('NA_SceneExporter')` (no create — returns nil cleanly). Write path: `model.attribute_dictionary('NA_SceneExporter', true)` to get-or-create. Creating a dictionary is a model change, so it must be inside a start_operation block. |
| `set_attribute` | instance_method | Object (returns the newly set value) | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "The set attribute is used to set the value of an attribute in an attribute dictionary with the given name.
