Documented note: "a bug prior to SketchUp 2015 would corrupt the model if the key is an empty string. This also includes values that will evaluate to empty strings, such as nil." | PRIMARY WRITE CALL for the scene serialiser. Exact arg names dict_name, key, value. Never pass an empty-string key. Value must be one of the ten documented types (see AttributeDictionary#[]= entry) — pass a JSON String: `model.set_attribute('NA_SceneExporter', 'scenes_v1', payload_hash.to_json)`. On Sketchup::Entity there is a further documented note that Geom::Point3d / Geom::Vector3d array values stored as VERTEX attributes are automatically transformed when the vertex is moved — irrelevant for model-level scene storage but a trap if you ever attach to vertices. |
| `get_attribute` | instance_method | Object (whatever was stored) or default_value | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The #get_attribute method is used to retrieve the value of an attribute in the entity's attribute dictionary. If default_value is provided and there is no matching attribute it returns the given value. It does not create an attribute with that name though." | Exact third arg name on Entity is `default_value`; on the Sketchup::Model doc page the same parameter is spelled `defaultvalue` (no underscore) and the dictionary arg is `dictname`. Ruby positional args mean the spelling does not matter at call sites — but do NOT try keyword args. Read path: `json = model.get_attribute('NA_SceneExporter', 'scenes_v1', nil); data = json ? JSON.parse(json) : nil`. Crucially it does NOT create the attribute, so it is safe to call outside an operation. |

The name of these dictionaries are \"SU_InstanceSet\" and \"SU_DefinitionSet\". The dictionaries cannot be deleted via ruby and an ArgumentError will be raised. The key/value pairs in the dictionary can be deleted safely." | Two arities on the same method name. Use to purge a stale scene payload before rewriting: `model.delete_attribute('NA_SceneExporter')`. Do not trust the return value below SketchUp 2019 — it lies (always true). Never call it on "SU_InstanceSet" or "SU_DefinitionSet": ArgumentError. Must be inside start_operation/commit_operation. |

SketchUp 2018 — Sketchup::Page. SketchUp 2020.0 — Sketchup::Layer, Sketchup::LineStyle. SketchUp 2020.1 — Sketchup::ComponentDefinition, Sketchup::Material, Sketchup::Style. | Sketchup::Page gained persistent_id in SketchUp 2018 — safe to rely on for 2026. Persists within ONE model across sessions, but is NOT a cross-model identity: a page copied into a different model will get a different PID. | `count` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2014 | READ-ONLY | Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries#count. "The count method is inherited from the Enumerable mix-in module." @see #length | SketchUp 2014+ only on this class. O(n) via Enumerable. |

| `start_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 (the three boolean args since SketchUp 7.0) | no | Sketchup::Model#start_operation. Verbatim: "The #start_operation method is used to notify SketchUp that a new operation (which can be undone) is starting. The +op_name+ argument is a description for the operation that is displayed adjacent to the Edit > Undo menu item. Make sure to provide a user friendly name for your operation. Starting with SketchUp 7.0, there are three additional booleans that one can pass in when starting an operation. It is recommended to always set +disable_ui+ to +true+. It's left to +false+ for default for compatibility reasons."  @param [String] op_name — "name of the operation visible in the UI". @param [Boolean] disable_ui — "if set to true, then SketchUp's tendency to update the user interface after each geometry change will be suppressed. This can result in much faster Ruby code execution if the operation involves updating the model in any way." @param [Boolean] next_transparent — "<b>Deprecated!</b> if set to true, then whatever operation comes after this one will be appended into one combined operation, allowing the user the undo both actions with a single undo command. This flag is a highly difficult one, since there are so many ways that a SketchUp user can interrupt a given operation with one of their own. <b>Use extreme caution</b> and test thoroughly when setting this to true." @param [Boolean] transparent — "if set to true, then this operation will append to the previous operation. This is particularly useful for creating observers that react to user actions without littering the undo stack with extra steps that Ruby is performing." @note "Operations in SketchUp are sequential and cannot be nested. If you start a new Ruby operation while another is still open, you will implicitly close the first one." @raise [RuntimeError] "if called within the context of Sketchup::Entities#build." @return [Boolean] +true+ if successful, +false+ if unsuccessful. | THE FOUR ARGUMENTS, EXACTLY: (1) op_name String — appears after 'Undo' in the Edit menu. (2) disable_ui — set TRUE always for batch writes; it suppresses per-change UI redraws and is the single biggest speedup. (3) next_transparent — DEPRECATED, causes the NEXT operation to be merged into this one; only historically needed because DefinitionList#load and Model#place_component commit their own operations internally and operations cannot nest. Do not use it. (4) transparent — causes THIS operation to be appended onto the PREVIOUS undo step, so no new Undo entry is created and the op_name is not displayed. TRANSPARENT SEMANTICS FOR THE UNDO STACK: a transparent operation does NOT get its own undo entry — it merges into the immediately preceding operation, so one Ctrl+Z reverses both. It is still ON the undo stack (it is not 'undo-free'), it just does not add a step. Use it ONLY from observer callbacks doing bookkeeping (e.g. auto-syncing your scene JSON after the user adds a page). For a user-initiated export/import, use transparent = false so the user gets a discrete, named, undoable step. CANONICAL SAFE PATTERN:   model.start_operation('Export Scenes to Attributes', true)   begin     model.set_attribute('NA_SceneExporter', 'scenes_v1', payload.to_json)     model.commit_operation   rescue => e     model.abort_operation     raise   end NEVER nest — a second start_operation implicitly commits the first. NEVER call inside Entities#build (RuntimeError). |
| `commit_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Sketchup::Model#commit_operation. "The commit_operation method commits an operation for undo." @return [Boolean] true if successful, false if unsuccessful. | Takes NO arguments. Must be called exactly once per start_operation. Failing to commit leaves the operation open, and the next start_operation anywhere in SketchUp (yours or another extension's) will implicitly absorb your changes into it — the classic 'my extension broke another extension's undo' bug. Always commit or abort in an ensure/rescue. |
| `abort_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Sketchup::Model#abort_operation. "The abort_operation method aborts the current operation started with the start_operation method." Normally called from a rescue clause to cancel an operation if errors occur. @note "Never abort a transparent operation. Doing so would abort the operation it chains to." @return [Boolean] true if successful, false if unsuccessful. | CRITICAL RULE: never abort_operation on an operation you started with transparent = true — because a transparent op is appended to the previous operation, aborting it rolls back the USER'S previous action too (e.g. undoes the page they just created). In a transparent observer write, rescue the error, log it, and still call commit_operation. |

Pages themselves are Sketchup::Entity subclasses so each page can carry its own attribute dictionaries — an alternative storage strategy to one big model-level blob. Note that in the destination model you rebuild pages via `dest.pages.add(name)` and then assign camera/rendering/shadow state onto the returned Page. |

RenderingOptions behaves like a keyed collection (`[]`, `[]=`, `each_key`, `keys`) with String keys such as 'BackgroundColor', 'DisplayFog', 'EdgeDisplayMode'. Values include Sketchup::Color and Length which are NOT JSON-native — encode Color as [r,g,b,a] via `.to_a` and Length as a Float of inches via `.to_f`. Restore by applying the same String keys into the destination model's rendering_options while that destination page is selected, then updating the page. |

SERIALISATION TRAP: 'ShadowTime' is a Ruby Time object — do NOT to_json it directly; write the integer epoch instead, which is exactly what the sibling key 'ShadowTime_time_t' already holds. If exact style fidelity matters, ship a .style file alongside the JSON and use Styles#add_style(path, select). Changing the selection is itself an undoable model change; clear it before a bulk import (`model.selection.clear`) inside your operation. |
| `tools` | instance_method | Sketchup::Tools | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Sketchup::Model#tools. Retrieves the current Tools object. | Use `model.tools.pop_tool` / `push_tool` if your exporter runs a custom tool. Irrelevant to attribute persistence. |
| `guid` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Sketchup::Model#guid. "Retrieves the globally unique identifier for the model as a string. The guid will change after the model is modified and saved." | TRAP: the guid CHANGES on every modify-and-save cycle, so it is NOT a stable model identity. Do not use it as a cache key or to detect 'is this the same source model'. Record it only as a snapshot-time provenance breadcrumb. |
| `close` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2015 | no | Sketchup::Model#close. "Closes the model. On Mac OS, only the active model can be closed. Optionally ignores changes and suppresses save prompts." @param [Boolean] ignore_changes (default: false). | VERSION-GATE: SketchUp 2015+. Passing true silently discards unsaved changes — including any attribute payload you just wrote. Never call with true after a write. |
| `valid?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Sketchup::Model#valid?. Determines whether the model object is still valid. | Guard any cached Model reference (especially across Mac multi-document sessions or after Model#close) before use. |
| `number_faces` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 7.1 | direct | Sketchup::Model#number_faces. Returns the number of faces in the model. | Cheap complexity metric — useful as a provenance field to warn before a heavy operation. |
| `find_entity_by_id` | instance_method | Array | SketchUp 2015 | READ-ONLY | Sketchup::Model#find_entity_by_id. Looks up entities by their (non-persistent) entityID. | Session-scoped IDs only — useless for cross-model or cross-session reconstruction. Prefer find_entity_by_persistent_id. |
| `find_entity_by_persistent_id` | instance_method | Array of entities / nil entries | SketchUp 2017 | READ-ONLY | Sketchup::Model#find_entity_by_persistent_id. | `georeferenced?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 7.1 | direct | Sketchup::Model#georeferenced?. | `edit_transform` | instance_method | Geom::Transformation | SketchUp 7.0 | encode | Sketchup::Model#edit_transform. | `drawing_element_visible?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.0 | READ-ONLY | Sketchup::Model#drawing_element_visible?. Determines whether a drawing element at the given instance path is visible, accounting for layer/tag and hidden state up the hierarchy. | VERSION-GATE: SketchUp 2020.0+. Accounts for the CURRENT page's visibility overrides, so useful when snapshotting what a scene actually shows. |
| `raytest` | instance_method | Array or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Sketchup::Model#raytest. Casts a ray into the model and returns [Geom::Point3d, Array<Sketchup::Drawingelement>] or nil. | Not needed for attribute persistence; listed for completeness of the Model surface. |
| `export` | instance_method | Object (Boolean) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Sketchup::Model#export. Note that write_image's options Hash is a normal Ruby Hash argument — this is unrelated to the attribute-dictionary Hash restriction. |
| `import` | instance_method | Object | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Sketchup::Model#import. Imports a file into the model. | Commits its own internal operation — call outside your start_operation block. |
| `place_component` | instance_method | Sketchup::Model or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Sketchup::Model#place_component. | `latlong_to_point` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Sketchup::Model#latlong_to_point. Converts a [latitude, longitude] pair to a model-space point. | Geom::Point3d serialises via `.to_a` => [x, y, z] Floats in INCHES. |
| `point_to_latlong` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Sketchup::Model#point_to_latlong. | `list_datums` | instance_method | Array<String> | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Sketchup::Model#list_datums. Returns the list of available geo datums. Companions #get_datum and #set_datum exist. | Array of plain Strings, JSON-safe and directly storable as an Array attribute. |

| `remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Sketchup::Model#remove_observer. Detaches a previously added observer. | Detach on extension reload to avoid duplicate observers. |

| `VERSION_2019` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2019 | direct | SKP file format version constant for Model#save / #save_copy. | Save-down target. |
