| `VERSION_2021` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2021 | direct | SKP file format version constant for Model#save / #save_copy. This is the NEWEST documented VERSION_* constant. | CEILING: there is no VERSION_2022 through VERSION_2026. On SketchUp 2026, call `model.save(path)` with NO version argument to write the current native format. Do not construct constant names dynamically (e.g. `Sketchup::Model.const_get("VERSION_#{year}")`) — it will NameError for 2022+. |
| `LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2021.0 | direct | Status code returned by Sketchup.open_file when called with `with_status: true`, indicating the model loaded successfully. | Compare the Integer returned by `Sketchup.open_file(path, with_status: true)` against this constant. |
| `LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS_MORE_RECENT` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2021.0 | direct | Status code from Sketchup.open_file indicating the model loaded but was created in a MORE RECENT version of SketchUp than the one running. | CRITICAL FOR A CROSS-MODEL TOOL: when you open a donor .skp to read its scene attributes, check for this status — a newer-format model may have silently lost features on load, so warn the user before trusting the scene payload. |
| `ProTrial` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 8.0 | direct | Product family constant compared against Model#get_product_family. | Note the CamelCase spelling — not PRO_TRIAL. |
| `ProLicensed` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 8.0 | direct | Product family constant compared against Model#get_product_family. | CamelCase spelling. |
| `MakeTrial` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 8.0 | direct | Product family constant compared against Model#get_product_family. | CamelCase spelling. |
| `Make` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 8.0 | direct | Product family constant compared against Model#get_product_family. | CamelCase spelling. |
| `Sketchup.version` | class_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Verbatim: "Gets the current version of sketchup in decimal form." @return [String] the decimal form of the version. Example: `version = Sketchup.version`. | Returns a STRING like "26.0.123" — do NOT use it for numeric comparison ("9.0" > "26.0" is true in String comparison, a classic bug). Store it as provenance in your JSON (`"created_with": Sketchup.version`) but gate features on version_number. `Sketchup.version.to_i` gives the major version and is an acceptable coarse gate. |
| `Sketchup.version_number` | class_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Verbatim: "Get the current version of sketchup as a whole number for comparisons. The number returned has the major, minor, and build values packed into an integer value as follows: - Major version = X - Minor version = Y - Build number = Z. SketchUp 6.0 - SketchUp 2015: XXYYYZZZ. SketchUp 2016+: XXYZZZZZZZ." @return [Integer] the whole number form of the version. Documented example: `if (15003000...15004000) === Sketchup.version_number; puts "SketchUp 15.3"; end` and `if Sketchup.version_number >= 1600000000; puts "New format"; end`. | THE CORRECT FEATURE GATE. Note the packing CHANGED at SketchUp 2016: 8 digits (XXYYYZZZ) for SU6-SU2015, 10 digits (XXYZZZZZZZ) from SU2016 onward — so the number jumps from ~15xxxxxx to 1600000000. Practical gates for this plugin: SketchUp 2016+ (Model#axes) `>= 1600000000`; SketchUp 2017+ (persistent_id) `>= 1700000000`; SketchUp 2018+ (Page#persistent_id) `>= 1800000000`; SketchUp 2019+ (reliable delete_attribute return) `>= 1900000000`; SketchUp 2020+ (Model#tags, drawing_element_visible?) `>= 2000000000`; SketchUp 2023+ (overlays) `>= 2300000000`; SketchUp 2025+ (AttributeDictionary#empty?) `>= 2500000000`. Store it as an Integer in your JSON schema for forward-compatibility checks. |
| `Sketchup.active_model` | class_method | Sketchup::Model | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Verbatim: "The active_model method returns the currently active SketchUp model. On the PC, this is the only model that one can have access to via the API, but Macintosh versions of SketchUp can have multiple models open at once, in which case the method will return the model that the user currently has focused." @return [Sketchup::Model] active model object if successful, false if unsuccessful. | MAY RETURN FALSE (not nil) on failure — the documented return line literally says "false if unsuccessful", so test with `model = Sketchup.active_model; return unless model`. On macOS the focused model can change between calls; capture it once into a local and pass it around, especially in a cross-model tool. |
| `Sketchup.platform` | class_method | Symbol (:platform_win or :platform_osx) | SketchUp 2014 | encode | Verbatim: "This methods returns a symbol indicating the current platform. It should be used over RUBY_PLATFORM as this returns a different value for Windows since SketchUp 2014. Older SketchUp versions still need to check RUBY_PLATFORM.include?('mswin') or RUBY_PLATFORM.include?('darwin'). Possible return values: - :platform_win - :platform_osx" | Returns a SYMBOL — exact spellings :platform_win and :platform_osx. A Symbol is NOT a storable attribute type; call `.to_s` before writing it into an attribute or JSON. Matters for path separators when writing exported scene images. |
| `Sketchup.is_pro?` | class_method | Boolean | SketchUp 7.0 | direct | "Returns a boolean flag indicating whether the application is SketchUp Pro." | Gate Pro-only exporters (e.g. certain Model#export formats). Boolean is directly storable as an attribute. |
| `Sketchup.read_default` | class_method | Object or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "The read_default method is used to retrieve the string associated with a value within the specified sub-section section of a .INI file or registry." @return the value of the default if unsuccessful. | APPLICATION-LEVEL preferences (registry on Windows, .plist on macOS) — the correct home for user SETTINGS that should follow the user across models. This is the complement to attribute dictionaries, which are the correct home for data that must travel WITH the model. Do not put scene payloads here; do put export folder paths and image size defaults here. Exact arg names: section, variable, default. |
| `Sketchup.write_default` | class_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The write_default method is used to set the string associated with a variable within the specified sub-section of a .plist file on the Mac or the registry on Windows." @return True if successful, false if unsuccessful. | SPELLING INCONSISTENCY: read_default's second arg is documented as `variable` while write_default's is `key`. Both are positional so it does not matter at call sites. Not undoable and not part of the model — never wrap in start_operation. |
| `Sketchup.undo` | class_method | nil | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The undo method is used undo the last transaction on the undo stack." @return (nil) | Useful in tests to verify your operation is undo-clean in exactly ONE step: perform the write, call Sketchup.undo, then assert `model.get_attribute(dict, key)` is back to its prior value. If it takes two undos, your operation bracketing is wrong. |
| `Sketchup.redo` | class_method | nil | SketchUp 2021.0 | no | "The redo method is used redo the last transaction on the redo stack." @return (nil) | VERSION-GATE: SketchUp 2021.0+ only — much newer than Sketchup.undo (SU6). Guard with `Sketchup.respond_to?(:redo)` if supporting older versions. |
| `Sketchup.open_file` | class_method | Boolean, or Integer status code | SketchUp 6.0 (with_status: SketchUp 2021.0; show_version_warning_dialog: SketchUp 2021.0) | no | "The open_file method is used to open a SketchUp model." Three overloads; with `with_status: true` it returns an Integer status code to compare against Sketchup::Model::LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS and LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS_MORE_RECENT. | KEYWORD ARGUMENTS, exact spellings `with_status:` and `show_version_warning_dialog:` — these are SketchUp 2021.0+ additions. This is how a cross-model tool opens the DONOR model to read its stored scene JSON. On Windows opening a file replaces the current model, so flush any pending writes first. Always use the with_status form and check for LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS_MORE_RECENT. |
| `Sketchup.file_new` | class_method | Module (the Sketchup module) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The file_new method is used to create a new file." @return The Sketchup module. | Returns the Sketchup module itself, NOT the new Model — you must follow with `Sketchup.active_model` to get the fresh model into which you will reconstruct scenes. |
| `Sketchup.temp_dir` | class_method | String | SketchUp 2014 | direct | "The temp_dir method is used to retrieve the OS temporary directory for the current user." @return a string containing the full temporary directory path. | VERSION-GATE: SketchUp 2014+. The correct scratch location for chunked-payload staging or intermediate exported images — avoids permission problems that plague writing next to the .skp or into the Plugins folder. |
| `SU_InstanceSet` | flag | String (dictionary name) | SketchUp 2018 | no | One of the two special attribute dictionaries introduced in SketchUp 2018. Documented note: "The dictionaries cannot be deleted via ruby and an ArgumentError will be raised. The key/value pairs in the dictionary can be deleted safely." | EXACT SPELLING, case-sensitive: SU_InstanceSet. If your importer wipes dictionaries before writing, SKIP this name or you get an ArgumentError. Its key/value pairs may be deleted individually via delete_key, just not the dictionary itself. |
| `SU_DefinitionSet` | flag | String (dictionary name) | SketchUp 2018 | no | The second special attribute dictionary introduced in SketchUp 2018. Cannot be deleted via Ruby; AttributeDictionaries#delete and Entity#delete_attribute raise ArgumentError on it. | EXACT SPELLING, case-sensitive: SU_DefinitionSet. Guard clause for a dictionary-clearing routine: `next if %w[SU_InstanceSet SU_DefinitionSet].include?(dict.name)`. Also treat 'dynamic_attributes' (Dynamic Components) and 'SU_' -prefixed names as reserved and do not touch them. |
| `Storable value types (authoritative whitelist)` | flag | closed set of ten types | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | Taken verbatim from the @param tag on Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#[]= in the official ruby-api-stubs source (Copyright 2026 Trimble Inc.). This is the ONLY authoritative statement of what an attribute value may be. The same set applies to Entity#set_attribute, whose own @param is loosely typed as [Object] but which is backed by the same storage. | PER-TYPE ROUND-TRIP BEHAVIOUR FOR JSON: • String — stores and returns verbatim. THE ONE TO USE for a JSON payload. Watch encoding: force UTF-8 on read (`str.force_encoding('UTF-8')`) before JSON.parse if you see invalid-byte errors. • Integer — round-trips exactly. Very large values risk platform Bignum edge cases; keep within 32-bit if paranoid. • Float — round-trips as a double. JSON-native. • Boolean (true/false) — round-trips exactly. JSON-native. • nil — DOCUMENTED AS ALLOWED but read back it is indistinguishable from 'key absent', AND the set_attribute doc warns that pre-2015 an empty-evaluating value (explicitly including nil) could CORRUPT THE MODEL. Never store nil; omit the key or store the JSON string "null". • Length — a Float subclass measured in INCHES. Stored as a Length, returned as a Length. `to_json` on a Length emits a bare number of inches, so on reconstruction you must re-wrap: `value.to_l`. Always record the unit convention in your schema. • Time — stored and returned as a Ruby Time. NOT reliably JSON-round-trippable (to_json gives a locale/format-dependent string). Store `time.to_i` epoch Integer instead and rebuild with `Time.at(n)`. • Array — supported, and is the only structured type. Elements must themselves be from the whitelist. NESTED ARRAYS ARE NOT GUARANTEED — TIG's community type list only ever demonstrates flat arrays ([1,2,3], ["a", 1.0, true]); nobody confirms nested-array round-trip. Do not rely on nesting; flatten or JSON-encode. • Geom::Point3d / Geom::Vector3d — stored as those classes, returned as those classes, in INCHES. `to_json` does NOT produce a useful shape; call `.to_a` => [x,y,z] Floats before JSON and rebuild with `Geom::Point3d.new(*arr)`. Special documented behaviour: Point3d/Vector3d Array values stored as VERTEX attributes are automatically transformed when the vertex moves. NOT STORABLE: Hash, Symbol, Struct, Range, Set, custom classes, Geom::Transformation, Geom::BoundingBox, Sketchup::Color, Sketchup::Entity references. |
| `Unsupported-type failure mode` | flag | undefined / nil on read, or raises |  | no | Community-established (not officially documented) behaviour for values outside the whitelist. For a Hash, get_attribute reads back nil — the value is silently not stored rather than raising. For custom classes, storage likewise fails. DanRathbun's canonical hash-flattening idiom on the SketchUp forum wraps each assignment in a rescue — `my_hash.each {|key,value| dict[key.to_s]= value rescue dict[key.to_s]= value.to_s }` — which demonstrates that SOME unsupported values DO raise. He states plainly: "A Sketchup::AttributeDictionary is not a hash, nor a subclass of Ruby's Hash" and "Some value (object types) can be directly saved to and read from attribute dictionaries, but not hashes... Test fully." | TREAT AS UNDEFINED BEHAVIOUR. Do not rely on either the silent-nil path or the raise path. Validate before writing:   STORABLE = [TrueClass, FalseClass, Integer, Float, Length, String, Time, Array, Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d].freeze   raise TypeError, "unstorable: #{v.class}" unless STORABLE.any? { |k| v.is_a?(k) } Symbols specifically: no documented support and no community confirmation — always call `.to_s`. Since Sketchup.platform returns a Symbol, this bites immediately if you store platform metadata naively. |
| `String length limit` | flag | Integer (character count) |  | encode | There is NO officially documented maximum length for a String attribute value anywhere in the SketchUp Ruby API docs. The community figure comes from Sketchucation, where TIG states there is "a length limit for a single string attribute [~20000]" and demonstrates working code that splits hex-encoded file data into 20,000-character chunks stored under sequentially-named keys ('x0001', 'x0002', ...), reassembled by sorting the keys and concatenating. Other forum participants report being unable to find any documented maximum. Separately, on the SketchUp forum, John_drivenupthewall reports that "20k of attributes was found to be increasing file size ten fold on a 3mb model", and mohan.s reports that storing all their JSON on the model "causes the lag (slowdown the Sketchup) because of huge data". | DESIGN RULE FOR THE SCENE SERIALISER: do not assume you can store an unbounded JSON blob. Chunk at a conservative 16,000 characters (below the reported ~20,000 ceiling to leave UTF-8 multibyte headroom, since the limit is likely bytes not characters). Pattern:   WRITE: json = payload.to_json; chunks = json.scan(/.{1,16000}/m)          dict = model.attribute_dictionary('NA_SceneImageExporter', true)          dict['chunk_count'] = chunks.length          chunks.each_with_index { |c, i| dict[format('chunk_%04d', i)] = c }   READ:  n = dict['chunk_count'].to_i          json = (0...n).map { |i| dict[format('chunk_%04d', i)] }.join          payload = JSON.parse(json) Zero-pad the index so lexical sort equals numeric sort. Store a 'schema_version' Integer and the Sketchup.version_number alongside. Consider Zlib::Deflate + Base64 before chunking to cut both chunk count and file bloat (Zlib ships with SketchUp's Ruby; Base64 keeps the result inside the String type safely — never store raw deflate bytes, which are binary and will break encoding). SketchUp staff tt_su's guidance is explicit: "I'd use big_json_data.to_json instead of inspect. Then use JSON.parse to read it", and he warns "If the latter then storing it as a single large blob might incur noticeable lag. (time and profile)". Community expert kengey's alternative — distribute data onto the objects it describes rather than one giant model attribute — maps naturally here: store each scene's JSON on its own Sketchup::Page (pages are Entities and take attribute dictionaries), plus a small model-level index. |

### API changes, SketchUp 2023 to 2026  —  157 entries

| Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Sketchup::Model#environments` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environments | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "The #environments method is used to retrieve the Environments object for this model." This is the entry point to the entire HDRI/IBL API. | Collection accessor, not a value. Guard with `model.respond_to?(:environments)`. On SketchUp 2024 and earlier this method does not exist and every environment field in your JSON must be omitted (write `"environment": null`) rather than written as false/0, so a 2024 export is distinguishable from a 2025 export where the user genuinely had no environment. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#[]` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environment or nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "The #[] method is used to retrieve an Environment by name." Returns nil when no environment of that name exists. | This is the primary lookup for reconstruction in a target model: after re-adding the HDR you serialised, fetch it back with `model.environments[name]`. Note the collection is name-keyed only — there is no integer index overload documented, unlike Sketchup::Styles#[] which takes name OR index. Do not assume `environments[0]` works. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#add` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environment | SketchUp 2025.0 (two-arg); SketchUp 2026.0 (single-arg path overload) | encode | "adds an Environment to the Environments". Two overloads. The two-argument form (name, path) exists since 2025.0. The single-argument (path) form was added in SketchUp 2026.0 — "Environment name will be derived from the file being imported." Supported file formats are HDR, EXR and SKE. Raises ArgumentError if the file cannot be loaded. | THIS IS THE RECONSTRUCTION CALL. There is no way to create an Environment from raw in-memory pixel data — you must have a file on disk. Cross-model workflow: (1) source model: `env.write_hdr(temp_dir)` returns the full path of the written file; (2) copy that file next to your JSON or base64 it into the JSON; (3) target model: write the bytes back to a temp file and call `model.environments.add(name, temp_path)`. ALWAYS call the two-argument form even on 2026 — it is available on both 2025 and 2026 and lets you control the name so it round-trips. Wrap in begin/rescue ArgumentError. Names may collide with an existing environment in the target model; check `environments[name]` first and reuse rather than re-adding. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#current` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environment or nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "used to get the current environment". Returns nil when no environment is currently set on the model. | Model-level, NOT page-level. This is the environment the viewport is currently showing. When you iterate pages to serialise them you will observe that `page.environment` tracks whatever page is active; `environments.current` is the model's live state. Serialise it as the model-level default under a separate `model_environment` key, then restore it last via `#current=`. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#current=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environment or nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "used to set the current environment". Accepts a Sketchup::Environment or nil. | Passing nil to UNSET the current environment was broken on first release and was fixed in SketchUp 2025.0.2 ("Fixed a bug where developers were unable to unset the current environment with the Ruby API"). If you must support 2025.0.0/2025.0.1 do not rely on `environments.current = nil`; gate it on `Sketchup.version_number` for the 2025.0.2 build or wrap in begin/rescue and verify with a read-back of `#current`. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#each` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environments | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "used to iterate over all the environments". Yields each Sketchup::Environment. | Use this to serialise the model's full environment library once, at model level, rather than duplicating HDR payloads per page. Emit an array of environment records each keyed by `name`, and have each page record reference the name string. Note the class does not document an Enumerable mixin, so `#map`/`#select` may not be available — collect manually: `envs = []; model.environments.each { |e| envs << e }`. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#remove` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "removes an Environment from the Environments". Returns true if removed, false if the environment was not found. | Destructive. Only relevant if your importer offers a 'replace existing environments' mode. Takes the Environment object, not a name — resolve via `environments[name]` first and nil-check. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#purge_unused` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environments | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "used to remove unused environments". Returns the Environments collection. | Do NOT call this mid-import. An environment you have added but not yet assigned to any page is 'unused' and will be destroyed. Add environments, assign them to pages, THEN purge if at all. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#size` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "retrieves the number of environments". Aliased as #length. | Useful as a cheap guard: `model.environments.size.zero?` means the model has no HDRI library and every page's environment record can be skipped. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#length` | instance_method | Integer | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | An alias for #size. | Identical to #size. Prefer #size for consistency with the rest of the SketchUp collection API. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "used to add an observer to the environments collection". Returns true if successful. | Takes a Sketchup::EnvironmentsObserver. Not needed for serialisation, but if your plugin caches the environment library you must attach one — there is no other change notification. Always pair with #remove_observer on model close to avoid a dangling reference. |
| `Sketchup::Environments#remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "used to remove an observer from the current object". Returns true if successful. | Call from your AppObserver#onQuit / onCloseModel handler. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#name` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "retrieves the name of the Environment". | THE join key for cross-model transfer. Write it as `"name": <String>`. On import, look up `model.environments[name]`; if nil, add the HDR file with `environments.add(name, path)` so the name is preserved exactly. Names are user-visible and may contain any characters — do not sanitise them in the JSON or the lookup will miss. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#name=` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "sets the name for an Environment". | Use only when the single-argument `add(path)` overload derived a name you need to correct. Not documented to enforce uniqueness (unlike Sketchup::Page#name= in 2026.0), so guard against collisions yourself by scanning `environments.each`. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#description` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "gets the description for an Environment". | Plain String, straight into JSON as `"description"`. May be an empty string. Restore with #description= after the environment exists in the target model. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#description=` | instance_method | String (argument) | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "sets the description for an Environment". | Assign the JSON string back directly. No validation documented. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#path` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "The #path method is used to get the file name of the image or SKE file used for the Environment." The official example is explicit: after `environments.add('Example', 'path/to/environment.hdr')`, `puts environment.path` outputs `'environment.hdr'`. | CRITICAL TRAP: despite the name, this returns only the BASE FILE NAME, not a usable absolute path. You cannot reopen it, and in a target model on another machine it is meaningless. Serialise it for diagnostics only (`"source_filename"`), and NEVER try to feed it back into `Environments#add`. The only supported way to move the actual image data between models is `Environment#write_hdr(dir)`. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#write_hdr` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "The #write_hdr method writes the HDR, EXR or SKE image of the environment to a file in its original file type." Parameter `path` (String) is "the directory where the image should be written". Returns "the full path of the written file". Raises ArgumentError "if the image is invalid", ArgumentError "if the file name is empty", and RuntimeError "if the file cannot be written". | THE EXPORT HALF OF CROSS-MODEL TRANSFER. Note the argument is a DIRECTORY, not a file path — a very easy mistake. The return value is the full written path, which you then read as binary. HDR/EXR files are commonly 5–100 MB, so base64-embedding them in a model attribute dictionary is usually a bad idea (attribute values are Strings and the SKP will bloat proportionally); prefer writing a sidecar file next to the .skp and storing only a relative filename plus a SHA-256 in the JSON. Always rescue all three exception classes — an environment sourced from 3D Warehouse can fail here. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#rotation` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "get the vertical rotation angle in degrees" of the Environment. | Float in DEGREES (not radians — unusual for the SketchUp API, which is normally radians; do NOT run this through .radians/.degrees). Serialise verbatim as `"rotation"`. THIS IS THE VALUE THAT DOES NOT READ CORRECTLY FROM A NON-ACTIVE PAGE — see the gotchas. Two scenes sharing one environment at different rotations will both report the active scene's rotation unless you activate each page first. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#rotation=` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "set the vertical rotation angle in degrees" of the Environment. | Degrees. To restore a per-scene rotation you must make the target page active FIRST, then set rotation, then call `page.update(PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT)` (or PAGE_USE_ALL) so the value is committed to that scene. Setting it while a different page is active writes to the wrong scene. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#skydome_exposure` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "get the exposure of the Environment" (the skydome/background exposure). | Float, JSON-direct as `"skydome_exposure"`. Same per-page read caveat as #rotation. No documented valid range — round-trip the raw Float rather than clamping. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#skydome_exposure=` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "set the exposure of the Environment". | Coerce your JSON number with `.to_f` before assigning — JSON.parse will hand you an Integer for a value like `1` and the API is typed Float. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#reflection_exposure` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "get the exposure of the Environment for reflections". | Independent of skydome_exposure — both must be serialised. JSON key `"reflection_exposure"`. Same per-page read caveat. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#reflection_exposure=` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "set the exposure of the Environment for reflections". | `.to_f` your JSON value. Restore alongside skydome_exposure while the target page is active. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#use_as_skydome?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "determine if the Environment is used as a skydome" — i.e. whether the HDRI is visible as the background. | Boolean, JSON key `"use_as_skydome"` (drop the ? in the key). Note this is orthogonal to #use_for_reflections? — an environment can light and reflect without being visible. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#use_as_skydome=` | instance_method | Boolean (argument) | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | Sets whether the Environment functions as the skydome (visible background). | Assign the JSON boolean directly. Beware JSON round-tripping producing the strings "true"/"false" if you ever stringify — coerce explicitly, since any non-nil non-false Ruby object is truthy and the string "false" would silently enable it. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#use_for_reflections?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "determine if the Environment is used for reflections". | Boolean, JSON key `"use_for_reflections"`. Pairs with the PBR material work also added in 2025.0 (metallic/roughness) — reflections are only visually meaningful on PBR materials. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#use_for_reflections=` | instance_method | Boolean (argument) | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | Sets whether the Environment applies to reflections. Note the parameter is documented singular: `use_for_reflection`. | Method name is plural (`use_for_reflections=`) even though the documented parameter name is singular — use the plural method name. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#linked_sun?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "determine if the Environment is linked to the sun" for shadow lighting. | IMPORTANT INTERACTION WITH ShadowInfo. When true, the environment drives (or is driven by) the sun position, so restoring ShadowInfo and the Environment independently can fight each other. Serialise this flag and restore it BEFORE writing linked_sun_position, and consider restoring ShadowInfo first then the environment. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#linked_sun=` | instance_method | Boolean (argument) | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | Sets whether the Environment links to the sun for shadow lighting. | Set this before #linked_sun_position= — setting a position while unlinked is likely to be discarded. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#linked_sun_position` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "get the position of the sun linked to the Environment". | NOT JSON-direct — Geom::Point3d must be decomposed. Encode as `pt.to_a.map(&:to_f)` giving `[x, y, z]` in INCHES (SketchUp's internal unit). Decode with `Geom::Point3d.new(*arr)`. Do not use `.to_s` (locale-formatted, lossy). Note the getter returns a Point3d even though conceptually it is a direction; preserve all three components. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#linked_sun_position=` | instance_method | Geom::Point3d | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "set the position of the sun linked to the Environment". | Reconstruct with `Geom::Point3d.new(arr[0], arr[1], arr[2])`. Only meaningful when #linked_sun? is true. |
| `Sketchup::Environment#thumbnail` | instance_method | Sketchup::ImageRep | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "get the thumbnail image of the Environment". | Returns a Sketchup::ImageRep. Do not attempt to serialise for transfer — the thumbnail is regenerated automatically when the environment is re-added in the target model. Useful only for building a picker UI (call `ImageRep#save_as` to a temp PNG and show it in an HtmlDialog). |
| `Sketchup::EnvironmentsObserver#onEnvironmentAdd` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "The onEnvironmentAdd method is called whenever an environment is added to the Sketchup::Environments." | Exact camelCase spelling matters — SketchUp silently ignores misspelled observer callbacks, giving no error. Parameters are (Sketchup::Environments, Sketchup::Environment) in that order. |
| `Sketchup::EnvironmentsObserver#onEnvironmentChange` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "The onEnvironmentChange method is called whenever the environment properties are changed." | Fires on rotation/exposure/flag edits. Use to invalidate any cached environment JSON. Do not perform model edits inside an observer callback. |
| `Sketchup::EnvironmentsObserver#onEnvironmentRemove` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "The onEnvironmentRemove method is called whenever an environment is removed from the Sketchup::Environments." | The passed Environment may already be invalid — do not call methods on it beyond identity comparison. |
| `Sketchup::EnvironmentsObserver#onEnvironmentSetCurrent` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | no | "The onEnvironmentSetCurrent method is called whenever the current environment is changed." | Because switching scenes changes the current environment, this fires during page activation. If you use the 'activate each page to read its environment' workaround, expect a burst of these callbacks — suppress your own handler while scanning. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_environment?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "The #use_environment? method is used to determine if the Environment settings are used in the scene." | This IS genuinely per-page and IS safe to read without activating the page — it is a stored flag, unlike the Environment property values. Serialise as `"use_environment"`. Guard: `page.respond_to?(:use_environment?)`. Restore this flag LAST, after assigning #environment=, since setting it true with no environment assigned is meaningless. |
| `Sketchup::Page#use_environment=` | instance_method | Boolean (argument) | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "The #use_environment= method is used to set if the Environment settings are used in the scene." | Direct boolean assignment. In SketchUp 2026.0+ this is part of the Page property set, so wrap in start_operation/commit_operation. On 2024 and earlier the method is absent — skip silently. |
| `Sketchup::Page#environment` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environment | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "The #environment method is used to retrieve the Environment for the scene." | MAJOR TRAP: this returns the model's live Environment object, whose mutable properties (rotation, exposures, flags, sun link) reflect the CURRENTLY ACTIVE page, not this page. Per the SketchUp forum thread and staff response, SketchUp keeps one viewport and one Environment object per model; a scene stores only whether-to-use flags plus the reference, so "at some time, you'll need to make the page active" to read the real values. Serialise only `page.environment.name` from here, and obtain rotation/exposures via the activate-each-page workaround. May be nil-ish/raise on a page with no environment — nil-check. |
| `Sketchup::Page#environment=` | instance_method | Sketchup::Environment (argument) | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "The #environment= method is used to set the Environment for the scene." | Takes an Environment OBJECT, not a name String. Reconstruction order that works: add the HDR to the target model's Environments collection → resolve the object via `environments[name]` → make the page active → `page.environment = env` → set rotation/exposures/flags on `env` → `page.use_environment = true` → `page.update(PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT)`. In 2026.0+ wrap the whole loop in one start_operation/commit_operation. |
| `PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 2025.0 (introduced with the Environments API) | no | Top-level constant, listed by Sketchup::Page#update as the flag for "Environment settings". Confirmed present in the official ruby-api-stubs _top_level.rb constant list alongside the other PAGE_USE_* flags. | The integer VALUE is not published by Trimble (the stubs define it as nil placeholder). NEVER hardcode a number — reference the constant symbolically. Because it does not exist on SketchUp 2024 and earlier, referencing it unguarded raises NameError at parse/execute time. Guard with `defined?(PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT)` and build your flags mask at runtime: `flags = PAGE_USE_ALL; flags |= PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT if defined?(PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT)`. It is unclear whether PAGE_USE_ALL already subsumes it, so OR it in explicitly. |
| `PAGE_USE_CAMERA` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Camera Location". | Safe on every supported version, no guard needed. Reference symbolically; values are unpublished. |
| `PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Drawing Style". | Note the spelling is RENDERING_OPTIONS (plural OPTIONS), matching Sketchup::RenderingOptions. |
| `PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Shadow Setting". | SPELLING TRAP: it is SHADOWINFO as one word, NOT PAGE_USE_SHADOW_INFO — inconsistent with the method Page#shadow_info which does use an underscore. |
| `PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Axes Location". | SPELLING TRAP: 'SKETCHCS' (SketchUp Coordinate System), not PAGE_USE_AXES. This is the flag corresponding to Page#use_axes?/#axes. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Hidden Geometry & Objects (SU 2019 and older)". | Legacy. On 2020+ prefer PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY | PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS, matching the split of Page#use_hidden? into #use_hidden_geometry? and #use_hidden_objects?. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 2020.1 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Hidden Geometry (SU 2020 and later)". | Guard with `defined?(PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY)` if you still support SU2019 or earlier. |
| `PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 2020.1 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Hidden Objects (SU 2020 and later)". | Guard with `defined?(PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS)` for pre-2020 support. |
| `PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Visible Layers". | Note it is LAYER (singular) VISIBILITY. Corresponds to Page#use_hidden_layers? and the per-page tag overrides in Page#layers / Page#layer_folders. |
| `PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES` | constant | Integer (bit flag) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "Active Section Planes". | Pairs with Page#use_section_planes? and the new 2026.0 Page#active_section_planes reader. |
| `PAGE_USE_ALL` | constant | Integer (bit flag mask) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Flag for Sketchup::Page#update meaning "All possible scene properties". Also the default value of the `flags` parameter of Sketchup::Pages#add. | Because its numeric value is fixed in each binary, it is NOT guaranteed to include PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT on 2025/2026. OR the environment flag in explicitly when it is defined. |
| `PAGE_NO_CAMERA` | constant | Integer (bit flag mask) | SketchUp 6.0 | no | Top-level constant confirmed present in the ruby-api-stubs _top_level.rb list; used as a flags argument meaning all scene properties except the camera. | Useful with Pages#add when creating a scene whose camera you intend to set explicitly afterwards from your JSON, avoiding a spurious capture of the current view. |
| `Sketchup::Page#active_section_planes` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::SectionPlane> or nil | SketchUp 2026.0 | READ-ONLY | "The active_section_planes method is used to retrieve the active section plane for the Sketchup::Page." Returns nil if the page does not use section planes. Official example: `model = Sketchup.active_model; pages = model.pages; page = pages.add('My Page'); page.active_section_planes`. | BIG WIN FOR SERIALISERS — before 2026.0 there was no supported way to read which section planes a scene had active without activating the page. Returns an ARRAY (nested contexts can each have one active plane), despite the singular wording in the description. THERE IS NO SETTER — no `active_section_planes=` exists. To restore, you must still activate the page, call `SectionPlane#activate` on each matched plane, then `page.update(PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES)`. Guard: `page.respond_to?(:active_section_planes)`. Cross-model matching: serialise each plane's `#name`, `#symbol` and `#get_plane` (a 4-element Array [a,b,c,d]) since object identity cannot cross models; match on name first, falling back to plane coefficients within a tolerance. |
| `Sketchup::Model#active_section_planes` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::SectionPlane> | SketchUp 2026.0 | READ-ONLY | "The #active_section_planes method returns all of the active section planes in the model." | Model-level counterpart to Page#active_section_planes; returns an Array (not nil). Use it to capture the model's baseline section state before you begin activating pages, so you can restore it afterwards. Guard: `model.respond_to?(:active_section_planes)`. |
| `Sketchup::SectionPlane#get_plane` | instance_method | Array (plane: [a, b, c, d] or [point, vector]) | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "The get_plane method is used to retrieve the plane that the section plane is on." Returns a plane as described in the Geom module. | The only geometric identity a section plane has across models. Normalise before writing JSON: a SketchUp 'plane' may come back as a 4-element numeric Array [a,b,c,d] OR as [Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d]. Handle both: if `plane.length == 4` write `plane.map(&:to_f)`, else write `{'point' => plane[0].to_a.map(&:to_f), 'normal' => plane[1].to_a.map(&:to_f)}`. Units are inches. |
| `Sketchup::SectionPlane#set_plane` | instance_method | Array (plane) | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "The set_plane method is used to set the plane that the section plane is on." Returns the updated SectionPlane. | Accepts the 4-element Array form directly, so you can pass your decoded JSON array straight in. |
| `Sketchup::SectionPlane#activate` | instance_method | self or nil | SketchUp 2014 | encode | "The activate method is used to make the section plane the active one of its parent component/group." Returns self if successful. | The restore call for section-plane state. Only ONE plane can be active per parent context, so activating a second plane in the same Entities collection deactivates the first — apply in the order captured from Page#active_section_planes. |
| `Sketchup::SectionPlane#active?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2014 | direct | "The active? method indicates whether the section plane is active or not." Returns true if active. | Reflects the model's live state, which follows the active page. Prefer Page#active_section_planes (2026.0) for a per-page answer; fall back to this after page activation on 2025 and earlier. |
| `Sketchup::SectionPlane#name` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2018 | direct | "The name method is used to retrieve the name of the section plane." | Best cross-model join key for section planes. May be an empty String if the user never named it — fall back to plane coefficients then. |
| `Sketchup::SectionPlane#name=` | instance_method | String (argument) | SketchUp 2018 | encode | "The name= method is used to set the name of a section plane." | Straight String assignment when recreating planes in the target model. |
| `Sketchup::SectionPlane#symbol` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2018 | direct | "The symbol method is used to retrieve the symbol of the section plane." | Despite the method name this returns a Ruby String, not a Ruby Symbol — JSON-direct. |
| `Sketchup::SectionPlane#symbol=` | instance_method | String (argument) | SketchUp 2018 | encode | "The symbol= method is used to set the symbol of a section plane." Note: "The symbol must be three characters or less." | HARD LIMIT of 3 characters — truncate or rescue on restore, because a malformed value from hand-edited JSON will raise. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#reorder` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2025.0 | encode | "used to reorder an existing Page object inside collection". Supports negative indices. Raises IndexError if the index is out of range. | ESSENTIAL for faithful scene reconstruction — before 2025.0 there was no API to set scene tab order, only the order in which you created pages. Serialise each page's ordinal index. On import, create all pages first, then loop `pages.reorder(page, target_index)` in ascending target order. Guard: `pages.respond_to?(:reorder)`; on 2024 and earlier fall back to creating pages in the correct order in the first place. Rescue IndexError — your JSON index may exceed the target model's page count if some pages failed to create. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#unique_name` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2026.0 | READ-ONLY | "used to generate a unique name for the page based on a base_name string". | Use on import when the target model already has a scene of the same name and you want to keep both. Guard: `pages.respond_to?(:unique_name)`. On 2025 and earlier implement your own suffix loop (`"#{base}-2"`, `-3`, …) checking `pages[name].nil?`. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#add` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page | SketchUp 6.0; behaviour modified SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | "used to add a new Page object to the collection". BEHAVIOUR CHANGE in 2026.0: "Modified Sketchup::Pages#add so that when adding a new Sketchup::Page, if the specified name is already taken, it automatically modifies the name to ensure it is unique." | BREAKS NAIVE IMPORTERS. On 2026.0+ you can no longer assume `pages.add(name).name == name` — if the name collided, SketchUp silently renamed it. ALWAYS capture the returned Page and read back `page.name` to build your old-name→new-name map, then rewrite any cross-references (e.g. LayOut viewport scene names) accordingly. On 2025 and earlier a duplicate name had a known bug rather than clean uniquing, so read-back is the correct defensive pattern on every version. The `index` third parameter also lets you insert at a position without needing #reorder. |
| `Sketchup::Page#name=` | instance_method | String (argument) | SketchUp 6.0; behaviour modified SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | "sets the name for a page's tab". BEHAVIOUR CHANGE in 2026.0: "Modified Sketchup::Page#name= so that if the given name is already used by another Sketchup::Page, it automatically adjusts the name to make it unique." | Same trap as Pages#add — the assignment may not stick verbatim on 2026.0+. Always `page.name = wanted; actual = page.name` and reconcile. Note this is a Page property change; in 2026.0 name is NOT in the list of properties that became undoable (only Axes/Camera/RenderingOptions/ShadowInfo are named), but wrap it anyway for a clean single undo step. |
| `Sketchup::Page#update` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 (PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT flag SketchUp 2025.0) | encode | "performs an update on the page properties based on the current view that the user has. What properties of the Page get updated are controlled via an integer whose bits corresponds to different properties." Accepts PAGE_USE_CAMERA, PAGE_USE_RENDERING_OPTIONS, PAGE_USE_SHADOWINFO, PAGE_USE_SKETCHCS, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_GEOMETRY, PAGE_USE_HIDDEN_OBJECTS, PAGE_USE_LAYER_VISIBILITY, PAGE_USE_SECTION_PLANES, PAGE_USE_ALL, PAGE_USE_ENVIRONMENT. | THE COMMIT CALL for anything that is model-live rather than page-stored — notably environment properties and section plane activation. It captures the CURRENT VIEW/model state into the page, so it is only correct while that page is the selected page. Sequence: `pages.selected_page = page` → mutate model-live state → `page.update(flags)`. Called with no arguments it defaults to updating from the current view. In 2026.0 this is undoable, so wrap it. |
| `Sketchup::Page#camera` | instance_method | Sketchup::Camera | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "retrieves the camera for a particular page". | Unlike Environment, Camera IS genuinely stored per page and reads correctly without activating the page. Serialise eye/target/up as `[x,y,z]` inch triples plus `perspective?`, `fov`, `image_width`, `aspect_ratio`, `focal_length` (Pro), `description`. Reconstruct with `Sketchup::Camera.new(eye, target, up, perspective, fov)` then assign scalar properties. In 2026.0 mutating a Page's Camera became UNDOABLE — this is one of the four named properties (Axes, Camera, RenderingOptions, ShadowInfo). |
| `Sketchup::Page#rendering_options` | instance_method | Sketchup::RenderingOptions | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "retrieves a RenderingOptions object for the page". | Iterate with `#each_pair` and write a key→value Hash. In 2026.0 mutating this via a Page became UNDOABLE (one of the four named properties). Combined with the 2024.0 strict `[]=`, your restore loop must be both operation-wrapped AND per-key rescued. |
| `Sketchup::Page#shadow_info` | instance_method | Sketchup::ShadowInfo | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "retrieves the ShadowInfo object for the page". | In 2026.0 mutating this via a Page became UNDOABLE. In 2026.1 `ShadowInfo#[]=` became strict (KeyError/TypeError) — see the ShadowInfo entry. Serialise only the writable keys. |
| `Sketchup::Page#axes` | instance_method | Sketchup::Axes | SketchUp 2016 | encode | "returns the drawing axes for the page". | Serialise via `axes.to_a` → [origin, xaxis, yaxis, zaxis] and encode each as an inch triple; restore with `axes.set(origin, xaxis, yaxis, zaxis)`. In 2026.0 mutating a Page's Axes became UNDOABLE — one of the four named properties in the breaking change. |
| `Sketchup::Page#style` | instance_method | Sketchup::Style | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "retrieves the style associated with the page". | There is NO Page#style= setter in any version. To restore a scene's style in a different model you must select the style in the model (Styles#selected_style=) while the page is active and then Page#update. Serialise the style's #name and, from 2025.0, #path. |
| `Sketchup::Style#path` | instance_method | String | SketchUp 2025.0 | direct | "The #path method gets the file path the Sketchup::Style was loaded from." | NEW AND USEFUL for cross-model style transfer: gives you a .style file path you can feed to `Styles#add_style(path, false)` in the target model. Guard: `style.respond_to?(:path)`. May be empty for a style that originated inside the model (never loaded from a file) — fall back to matching on #name. Unlike Environment#path, this is documented as a file PATH, but treat it as machine-local and verify with File.exist? before use. |
| `Sketchup::Styles#remove_style` | instance_method | nil | SketchUp 2026.0 | no | "remove a Style from the Styles". Raises ArgumentError if the style is not found or if only one style remains. | Only relevant to a 'clean target model before import' mode. NOTE THE HARD CONSTRAINT: it raises if this would leave zero styles — a model must always have at least one. Guard: `styles.respond_to?(:remove_style)`. |
| `Sketchup::Styles#add_style` | instance_method | Boolean (pre-2026.0) or Sketchup::Style/nil (2026.0+) | SketchUp 6.0; return value modified SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | "create and load a style from the given file". CHANGED in 2026.0: "Modified Sketchup::Styles#add_style to return the added style." | RETURN TYPE CHANGED — a serialiser that does `if styles.add_style(path, false)` still works (a Style object is truthy, and false/nil are falsy), but code that does `== true` breaks on 2026.0. Portable pattern: `result = styles.add_style(path, false); style = result.is_a?(Sketchup::Style) ? result : styles[name_from_file]`. Also note the second parameter gained a default only in 2026.0 — always pass it explicitly for portability. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#empty?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "check if the attribute dictionary is empty". | Convenience only. Guard with `dict.respond_to?(:empty?)`; on 2024 and earlier use `dict.length.zero?` (or `dict.keys.empty?`), which works on every version — recommended for a serialiser that must run on 2023+. |
| `Sketchup::Model#attribute_dictionary` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionary or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "Returns the Sketchup::AttributeDictionary object that is specified by name." Pass create = true to create it if absent. | UNCHANGED 2023→2026. This remains the correct place to park your scene JSON for cross-model transfer. Use a namespaced dictionary name to avoid collisions. Returns nil when create is false and the dictionary does not exist — always nil-check. |
| `Sketchup::Model#set_attribute` | instance_method | Object | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "This method is used to set the value of an attribute in an attribute dictionary with the given name." | UNCHANGED 2023→2026, and no SKP file-format change in that window affects it. Permitted attribute value types are documented as: Boolean, Integer, Float, Length, nil, String, Time, Array, Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d — NOT Hash. So store your scene data as ONE String: `model.set_attribute(DICT, 'scenes_json', JSON.generate(data))`. Very large Strings (multi-MB) are stored fine but bloat the SKP and slow save/load; if you embed base64 HDRI payloads consider chunking across numbered keys or, better, using sidecar files. |
| `Sketchup::Model#get_attribute` | instance_method | Object or nil | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "The get_attribute method gets the value of an attribute that in the AttributeDictionary with the given name." | Reads back your JSON String in the target model. Always pass a default and always rescue JSON::ParserError — a model round-tripped through an older SketchUp, or through a third-party exporter, can return a truncated or nil value. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#[]=` | instance_method | Object | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "used to set the value of an attribute with a given key". | Same permitted type list as Model#set_attribute. Note Time values are stored as Time and come back as Time — relevant if you mirror ShadowInfo's ShadowTime into an attribute rather than into JSON; prefer serialising the integer ShadowTime_time_t instead for portability. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions#[]=` | instance_method | varies by key | SketchUp 6.0; strict behaviour SketchUp 2024.0 | encode | BREAKING CHANGE, SketchUp 2024.0: "Sketchup::RenderingOptions#[]= will now raise an ArgumentError if the given value cannot be set for the given key." | THIS BREAKS 2023-ERA SCENE RESTORERS. Prior to 2024.0 an invalid assignment failed silently; from 2024.0 it raises ArgumentError and aborts your restore loop mid-way, leaving the scene half-written. Wrap EVERY key individually: `data.each { |k, v| begin; ro[k] = v; rescue ArgumentError, TypeError, KeyError => e; log(k, e); end }`. Also filter out keys your JSON contains that do not exist in the target (older) SketchUp — check `ro.keys.include?(k)` first, since `ro.keys` reflects the running version. |
| `"AmbientOcclusion"` | rendering_option_key | Boolean | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Rendering option key enabling ambient occlusion. Added in SketchUp 2024.0 as part of "Added rendering options support for Ambient Occlusion". | EXACT SPELLING: one word, CamelCase, no underscore, no 'Enabled' suffix — `"AmbientOcclusion"`. Absent on SketchUp 2023 and earlier; writing it there raises nothing pre-2024 (silent) but is simply ignored. Detect availability with `rendering_options.keys.include?('AmbientOcclusion')` rather than a version number, which also covers the graphics-engine dependency (AO requires the new graphics engine — see Sketchup::View#graphics_engine). |
| `"AmbientOcclusionDistance"` | rendering_option_key | Float | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Rendering option key controlling the ambient occlusion distance. Added in SketchUp 2024.0. | Float. Coerce with `.to_f` on restore — JSON gives Integer for whole numbers and the strict 2024.0+ `[]=` may reject an Integer where a Float is required. |
| `"AmbientOcclusionIntensity"` | rendering_option_key | Float | SketchUp 2024.0 | direct | Rendering option key controlling the ambient occlusion intensity. Added in SketchUp 2024.0. | Float. Same `.to_f` coercion advice. Note the trio (AmbientOcclusion / …Distance / …Intensity) all landed together in 2024.0. |
| `"AmbientOcclusionColor"` | rendering_option_key | Sketchup::Color | SketchUp 2026.0 | encode | Rendering option key for the ambient occlusion colour. Added in SketchUp 2026.0: "Added rendering options support for Ambient Occlusion: Sketchup::RenderingOptions keys added: \"AmbientOcclusionColor\", \"AmbientOcclusionMultiplier\"." | NEWEST rendering-option key and the only new colour key in the 2023–2026 window. Sketchup::Color is NOT JSON-direct: encode as `color.to_a` → [r, g, b, a] Integers 0–255; decode with `Sketchup::Color.new(r, g, b, a)`. Absent on SketchUp 2025 and earlier — feature-detect with `rendering_options.keys.include?('AmbientOcclusionColor')`; assigning it on 2025 raises ArgumentError under the 2024.0 strict rule. |
| `"AmbientOcclusionMultiplier"` | rendering_option_key | Float | SketchUp 2026.0 | direct | Rendering option key for the ambient occlusion multiplier. Added in SketchUp 2026.0. | Float, `.to_f` on restore. Feature-detect via `rendering_options.keys.include?` — it does not exist on 2025 and will raise ArgumentError there. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetAOEnabled` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2024.0 | no | Observer constant passed to Sketchup::RenderingOptionsObserver#onRenderingOptionsChanged identifying a change to the AmbientOcclusion enabled flag. Added SketchUp 2024.0. | Note the abbreviation: 'AO', not 'AmbientOcclusion', in the CONSTANT even though the KEY string spells it out fully. Guard with `defined?(Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetAOEnabled)` — referencing it on 2023 raises NameError. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetAODistance` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2024.0 | no | Observer constant for a change to the ambient occlusion distance. Added SketchUp 2024.0. | Guard with defined?. Only needed if you observe rendering option changes to invalidate a cache. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetAOIntensity` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2024.0 | no | Observer constant for a change to the ambient occlusion intensity. Added SketchUp 2024.0. | Guard with defined?. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetAOColor` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2026.0 | no | Observer constant for a change to the ambient occlusion colour. Added SketchUp 2026.0. | Guard with `defined?(Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetAOColor)`. Note the docs also list a ROPSetAOColorEnabled constant in the full constant table which is not mentioned in the release notes — do not rely on it. |
| `Sketchup::RenderingOptions::ROPSetAOMultiplier` | constant | Integer | SketchUp 2026.0 | no | Observer constant for a change to the ambient occlusion multiplier. Added SketchUp 2026.0. | Guard with defined?. |
| `Sketchup::ShadowInfo#[]=` | instance_method | varies by key | SketchUp 6.0; strict behaviour SketchUp 2026.1 | encode | BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, SketchUp 2026.1: "Modified Sketchup::ShadowInfo#[]= to be stricter and provide better error feedback. It will now raise a KeyError for invalid or read-only keys, and a TypeError for values of an incorrect type." | THE SINGLE MOST LIKELY BREAKAGE FOR AN EXISTING SCENE SERIALISER. The classic implementation is `shadow_info.each_pair { |k,v| out[k] = v }` on export and `data.each { |k,v| si[k] = v }` on import. That round-trip loop now RAISES on 2026.1+ because the exported hash contains READ-ONLY derived keys: SunDirection, SunRise, SunSet, SunRise_time_t, SunSet_time_t (and arguably ShadowTime_time_t). Fix: maintain an explicit WRITABLE allow-list — City, Country, Dark, DayOfYear, DaylightSavings, DisplayNorth, DisplayOnAllFaces, DisplayOnGroundPlane, DisplayShadows, EdgesCastShadows, Latitude, Light, Longitude, NorthAngle, ShadowTime, TZOffset, UseSunForAllShading — and rescue KeyError, TypeError and ArgumentError per key. Also coerce types: Dark/Light/Latitude/Longitude/NorthAngle/TZOffset need `.to_f`, DayOfYear needs `.to_i`, booleans need real true/false. |
| `"ShadowTime"` | shadowinfo_key | Time | SketchUp 6.0 | encode | "the current time used for shadow calculations" — a Ruby Time object. | Time is NOT JSON-direct. Serialise as `time.to_i` (Unix epoch seconds, UTC) and restore with `Time.at(int)`. Note ShadowTime is one of the WRITABLE keys, so it must be in your restore allow-list. Beware timezone: SketchUp interprets ShadowTime in the model's local/site time; round-tripping via to_i/Time.at preserves the instant, which is what you want. |
| `"ShadowTime_time_t"` | shadowinfo_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "ShadowTime in Unix epoch seconds". | Integer — the JSON-friendly mirror of ShadowTime. SAFEST EXPORT VALUE. On restore prefer writing the plain `ShadowTime` key (as a Time) rather than this one; the _time_t variants are derived and are prime candidates for the 2026.1 read-only KeyError. |
| `"SunDirection"` | shadowinfo_key | Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "the calculated direction of the sun" as a Geom::Vector3d. | DERIVED AND READ-ONLY. Do not attempt to restore it — it is computed from Latitude/Longitude/TZOffset/ShadowTime/NorthAngle. Writing it raises KeyError on 2026.1+. If you export it (for diagnostics) encode as `vec.to_a.map(&:to_f)` and explicitly EXCLUDE it from the restore allow-list. |
| `"SunRise"` | shadowinfo_key | Time | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "the sunrise time for the location and date". | DERIVED AND READ-ONLY — exclude from restore. Encode as to_i only if you want it for reporting. |
| `"SunSet"` | shadowinfo_key | Time | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "the sunset time for the location and date". | DERIVED AND READ-ONLY — exclude from restore. |
| `"SunRise_time_t"` | shadowinfo_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "SunRise in Unix epoch seconds". | DERIVED AND READ-ONLY — exclude from restore. Note the underscore-lowercase suffix `_time_t` breaks the CamelCase convention of every other key; copy it exactly if you reference it. |
| `"SunSet_time_t"` | shadowinfo_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "SunSet in Unix epoch seconds". | DERIVED AND READ-ONLY — exclude from restore. |
| `"City"` | shadowinfo_key | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Location name from the geo-location settings; the UI calls it 'Location'. | Writable. Exact key is "City" even though the UI says Location — do not rename. String, JSON-direct. |
| `"Country"` | shadowinfo_key | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Country from the geo-location settings. | Writable. String, JSON-direct. |
| `"Latitude"` | shadowinfo_key | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Geographic latitude from the geo-location settings. | Writable. `.to_f` on restore — a whole-degree latitude in JSON parses as Integer and may raise TypeError on 2026.1+. Restore Latitude and Longitude BEFORE ShadowTime so the derived sun values recompute correctly. |
| `"Longitude"` | shadowinfo_key | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Geographic longitude from the geo-location settings. | Writable. `.to_f` on restore. |
| `"TZOffset"` | shadowinfo_key | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Timezone offset, from the Shadows panel. | Writable. `.to_f` — half-hour timezones exist (e.g. 5.5), so never `.to_i` this. |
| `"NorthAngle"` | shadowinfo_key | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The north direction angle. | Writable. In DEGREES. `.to_f` on restore. Affects the derived SunDirection, so restore it before reading anything back. |
| `"Dark"` | shadowinfo_key | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The shadow darkness level, from the Shadows panel. | Writable. `.to_f`. Note the key is just "Dark", not "Darkness". |
| `"Light"` | shadowinfo_key | Float | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The shadow light level, from the Shadows panel. | Writable. `.to_f`. Key is "Light", not "Lightness". |
| `"DayOfYear"` | shadowinfo_key | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The day number within the year. | Writable. Integer 1–366; `.to_i` on restore. Redundant with ShadowTime — set one or the other, not both, or the later assignment wins unpredictably. Prefer ShadowTime. |
| `"DaylightSavings"` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Daylight saving time indicator. | Writable. Ensure a real true/false — a JSON string "false" is truthy in Ruby and would silently enable DST, and on 2026.1+ would raise TypeError instead (which is preferable, but only on that version). |
| `"DisplayShadows"` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The overall shadow display toggle. | Writable. The master on/off for shadows in the scene. |
| `"DisplayNorth"` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | The north-indicator display setting. | Writable. |
| `"DisplayOnAllFaces"` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Toggle for displaying shadows on all faces. | Writable. Exact capitalisation: DisplayOnAllFaces. |
| `"DisplayOnGroundPlane"` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Toggle for displaying shadows on the ground plane. | Writable. Exact capitalisation: DisplayOnGroundPlane. |
| `"EdgesCastShadows"` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Toggle for edges casting shadows. | Writable. |
| `"UseSunForAllShading"` | shadowinfo_key | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Toggle for using the sun for all shading. | Writable. INTERACTS WITH THE 2025.0 ENVIRONMENT API — when an environment is linked to the sun (Environment#linked_sun?), this setting and the environment's sun link can conflict. Restore ShadowInfo first, then the Environment. |
| `Sketchup::View#graphics_engine` | instance_method | Symbol | SketchUp 2024.0 | READ-ONLY | Added SketchUp 2024.0. Reports which graphics engine the viewport is using. | Indirectly relevant: Ambient Occlusion and Environments (IBL) require the new graphics engine. If your importer sets AO or environment keys on a machine running the legacy engine the values may be stored but not rendered. Guard: `view.respond_to?(:graphics_engine)`. Use it to warn the user rather than to gate the write. |
| `Sketchup::Model#start_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The #start_operation method is used to notify SketchUp that a new operation (which can be undone) is starting." | NOW MANDATORY FOR SCENE WRITES ON 2026.0+. The 2026.0 breaking change states that modifying a Page's Axes, Camera, RenderingOptions or ShadowInfo is undoable and "Scene changes are expected to be called between Sketchup::Model#start_operation and Sketchup::Model#commit_operation to not flood the undo stack", adding that "Extensions that don't follow this requirement will be rejected for updates or publication to the Extension Warehouse." Pass disable_ui = true for a bulk import (large speed win). Wrap your ENTIRE page-restore loop in ONE operation, not one per page. |
| `Sketchup::Model#commit_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0 | no | "The commit_operation method commits an operation for undo." | Pair with start_operation in an ensure block, and call Model#abort_operation on exception so a failed import does not leave a half-built scene set. Note: activating pages (`pages.selected_page =`) inside an operation is unusual but necessary for environment restore; test the undo behaviour on 2026. |
| `Sketchup.version` | class_method | String | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | "Gets the current version of sketchup in decimal form." | Returns a String like "26.0.429". Stamp it into your JSON as `"authored_with_sketchup"` so the importer can reason about provenance. DO NOT parse it for feature gating — use respond_to?/defined? instead, or Sketchup.version_number for numeric comparison. |
| `Sketchup.version_number` | class_method | Integer | SketchUp 6.0 | direct | Retrieves the SketchUp version as a whole number for comparisons. Packs major, minor and build values into an integer, format XXYYYZZZ for versions 6.0–2015 and XXYZZZZZZZ for 2016 and later. | Use ONLY where respond_to? cannot help — e.g. distinguishing SketchUp 2025.0.2 (where `Environments#current = nil` was fixed) from 2025.0.0, since the method exists in both. The 2016+ format is XXYZZZZZZZ so major-version thresholds are e.g. `Sketchup.version_number >= 2500000000` for SU2025 — verify the exact threshold empirically on a target install rather than trusting arithmetic, and prefer respond_to? everywhere else. |
| `Sketchup.open_file` | class_method | Integer or false | SketchUp 6.0; with_status: SketchUp 2021.0; show_version_warning_dialog: SketchUp 2026.0 | no | Opens a SketchUp model file. CHANGED in 2026.0: "Added `:show_version_warning_dialog` optional argument to Sketchup.open_file to control whether the version compatibility dialog can appear." | DIRECTLY USEFUL for a cross-model scene transfer tool that opens a source .skp headlessly to harvest scenes. On 2026.0+ pass `show_version_warning_dialog: false` to stop a modal blocking a batch run. Guard by version — passing an unknown keyword on 2025 raises ArgumentError. Pattern: `args = { with_status: true }; args[:show_version_warning_dialog] = false if Sketchup.version.to_i >= 26; Sketchup.open_file(path, **args)`. |
| `Sketchup.send_action` | class_method | Boolean | SketchUp 6.0; deprecated SketchUp 2026.2 | no | DEPRECATED in SketchUp 2026.2: "Deprecated Sketchup.send_action. This method is no longer being actively maintained due to its asynchronous nature and fragility." | RELEVANT IF YOUR EXISTING CODE USES IT. Older scene tools call `Sketchup.send_action('viewZoomExtents:')` or similar around scene capture. It is now deprecated and asynchronous, meaning it may run AFTER your Page#update, capturing the wrong view. Replace with direct API calls (e.g. `view.zoom_extents`) before the deprecation becomes a removal. |
| `Sketchup.mdi?` | class_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2026.2 | READ-ONLY | "Can be used to determine if SketchUp is running as MDI or SDI." Added SketchUp 2026.2. | Relevant to a cross-model tool: in MDI mode multiple models are open at once and `Sketchup.active_model` can change under you between calls. Capture the Model object into a local variable at the start of your import and use that, never re-calling Sketchup.active_model mid-loop. Guard: `Sketchup.respond_to?(:mdi?)`. |
| `Sketchup::Style#duplicate` | instance_method | Sketchup::Style | SketchUp 2026.2 | no | "The #duplicate method creates a copy of the style with all its properties." Added SketchUp 2026.2. | Useful for a scene importer that must not mutate an existing shared style: duplicate, rename, apply. Guard: `style.respond_to?(:duplicate)`. Same-model only — it does not help you move a style across models; use Style#path + Styles#add_style for that. |
| `Sketchup::Material#duplicate` | instance_method | Sketchup::Material | SketchUp 2026.2 | no | Creates a copy of the material with all its properties. Added SketchUp 2026.2. | Peripheral to scenes but listed for completeness of the 2026.2 additions. Guard with respond_to?. |
| `Sketchup::Overlay` | constant | Class | SketchUp 2023.0 | no | New class in SketchUp 2023.0. Camera aspect_ratio interacts with viewport size. From 2025.0 all screen coordinates are LOGICAL pixels, so a width/height that produced a given output on 2024 produces a different physical result on 2025+ at non-100% DPI. Pass `logical_pixels: false` explicitly on 2025+ to keep physical-pixel semantics. Guard the keyword by version — it raises ArgumentError on 2024. |

| `Sketchup::Face#coplanar_with?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | Added SketchUp 2025.0. | Not scene state. Listed for completeness of 2025.0 additions. |

For cross-model transfer serialise `{layer_name => page.get_drawingelement_visibility_equivalent}` by pairing with Layer#visible? — better, iterate ALL model layers and record `page.layers.include?(layer)` XOR `layer.visible?` semantics explicitly, then restore with `page.set_visibility(layer, bool)`. |
| `Sketchup::Page#layer_folders` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::LayerFolder> or nil | SketchUp 2021.0 | encode | "retrieves the hidden layer folders associated with a page". | UNCHANGED 2023–2026. Restore with Page#set_visibility, which accepts a LayerFolder as of 2021.0. Guard: `page.respond_to?(:layer_folders)`. |
