| `JSON (stdlib)` | constant | Module | Available in all supported SketchUp versions (SketchUp 2023 ships Ruby 2.7.x; SketchUp 2024–2026 ship Ruby 3.2.x) | direct | Ruby standard library JSON module. SketchUp 2024.0 upgraded Ruby to 3.2.2; there has been no further Ruby upgrade in any 2025 or 2026 release note, so SketchUp 2025 and 2026 run Ruby 3.2.x with the JSON version bundled in that Ruby. | STDLIB IMPLICATIONS: (1) `require 'json'` works on 2023 (Ruby 2.7) through 2026 (Ruby 3.2) — no change needed. (2) On Ruby 3.2 `JSON.parse` still returns String keys by default; do not rely on symbol keys. (3) Ruby 3.x removed the automatic `Object#taint`/`untaint` and changed some Hash iteration/keyword-argument semantics — if your 2023-era code uses `def foo(opts = {})` and callers pass keywords, that silently changed between Ruby 2.7 and 3.2; audit any options-hash APIs in your serialiser. (4) Ruby 3.2 makes `Struct` keyword_init changes and removed `File.exists?` deprecation warnings differently — prefer `File.exist?`. (5) Encoding: write JSON as UTF-8 explicitly (`File.write(path, json, mode: 'wb')` or `.encode('UTF-8')`) because SketchUp attribute Strings on Windows can carry a non-UTF-8 external encoding; call `.force_encoding('UTF-8')` on the String you read back from `Model#get_attribute` before `JSON.parse`, otherwise JSON::ParserError on non-ASCII scene names. (6) Do NOT `require` a gem-installed newer json — SketchUp does not ship a usable gem environment for extensions. |
| `Ruby version shipped with SketchUp` | flag | String | Ruby 3.2.2 landed in SketchUp 2024.0 | READ-ONLY | Per the official release notes: SketchUp 2023.0 shipped Ruby 2.7.x (2023.0.2 upgraded Ruby to 2.7.7 per SketchUp staff on the forum); SketchUp 2024.0 states "Upgrade Ruby to 3.2.2"; no Ruby upgrade line appears under 2024.0.1, 2024.0.2, 2025.0, 2025.0.2, 2025.0.3, 2026.0, 2026.1 or 2026.2. Subsequent releases only bumped OpenSSL (2024.0.2 → 3.2.2, 2025.0 → 3.3.1, 2025.0.2 → 3.4.1). | ANSWER TO 'does 2026 ship Ruby 3.x': YES — SketchUp 2026 ships Ruby 3.2.x (3.2.2 per the release notes; some users report RUBY_VERSION reporting a 3.2.x patch such as 3.2.5, so read RUBY_VERSION at runtime rather than assuming the patch level). NOT Ruby 3.4 — a Trimble Developer Announcement by Craig Trickett dated 3 August 2026 states "In an upcoming release, we will be updating our Ruby version to 3.4" and "We will soon be shipping this Ruby update to a SketchUp Labs release", so 3.4 is FUTURE, Labs-first, and not in 2026.x shipping builds. The practical Ruby boundary for a serialiser is 2023 (Ruby 2.7) vs 2024+ (Ruby 3.2) — that is where keyword-argument and frozen-string-literal behaviour changes bite, not anywhere in 2025/2026. |
| `Attribute dictionaries / SKP file format` | flag | Boolean, Integer, Float, Length, nil, String, Time, Array, Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d | SketchUp 6.0 (unchanged through SketchUp 2026.2) | direct | The documented set of types that can be stored in a SketchUp attribute dictionary. No release note from SketchUp 2023.0 through 2026.2 changes this list, changes attribute dictionary behaviour on the Sketchup:: side, or announces an SKP file format change relevant to attribute storage. | ANSWER TO 'did 2026 change attribute dictionaries or the file format for cross-model transfer': NO on the SketchUp side. The ONLY attribute-dictionary work in the window is on the LAYOUT side in 2026.0 — Layout::Entity/Document/Page gained #set_attribute, #get_attribute, #delete_attribute and #attribute_dictionary, plus a new Layout::Dictionary class. That is new capability for LayOut documents, not a change to Sketchup:: model attributes. The only Sketchup:: change is the convenience method AttributeDictionary#empty? in 2025.0. Hash is NOT a permitted value type, so continue storing your scene payload as a single JSON String. Cross-model transfer of a model-level attribute dictionary is unaffected by SKP version as long as the target SketchUp can open the file at all. |
| `Sketchup::Model#drawing_element_visible?` | instance_method | Boolean | SketchUp 2020.0 (regression fixed SketchUp 2026.0) | READ-ONLY | BUG FIX in SketchUp 2026.0: "Fixed SketchUp 2021.1 regression in Sketchup::Model#drawing_element_visible? throwing an exception when the last element of an instance path was a Sketchup::Group or Sketchup::ComponentInstance." | RELEVANT IF YOUR SERIALISER WALKS INSTANCE PATHS to record per-scene entity visibility. On SketchUp 2021.1 through 2025.x this raises when the leaf of the InstancePath is a Group or ComponentInstance — exactly the common case. Wrap in begin/rescue and treat the exception as 'unknown' on those versions; the call is reliable only from 2026.0. |
| `Geom::Transformation#inverse / #invert!` | instance_method | Geom::Transformation | SketchUp 2026.0 (extended SketchUp 2026.1) | no | BREAKING CHANGE, SketchUp 2026.0: "Additional error checking is performed regarding non-invertible transforms (typically this means the matrix has zero scale on one or more axes). Calling Geom::Transformation#inverse or Geom::Transformation#invert! with a non-invertible transform will raise an ArgumentError. In addition, calling Sketchup::ComponentInstance#transformation= or Sketchup::Group#transformation= with a non-invertible transform as an argument will also raise an ArgumentError." Extended in SketchUp 2026.1 to ComponentInstance#transform!, Group#transform!, Image#transform!, Image#transformation=, Entities#transform_entities and Entities#add_instance. | MATTERS IF YOU SERIALISE TRANSFORMS ALONGSIDE SCENES. A 16-float transformation array round-tripped through JSON can lose enough precision, or carry a degenerate scale, that reconstructing it produces a non-invertible matrix. On 2026.0+ that now RAISES ArgumentError where it previously failed silently. Validate before applying: check the determinant, or wrap every `transformation=`/`transform!` in a rescue ArgumentError. Serialise transforms with full Float precision (JSON default is fine) and never round-trip through a formatted String. |
| `Sketchup::View#line_width=` | instance_method | Float | SketchUp 6.0 (behaviour changed SketchUp 2026.0) | encode | BUG FIX / BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, SketchUp 2026.0: "Fixed a crash when setting line width with Sketchup::View#line_width= to 0.0 with the new graphics engine. Negative values will raise an ArgumentError and positive values will be clamped to a minimum of 1.0." | Only relevant if your scene tool draws a viewport preview. A 0.0 or negative width from a config file now raises on 2026.0+ instead of crashing (2026.0) or being ignored (earlier). Clamp your own values to >= 1.0. |
| `Sketchup::Tool#onKeyDown` | instance_method | Object | SketchUp 6.0 (regressed 2026.0 Windows, fixed 2026.1) | no | REGRESSION AND FIX: introduced in SketchUp 2026.0 on Windows where the event did not trigger when Return/Enter was pressed; fixed in SketchUp 2026.1. | If your scene tool has a modal Tool that confirms on Enter, it is broken specifically on SketchUp 2026.0 Windows. Provide an alternative confirm path (button in an HtmlDialog, or handle onReturn/onUserText) for that build. |

### Cross-model data retrieval  —  123 entries

| Name | Kind | Value type | Ver | Ser. | Description | Serialise / rebuild |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Sketchup::Skp.read_guid` | class_method | String | 2021.0 | READ-ONLY | Reads the GUID (globally unique identifier) for an external SketchUp model file WITHOUT loading it. The module overview states verbatim: "The Skp module is used to read metadata from external SketchUp files without loading the whole file." GUIDs let you determine whether a ComponentDefinition and a Model match, so components can be reused rather than duplicated on insertion. Raises ArgumentError if the file is missing or is not a valid SketchUp model. | THIS IS THE ONLY RUBY API THAT READS ANYTHING OUT OF AN UNOPENED .skp. It returns only the GUID — there is no read_attribute / read_pages / read_thumbnail sibling. Use it as a cheap cache-validity check: store model.guid inside your sidecar JSON at write time (read the guid AFTER saving, since it changes on modify+save), then on read compare Sketchup::Skp.read_guid(path) against the stored value. If they match, trust the sidecar and skip the expensive definitions.load probe entirely. Wrap in begin/rescue ArgumentError. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#load` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Loads a .skp file from disk into the active model's DefinitionList as a ComponentDefinition. Raises IOError if the file is invalid or empty; raises RuntimeError on version conflicts (file saved by a newer SketchUp). CRITICAL BEHAVIOUR (confirmed on the forum): it adds the definition to the list but places NO instance in model.entities — nothing appears in the viewport. The loaded definition's #entities contains model B's ROOT entities, so a component that sat at model B's root becomes a nested instance inside the new definition (the 'double wrapped component' effect). | ROUTE 1/2 VERDICT: The returned ComponentDefinition does NOT carry model B's model-level attribute dictionaries (those set with model.set_attribute). Nor does it carry model.description, model.pages, model.styles, model.shadow_info or model.rendering_options. It DOES carry dictionaries that were attached to ComponentDefinition objects inside model B, reachable by walking defn.entities.grep(Sketchup::ComponentInstance) then inst.definition.attribute_dictionaries. Loading also drags model B's nested definitions and materials into model A, so cleanup matters. ALWAYS load from a unique temp copy (FileUtils.cp into Sketchup.temp_dir) because load caches on path/GUID and will return a stale definition for a path it has seen before. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#load (allow_newer)` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition | 2021.0 | READ-ONLY | Overload that permits loading a .skp saved by a NEWER SketchUp version than the running one. Documented warning: when a newer model is loaded some information may have been skipped, and extensions should be careful not to save over the file they loaded from. | Pass allow_newer: true when probing user files of unknown vintage — otherwise a 2027-saved file raises RuntimeError and your read fails. NOTE the documented default is false despite an erroneous docs statement to the contrary (api-issue-tracker #997). The keyword is spelled exactly `allow_newer:` (snake case, trailing colon). Never write back to a file you loaded with allow_newer: true. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#load_from_url` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition | 7.0 | READ-ONLY | Downloads and loads a .skp component from a URL. Throws an exception on error when no load handler is supplied. | Not relevant to a local disk workflow. Would allow reading a scene payload from a network-hosted .skp if you ever host template models. Blocking call; no timeout control. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#load_from_url (with handler)` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition | 7.0 | READ-ONLY | As above but reports progress through a handler object implementing onPercentChange, cancelled?, onSuccess and onFailure. | Handler method names are exactly `onPercentChange`, `cancelled?`, `onSuccess`, `onFailure` (camelCase, not snake_case). Only relevant if scene payloads are ever served remotely. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#import` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition | 2021.1 | READ-ONLY | Imports a NON-SketchUp 3D model file (via C API importers found in the Importers/ directory) as a ComponentDefinition. Raises IOError if the file is missing, ArgumentError if no importer is found for the extension. | Do NOT use this for .skp files — use #load. Listed here only so it is not mistaken for a .skp route. Unlike Model#import it returns a definition rather than placing geometry. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#remove` | instance_method | Boolean | 2018 | no | Removes a definition AND all of its instances from the model. Raises ArgumentError if the definition is currently open for editing (is part of model.active_path). | THE cleanup call for route 1/2. Only exists in SketchUp 2018 and later — on 2015-2017 it raises NoMethodError. Call it inside an ensure block immediately after reading the payload. DANGER: api-issue-tracker #75 documents model corruption and crash when remove is called inside a start_operation and the user subsequently presses Undo — so remove inside an operation you then ABORT (never commit-then-undo). Fallback if remove raises: definition.entities.clear! — SketchUp does not permit empty definitions and self-purges them. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#purge_unused` | instance_method | Sketchup::DefinitionList | 6.0 | no | Removes ALL unused definitions from the model. Documented warning: "Silently purging can cause data loss." | DO NOT USE for cleanup after a probe read. It is indiscriminate — it will also delete every legitimately unused definition the user has parked in their In-Model component browser, which is destructive and unrecoverable within your operation. Use DefinitionList#remove(defn) targeted at your own definition instead. Note: because it removes unused definitions, a payload carrier definition with NO instance in model B is vulnerable to being purged by the user or by any other extension — hence the recommendation to place a hidden locked instance in model B's root. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#[]` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition or nil | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Retrieves a definition by integer index (0..length-1), by String name, or by unique GUID String. Returns nil if not found. | Use the String-name overload to find or re-find your carrier definition: model.definitions['Na_SceneImageExporter_Carrier']. Because the same method accepts index/name/guid, passing an Integer that happens to be a valid index will NOT do a name lookup — coerce your key to String explicitly. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#at` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition or nil | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Alias for #[] with identical index / name / guid overloads and behaviour. | No functional difference from #[]. Prefer #[] for readability. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#add` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition | 6.0 | no | Adds a new, empty component definition with the given name String to the list. | This is how you create the payload CARRIER definition inside model B: defn = model.definitions.add('Na_SceneImageExporter_Carrier'). An empty definition is fragile — add at least one entity (entities.add_cpoint(ORIGIN) is the cheapest) or SketchUp may drop it. If the name already exists this returns/creates a name-collided definition — check with definitions[name] first. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#each` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Iterates every definition in the list. Documented warning: "Don't remove content from this collection while iterating." | To find a previously loaded definition by its source path use definitions.find { |d| d.path == filepath } — but snapshot to an Array first (definitions.to_a) if you intend to remove anything, or you will skip elements and can crash. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#length` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Number of definitions in the list. | Useful as a pollution assertion: record definitions.length before the probe and confirm it returns to the same value after cleanup. Note it will not match if model B contained nested definitions that also got loaded. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#size` | instance_method | Integer | 2014 | READ-ONLY | Alias of #length. | Added as an explicit alias in SketchUp 2014; on 6.0-2013 only #length exists. Use #length for maximum compatibility. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#count` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Definition count. Since SketchUp 2014 this comes from Ruby's Enumerable mix-in rather than being a native method. | Because it is Enumerable#count from 2014 onward it walks the collection — O(n) rather than O(1). Prefer #length in hot loops. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#unique_name` | instance_method | String | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Generates a name not already in use, derived from the supplied base String (e.g. "Joe" becomes "Joe #2"). | Use when creating a throwaway carrier so you never collide with a user's component. But for the payload carrier you WANT a stable, predictable name so the reader can find it — do not use unique_name there. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Attaches a DefinitionsObserver to receive notifications about definitions being added or removed. | Detach any of your own observers BEFORE running a probe load, or your onComponentAdded callback will fire for the temporary definition and for every nested definition dragged in from model B. Re-attach in the ensure block. |
| `Sketchup::DefinitionList#remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Detaches a previously attached observer. | Pair with add_observer around the probe. See above. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#attribute_dictionaries` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries or nil | 6.0 | direct | Returns the AttributeDictionaries collection attached to this definition. Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. Returns nil when the definition has no dictionaries at all (the collection is not instantiated until needed). | THIS IS THE CROSS-MODEL DATA CHANNEL. Dictionaries stored here survive: definition -> model.save / ComponentDefinition#save_as -> definitions.load in another model. Confirmed by eneroth3 ("A component instance doesn't correspond to a model, but a component definition do. If you store the attributes to the definition, they'll be exported with the model") and by the save-as workaround in the 'Load definition from file will lost information' thread where d.attribute_dictionaries['dynamic_attributes'] is readable after load. ALWAYS nil-guard: `defn.attribute_dictionaries&.each` — a bare .each on nil raises. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#attribute_dictionary` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionary or nil | 6.0 | direct | Retrieves the named dictionary on this definition. Returns nil if absent unless the second argument is true, in which case an empty dictionary is created and returned. | Write side: defn.attribute_dictionary('Na_SceneImageExporter_Payload', true). Read side: defn.attribute_dictionary('Na_SceneImageExporter_Payload') and test for nil. The `create` flag defaults to FALSE — forgetting it is the single most common cause of 'my write silently did nothing'. Dictionary names are case-sensitive Ruby Strings and must match byte-for-byte between writer and reader; namespace them as AuthorNamespace_SpecificPlugin_DictionaryName per DanRathbun's convention. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#set_attribute` | instance_method | Object (the value written) | 6.0 | direct | Sets an attribute on this definition, creating the named dictionary if it does not exist. Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. | The convenience path — set_attribute auto-creates the dictionary, unlike attribute_dictionary(name) which does not. For chunked JSON prefer grabbing the dictionary once with attribute_dictionary(name, true) and using dict[key]= in a loop: set_attribute re-resolves the dictionary by name on every call, which is measurably slower over hundreds of chunk keys. Value must be one of the supported types (see AttributeDictionary#[]=) — a Hash will NOT round-trip. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#get_attribute` | instance_method | Object or nil | 6.0 | direct | Retrieves an attribute value from the named dictionary on this definition, returning default_value when the dictionary or key is absent. | Safest reader — never raises for a missing dictionary. Use the default argument to distinguish 'absent' from 'stored nil': get_attribute(DICT, 'chunk_count', 0). |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#delete_attribute` | instance_method | Object | 6.0 | no | Deletes an entire dictionary from the definition, or a single key within it. Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. | Use the one-argument form to wipe a stale payload wholesale before rewriting — safer than deleting chunk keys individually, because an old payload with MORE chunks than the new one leaves orphan chunk_NNNN keys that inflate the file forever. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#entities` | instance_method | Sketchup::Entities | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "retrieves a collection of all the entities in the component definition". For a definition produced by definitions.load(path), this collection IS model B's root-level entities. | ROUTE 2 MECHANISM. After defn = model.definitions.load(temp_path), do defn.entities.grep(Sketchup::ComponentInstance) to find the hidden carrier instance placed in model B's root, then read carrier.definition.attribute_dictionaries. Recurse into inst.definition.entities if the carrier may be nested deeper. Groups appear as Sketchup::Group — grep for both Sketchup::ComponentInstance and Sketchup::Group if the carrier might be a group (a Group's definition is reached via group.definition, 2015+, or group.entities.parent on older versions). |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#instances` | instance_method | Array<Sketchup::ComponentInstance> | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Returns an Array of every ComponentInstance that references this definition. | After definitions.load the returned Array is EMPTY, because load places no instance. That emptiness is exactly what makes the loaded definition safe to remove — but also what makes it a purge_unused target. Use defn.instances.empty? as a sanity assertion that load did not accidentally place geometry. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#path` | instance_method | String | 6.0 | direct | "retrieve the path where the component was loaded". Returns an empty String for internally-created (non-file-backed) components. | THIS IS THE CACHE KEY that makes definitions.load misbehave. SketchUp associates the loaded definition with this path, and a subsequent load(same_path) returns the EXISTING definition rather than re-reading the file. Defeat it by copying to a unique temp filename per read. You can also use definitions.find { |d| d.path == filepath } to detect a pre-existing association before loading. Note ComponentDefinition#save_as mutates #path without recording to the undo stack (api-issue-tracker #74). |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#name` | instance_method | String | 6.0 | direct | The definition's name as shown in the Component Browser. | When model B is loaded as a definition its name is derived from model B's filename, and SketchUp will de-duplicate with a ' #2' suffix if a definition of that name already exists in model A. Never match your carrier by the loaded wrapper's name — match by the presence of your dictionary instead, which is collision-proof. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#name=` | attribute | String | 6.0 | direct | Sets the definition name. | Setting a name that collides raises or auto-renames depending on version — do not rely on it. Irrelevant to the read path; relevant only when creating the carrier in model B. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#description` | instance_method | String | 6.0 | direct | The definition's description field. | EVIDENCE ANCHOR for the feasibility verdict: users report d.description returns nil after Sketchup.active_model.definitions.load('model.skp'), because model B's MODEL-level description does not become the loaded DEFINITION's description. The same wall applies to model-level attribute dictionaries. However, when a definition is exported via save_as and then loaded, its OWN description does survive. Do not use description as a payload store — it is user-visible in the Component Browser and length-limited in the UI. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#description=` | attribute | String | 6.0 | direct | Sets the definition description. | Avoid as a data channel — visible to users, editable by users, and not designed for structured data. Use an attribute dictionary. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#guid` | instance_method | String | 2014 | direct | Base-64 encoded unique identifier for the definition. Documented to change after the definition is modified and the component edit is exited. | Pairs with Sketchup::Skp.read_guid(filepath) — the documented usage example for read_guid is exactly comparing definition.guid against the file's guid to decide whether a reload is needed. GOTCHA (forum-confirmed): inside a start_operation the guid does NOT change until the operation commits, so a modify-then-check-guid loop inside one operation will see a stale value. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#load_time` | instance_method | Time | 2025.0 | direct | Returns the time at which this component definition was loaded. | New in 2025.0. Useful for staleness detection on cached probe results, and to distinguish a freshly loaded definition from one already resident in model A. Guard with respond_to?(:load_time) for pre-2025 compatibility. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#group?` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | True when the definition backs Group elements rather than components. | When walking defn.entities looking for the carrier, skip group-backed definitions if you deliberately used a Component (not a Group) as the carrier — Groups get their definitions silently re-made-unique on edit, which can orphan attributes. PREFER A COMPONENT AS THE CARRIER, NOT A GROUP. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#image?` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | True when the definition represents an imported raster image entity. | Filter these out when walking defn.entities — images are definition-backed and will otherwise show up in your grep for ComponentInstance-like content. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#internal?` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | True when the definition is internal to the Component Browser (not backed by an external file). | A definition created with definitions.add is internal? == true and path == ''. A definition produced by definitions.load is internal? == false with a populated path. Use as an assertion that your load actually read from disk. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#hidden?` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | direct | "determine if this component definition is hidden in the component browser". | Set the carrier definition hidden so it does not clutter the user's In-Model component list in model B. Note this is a DEFINITION-browser flag, distinct from ComponentInstance#hidden= which hides the geometry in the viewport — you want BOTH. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#live_component?` | instance_method | Boolean | 2021.0 | READ-ONLY | Identifies SketchUp Live Components. | Live Components carry their own reserved dictionaries and regenerate their geometry — never attach a payload to one, and skip them when walking entities. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#manifold?` | instance_method | Boolean | 2026.2 | READ-ONLY | Determines whether the definition is a manifold solid, without requiring an instance. | New in 2026.2, replaces the deprecated ComponentInstance#manifold?. Irrelevant to payload transport; listed for completeness of the 2026 surface. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#invalidate_bounds` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | "forces the update of the bounding box" for the definition; documented as needing to be called within an operation. | Not needed for a read-only probe. Only relevant if you ever mutate carrier geometry. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#save_as` | instance_method | Boolean | 7.0 | no | Saves this definition out to the given .skp path. DanRathbun's definitive characterisation: when a definition is saved as a .skp, "that definition becomes the global model in the new file". | THE KEY ASYMMETRY. Because the definition becomes the new file's global model, any dictionaries you want in the exported file must be copied DOWN onto the definition BEFORE calling save_as — model-level dictionaries are not carried automatically. And the reverse never happens either: on re-insertion "the dictionaries attached to the saved file remain as definition-level attributes" and will "NOT be automatically recopied up to the model object level". SIDE EFFECT: save_as mutates definition#path and this change is NOT recorded on the undo stack (api-issue-tracker #74) — so an abort_operation will not restore the old path. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#save_as (versioned)` | instance_method | Boolean | 2022.0 | no | Saves the definition to a .skp in a specific SketchUp file-format version. | Use Sketchup::Model::VERSION_2021 etc. constants. Down-saving to an older format may silently drop newer data — verify your payload dictionary survives if you down-save. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#save_copy` | instance_method | Boolean | 2022.0 | no | Saves a copy of the definition to disk WITHOUT changing the definition's associated #path. | PREFER THIS OVER save_as when exporting a payload carrier, precisely because it leaves #path alone and therefore does not corrupt the DefinitionList path-association cache or leave an un-undoable path mutation behind. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#save_copy (versioned)` | instance_method | Boolean | 2022.0 | no | Saves a copy in a specified SketchUp file-format version without changing #path. | Same caveats as save_as(path, version) regarding down-save data loss. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#count_instances` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "count the number of unique component instances" of this definition. | Returns 0 for a definition produced by definitions.load. A cheap pre-remove assertion. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#count_used_instances` | instance_method | Integer | 2016 | READ-ONLY | "count the total number of component instances...taking into account the full hierarchy". | Use this rather than count_instances when deciding whether removing a definition will destroy user geometry — it accounts for instances nested inside other definitions. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#add_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Attaches a DefinitionObserver. | Not needed for the probe. Detach your own observers before probing to avoid spurious callbacks. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentDefinition#remove_observer` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Detaches a DefinitionObserver. | Pair with add_observer. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentInstance#definition` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentDefinition | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Retrieves the ComponentDefinition backing this instance. | THE HOP THAT MAKES ROUTE 2 WORK. After defn = model.definitions.load(path), for each inst in defn.entities.grep(Sketchup::ComponentInstance) call inst.definition.attribute_dictionaries — the payload lives on the DEFINITION, never on the instance. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentInstance#attribute_dictionaries` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries or nil | 6.0 | no | Dictionaries attached to the INSTANCE (not the definition). Inherited from Sketchup::Entity. | DO NOT STORE THE PAYLOAD HERE. The 'How to bring along attributes dictionaries with the instance?' thread is entirely about this mistake: instance-level dictionaries are LOST when the component is saved out and re-imported, because an instance has no counterpart in the exported model. DanRathbun's corrective code is literally `instance.definition.set_attribute(dict, attribute, value)`. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentInstance#hidden=` | attribute | Boolean | 6.0 | direct | Hides or shows the instance in the viewport. Inherited from Sketchup::DrawingElement. | Set true on the carrier instance in model B so it is invisible to the user. A hidden instance STILL counts as a use of its definition, which protects the carrier definition from purge_unused — that is precisely why an instance is needed rather than a bare definition. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentInstance#locked=` | attribute | Boolean | 6.0 | direct | Locks the instance so the user cannot move, edit or (easily) delete it. | Set true on the carrier instance as a second layer of protection. A locked instance can still be erased via Edit > Delete after unlocking, so treat the carrier as best-effort, not guaranteed. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentInstance#name=` | attribute | Sketchup::ComponentInstance | 6.0 | direct | Sets the instance name (Entity Info name, distinct from the definition name). | Give the carrier instance a recognisable name so a user inspecting Outliner understands what it is, e.g. 'Na_SceneImageExporter_Carrier (do not delete)'. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentInstance#persistent_id` | instance_method | Integer | 2014 | direct | Identifier that persists across sessions and save/load cycles within the SAME model. | Persistent IDs are stable within one model but are NOT preserved across definitions.load into a different model — do not use them as cross-model foreign keys for scene layer/tag visibility state. Use names or GUIDs for cross-model identity. |
| `Sketchup::ComponentInstance#guid` | instance_method | String | 2014 | direct | Base-64 encoded unique id for the instance. | Instance GUIDs are not a reliable cross-model key either. Prefer definition names plus your own generated UUID stored in the payload dictionary. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#[]` | instance_method | Object or nil | 6.0 | direct | "used to retrieve the attribute with a given key". Returns nil for an absent key. | Faster than get_attribute in a loop because the dictionary is already resolved. Keys are case-sensitive Strings — 'ChunkCount' and 'chunk_count' are different keys. Cannot distinguish a stored nil from an absent key; store an explicit sentinel or a chunk_count key instead. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#[]=` | instance_method | Object | 6.0 | direct | "used to set the value of an attribute with a given key". | SUPPORTED VALUE TYPES (documented): Boolean, Integer, Float, Length, nil, String, Time, Array, Geom::Point3d, Geom::Vector3d. HASH IS NOT SUPPORTED — a Hash must be JSON-serialised to a String first. Arrays may nest supported types. Symbols are not supported and will be coerced or dropped. This is the write call for each JSON chunk: dict['chunk_0000'] = slice. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#delete_key` | instance_method | Object or nil | 6.0 | no | "used to delete an attribute with a given key". Returns the deleted value or nil. | Note the name is delete_key, NOT delete — Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries#delete deletes a whole dictionary, Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#delete_key deletes one key. Getting these confused silently destroys the wrong thing. Never call it while inside an #each over the same dictionary. Snapshot dict.keys to an Array first. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#each` | instance_method | Object | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "iterate through all of the attributes", yielding key and value. Documented warning: "Don't remove content from this collection while iterating over it with #each. This would change the size of the collection and cause elements to be skipped." | Key ORDER IS NOT GUARANTEED to be insertion order — never reassemble chunked JSON by iterating; always reassemble by index from 0 to chunk_count-1 using a zero-padded key format so ordering is explicit. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#each_pair` | instance_method | Object | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "an alias for #each". | Identical semantics and identical ordering caveat. DanRathbun's model-to-definition copy idiom uses this: dict.each_pair { |key, val| dictcopy[key] = val }. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#each_key` | instance_method | nil | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "used to iterate through all of the attribute keys". | Returns nil, not the dictionary — do not chain off it. Same no-mutation-while-iterating rule. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#keys` | instance_method | Array<String> | 6.0 | direct | "retrieve an array with all of the attribute keys". | Returns a plain Ruby Array snapshot, so it IS safe to iterate this while calling delete_key — that is the correct idiom for wiping stale chunks: dict.keys.grep(/\Achunk_/).each { |k| dict.delete_key(k) }. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#values` | instance_method | Array<Object> | 6.0 | direct | "retrieve an array with all of the attribute values". | Order corresponds to #keys but is not guaranteed to be insertion order — do not join #values to reassemble chunked data. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#length` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "retrieve the size (number of elements)" in the dictionary. | Use to sanity-check a payload dictionary: expected length == chunk_count + number of metadata keys. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#size` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "an alias of #length". | No behavioural difference. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#count` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Element count; "inherited from the Enumerable mix-in module". | Being Enumerable#count it walks the collection — prefer #length. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#empty?` | instance_method | Boolean | 2025.0 | READ-ONLY | "check if the attribute dictionary is empty". | New in SketchUp 2025.0 — on earlier versions use dict.length.zero?. Guard with respond_to?(:empty?) if you support 2021-2024. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary#name` | instance_method | String | 6.0 | direct | "retrieve the name of an attribute dictionary". | Used when enumerating entity.attribute_dictionaries to discover which dictionaries an unknown entity carries: ent.attribute_dictionaries&.map(&:name). |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionary (nested dictionaries)` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionary or nil | 6.0 | direct | An AttributeDictionary is itself a Sketchup::Entity, so it inherits the full attribute API and can therefore own CHILD dictionaries. DanRathbun: "An attribute dictionary is also an Entity, so it can have child attribute dictionaries." | Enables hierarchical storage — e.g. one child dictionary per scene, keyed by scene name, instead of one giant blob. This is genuinely useful for a scene exporter: it lets you read/update ONE scene without rewriting the whole payload. CAUTION: nested dictionaries are poorly exercised by third-party tools and by SketchUp's own file repair; the flat chunked-key approach is more battle-tested. Also, an AttributeDictionary entity has an entityID and persistent_id like any other entity. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries#[]` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionary or nil | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "Get an AttributeDictionary by name. Returns nil if there is none with the given name." | Reached via entity.attribute_dictionaries['Name']. Because entity.attribute_dictionaries itself returns nil when the entity has no dictionaries at all, always use the safe-navigation form entity.attribute_dictionaries&.[]('Name') or prefer entity.attribute_dictionary('Name') which handles the nil collection for you. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries#delete` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries | 6.0 | no | "destroys a given AttributeDictionary. This AttributeDictionary can be passed directly or identified by its string name." Raises ArgumentError if you attempt to delete the reserved dictionaries "SU_InstanceSet" or "SU_DefinitionSet". | RESERVED NAMES, exact spelling: "SU_InstanceSet" and "SU_DefinitionSet" — these cannot be deleted and must never be used as your own dictionary name. Also avoid "dynamic_attributes" (Dynamic Components) and NEVER create a dictionary whose name is the empty string "" — DanRathbun warns the DC extension uses such a dictionary and it will interfere. Use delete (not delete_key) to wipe a whole payload dictionary before a rewrite. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries#each` | instance_method | Object | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "used to iterate through all of the attributes dictionaries", yielding each Sketchup::AttributeDictionary. | The discovery loop for an unknown entity. Same don't-mutate-while-iterating rule as everywhere else in this API. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries#length` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "returns the number of attribute dictionary objects in the collection". | Zero-length collections are generally not instantiated at all — you get nil from entity.attribute_dictionaries rather than an empty collection. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries#size` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "an alias of length". | No behavioural difference. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries#count` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Count of dictionaries; inherited from the Enumerable mix-in and aliased to length. | Prefer #length. |
| `Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries.new` | constant | n/a | 6.0 | no | There is no public constructor. "Cannot be instantiated directly as Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries.new"; likewise AttributeDictionary objects can only be created through a factory method (entity.attribute_dictionary(name, true) or entity.set_attribute). | Listed so nobody wastes time trying. The ONLY ways to create a dictionary are entity.attribute_dictionary(name, true) and entity.set_attribute(dict_name, key, value). |
| `Sketchup::Model#attribute_dictionaries` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionaries or nil | 6.0 | direct | "retrieves the AttributeDictionaries object that is associated with the Model". The Model behaves exactly like any other Entity in this respect — DanRathbun: "Every entity's dictionary objects must be accessed via it's own collection...upon the object reference itself." | THIS IS THE STORE THAT DOES NOT TRANSFER. Model-level dictionaries persist correctly through model.save and re-open of THAT model, but they are NOT visible on the ComponentDefinition produced by definitions.load in a different model, and they are NOT recreated at model level when the file is inserted as a component. Treat model-level storage as the canonical local copy only, and always mirror the payload onto a carrier definition for cross-model reads. |
| `Sketchup::Model#attribute_dictionary` | instance_method | Sketchup::AttributeDictionary or nil | 6.0 | direct | Returns the named model-level dictionary, creating it if the second argument is true. | Write side in model B. Remember create defaults to false. Wrap writes in start_operation/commit_operation so the user can undo them and so model.modified? behaves sensibly. |
| `Sketchup::Model#set_attribute` | instance_method | Object | 6.0 | direct | Sets a model-level attribute, creating the dictionary if needed. | Persists across save/close/reopen of the SAME model (users who report otherwise are usually failing to save, or writing after the save). eneroth3's guidance: "Model attributes should be kept to a minimum" because they bloat files and travel with the document — reserve them for genuinely project-specific data such as your scene payload, not global plugin preferences (use Sketchup.write_default or an external JSON in AppData for those). |
| `Sketchup::Model#get_attribute` | instance_method | Object or nil | 6.0 | direct | Retrieves a model-level attribute with an optional default fallback. | Read side when model B IS the active model. Returns defaultvalue when the dictionary or key is missing, so it never raises. |
| `Sketchup::Model#definitions` | instance_method | Sketchup::DefinitionList | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "Retrieves definition list containing all component definitions" in the model. | Entry point for the whole route-1/2 probe: Sketchup.active_model.definitions.load(temp_path). |
| `Sketchup::Model#start_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | "Notifies SketchUp that new undoable operation is starting." The second argument, when true, suppresses SketchUp's tendency to update the user interface after each geometry change. | ALWAYS pass disable_ui = true for a probe load — it is a large speed win and stops the Component Browser flashing. GOTCHA (forum-confirmed): with the operation open, component definition GUIDs do NOT update until commit, and repeated definitions.load of the same file within one operation reuses the existing definition rather than importing fresh copies. Do not attempt two probe reads of the same path inside one operation. Never nest operations; never abort a transparent operation. |
| `Sketchup::Model#abort_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Aborts the current operation, rolling the model back to its state at start_operation without adding an entry to the undo stack. Documented warning: "Never abort transparent operation." | ROUTE 6 VERDICT: abort_operation is the RIGHT wrapper for a temporary probe — the forum consensus is that it exists precisely for "temporary manipulation of model contents and then restoring the model to the original condition without clutter on the undo stack", and it is preferred over commit_operation + Sketchup.undo. BUT there is NO documentation and no forum statement confirming that it rolls back a definitions.load specifically, and the DefinitionList path/GUID association cache is a separate internal cache that an abort is unlikely to clear. THEREFORE: do not rely on it alone. Explicitly call definitions.remove(defn) in an ensure block BEFORE abort_operation, and additionally load from a unique temp path every time so a surviving cache entry cannot poison the next read. Also note aborting still wipes the REDO stack (per Fredo6, the redo stack is actually wiped at commit_operation, but assume the worst). |
| `Sketchup::Model#commit_operation` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Commits the current operation, making it a single undoable step. | Use for the WRITE side in model B (stashing the payload should be undoable). Do NOT use for the probe read in model A — committing pollutes the undo stack with a meaningless 'Read External Scene Data' entry and, worse, a subsequent user Undo after a definition removal is the documented crash path in api-issue-tracker #75. |
| `Sketchup::Model#import` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Loads a file into the model, dispatching on file extension. The Hash-options signature is 2018+; the show_summary Boolean signature is 2017 and earlier. Returns Boolean, NOT the created definition. | ROUTE 3 PARTIAL VERDICT: importing a .skp does NOT put model B's entities into model.entities — "the entities...don't go directly in model.entities. They are packaged in a new component." Same wrapping as definitions.load, but WORSE for a probe: it returns only true/false so you must diff the definition list to find what arrived, it can place geometry, and it renames colliding materials and components. Use definitions.load, never import, for .skp payload reads. 2026.2 added :preserve_origin and :tags_handling options but only for the IFC importer. |
| `Sketchup::Model#path` | instance_method | String | 6.0 | direct | Full file path of the model; an empty String if the model has never been saved. | Always guard with model.path.empty? before deriving a sidecar filename — an unsaved model has no path and your sidecar write will blow up or land in the wrong place. |
| `Sketchup::Model#title` | instance_method | String | 6.0 | direct | Filename without extension, or an empty String for an unsaved model. | Convenient human-readable label to embed in the payload so a user can see which source model a scene set came from. |
| `Sketchup::Model#guid` | instance_method | String | 6.0 | direct | "Retrieves globally unique identifier; changes after modification and save." | THE PAIR FOR Sketchup::Skp.read_guid. Because it changes on every modify-and-save cycle, capture it AFTER model.save when stamping a sidecar, otherwise your validity check will always fail. Its volatility makes it a perfect cache-invalidation token but a terrible permanent identity — generate and store your own UUID in the payload dictionary for stable identity. |
| `Sketchup::Model#modified?` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | True when the model has unsaved changes. | A probe read that leaves model A modified? == true is a failed cleanup — assert this before and after. Note that even a fully aborted operation can flip modified? in some SketchUp versions; if so, warn the user rather than silently saving. |
| `Sketchup::Model#valid?` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "Returns false if the model has been closed." | Essential if you ever hold a Model reference across a Sketchup.open_file — on Windows the old model is destroyed and every reference to it becomes invalid. |
| `Sketchup::Model#save` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Saves the model to its current path, to a given path, or to a given path in a specific SketchUp file-format version. | save(path) CHANGES the model's working file. Use save_copy when you only want a snapshot. Read model.guid only after this returns true. |
| `Sketchup::Model#save_copy` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Saves a copy without changing the working file path. | Useful for producing a probe-able snapshot of the CURRENT model without disturbing the user's file association. |
| `Sketchup::Model#save_thumbnail` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | "Saves thumbnail image to file; supports bmp, jpg, png, tif, pct, gif" — for the CURRENTLY OPEN model only. | ROUTE 4 NOTE: this is the OPEN-model variant and is useless for inspecting a file on disk. The file-on-disk variant is Sketchup.save_thumbnail(skp_filename, image_filename) at module level — see that entry. |
| `Sketchup::Model#close` | instance_method | nil | 2021.0 | no | Closes the model. "On Mac OS, only the active model can be closed." | Added in SketchUp 2021.0, largely to let Mac extensions tidy up the window pile-up caused by Sketchup.open_file. On Windows it does not enable a background-read workflow, because Windows only ever has one model and closing it does not give you a second one to read from. Passing ignore_changes = true discards unsaved user work — never call it on a model you did not create. |
| `Sketchup::Model#pages` | instance_method | Sketchup::Pages | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "Retrieves Pages object containing all pages in the model" — the scenes. | CONFIRMS THE PROBLEM STATEMENT: pages are strictly model-level. A ComponentDefinition has no #pages, so definitions.load can never bring scenes across. Scenes MUST be serialised to JSON and shipped through an attribute dictionary (or a sidecar file); there is no structural route. |
| `Sketchup::Model#entities` | instance_method | Sketchup::Entities | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "Returns Entities object containing entities in the root of model". | Where the hidden carrier instance must live in model B — definitions.load reconstructs exactly this collection as the loaded definition's #entities. |
| `Sketchup::Entities#add_instance` | instance_method | Sketchup::ComponentInstance | 6.0 | no | Places an instance of the given definition at the given transformation. | model.entities.add_instance(carrier_defn, IDENTITY) is what pins the carrier definition into model B's root so it survives purge_unused and is reachable after definitions.load. IDENTITY is a top-level SketchUp constant (Geom::Transformation identity). |
| `Sketchup::Entities#add_cpoint` | instance_method | Sketchup::ConstructionPoint | 6.0 | no | Adds a construction point to the entities collection. | The cheapest legal content for an otherwise-empty carrier definition: defn.entities.add_cpoint(ORIGIN). ORIGIN is a top-level SketchUp constant (Geom::Point3d at 0,0,0). WARNING: api-issue-tracker #75 shows a CPoint inside a definition is one of the three conditions for the remove-inside-operation-then-undo crash — so if you use a cpoint in a carrier, be extra sure you never commit-then-undo the removal. |
| `Sketchup::Entities#clear!` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Removes all entities from the collection. | THE PRE-2018 / FALLBACK CLEANUP for a loaded definition: definition.entities.clear! — SketchUp does not permit empty definitions to exist, so the definition is automatically purged. Use as the rescue path when definitions.remove raises. |
| `Sketchup.open_file` | class_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Opens a .skp file. "Returns true if opening the file succeeded, false otherwise." This original signature is DEPRECATED in favour of the with_status form. | ROUTE 3 VERDICT — UNUSABLE FOR A BACKGROUND READ. On MS Windows "the same window is used for each file, so the previous file gets closed" — calling this DESTROYS model A, discarding its undo stack and prompting the user about unsaved changes. "on the PC, this is the only model that one can have access to via the API" — SketchUp for Desktop on Windows does NOT support multiple simultaneously scriptable models from Ruby. On macOS each file gets its own window and Sketchup.active_model returns whichever the user has focused, which is non-deterministic and unscriptable. There is NO non-destructive variant. Do not build on this. |
| `Sketchup.open_file (with_status)` | class_method | Integer or false | 2021.0 | no | Opens a .skp and returns an integer status code instead of a bare Boolean, or false on failure. "Starting with SketchUp 2021.0 SketchUp attempts to load newer SketchUp models. If a newer model is loaded some information might have been skipped and extensions should be careful to not save over the file they loaded from." | Keyword spelled exactly `with_status:`. Same destructive Windows behaviour as the base form. Compare the return against LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS and LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS_MORE_RECENT. |
| `Sketchup.open_file (show_version_warning_dialog)` | class_method | Integer or false | 2026.0 | no | 2026.0 addition: "Added :show_version_warning_dialog optional argument to Sketchup.open_file to control whether the version compatibility dialog can appear." | Keyword spelled exactly `show_version_warning_dialog:`. Pass false for unattended/batch work so a modal dialog cannot block a script. Requires with_status: true alongside it. Still destructive on Windows — this only suppresses a dialog, it does not make open_file non-destructive. |
| `LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS` | constant | Integer | 2021.0 | direct | Status constant returned by Sketchup.open_file(..., with_status: true) indicating the file loaded cleanly with no version concerns. | Exact spelling, all caps with underscores. Top-level constant (not namespaced under Sketchup). |
| `LOAD_STATUS_SUCCESS_MORE_RECENT` | constant | Integer | 2021.0 | direct | Status constant indicating the file loaded but was saved by a MORE RECENT SketchUp version, so some information may have been skipped. | When you see this, treat the model as read-only — the docs explicitly warn extensions "should be careful to not save over the file they loaded from". The DefinitionList#load equivalent situation is controlled by allow_newer:. |
| `Sketchup.active_model` | class_method | Sketchup::Model or false | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | "Returns the currently active SketchUp model." Returns false if unsuccessful. | On Windows there is exactly ONE model and this is it. On macOS, where multiple models can be open, "the method will return the model that the user currently has focused" — you cannot enumerate or address the others from Ruby. This is the structural reason there is no multi-model Ruby workflow. |
| `Sketchup.save_thumbnail` | class_method | Boolean | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Generates a thumbnail image for ANY .skp file on disk, given the source .skp path and an output image path. | ROUTE 4 VERDICT: this IS a genuine read-a-file-on-disk-without-opening-it API — but it only produces a raster thumbnail. It cannot surface attributes, scenes, styles or any structured data. Useful in your exporter's UI as a preview of the source model alongside its scene list, nothing more. |
| `Sketchup.file_new` | class_method | Sketchup (the module) | 6.0 | no | "The file_new method is used to create a new file." | On Windows this replaces the active model just like open_file. Do not use it as part of a read strategy. |
| `Sketchup.temp_dir` | class_method | String | 6.0 | direct | "Used to retrieve the OS temporary directory for the current user." | USE THIS, not ENV['TEMP'] or '/tmp' — it is correct and writable on both Windows and macOS and respects the current user. This is where you place the unique per-read copy of model B that defeats the DefinitionList path cache. Returns forward-slash paths on Windows in most versions; normalise with File.join rather than string concatenation. |
| `Sketchup.version` | class_method | String | 6.0 | direct | Returns the SketchUp version "in decimal form" as a String. | String comparison is unreliable ('2026.0' vs '2021.1'); use version_number for gating. Stamp it into your payload for provenance. |
| `Sketchup.version_number` | class_method | Integer | 6.0 | direct | Returns the version as an Integer for numeric comparison. Format changed between releases: SketchUp 6.0 through 2015 use XXYYYZZZ; SketchUp 2016 and later use XXYZZZZZZZ. | Gate feature use with this, e.g. definitions.remove requires >= 18000000, Sketchup::Skp requires >= 21000000, AttributeDictionary#empty? requires >= 25000000. Beware the format change at 2016 if you ever support ancient versions. |
| `Sketchup.is_pro?` | class_method | Boolean | 6.0 | direct | Returns whether the running SketchUp is Pro. | Irrelevant to attribute reading; relevant if your exporter uses Pro-only exporters for the image output. |
| `Sketchup.read_default` | class_method | Object | 6.0 | encode | Reads a preference from the .INI/registry on Windows or the .plist on Mac, within SketchUp's own preferences store. | For GLOBAL plugin preferences, not per-document data — it is NOT part of any .skp and will not travel with a model. Cannot handle Length objects; convert to Float first. Documented cross-platform issues on macOS since SketchUp 2018's JSON format change; DanRathbun and others recommend an external JSON file under AppData/Application Support instead. |
| `Sketchup.write_default` | class_method | Boolean | 6.0 | encode | Writes a preference into SketchUp's preferences store. | Same caveats as read_default. Use for 'last export folder', 'default image size' — never for scene payloads. |
| `Sketchup.undo` | class_method | nil | 6.0 | no | Programmatically triggers an Undo. | DO NOT USE after removing a component definition inside an operation — api-issue-tracker #75 documents model corruption and crash from exactly the sequence start_operation / modify definition / definitions.remove / commit_operation / Sketchup.undo. Prefer start_operation + abort_operation for temporary work. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#add` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page | 6.0 | no | Adds a new page (scene) to the model. 2026.0 change: "Modified Sketchup::Pages#add so that when adding a new Sketchup::Page, if the specified name is already taken, it automatically modifies the name to ensure it is unique." Prior to 2026.0 it did NOT enforce unique names. | THE RECONSTRUCTION ENTRY POINT in model A. CRITICAL 2026 BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: because add now auto-renames on collision, the Page you get back may NOT have the name you asked for — always capture the return value and read back page.name rather than assuming, and re-key your JSON mapping off the returned object. On pre-2026 SketchUp you can silently create duplicate scene names. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#unique_name` | instance_method | String | 2026.0 | READ-ONLY | Generates a page name not already in use, derived from base_name. | New in 2026.0. Use it to pre-compute the name you will actually get, so your JSON-to-Page mapping is deterministic. Guard with respond_to?(:unique_name) for older versions. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#erase` | instance_method | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Removes a page from the collection. | Needed for a 'replace all scenes' import mode. Snapshot pages.to_a before erasing — never erase while iterating pages#each. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#reorder` | instance_method | nil | 2025.0 | no | Repositions a page within the collection; supports negative indices. | New in 2025.0. Before 2025 the only way to control scene order was the index argument to Pages#add at creation time — so on 2021-2024 you must create scenes in the correct order. Guard with respond_to?(:reorder). |
| `Sketchup::Pages#selected_page` | instance_method | Sketchup::Page | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Returns the currently active page. | Record it before a bulk import and restore it afterwards, otherwise the user's view jumps. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#selected_page=` | attribute | Boolean | 6.0 | no | Sets the active page. Documented to TRIGGER AN ANIMATED TRANSITION to that page. | The animation is asynchronous — setting selected_page then immediately reading the camera gives you the OLD camera. For a scene image exporter you must either set the transition time to zero (model.options['PageOptions']['TransitionTime'] = 0) or drive the camera directly rather than relying on page selection. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#size` | instance_method | Integer | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Total page count. | Also the default value for the index argument of Pages#add, i.e. append at the end. |
| `Sketchup::Pages#each` | instance_method | nil | 6.0 | READ-ONLY | Iterates all pages. | The serialisation loop on the export side. Do not mutate the collection inside the block. |
| `PAGE_USE_ALL` | constant | Integer | 6.0 | direct | Default flags value for Sketchup::Pages#add, meaning the new page saves all scene properties (camera, shadows, style, tags/layer visibility, section planes, hidden geometry, axes). | Top-level constant. Serialise the per-page flags value from the source model and pass it through on reconstruction, or the reconstructed scenes will not remember the same property set. |
| `SUModelCreateFromFile (C API)` | instance_method | SUModelRef | n/a (C API / SDK) | READ-ONLY | C API function that loads a .skp from disk into memory as a full SUModelRef, entirely outside SketchUp's document window. Paired with SUModelGetAttributeDictionary / SUModelGetNumAttributeDictionaries to read MODEL-LEVEL attribute dictionaries from an unopened file. | THIS IS THE ONLY ROUTE THAT TRULY ANSWERS THE FEASIBILITY QUESTION AS ASKED — it reads model-level dictionaries from model B without model B ever becoming the active model. It requires a compiled Ruby C extension (see SketchUp/ruby-c-extension-examples as a boilerplate, and SketchUp/sketchup-live-c-api which has been the officially supported pattern since SketchUp 2019.2). Cost: you must ship a per-platform, per-Ruby-ABI .so/.bundle/.dll and rebuild for each SketchUp Ruby version. For a scene exporter this is almost certainly disproportionate — use the carrier-definition plus sidecar-JSON architecture instead, and keep this in reserve. |
